# Visnu-purana. Fifth Book

## Book Five: Kṛṣṇa

## Chapter 1 - The union of Vasudeva and Devakī

## \|\| 5.1.1 \|\|

*sanskrit not found \|\| 1 \|\|*

You’ve described all the royal lineages in detail and duly recounted the deeds of each. 1

## \|\| 5.1.2 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*nṛpāṇāṃ kathitaḥsarvo bhavatā vaṃśavistaraḥ \|*  
*vaṃśānucaritaṃ caiva yathāvadanuvarṇitam \|*  
*aṃśāvatāro brahmarṣe yo 'yaṃ yadukulodbhavaḥ \|*  
*viṣṇostaṃ vistareṇāhaṃ śrotumicchāmi tattvataḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Now I wish to hear about that aspect of Viṣṇu, holy sage, who came to earth and manifested in the Yadu clan. 2

## \|\| 5.1.3 \|\|

*cakāra yāni karmāṇi bhagavān puruṣottamaḥ \|*  
*aṃśāṃśenāvatīryorvyāṃ tatra tāni mune vada \|\| 3 \|\|*

Tell me about the acts performed by Lord Kṛṣṇa, the supreme being, sage, after he appeared in the world as an aspect of Viṣṇu’s aspect. 3

## \|\| 5.1.4 \|\|

*śrīpāraśara uvāca*  
*maitreya śrūyatāmetadyatpṛṣṭo 'hamiha tvayā \|*  
*viṣṇoraṃśāṃśasaṃbhūticaritaṃ jagato hitam \|\| 4 \|\|*

Listen, Maitreya, to this account, about which you’ve questioned me, concerning the deeds of Kṛṣṇa, who appeared as an aspect of Viṣṇu’s aspect to benefit the world. 4

## \|\| 5.1.5 \|\|

*devakasya sutāṃ pūrvaṃ vasudevo mahāmune \|*  
*upayeme mahābhāgāṃ devakīṃ devatopamām \|\| 5 \|\|*

Long ago, Vasudeva married Devaka’s daughter, fortunate Devakī, great sage, who was the equal of a goddess. 5

## \|\| 5.1.6 \|\|

*kaṃsastayorvararathaṃ codayāmāsa sārathiḥ \|*  
*vasudevasya devakyā saṃyoge bhojanandanaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

After their wedding, Kaṃsa, benefactor of the Bhoja people, served as driver of the couple’s bridal carriage. 6

## \|\| 5.1.7 \|\|

*āthāntarikṣe vāguccaiḥ kaṃsamābhāṣya sādaram \|*  
*meghagaṃbhīranirghoṣaṃ samābhāṣyedamabravīt \|\| 7 \|\|*

As they made their way, a loud voice sounded in the sky, rumbling like thunder, and addressed these words to Kaṃsa: 7

## \|\| 5.1.8 \|\|

*yāmetāṃ vahase mūḍha saha bhartrā rathe sthitām \|*  
*asyās tavāṣṭamo garbhaḥ prāṇān apahariṣyati \|\| 8 \|\|*

‘Fool! The eighth child born of this woman whom you’re carrying with her husband in your chariot will end your life.’ 8

## \|\| 5.1.9 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityākarṇyaṃ samutpāṭya khaḍgaṃ kaṃso mahābalaḥ \|*  
*devakīṃ hantumārabdho vasudevo 'bravīdidam \|\| 9 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, mighty Kaṃsa drew his sword to strike at Devakī, but Vasudeva intervened, 9

## \|\| 5.1.10 \|\|

*na hantavyā mahābhāga devakī bhavatānagha \|*  
*samarpayiṣye sakalāngarbhānasyodarodbhavān \|\| 10 \|\|*

‘Don’t kill Devakī, great king! I’ll give you all the children delivered from her womb.’ 10

## \|\| 5.1.11 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tathetyāha tataḥ kaṃso vasudevaṃ dvijottama \|*  
*na ghātayāmāsa ca tāṃ devakīṃsatyagauravāt \|\| 11 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Kaṃsa acceded to this proposal, best of brahmins, and, as he respected Vasudeva, he spared the girl. 11

## \|\| 5.1.12 \|\|

*etasminnevakāle tu bhūribhārāvapīḍitā \|*  
*jagāma dharaṇī merau samājaṃ tridivaukasām \|\| 12 \|\|*

At that same time, the Earth, oppressed by her great burden, approached the thirteen deities assembled on Mount Meru. 12

## \|\| 5.1.13 \|\|

*sa brahmakānsurānsarvānpraṇipatyātha medini \|*  
*kathayāmāsa tatsarvaṃ khedātkaruṇabhāṣiṇī \|\| 13 \|\|*

Bowing to the gods with Brahmā at their head, the Earth recounted all her woes, her voice beset with misery: 13

## \|\| 5.1.14 \|\|

*bhūmiruvāca*  
*agniḥsuvarṇasya gururgavāṃ sūryaḥ paro guruḥ \|*  
*mamāpyakhilalo kānāṃ kururnārāyaṇo garuḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

The Earth:

Just as fire masters gold and the sun is lord of cattle, Nārāyaṇa is my sovereign, as he is of all the worlds. 14

## \|\| 5.1.15 \|\|

*prajāpatipatirbrahma pūrveṣāmapi pūrvajaḥ \|*  
*kalākāṣṭhānimeṣātmā kālaścāvyaktamūrtimān \|\| 15 \|\|*

He is Brahmā, lord of patriarchs, who arose before the earliest beings. He is formless Time composed of seconds, hours and minutes. 15

## \|\| 5.1.16 \|\|

*tadaṃśabhūtaḥsarvaiṣāṃ samūho vaḥsurottamāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Your entire assembly is just his aspect, best of gods. 16

## \|\| 5.1.17 \|\|

*ādityā sarutaḥsādhyā rudrā vasvaśvivahnayaḥ \|*  
*pitaro ye ca lokānāṃ sraṣṭāro 'tripurogamāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

The Ādityas, Maruts, Sādhyas, Rudras, Vasus, Aśvins, Vahnis and the ancestors led by Atri who made the worlds— 17

## \|\| 5.1.18 \|\|

*ete tasyāprameyasya viṣṇo rūpaṃ mahātmanaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

All these are forms of Viṣṇu, the immeasurable heart of greatness. 18

## \|\| 5.1.19 \|\|

*yakṣarākṣasadaiteyapiśācoragadānavāḥ \|*  
*gandharvāpsarasaścaivarūpaṃ viṣṇormahātmanaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

The yakṣas, rākṣasas and Daityas, the piśācas, nāgas and Dānavas, the gandharvas and apsarases are all but forms of mighty Viṣṇu. 19

## \|\| 5.1.20 \|\|

*graharkṣatārakācitragaganāgnijalānilāḥ \|*  
*ahaṃ ca viṣayāścaiva sarva viṣṇumayaṃ jagat \|\| 20 \|\|*

The sky with shining planets, constellations, stars, fire, water, wind, myself the Earth, the objects of the senses and all this world consist of him. 20

## \|\| 5.1.21 \|\|

*tathā cānekarūpasya yasya rūpāṇya harniśam \|*  
*bādhyabādhakatāṃ yānti kallolā iva sāgare \|\| 21 \|\|*

The forms of this many-formed being annul one another day and night, then are themselves annulled, like waves upon the ocean. 21

## \|\| 5.1.22 \|\|

*tatsāṃpratamamī daityāḥ kālanemipurogamāḥ \|*  
*martyalokaṃ samākramya bādhante 'harniśaṃ prajāḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

At this very moment, Daityas led by Kālanemi have overrun the world of men and continually oppress the populace. 22

## \|\| 5.1.23 \|\|

*kālanemirhato yo 'sau viṣṇunā prabhaviṣṇunā \|*  
*ugrasenasutaḥ kaṃsaḥsaṃbhūtaḥsa mahāsuraḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

The great demigod Kālanemi, slain by mighty Viṣṇu, has been reborn as Kaṃsa, son of Ugrasena. 23

## \|\| 5.1.24 \|\|

*āriṣṭo dhenukaḥ keśī pralaṃbo narakas tathā \|*  
*suṃdo 'surastathātyugro bāṇaścāpi baleḥsutaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Ariṣṭa, Dhenuka, Keśin, Pralamba, Naraka, the demigod Sunda and fearsome Bāṇa, son of Bali— 24

## \|\| 5.1.25 \|\|

*tathānye ca mahāvīryā nṛpāṇāṃ bhavaneṣu ye \|*  
*samutpannā durātmānastānna saṃkhyātumutsahe \|\| 25 \|\|*

I cannot count the number of these and other powerful, evil beings who’ve taken birth in the homes of men. 25

## \|\| 5.1.26 \|\|

*akṣohiṇyotra bahulā divyamūrtidharāḥsurāḥ \|*  
*mahābalānāṃ dṛptānāṃ daityendraṇāṃ mamopari \|\| 26 \|\|*

Many armies of strong and brilliant Daitya kings press down on me, gods of heavenly form. 26

## \|\| 5.1.27 \|\|

*tadbhūribhāra pīḍārtā na śaknomyamareśvarāḥ \|*  
*bibhartumātmānamahamiti vijñāpayāmi vaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

Suffering beneath the weight of this great burden, I can no longer support myself. That’s why I’m telling you, immortal lords. 27

## \|\| 5.1.28 \|\|

*kriyatāṃ tanmahābhāgā mama bhārāvatāraṇam \|*  
*yathā rasātalaṃ nāhaṃ gaccheyamativihvalā \|\| 28 \|\|*

Relieve me of this load, you fortunate beings, before I sink in misery to the lower realms. 28

## \|\| 5.1.29 \|\|

*ityākarṇya dharāvākyamaśeṣais tridaśeśvaraiḥ \|*  
*bhuvo bhārāvatārārthaṃ brahmā prāha pracoditaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Having heard the Earth’s complaint in full, Brahmā, urged by the thirteen gods, spoke these words to ease the burden that oppressed her. 29

## \|\| 5.1.30 \|\|

*brahmovāca*  
*yathāha vasudhā sarvaṃ satyameva divaukasaḥ \|*  
*ahaṃ bhavo bhavantaś ca sarve nārāyaṇātmakāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

Brahmā:

All the Earth has said is true, you denizens of heaven. Myself, Śiva, all of you and everything consists of Nārāyaṇa. 30

## \|\| 5.1.31 \|\|

*vibhūtayaś ca yāstasya tāsāmeva parasparam \|*  
*ādhikyanyū6natābādhyabādhakatvena vartate \|\| 31 \|\|*

Manifestations of his greatness, overcoming one another and being overcome, are at times more powerful, at other times less so. 31

## \|\| 5.1.32 \|\|

*tadāgacchata gacchāmaḥ kṣīrābdhestaṭamuttamam \|*  
*tatrārādhya hariṃ tasmai sarvaṃ vijñāpayāma vai \|\| 32 \|\|*

Come, therefore. Let us proceed to the Milky Ocean. We’ll worship Hari on its northern shore and refer all this to him. 32

## \|\| 5.1.33 \|\|

*sarvathaiva jagatyarthe sa sarvātmā jaganmayaḥ \|*  
*sattvāṃśenāvatīryorvyāṃ dharmasya kurute sthitim \|\| 33 \|\|*

That universal being, who is at the heart of everything, always manifests an aspect of himself on earth to uphold virtue and to aid the world. 33

## \|\| 5.1.34 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvā prayayo tatra saha devaiḥ pitāmahaḥ \|*  
*samāhitamanāścaivaṃ tuṣṭāva garuḍadhvajam \|\| 34 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, the grandsire of the world set out with all the other gods, and, having gathered his thoughts, he then praised Viṣṇu, whose emblem is Garuḍa. 34

## \|\| 5.1.35 \|\|

*brahmovāca*  
*dve vidye tvamanāmnāyaparā caivāparā tathā \|*  
*ta eva bhavato rūpe mūrtāmūrtātmike prabho \|\| 35 \|\|*

Brahmā:

You are both kinds of knowledge: that which is handed down in sacred texts and that which isn’t. You have two states, formed and formless, Lord. 35

## \|\| 5.1.36 \|\|

*dve brahmaṇī tvaṇīyotisthūlātmansarva sarvavit \|*  
*śabdabrahmaparaṃ caiva brahma brahmamayasya yat \|\| 36 \|\|*

You are two forms of the Absolute, gross and subtle. You are all and you know all. You are beyond the spiritual power of sound, you are the Absolute and that which consists of the Absolute. 36

## \|\| 5.1.37 \|\|

*ṛgvedastvaṃ yajurvedaḥsāmavedastvatharvaṇaḥ \|*  
*śikṣākalpo niruktaṃ ca cchando jyotiṣameva ca \|\| 37 \|\|*

You are the Ṛg, Yajur, Sāma and Atharva Vedas. You are the art of Vedic pronunciation, ritual practice, the explanations, metres and the science of the calendar. 37

## \|\| 5.1.38 \|\|

*itihāsapurāṇe ca tathā vyākaraṇaṃ prabho \|*  
*mīmāṃsā nyāyaśāstraṃ ca dharmaśāstrāṇyadhokṣaja \|\| 38 \|\|*

You are the histories, myths and grammar, Lord. You are philosophy, logic and the treatises on law, Adhokṣaja. 38

## \|\| 5.1.39 \|\|

*ātmā'tmadehaguṇavadvicārācāri yadvaca \|*  
*tadapyādyapate nānyadadhyātmātmasvarūpavat \|\| 39 \|\|*

You are the doctrine that investigates the links between the body, soul and qualities. That doctrine is none other than a form of your highest self, primeval Lord. 39

## \|\| 5.1.40 \|\|

*tvamavyaktamanirdeśyamacintyānāmavarmavat \|*  
*apāṇipādarūpaṃ ca śuddhaṃ nityaṃ parātparam \|\| 40 \|\|*

You are unmanifest, indescribable, inconceivable, lacking name and colour, hands and feet. You are Viṣṇu, the eternal highest state. 40

## \|\| 5.1.41 \|\|

*śṛṇoṣyakarṇaḥ paripaśyasi tvamacakṣurūpo bahurūparūpaḥ \|*  
*apādahasto javano grahītā tvaṃ vetsi sarvaṃ ca sarvavedyaḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

You hear without ears and see without eyes. You are one but have many forms. You move without feet and grasp without hands. You know all but are not to be known by all. 41

## \|\| 5.1.42 \|\|

*aṇoraṇīyāṃ samasatsvarūpaṃ tvāṃ paśyato jñānanivṛttiragryā \|*  
*dhīrasya dhīrasya bibharti nānyadvareṇyarūpātparataḥ parātman \|\| 42 \|\|*

Beholding you as smaller than the smallest and having nonexistent form, one’s ignorance will vanish. Nothing else beyond your blissful form supports the wisdom of the wise. You are the highest being. 42

## \|\| 5.1.43 \|\|

*tvaṃ viśvanābhir bhuvanasya goptā sarvāṇi bhūtāni tavāntarāṇi \|*  
*yadbhūtabhavyaṃ yadaṇoraṇīyaḥ pumāṃs tvam ekaḥ prakṛteḥ parastāt \|\| 43 \|\|*

You are everything, the beginning and the protector of the world. All beings exist in you. You are all that has existed and all that will exist. You are more subtle than the subtlest. You alone are Spirit, which stands apart from primal Nature. 43

## \|\| 5.1.44 \|\|

*ekaścaturdhā bhagavānhutāśo varcovibhūtaṃ jagato dadāsi \|*  
*tvaṃ viśvataścakṣuranantamūrte tredhā padaṃ tvaṃ nidadhāsi dhātaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

You are one but also fourfold lordly fire, consumer of the sacrifice, which sheds its light and splendour on the world. You are the eye of all. Your forms are endless. You, arranger of the universe, took three steps. 44

## \|\| 5.1.45 \|\|

*yathāgnireko bahudhā samidhyate vikārabhedair avikārarūpaḥ \|*  
*tathā bhavānsarvagataikarūpī rūpāṇyaśeṣāṇyanupuṣyatīśa \|\| 45 \|\|*

Just as fire, kindled in many ways, is always one, and its essential nature doesn’t alter with its changing form, you, Lord, have one all-pervading form, though the forms you animate are many. 45

## \|\| 5.1.46 \|\|

*ekaṃ tavāgryaṃ paramaṃ padaṃ yatpaśyanti tvāṃ sūrayo jñānadṛśyam \|*  
*tvatto nānyatkiñcidasti svarūpaṃ yadvā bhūtaṃ yacca bhavyaṃ parātman \|\| 46 \|\|*

You are the single foremost highest state, whom sages see with the eye of knowledge. There is nothing that has been or that will be, supreme spirit, other than your form. 46

## \|\| 5.1.47 \|\|

*vyaktāvyaktasvarūpastvaṃ samaṣṭivyaṣṭirūpavān \|*  
*sarvajñaḥsarvanitsarvaśaktijñānabalardhimān \|\| 47 \|\|*

Your form is manifest and unmanifest. You are both totality and individuality, all-knowing and all-seeing, possessor of omnipotence, knowledge, strength and plenitude. 47

## \|\| 5.1.48 \|\|

*anyūnaścāpyavṛddhiś ca svādhīno nādimānvaśī \|*  
*klamatandrī bhayakrodhakāmādibhir asaṃyutaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

You are not subject to loss or gain. You are independent, beginningless and powerful. You are untouched by fatigue, sloth, fear, anger, desire and other faults. 48

## \|\| 5.1.49 \|\|

*niravadyaḥ paraḥ prāpterniradhiṣṭho 'kṣaraḥ kramaḥ \|*  
*sarveśvaraḥ parādhāro dhāmnāṃ dhāmātmako 'kṣayaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

Blameless, supreme, tranquil, sovereign, your progress is unalterable, you are lord of all, the ultimate foundation of everything, the indestructible abode of all abodes. 49

## \|\| 5.1.50 \|\|

*sakalāvaraṇānītanirālaṃbanabhāvana \|*  
*mahāvibhūtisaṃsthāna namaste puruṣottama \|\| 50 \|\|*

Beyond all envelopments, unreached by imagination, the locus of great energy—highest spirit, we bow to you. 50

## \|\| 5.1.51 \|\|

*nākāraṇātkāraṇādvā kāraṇākāraṇānna ca \|*  
*śarīragrahaṇaṃ vāpi dharmatrāṇāya kevalam \|\| 51 \|\|*

Not without a cause, not with a cause, but neither with nor without a cause, you are the highest being, taking human form to uphold virtue. 51

## \|\| 5.1.52 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityevaṃ saṃstavaṃ śrutvā manasā bhagavānajaḥ \|*  
*brahmāṇamāha prītena viśvarūpaṃ prakāśayan \|\| 52 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Having mentally perceived this song of praise, Hari, the unborn lord at the heart of all, the bearer of universal form, replied to Brahmā. 52

## \|\| 5.1.53 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*bhobho brahmaṃstvayā mattaḥsaha devair yadiṣyate \|*  
*taducyatāmaśeṣaṃ ca siddhamevāvadhāryatām \|\| 53 \|\|*

The Lord:

Greetings, Brahmā. Tell me all that you and the other gods desire from me, and success will be assured. 53

## \|\| 5.1.54 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tato brahmā harerdivya viśvirūpamavekṣya tat \|*  
*tuṣṭāva bhūyo deveṣu sādhvasāvanatātmasu \|\| 54 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Then Brahmā, beholding the divine and universal form of Hari, praised him again, while all the gods bowed down in trepidation. 54

## \|\| 5.1.55 \|\|

*brahmovāca*  
*namo namastestu sahasrakṛtvaḥsahasrabāho bahuvaktrapāda \|*  
*namonamaste jagataḥ pravṛttivināśasaṃsthānakarāprameya \|\| 55 \|\|*

Brahmā:

We bow to you and bow again, deity of a thousand forms, a thousand arms and many limbs and faces. We bow to you and bow again, boundless cause of creation, preservation and destruction of the world. 55

## \|\| 5.1.56 \|\|

*sūkṣmātisūkṣmātibṛhatpramāma garīyasāmapyatigauravātman \|*  
*pradhānabuddhīndriyavatpradhānamūlātparātmanbhagavanprasīda \|\| 56 \|\|*

Your subtlety is beyond the subtle; your measure beyond measure. You are greater than the greatest. You are at the heart of intellect and senses. You are the supreme heart that lies beyond the root of both. Be merciful to us, Lord. 56

## \|\| 5.1.57 \|\|

*eṣā mahī deva mahīprasūtair mahāsuraiḥ pīḍitaśailabandhā \|*  
*parāyaṇaṃ tvāṃ jagatāmupaiti bhārāvatārārthamapārasāra \|\| 57 \|\|*

The Earth, whose ranges are oppressed by mighty demons reborn there, has come to you, Lord, hoping that you’ll relieve her burden, as you are the final refuge of all the worlds and your limits are unattainable. 57

## \|\| 5.1.58 \|\|

*ete vayaṃ vṛtraripustathāyaṃ nāsatyadastrau varuṇastathaiva \|*  
*ime ca rudrā vasavaḥsasūryāḥsamīraṇāgniprasukhāstathānye \|\| 58 \|\|*

We’re all here: this is Indra, slayer of the demon Vṛtra. Here are the Aśvins, Nāsatya and Dasra. This is Varuṇa, and here are the Rudras, Vasus, Suns and others led by Winds and Fires. 58

## \|\| 5.1.59 \|\|

*surāḥsamastāḥsuranāthakāryamebhir mayā yacca tadīśa sarvām \|*  
*ājñāpayājñāṃ paripālayantastavaiva tiṣṭhāma sadāstadoṣāḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

These are all the deities, Lord of gods. Tell us all that they and I must do, Lord, as we stand ever ready to follow your commands, faultless deity. 59

## \|\| 5.1.60 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ saṃstūyamānastu bhagavānparameśvaraḥ \|*  
*ujjahārātmanaḥ keśau sitakṛṣṇau mahāmune \|\| 60 \|\|*

Parāśara:

While they praised him in this manner, the lord, the supreme master, plucked two hairs, great sage, one white, one black. 60

## \|\| 5.1.61 \|\|

*uvāca ca surānetau matkeśau vasudhātale \|*  
*avatīrya bhuvo bhārakleśahāniṃ kariṣyataḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

‘Having descended to the world,’ he told he gods, ‘these two hairs of mine will ease the burden of the Earth. 61

## \|\| 5.1.62 \|\|

*surāś ca sakalāḥsvāṃśair avatīrya mahītale \|*  
*kurvantu yuddhamunmattaiḥ pūrvotpannair mahāsuraiḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

Manifesting aspects of themselves on earth, let all the gods wage war against the furious and powerful demigods already there. 62

## \|\| 5.1.63 \|\|

*tataḥ kṣayamaśeṣāste daiteyā dharamītale \|*  
*prayāsyanti na saṃdeho maddṛpātavicūrṇitāḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

Then all the Daityas in the world will go to their destruction. There’s no doubt that when my gaze falls upon them, they’ll be turned to dust. 63

## \|\| 5.1.64 \|\|

*vasudevasya yā patnī devakī devatopamā \|*  
*tatrāyamaṣṭamo garbho matkeśo bhavitā surāḥ \|\| 64 \|\|*

Vasudeva’s wife is Devakī, the equal of a goddess. One of my hairs, you deities, will be her eighth-born child. 64

## \|\| 5.1.65 \|\|

*avatīrya ca tatrāyaṃ kaṃsaṃ ghātayitā bhuvi \|*  
*kālanemīṃ samudbhūtamityuktvāntardadhe hariḥ \|\| 65 \|\|*

Having descended to the earth, this being will slay Kaṃsa, a manifestation of the demon Kālameni.’ So saying, Hari withdrew from sight. 65

## \|\| 5.1.66 \|\|

*adṛśyāya tatastasmai praṇipatya mahāmune \|*  
*merupṛṣṭhaṃ surā jagmuravateruś ca bhūtale \|\| 66 \|\|*

The deities bowed before that great unseen being, then returned to the summit of Mount Meru whence they descended to the world. 66

## \|\| 5.1.67 \|\|

*kaṃsāya cāṣṭamo garbho devakyā dharaṇīdharaḥ \|*  
*bhaviṣyatītyāca cakṣe bhagavānnārado muniḥ \|\| 67 \|\|*

But Nārada, the lordly sage, told Kaṃsa that Viṣṇu, supporter of the earth, would manifest as Devakī’s eighth child. 67

## \|\| 5.1.68 \|\|

*kaṃso 'pi tadupaśrutya nāradātkupitastataḥ \|*  
*devakīṃ vasudevaṃ ca gṛhe guptāvadhārayat \|\| 68 \|\|*

Hearing this from Nārada, furious Kaṃsa imprisoned Devakī and Vasudeva in his palace. 68

## \|\| 5.1.69 \|\|

*vasudevena kaṃsāya tenaivoktaṃ yathā purā \|*  
*tathaiva vasudevo 'pi putram arpitavān dvija \|\| 69 \|\|*

As he’d promised previously, brahmin, Vasudeva surrendered every child to Kaṃsa as soon as it was born. 69

## \|\| 5.1.70 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipoḥ putrāḥṣaḍgarbhā iti viśrutāḥ \|*  
*viṣṇuprayuktā stānnidrākramādgarbhānayojayat \|\| 70 \|\|*

It’s said that Hiraṇyakaśipu’s sons were born as the first six infants. At the command of Viṣṇu, the goddess Nidrā placed them one by one in the womb of Devakī. 70

## \|\| 5.1.71 \|\|

*yoganidrā mahāmāyā vaiṣṇavī mohitaṃ yayā \|*  
*avidyayā jagatsarvaṃ tāmāha bhagavānhariḥ \|\| 71 \|\|*

Lord Hari spoke to Yoganidrā, a form of his own deceptive power, who veils the world in ignorance. 71

## \|\| 5.1.72 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*nidre gaccha mamādeśātpātālātalasaṃśrayān \|*  
*ekaikatvena ṣaḍgarbhāndevakījaṭharaṃ naya \|\| 72 \|\|*

The Lord:

Nidrā, go at my command and bring six infants from the depths of Pātāla and place them one by one in Devakī’s womb. 72

## \|\| 5.1.73 \|\|

*hateṣu teṣu kaṃsena śeṣākhyoṃśastato mama \|*  
*aṃśāṃśonādare tasyāḥsaptamaḥ saṃbhaviṣyati \|\| 73 \|\|*

When they’ve all been slain by Kaṃsa, an aspect of Śeṣa, and therefore an aspect of an aspect of me, will become the seventh infant in her belly. 73

## \|\| 5.1.74 \|\|

*gokule vasudevasya bhāryānyā rohiṇī sthitā \|*  
*tasyāḥsa saṃbhūtisamaṃ devi neyastvayodaram \|\| 74 \|\|*

Another of Vasudeva’s wives, Rohiṇī, resides in the cattle camp of Gokula. Before the child is born, goddess, take him to that woman. 74

## \|\| 5.1.75 \|\|

*saptamo bhojarā jasya bhayādrodhoparodhataḥ \|*  
*devakyāḥ pitito garbha iti loko vadiṣyati \|\| 75 \|\|*

Folk will say that Devakī miscarried the seventh baby because she feared the King of Bhoja and the stress of prison life. 75

## \|\| 5.1.76 \|\|

*garbhasaṃkarṣaṇātso 'tha loke saṃkarṣaṇeti vai \|*  
*saṃjñāmavāpsyate vīraḥ śvetādriśikharopamaḥ \|\| 76 \|\|*

But as he’ll be drawn out (saṃkarṣaṇāt) from the womb, he’ll be called Saṃkarṣaṇa. He’ll be a hero as resplendent as the summit of a snowy mountain. 76

## \|\| 5.1.77 \|\|

*tato 'haṃ saṃbhaviṣyāmi devakījaṭhare śubhe \|*  
*bhargaṃ tvayā yaśodāyā gantavyamavilaṃbitam \|\| 77 \|\|*

I’ll then enter the womb of Devakī, fair one, and you will enter Yaśodā’s. 77

## \|\| 5.1.78 \|\|

*prāvṛṭkāle ca nabhasi kṛṣṇāṣṭamyāmahaṃ niśi \|*  
*utpatsyāmi navamyāṃ tu prasūtiṃ tvamavāpsyasi \|\| 78 \|\|*

I’ll be born on the eighth night of the dark fortnight of the month of Nabhas during the rainy season, and you’ll be born just one night later. 78

## \|\| 5.1.79 \|\|

*yaśodāśayane māṃ tu devakyāstvāmanindite \|*  
*macchaktiproritamatirvasudevo nayiṣyati \|\| 79 \|\|*

With his mind in my control, Vasudeva will bear me to Yaśodā’s bed, and he’ll take you to Devakī’s, blameless goddess. 79

## \|\| 5.1.80 \|\|

*kaṃsaś ca tvāmupādāya devi śailaśilātale \|*  
*prakṣepsyatyantārikṣe ca saṃsthānaṃ tvamavāpsyasi \|\| 80 \|\|*

Kaṃsa will seize you, goddess, and dash you on a rock, but you’ll escape into the sky. 80

## \|\| 5.1.81 \|\|

*tatastvāṃ śatadṛkchakraḥ praṇamya mama gauravāt \|*  
*praṇipātānataśirā bhaginītve grahīṣyati \|\| 81 \|\|*

On account of his respect for me, hundred-eyed Indra will honour you and, bowing his head, will accept you as his sister. 81

## \|\| 5.1.82 \|\|

*tvaṃ ca śuṃbhaniśuṃbhādīnhatvā daityānsahasraśaḥ \|*  
*sthānair anekaiḥ pṛthivīmaśeṣāṃ maṇḍayiṣyasi \|\| 82 \|\|*

Once you’ve defeated Śumbha, Niśumbha and other Daityas by the thousand, you’ll adorn the world in many places. 82

## \|\| 5.1.83 \|\|

*tvaṃ bhūtiḥ sannatiḥ kṣāntiḥ kāntirdyauḥ pṛthivī dhṛtiḥ \|*  
*lajjāpuṣṭī ruṣā yā tu kācidanyā tvameva sā \|\| 83 \|\|*

You are prosperity, progeny, fame, patience, heaven and earth, firmness, modesty, nourishment, dawn and every other feminine quality. 83

## \|\| 5.1.84 \|\|

*ye tvāmāryeti durgeti vedagarbhāṃbiketi ca \|*  
*bhadreti bhadrakālīti kṣemadā bhagyadeti ca \|\| 84 \|\|*

Those who address you as Āryā, Durgā, Vedagarbhā, Ambikā, Bhadrā, Bhadrakālī, Kṣemyā and Kṣemakarī, 84

## \|\| 5.1.85 \|\|

*prātaścaivāparāhne ca stoṣyantyānamramūrtayaḥ \|*  
*teṣāṃ hi prārthitaṃ sarvaṃ matprasādādbhaviṣyati \|\| 85 \|\|*

And who bow down and praise you in the morning and the afternoon, will have all their wishes granted by my grace. 85

## \|\| 5.1.86 \|\|

*surāmāṃsopaharaiś ca bhakṣyabhojyaiś ca pūjitā \|*  
*nṝṇāmaśeṣasāmāṃstvaṃ prasannā saṃpradāsyasi \|\| 86 \|\|*

Men will worship you with offerings of liquor, meat and various foods and, if you’re satisfied, you may grant them all that they desire. 86

## \|\| 5.1.87 \|\|

*sanskrit not found \|\| 87 \|\|*

By my mercy, madam, it’s certain that they’ll never be in danger. Go now, goddess, and do as I command. 87

## Chapter 2 - The deities praise Devakī

## \|\| 5.2.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*yathoktaṃ sā jagaddhātrā devadevena vai tathā \|*  
*ṣaḍgarbhagarbavinyāsaṃ cakre cānyasya karṣaṇam \|\| 1 \|\|*

As besought by Viṣṇu, god of gods, Yoganidrā, nursemaid of the world, then placed six infants one by one in Devakī’s womb and bore away the seventh. 1

## \|\| 5.2.2 \|\|

*saptame rohiṇīṃ garbhe prāpte garbhaṃ tato hariḥ \|*  
*lokatra yopakārāya devakyāḥ praviveśa ha \|\| 2 \|\|*

When Devakī had borne that seventh child to Rohiṇī, Hari entered her womb to benefit the threefold worlds. 2

## \|\| 5.2.3 \|\|

*yoganidrā yaśodāyāstasminneva tathā dine \|*  
*saṃbhūtā jaṭhare tadvadyathoktaṃ parameṣṭhinā \|\| 3 \|\|*

On that very day, Yoganidrā entered Yaśodā’s belly, as the highest being had instructed. 3

## \|\| 5.2.4 \|\|

*tato grahagaṇaḥsamyakpracacāra divi dvija \|*  
*viṣṇoraṃśe bhuvaṃ yāte ṛtavaścābabhuḥ śubhāḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

When Viṣṇu’s aspect came into the world, all the planets proceeded in perfect order through the heavens, brahmin, and the weather was delightful. 4

## \|\| 5.2.5 \|\|

*na sehe devakīṃ draṣṭaṃ kaśtidapyatitejasā \|*  
*jājvalyamānāṃ tāṃ dṛṣṭvā manāṃsi kṣobhamāyayuḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

None could set their eyes on Devakī because she shone so brightly, and the minds of those who saw her glowing thus were filled with wonder. 5

## \|\| 5.2.6 \|\|

*adṛṣṭāḥ puruṣaiḥ strībhir devakīṃ devatāgaṇāḥ \|*  
*bibhrāṇāṃ vapuṣā viṣṇuṃ tuṣṭuvustāmaharniśam \|\| 6 \|\|*

By day and night, hosts of gods, unseen by men and women, praised Devakī with Viṣṇu in her womb. 6

## \|\| 5.2.7 \|\|

*devatā ūcuḥ*  
*prakṛtistvaṃ parā sūkṣmā brahmagarbhābhavatpurā \|*  
*tato vāṇī jagaddhāturvedagarbhāsi śobhane \|\| 7 \|\|*

The deities:

You were prakṛti, the supreme and subtle natural state, in which Brahmā arose in former times. You were the power of speech of the deity who supports the world, and the womb that held the Vedas, splendid woman. 7

## \|\| 5.2.8 \|\|

*sṛjyasvarūpagarbhāsi sṛṣṭibhūtā sanātane \|*  
*bījabhūtā tu sarvasya yajñabhūtā bhavastrayī \|\| 8 \|\|*

You are the womb of creation and creation itself, eternal being, the seed of all and womb of the threefold sacrifice. 8

## \|\| 5.2.9 \|\|

*phalagarbhā tvamevejyā vahnigarbhā tathāraṇiḥ \|*  
*aditirdevagarbhā tvaṃ daityagarbhā tathā ditiḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

You are the womb of the outcome, offering, fire and kindling. As Aditi, you are the womb of gods; as Diti, the Daityas’s womb. 9

## \|\| 5.2.10 \|\|

*jyotsnā vāsaragarbhā tvaṃ jñānagarbhāsi sannatiḥ \|*  
*nayagarbhā parā nītirlajjā tvaṃ praśrayodvahā \|\| 10 \|\|*

You are light, the womb of day. You are humility, the womb of knowledge. You are prudence, womb of conduct. You are modesty, origin of respect. 10

## \|\| 5.2.11 \|\|

*kāmagarbhā tathecchā tvaṃ tuṣṭiḥ saṃtoṣagarbhiṇī \|*  
*medhā ca bodhagarbhasi dhairyagarbhodvahā dhṛtiḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

You are desire, the womb of love. You are satisfaction, contentment’s womb. You are intelligence, womb of understanding. You are resolve, the womb of firmness. 11

## \|\| 5.2.12 \|\|

*graharkṣatārakāgarbhā dyaurasyākhilahaitukī \|*  
*etā vibhūtayo devi tathānyāś ca sahasraśaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

You are the heavens, womb of planets, stars and lunar mansions. You are the cause of all the world. These are your powers, goddess, and you have others by the thousand. 12

## \|\| 5.2.13 \|\|

*tathā saṃkhyā jagaddhātrī sāṃprataṃ jaṭhare tava \|*  
*samudrādrinadīdvīpavanapattanabhūṣaṇā \|*  
*grāmakharvaṭakheṭāḍhyā samastā pṛtivī śubhe \|\| 13 \|\|*

Similarly, at this moment, within your womb, glorious nursemaid of the universe, rests all the world, resplendent with its countless seas, rivers, lands, forests and cities, replete with hamlets, towns and villages. 13

## \|\| 5.2.14 \|\|

*samastavahnayoṃ'bhāṃsi sakalāś ca samīraṇāḥ \|*  
*mahoragās tathā yakṣā rākṣasāḥ pretagudyakāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

All the fires, winds and waters; the sky providing space for everything, adorned with planets, stars and mansions, and filled with airborne chariots by the hundred; 14

## \|\| 5.2.15 \|\|

*graharkṣatārakācitravimānaśatasaṃkutam \|*  
*avakāśamaśeṣasya yaddadāti nabhasthalam \|\| 15 \|\|*

The earthly, aerial and heavenly spheres; the realms of Mahar, Jana, Tapas and Brahmā; and that deity’s cosmic egg in full, you splendid woman; 15

## \|\| 5.2.16 \|\|

*bhūlokaś ca bhuvarlokaḥsvarloko 'tha maharjanaḥ \|*  
*tapaś ca brahmalokaś ca brahmāṇḍamakhilaṃ śubhe \|\| 16 \|\|*

And all the beings who occupy these realms: gods, Daityas, gandharvas, cāraṇas, great serpents, yakṣas, rākṣasas, ghosts and guhyakas, 16

## \|\| 5.2.17 \|\|

*tadantare sthitā devā daityagandharvacāraṇāḥ \|*  
*mahoragās tathā yakṣā rākṣasāḥ pretaguhyakāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Humankind, beasts and all other living things, you best of women—all these comprise the everlasting lord of all, and exist within him, creator of the universe. 17

## \|\| 5.2.18 \|\|

*manuṣyāḥ paśavaścānye ye ca jīvā yaśasvini \|*  
*tair antasthair ananto 'sau sarvagaḥ sarvabhāvanaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Viṣṇu, whose every aspect, including forms and deeds, is beyond the realm of comprehension, now lies within your womb. 18

## \|\| 5.2.19 \|\|

*rūpakarmasvarūpāṇi na paricchedagocare \|*  
*yasyākhilapramāṇāni sa viṣṇur garbhagas tava \|\| 19 \|\|*

You are svāhā. You are svadhā. You are knowledge, nectar and the sky’s own light. You came to earth to protect the worlds. 19

## \|\| 5.2.20 \|\|

*tvaṃ svāhā tvaṃ svadhā vidyā svadhā tvaṃ jyotiraṃbare \|*  
*tvaṃ sarvalokarakṣārthamavatīrṇā mahītale \|\| 20 \|\|*

Be merciful, goddess, and bless this world. Bear the lord with fondness, gracious woman, as the world is borne by him. 20

## Chapter 3 - The advent of Kṛṣṇa

## \|\| 5.3.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ saṃstūyamānā sā devair devamadhārayat \|*  
*garbheṇa puṇḍarīkākṣaṃ jagatastrāṇakāraṇam \|\| 1 \|\|*

Thus praised by gods, Devakī bore in her womb heavenly Viṣṇu, the lotus-eyed saviour of the world. 1

## \|\| 5.3.2 \|\|

*tato 'khilajagatpadmabodhāyācyutabhānunā \|*  
*devakīpūrvasaṃdhyāyāmāvirbhūtaṃ mahātmanā \|\| 2 \|\|*

Just as the rising sun at dawn wakes the lotus flower, the great eternal Viṣṇu appeared in the womb of Devakī so that the world might blossom. 2

## \|\| 5.3.3 \|\|

*tajjanmadinamatyarthamāhlādyamaladiṅmukham \|*  
*babhūva karvalokasya kaumudī śaśino yathā \|\| 3 \|\|*

The day of his birth spread great joy in all directions, just as moonlight graces the surface of the earth. 3

## \|\| 5.3.4 \|\|

*saṃtaḥsaṃtoṣamadhakaṃ praśamaṃ cañjamārutāḥ \|*  
*prasādaṃ nimnagā yātā jāyamāne janārdane \|\| 4 \|\|*

Good people experienced deep contentment, cruel winds were stilled and streams flowed with tranquillity when Janārdana was born. 4

## \|\| 5.3.5 \|\|

*siṃdhavo nijaśabdena vādyaṃ cakrurmanoharam \|*  
*jagurgandharvapatayo nanṛtuścāpsarogaṇāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Rivers made sweet music with their murmuring, gandharva lords sang praises, while hosts of apsarases danced. 5

## \|\| 5.3.6 \|\|

*sasṛjuḥ puṣpavarṣāṇi devā bhuvyantarikṣagāḥ \|*  
*jajvaluścāgnayaḥ śāntā jāyamāne janārdane \|\| 6 \|\|*

The deities, coursing through the heavens, showered flowers upon the earth, and fires burned bright and steadily when Janārdana appeared. 6

## \|\| 5.3.7 \|\|

*madaṃ jagurjurjaladāḥ puṣpavṛṣṭimuco dvija \|*  
*ardharātre 'khilādhāre jāyamāne janārdane \|\| 7 \|\|*

At midnight, brahmin, when Janārdana, foundation of the universe, was born, thunderclouds rumbled deeply while shedding flowers like rain. 7

## \|\| 5.3.8 \|\|

*phullendīvarapatrābhaṃ caturbāhumudīkṣya tam \|*  
*śrīvatsavakṣasaṃ jātaṃ tuṣṭāvānakadundubhiḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Beholding the newborn infant, as blue as the petal of a waterlily, in four-armed form with Śrīvatsa on his chest, Vasudeva praised him. 8

## \|\| 5.3.9 \|\|

*abhiṣṭraya ca taṃ vāgbhiḥ prasannābhir mahāmatiḥ \|*  
*vijñāpayāmāsa tadā kaṃsādbhīto dvijottama \|\| 9 \|\|*

When that wise man had hailed the infant with lofty words, best of brahmins, fearing Kaṃsa, he added this: 9

## \|\| 5.3.10 \|\|

*vasudeva uvāca*  
*jātosi devadeveśa śaṅkhacakragadādharam \|*  
*divyarūpamidaṃ deva prasādenopasaṃhara \|\| 10 \|\|*

Vasudeva:

I recognise you, Lord, god of gods, bearer of the conch, the mace and discus. Be merciful, Lord, and withdraw this heavenly form. 10

## \|\| 5.3.11 \|\|

*adyaiva deva kaṃsoyaṃ kurute mama ghātanam \|*  
*avatīrma iti jñātvā tvamasminmamamandire \|\| 11 \|\|*

This very day, Lord, Kaṃsa will take revenge on me when he learns that you’ve appeared in my abode. 11

## \|\| 5.3.12 \|\|

*devakyuvāca*  
*yo 'nantarūpo 'khilaviśvarūpo garbhepi lokānvapuṣā bibharti \|*  
*prasītadāmeṣa sa devadevo yo māyayāviṣkṛtabālarūpaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Devakī:

May this god of gods—whose forms are boundless and universal, who embraced the worlds within himself while still within my womb, and who, by his own illusory power, assumed this infant form—be merciful. 12

## \|\| 5.3.13 \|\|

*upasaṃhara sarvātmanrūpametaccaturbhujam \|*  
*jānātu māvatāraṃ te kaṃsoyaṃ ditijanmajaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Withdraw this four-armed form, universal being, so that Diti’s grandson Kaṃsa may never learn of your descent. 13

## \|\| 5.3.14 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*stuto 'haṃ ya ttvayā pūrvaṃ putrārthinyā tadadya te \|*  
*saphalaṃ devi saṃjātaṃ jāto 'haṃ yattavodarāt \|\| 14 \|\|*

The Lord:

Since you praised me in former times, hoping for a son, today your wish will be fulfilled, princess, as I’ve been delivered from your womb. 14

## \|\| 5.3.15 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvā bhagavāṃstūṣṇīṃ babhūva munisattama \|*  
*vasudevopi taṃ rātrāvādāya prayayau bahiḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After he had spoken, the lord fell silent, best of sages, and Vasudeva picked him up and carried him through the darkness. 15

## \|\| 5.3.16 \|\|

*mohitāścābhavestatra rakṣiṇo yoganidrayā \|*  
*mathurādvārapālāś ca vrajatyānakadundubhau \|\| 16 \|\|*

The guards at the gates of Mathurā fell under Yoganidrā’s spell, and Vasudeva slipped past undetected. 16

## \|\| 5.3.17 \|\|

*varṣatāṃ jaladānāṃ ca toyamātyulbaṇaṃ niśi \|*  
*saṃvṛtyānuyayau śeṣaḥ phaṇair ānakadundubhim \|\| 17 \|\|*

The serpent Śeṣa followed Vasudeva, and with his hoods gave shelter to the infant from the deluge falling from the clouds that night. 17

## \|\| 5.3.18 \|\|

*yamunāṃ cātigaṃbhīrāṃ nānāvartaśatākulām \|*  
*vasudevo vahanviṣṇuṃ jānumātravahāṃ yayau \|\| 18 \|\|*

As Vasudeva carried Viṣṇu, the Yamunā, profound and swirling with a hundred varied eddies, reached only to his knees. 18

## \|\| 5.3.19 \|\|

*kaṃsasya karadānāya tatraivābyāgatāstaṭe \|*  
*nandādīngopavuddhāṃś ca yamunāyā dadarśa saḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

Nanda and the other cowherd elders assembled on the riverbank with tribute for King Kaṃsa, where Vasudeva saw them. 19

## \|\| 5.3.20 \|\|

*tasminkāle yaśodāpi mohitā yoganidrayā \|*  
*tāmeva kanyāṃ maitreya prasūtā mohite jane \|\| 20 \|\|*

At that moment Yaśodā delivered Yoganidrā in the form of a baby girl, Maitreya, but she and all the others present succumbed to the goddess’s spell. 20

## \|\| 5.3.21 \|\|

*vasudevopi vinyasya bālamādāya dārikām \|*  
*yaśodāśayanāttūrṇamājagāmāmitadyutiḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

Vasudeva of boundless splendour laid the boy in Yaśodā’s bed and hastened back with that young girl. 21

## \|\| 5.3.22 \|\|

*dadṛśe ca prabuddhā sā yaśodā jātamātmajam \|*  
*nīlotpaladalaśyāmaṃ tatotyarthaṃ mudaṃ yayau \|\| 22 \|\|*

When she woke, Yaśodā saw the newborn baby boy, dark as an azure lotus petal, and was filled with greatest joy. 22

## \|\| 5.3.23 \|\|

*ādāya vasudevo 'pi dārikāṃ nijamandire \|*  
*devakīśayane nyasya yathāpūrvamatiṣṭhata \|\| 23 \|\|*

Vasudeva took the girl to his abode, placed her in Devakī’s bed and waited by her side. 23

## \|\| 5.3.24 \|\|

*tato bāladhvaniṃ śrutvā rakṣiṇaḥ sahasotthitāḥ \|*  
*kaṃsāyāvedayāmāsurdevakīprasavandvija \|\| 24 \|\|*

When they heard the infant’s cries, brahmin, the guards leapt up and told the king that Devakī had given birth. 24

## \|\| 5.3.25 \|\|

*kaṃsastūrṇamupetyaināṃ tato jagraha bālikām \|*  
*muñcamuñceti devakyā sannakaṇṭhyā nivāritaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

In rushed Kaṃsa to seize the child, but Devakī tried to stop him. ‘Let her go! Let her go!’ she cried. 25

## \|\| 5.3.26 \|\|

*cikṣepa ca śilāpṛṣṭhe sā kṣiptā viyati sthitā \|*  
*avāpa rūpaṃ sumahatsāyudhāṣṭamahābhujam \|\| 26 \|\|*

He sought to dash the infant on a rock, but as soon as she had left his hand, she rose into the air above and assumed a mighty eight-armed form, a weapon in each hand. 26

## \|\| 5.3.27 \|\|

*prajahāsa tathaivoccaiḥ kaṃsaṃ ca ruṣitābravīt \|*  
*kiṃ mayā kṣiptayā kaṃsa jātā yastvāṃ vadhiṣyati \|\| 27 \|\|*

This furious being laughed aloud and said to him, ‘Why throw me to the ground, Kaṃsa? The one who’ll slay you is already born. 27

## \|\| 5.3.28 \|\|

*sarvasvabhūto devānāmāsīnmṛtyuḥ purā sa te \|*  
*tadetatsaṃpradhāryāśu kriyatāṃ hitamātmanaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

He is the essence of all the gods who caused your death in former times. Consider this, then quickly do what’s best for you.’ 28

## \|\| 5.3.29 \|\|

*ityuktvā prayayau devī divyasraggandhabhūṣaṇā \|*  
*paśyato bhojarājasya stutā siddhair vihāyasā \|\| 29 \|\|*

So saying, the goddess, resplendent in divine garlands and perfumes, departed, while the siddhas in the heavens sang her praises and the king of Bhoja stood and stared. 29

## Chapter 4 - Kaṃsa plots to kill Kṛṣṇa

## \|\| 5.4.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*kaṃsastatodvignamanāḥ prāha sarvānmahāsurān \|*  
*pralaṃbakeśipramukhānāhūyāsurapuṅgavān \|\| 1 \|\|*

Kaṃsa’s mind was troubled, so he summoned all the foremost demons, led by Keśin and Pralamba, and addressed those mighty beings. 1

## \|\| 5.4.2 \|\|

*kaṃsa uvāca*  
*he pralaṃba mahābāho keśin dhenuka pūtane \|*  
*ariṣṭādyāstathaivānye śrūyatāṃ vacanaṃ mama \|\| 2 \|\|*

Kaṃsa:

‘Strong-armed Pralamba, Keśin, Dhenuka, Pūtanā, Ariṣṭa and the rest of you must heed my words. 2

## \|\| 5.4.3 \|\|

*māṃ hantumamarair yatnaḥ kṛtaḥ kila durātmabhiḥ \|*  
*madvīryatāpitānvīro na tvetāngaṇayāmyaham \|\| 3 \|\|*

Those wicked gods, scorched by my prowess, tried to kill me, but I pay them no regard, you heroes. 3

## \|\| 5.4.4 \|\|

*kimindreṇālpavīryeṇa kiṃ hareṇaikacāriṇā \|*  
*hariṇā vāpi kiṃ sādhyaṃ chidreṣvasuraghātinā \|\| 4 \|\|*

I scorn that weakling Indra and that lonely yogin Śiva. What can Hari achieve by merely striking at our weaknesses? 4

## \|\| 5.4.5 \|\|

*kimādityaiḥ kiṃ vasubhir alpavīryaiḥ kimagnibhiḥ \|*  
*kiṃvānyair amaraiḥ sarvair madbāhubalanirjitaiḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

I scorn the Ādityas, accompanied by the timid Vasus. I scorn the Agnis and all the other deities defeated by my mighty arms. 5

## \|\| 5.4.6 \|\|

*kiṃ na dṛṣṭomarapatirmayā saṃyugametya saḥ \|*  
*pṛṣṭhenaiva vahanbāṇānapagacchannavakṣasā \|\| 6 \|\|*

Did I not see Indra, king of gods, retreating from the battlefield with arrows in his back? He couldn’t take them on the chest. 6

## \|\| 5.4.7 \|\|

*madrāṣṭre vāritā vṛṣṭiryadā śakreṇa kiṃ tadā \|*  
*madbāṇabhinnair jaladair āpo muktāyathepsitāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

When Indra stopped the rains from falling on my kingdom, did not the clouds, split asunder by my missiles, release their showers, just as I desired? 7

## \|\| 5.4.8 \|\|

*kimurvyāmavanīpālā madbāhubalabhīravaḥ \|*  
*te sarve sannatiṃ yātā jarāsaṃdhamṛte guram \|\| 8 \|\|*

Don’t all the kings on earth—except my father-in-law, Jarāsandha—fearing the strength of my arms, submit to me? 8

## \|\| 5.4.9 \|\|

*amareṣu mamāvajñā jāyate daityapuṅgavāḥ \|*  
*hāsyaṃ me jāyate vīrās teṣu yatnapareṣvapi \|\| 9 \|\|*

I despise those gods, you bulls among the Daitya heroes. I mock them as they make their feeble efforts. 9

## \|\| 5.4.10 \|\|

*tathāpi khalu duṣṭānāṃ teṣāmapyadhikaṃ mayā \|*  
*apakārāya daityendrā yatanīyaṃ durātmanām \|\| 10 \|\|*

Therefore, Daitya kings, I’ll strive to worst those base and evil-minded beings. 10

## \|\| 5.4.11 \|\|

*tadye yaśasvinaḥ kecitpṛthivyāṃ ye ca yajvanaḥ \|*  
*kāryo devāpakārāya teṣāṃ sarvātmanā vadhaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

To these ends, let each ascetic and every sacrificer in the world be put to death to disempower the gods. 11

## \|\| 5.4.12 \|\|

*utpannaścāpi me mṛtyurbhūtapūrvaḥsa vai kila \|*  
*ityetaddārikā prāha devakīgarbhasaṃbhavā \|\| 12 \|\|*

The infant girl, brought forth from the womb of Devakī, told me that the being who caused my death in previous lives is born again. 12

## \|\| 5.4.13 \|\|

*tasmādbāleṣu ca paro yatnaḥ kāryo mahītale \|*  
*yatrodriktaṃ balaṃ bāle sa hantavyaḥ prayatnataḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Make every effort, then, to search out all the boys on earth, and be sure to kill those with special powers.’ 13

## \|\| 5.4.14 \|\|

*ityājñāpyāsurānkasaḥ praviśyāśu gṛhaṃ tataḥ \|*  
*mumoca vasudevaṃ ca devakīṃ ca nirodataḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

After Kaṃsa gave this order to the Daityas, he returned to his abode and freed Vasudeva and Devakī from confinement. 14

## \|\| 5.4.15 \|\|

*kaṃsa uvāca*  
*yuvayorghātitā garbhā vṛthaivaite mayādhunā \|*  
*kopyanya eva nāśāya mama garbhaḥsamudyataḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Kaṃsa:

I killed your children for no purpose, as the one who’s destined to destroy me has escaped. 15

## \|\| 5.4.16 \|\|

*tadalaṃ paritāpena nunaṃ tadbhāvino hi te \|*  
*arbhakā yuvayordeṣāccāyuṣo yadviyojitāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Forget the woe that you’ve endured. No other child of yours will die before his natural span expires. 16

## \|\| 5.4.17 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityāśvāsya vimuktvā ca kaṃsastau pariśaṅkitaḥ \|*  
*antargṛhaṃ dvijaśreṣṭha praviveśa tataḥ svakam \|\| 17 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Reassuring Devakī and Vasudeva with these words and having set them free, best of brahmins, Kaṃsa retreated to the inner chambers, filled with doubt. 17

## Chapter 5 - Kṛṣṇa slays Pūtanā

## \|\| 5.5.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*vimukto vasudevo 'pi nandasya śakaṭaṃ gataḥ \|*  
*prahṛṣṭaṃ dṛṣṭavānnandaṃ putro jāto mameti vai \|\| 1 \|\|*

Now free, Vasudeva went to Nanda’s wagon, where he saw the joyous herdsman who thought the child was his. 1

## \|\| 5.5.2 \|\|

*vasudevopi taṃ prāha diṣṭyādiṣṭyeti sādaram \|*  
*vārdhake 'pi samutpannastanayo 'yaṃ tavādhunā \|\| 2 \|\|*

Vasudeva hailed him graciously, saying, ‘What a lucky man you are, for even in old age, you have a son! 2

## \|\| 5.5.3 \|\|

*datto hi vārṣikaḥsarvo bhavadbhir nṛpateḥ karaḥ \|*  
*yadarthamāgatāstasmānnātra stheyaṃ mahādhanaiḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

You’ve paid in full your annual tribute to the king (the reason that you came), but wealthy people shouldn’t tarry here. 3

## \|\| 5.5.4 \|\|

*yadarthamāgatāḥ kāryaṃ tanniṣpannaṃ kimasyate \|*  
*bhavadbhir gamyatāṃ nanda tacchīghraṃ nijagokulam \|\| 4 \|\|*

When the task that brought you here is done, why stay longer, Nanda? Return as quickly as you can to your own cattle camp. 4

## \|\| 5.5.5 \|\|

*mamāpi bālakastatra rohiṇī prabhavo hi yaḥ \|*  
*sa rakṣaṇīyo bhavatā yathāyaṃ tanayo nijaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Rohiṇī has given birth to my own son there. Protect him as you would your own.’ 5

## \|\| 5.5.6 \|\|

*ityuktāḥ prayayurgopā nandagopapurogamāḥ \|*  
*śakaṭāropitair bhāḍaiḥ karaṃ dattvā mahābalāḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Hearing this, the sturdy cattle-herders led by Nanda, having paid their tribute, set off with fully laden wagons. 6

## \|\| 5.5.7 \|\|

*vasatāṃ gokule teṣāṃ pūtanā bālaghātinī \|*  
*suptaṃ kṛṣṇamupādāya rātrau tasmai stanaṃ dadau \|\| 7 \|\|*

Once they’d settled back in camp, the child-killing demon Pūtanā snatched the baby Kṛṣṇa as he slept one night and offered him her breast. 7

## \|\| 5.5.8 \|\|

*yasmai yasmai stanaṃ rātrau pūtanā saṃprayacchati \|*  
*tasyatasya kṣaṇenāṅgaṃ bālakasyopahanyate \|\| 8 \|\|*

Any child that’s suckled by that fiend at night will die at once. 8

## \|\| 5.5.9 \|\|

*kṛṣṇastu tatstanaṃ gāḍhaṃ karābhyāmatipīḍitam \|*  
*gṛhītvā prāṇasahitaṃ papaukrodhasamanvitaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa, however, filled with rage, held her breast and squeezed it tight between his hands, then sucked the life right out of her. 9

## \|\| 5.5.10 \|\|

*sātimuktamahārāvā vicchinnastrāyubandhanā \|*  
*papāta pūtanā bhūmau mriyamāṇātibhīṣaṇā \|\| 10 \|\|*

Pūtanā screamed, her sinews snapped and that horrid fiend fell dying to the ground. 10

## \|\| 5.5.11 \|\|

*tannādaśrutisaṃtrastāḥ pravuddhāste vrajaukasaḥ \|*  
*dadṛśuḥ pūtanotsaṃge kṛṣṇaṃ tāṃ ca nipātitām \|\| 11 \|\|*

Startled by the demon’s cries, the residents of the camp awoke and found the baby on her lap, but she lay dead already. 11

## \|\| 5.5.12 \|\|

*ādāya kṛṣṇaṃ saṃtrastā yaśodāpi dvijottama \|*  
*gopucchabhrāmaṇenātha bāladoṣamapākarot \|\| 12 \|\|*

Frantic Yaśodā picked up Kṛṣṇa, best of brahmins, and waved a cow-tail whisk with one hand to ward off any evil force that might beset the child. 12

## \|\| 5.5.13 \|\|

*gokarīṣamupādāya nandagopopi mastake \|*  
*kṛṣṇasya pradado rakṣāṃ kurvaścaitadudīrayan \|\| 13 \|\|*

The cattle-herder Nanda took some powdered dung and sprinkled it on Kṛṣṇa’s head to give the child protection, then spoke this benediction: 13

## \|\| 5.5.14 \|\|

*nandagopa uvāca*  
*rakṣatu tvāmaśeṣāṇāṃ bhūtānāṃ prabhavo hariḥ \|*  
*yasya nābhisamudbhūtapaṅkajādabhavajjagat \|\| 14 \|\|*

Nanda:

May Hari, lord of all creation, protect you. From the lotus at his navel came the world. 14

## \|\| 5.5.15 \|\|

*yena daṃṣṭrāgravidhṛtā dhārayatyavanirjagat \|*  
*varāharūpadhṛgdevaḥsa tvāṃ rakṣatu keśavaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

May Keśava protect you. In the form of a boar, the deity drew the earth up with his tusk. 15

## \|\| 5.5.16 \|\|

*nakhāṅkuravinirbhinnavairivakṣasthalo vibhuḥ \|*  
*nṛsiṃharūpī sarvatra rakṣatu tvāṃ janārdanaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

May Keśava protect you from everything. In the form of Nṛsiṃha—half-man, half-lion—the lord tore apart the chest of his adversary with his claws. 16

## \|\| 5.5.17 \|\|

*vāmano rakṣatu sadā bhavantaṃ yaḥ kṣaṇādabhūt \|*  
*trivikramaḥ kramākrāntatrailokyaḥ sphuradāyudhaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

May he protect you always—he, who, as the dwarf, paced out the threefold world with three heroic strides in just one moment, his weapon glinting. 17

## \|\| 5.5.18 \|\|

*śiraste pātu govindaḥ kaṇṭhaṃ rakṣatu keśavaḥ \|*  
*guhyaṃ ca jaṭharaṃ viṣṇurjaghe pādau janārdanaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

May Govinda protect your head, Keśava your neck, Viṣṇu your genitals and belly and Janārdana your legs and feet. 18

## \|\| 5.5.19 \|\|

*mukhaṃ bāhū prabāhū ca manaḥ sarvedriyāṇi ca \|*  
*rakṣatvavyāhataiśvaryastava nārāyaṇo 'vyayaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

May eternal and irresistible Nārāyaṇa protect your head, your upper arms and forearms, your mind and all your senses. 19

## \|\| 5.5.20 \|\|

*śārṅgacakragadāpāṇeḥ śaṅkhanādahatāḥ kṣayam \|*  
*gacchantu pretakūṣmāṇḍarākṣasā ye tavāhitāḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

May any ghosts, kūṣmāṇḍas or rāksasas who trouble you meet their doom when struck by the blast of the conch sounded by the lordly wielder of the bow, the mace and discus. 20

## \|\| 5.5.21 \|\|

*tvāṃ pātu dikṣu vaikuṇṭho vidikṣu madhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*hṛṣīkeśoṃ'bare bhūmau rakṣatu tvāṃ mahīdharaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

May Vaikuṇṭha protect you at the cardinal points, and Madhusūdana at the points between. May Hṛṣīkeśa protect you in the sky, and Mahīdhara on earth. 21

## \|\| 5.5.22 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ kṛtasvastyayano nandagopena bālakaḥ \|*  
*śāyitaḥ śakaṭasyādho bālaparyaṅkikātale \|\| 22 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After the cattle-herder Nanda blessed the child with this benediction, he laid him in a cot beneath a wagon. 22

## \|\| 5.5.23 \|\|

*te ca gopā mahaddṛṣṭvā pūtanāyāḥ kalevaram \|*  
*mṛtāyāḥ paramaṃ trāsaṃ vismayaṃ ca tadā yayuḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

The other herdsmen, staring at the giant form of Pūtanā now lying dead, were filled with horror and amazement. 23

## Chapter 6 - Kṛṣna’s childhood

## \|\| 5.6.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*kadācicchakaṭasyādhaḥ śayāno madhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*cikṣepa caraṇāvūrdhvaṃ stanyārthī praruroda ha \|\| 1 \|\|*

One day, Madhusūdana, lying beneath the wagon and crying for the breast, kicked his legs about. 1

## \|\| 5.6.2 \|\|

*tasya pādaprahāreṇa śakaṭaṃ parivartitam \|*  
*vidhvastakuṃbhabhāṇḍaṃ tadviparītaṃ papāta vai \|\| 2 \|\|*

But when his feet touched its timbers, the wagon tipped and landed upside down, smashing all the pots and other vessels that it carried. 2

## \|\| 5.6.3 \|\|

*tato hāhākṛtaḥ sarvo goparopījano dvija \|*  
*ājagāmātha dadṛśe bālamutānaśāyinam \|\| 3 \|\|*

All the herding men and women cried in anguish, brahmin, and, rushing to him, saw the infant in his cot. 3

## \|\| 5.6.4 \|\|

*gopāḥ keneti kenedaṃ śakaṭaṃ parivartitam \|*  
*tatraiva bālakāḥ procurbālenānena pātitam \|\| 4 \|\|*

‘Who tipped the wagon?’ asked the herdsmen. ‘That baby,’ said some children in reply. 4

## \|\| 5.6.5 \|\|

*rudatā dṛṣṭamasmābhiḥ pādavikṣepapātitam \|*  
*śakaṭaṃ parivṛttaṃ vai naitadanyasya ceṣṭitam \|\| 5 \|\|*

‘We heard him crying, then he kicked the wagon with his feet, but no-one else has touched it.’ 5

## \|\| 5.6.6 \|\|

*tataḥ punaratīvāsangopā vismayacetasaḥ \|*  
*nandagopopi jagrāha bālamatyantavismitaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

The herdsmen were all filled with wonder. Most surprised of all was Nanda, who lifted up the child. 6

## \|\| 5.6.7 \|\|

*yaśodā śakaṭārūḍhabhagnabhāṇḍakapālikā \|*  
*śakaṭaṃ cārcayāmāsa dadhipuṣpaphalākṣataiḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Yaśodā then made an offering to the wagon and the broken pots with yoghurt, flowers, fruit and grains. 7

## \|\| 5.6.8 \|\|

*gargaś ca gokule tatra vasudevapracoditaḥ \|*  
*pracchanna eva gopānāṃ saṃskārāna karottayoḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Without the knowledge of the herdsmen, Vasudeva later sent the priest named Garga to carry out the birth rites for both infants in the camp. 8

## \|\| 5.6.9 \|\|

*jyeṣṭhaṃ ca rāmamityāha kṛṣṇaṃ caiva tathāvaram \|*  
*gargo matimatāṃ śreṣṭho nāma kurvanmahāmatiḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Prudent Garga, foremost of the wise, chose their names and called the elder Balarāma and the other Kṛṣṇa. 9

## \|\| 5.6.10 \|\|

*svalpenaiva tu kālena raṅgiṇau tau tadā vrahe \|*  
*ghṛṣṭajānukarau vipra babhūvaturubhāvapi \|\| 10 \|\|*

Before too long, these two boys were crawling round the cattle camp, brahmin, with scratches on their hands and knees. 10

## \|\| 5.6.11 \|\|

*karīṣabhasmadigdhāṅgau bhramamāṇāvitastataḥ \|*  
*na nivārayituṃ sehe yaśodā tau na rohiṇī \|\| 11 \|\|*

They roamed about in dung and ashes, but neither Yaśodā nor Rohiṇī could restrain them. 11

## \|\| 5.6.12 \|\|

*govāṭamadhye krīḍantau vatsavāṭaṃ gatau punaḥ \|*  
*tadaharjātagovatsapucchākarṣaṇatatparau \|\| 12 \|\|*

First, they played in the cowstall and then in the nursery pen, where they amused themselves by pulling the tails of the day-old calves. 12

## \|\| 5.6.13 \|\|

*yadā yaśodā tau bālāvekasthānacarāvubhau \|*  
*śaśāka no vārayituṃ krīḍantāvaticañcalau \|\| 13 \|\|*

Yaśodā couldn’t keep those feisty, fun-filled boys in place. 13

## \|\| 5.6.14 \|\|

*dāmnā madhye tato baddhā babandha tamulūkhale \|*  
*kṛṣṇamakliṣṭakarmāṇamāha cedamamarṣitā \|\| 14 \|\|*

So she tied Kṛṣṇa to a heavy wooden mortar with a rope around his middle, and in frustration scolded the lively child: 14

## \|\| 5.6.15 \|\|

*yadi śaknoṣi gaccha tvamaticañcalaceṣṭita \|*  
*ityuktvātha nijaṃ karma sā cakāra kuṭuṃbinī \|\| 15 \|\|*

‘Now let’s see if you’ll escape,’ she said as she resumed her chores. ‘You’re just too active!’ 15

## \|\| 5.6.16 \|\|

*vyāgrāyāmatha tasyāṃ sa karṣamāma ulūkhalam \|*  
*yamalārjunamadhyena jagāma kamalekṣaṇaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

While Yaśodā was busy with her duties, the lotus-eyed boy dragged the mortar between two arjuna trees. 16

## \|\| 5.6.17 \|\|

*karṣatā vṛkṣayormadhye tiryaggatamulūkhalam \|*  
*bhagnāvuttuṅgaśākhāgrau tena tau yamalārjunau \|\| 17 \|\|*

As he pulled the weight along, it lodged between the trunks, whose branches stretched towards the skies. Both trees toppled over! 17

## \|\| 5.6.18 \|\|

*tataḥ kaṭakaṭaśabdasamākarmanatatparaḥ \|*  
*ājagāma vrajajano dadarśa ca mahādrumau \|\| 18 \|\|*

Hearing the mighty crash, the residents of the camp rushed up and saw the two huge arjunas. 18

## \|\| 5.6.19 \|\|

*navodgatālpadantāṃśusitahāsaṃ ca bālakam \|*  
*tayormadhyagataṃ dāmnā baddhaṃ gāḍhaṃ tathodare \|\| 19 \|\|*

Their trunks were broken and branches shattered, lying on the ground. The boy, whose smile was bright with new milk teeth, sat between the fallen trunks, the cord still tied around his middle. 19

## \|\| 5.6.20 \|\|

*tataś ca tāmodaratāṃ sa yayau dāmabandhanāt \|\| 20 \|\|*

And so they called him Dāmodara, because he had a rope (dāman) around his waist (udara). 20

## \|\| 5.6.21 \|\|

*gopavṛddhāstataḥ sarve nandagopapurogamāḥ \|*  
*mantrayāmāsurudvignā mahotpātātibhīravaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

All the elder herdsmen, with Nanda at their head, were troubled and conferred together, fearing some evil presence. 21

## \|\| 5.6.22 \|\|

*sthāneneha na naḥ kāryaṃ vrajāmonyanmahāvanam \|*  
*utpātā bahavo hyatra dṛśyante nāśahetavaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

‘We can’t stay here. Let’s find another forest. We’ve seen many evil omens that portend our doom: 22

## \|\| 5.6.23 \|\|

*pūtanāyā vināśaś ca śakaṭasya viparyayaḥ \|*  
*vinā vātādidoṣeṇa drumayoḥ patanantathā \|\| 23 \|\|*

The death of Pūtanā, the upturned wagon, now these trees have fallen without a breath of wind. 23

## \|\| 5.6.24 \|\|

*vṛndāvanamitaḥsthānāttasmādgacchāma mā ciram \|*  
*yāvadbhaumamahotpātadoṣo nābhibhavevdrajam \|\| 24 \|\|*

Let’s move at once from here to Vṛndāvana, before this local evil presence ruins our camp.’ 24

## \|\| 5.6.25 \|\|

*iti kṛtvā matiṃ sarve gamane te vrajaukasaḥ \|*  
*ūcuḥ svaṃ svaṃ kulaṃ śīghraṃ gamyatāṃ mā vilaṃbatha \|\| 25 \|\|*

So saying, all the residents of the camp agreed to leave and instructed every family to depart without delay. 25

## \|\| 5.6.26 \|\|

*tataḥ kṣaṇena prayayuḥ śakaṭair godhanais tathā \|*  
*yūthaśo vatsapālāś ca kālayanto vrajaukasaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

A short time later, the herdsmen left with all their cattle and their wagons, with one child per herd to mind the calves. 26

## \|\| 5.6.27 \|\|

*dravyāvayavanirdhūtaṃ kṣaṇamātreṇa tattathā \|*  
*kākabhāsasamākīrṇaṃ vrajasthānamabhūddūja \|\| 27 \|\|*

Before long, the campsite, strewn with disused chattels, brahmin, was left to crows and vultures. 27

## \|\| 5.6.28 \|\|

*vṛndāvanaṃ bhagavatā kṛṣṇenā kliṣṭakarmaṇā \|*  
*śubhena manasā dhyātaṃ gavaṃ siddhimabhīpsatā \|\| 28 \|\|*

Untiring Lord Kṛṣṇa, wishing only for the increase of the herd, set his joyous mind on Vṛndāvana. 28

## \|\| 5.6.29 \|\|

*tatastatrātirūkṣe 'pi gharmakāle dvijottama \|*  
*prāvṛṭkāla ivodbhūtaṃ navaśaṣpaṃ samantataḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

For there, even in the harshest heat, best of brahmins, fresh grass flourishes everywhere, as in the rainy season. 29

## \|\| 5.6.30 \|\|

*sa samāvāsitaḥ sarvo vrajo vṛndāvane tataḥ \|*  
*śakaṭāvāṭaparyantaścandrārdhākārasaṃsthitaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

In Vṛndāvana, they set up camp and placed the wagons in a crescent round its edge. 30

## \|\| 5.6.31 \|\|

*vatsapālau ca saṃvṛttau rāmadāmodarau tataḥ \|*  
*eka sthanasthitau goṣṭhe ceraturbālalīlayā \|\| 31 \|\|*

Now that Balarāma and Dāmodara were a little older, they tended all the calves together and stayed within the camp itself, intent on childish games. 31

## \|\| 5.6.32 \|\|

*barhipatrakṛtāpīḍau vanyapuṣpāvataṃsakau \|*  
*gopaveṇukṛtātodyapatravādyakṛtasvanau \|\| 32 \|\|*

They added peacock feathers to their wreaths of forest flowers, playing tunes on rustic flutes, homely tambourines and instruments made from leaves. 32

## \|\| 5.6.33 \|\|

*kākapakṣadharau bālau kumārāviva pāvakī \|*  
*hasaṃtau ca ramantau ca ceratuḥ sma mahāvanam \|\| 33 \|\|*

Their hair was trimmed in ‘crow’s-wing’ style, and they looked like sons of Agni as they roamed and laughed and played in that great forest. 33

## \|\| 5.6.34 \|\|

*kvacidvahantāvanyonyaṃ krīḍamānau tathā paraiḥ \|*  
*gopaputraiḥ samaṃ vatsāṃścārayantau viceratuḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

At times they joked together, at times they played with others. Roving with the herdsmen’s sons, they led the calves to pasture. 34

## \|\| 5.6.35 \|\|

*kālena gacchatā tau tu saptavarṣau mahāvraje \|*  
*sarvasya jagataḥ pālau vatsapālau babhūvatuḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

With the passage of time, by the age of seven, these two universal lords ruled the calves in the cattle camp. 35

## \|\| 5.6.36 \|\|

*prāvṛṭkālastatotīva meghaughasthagitāṃbaraḥ \|*  
*babhūva vāridhārābhir aikyaṃ kurvandiśāmiva \|\| 36 \|\|*

When the annual rains arrived, the sky was filled with thunderclouds, binding together all four quarters as if with torrents of rain. 36

## \|\| 5.6.37 \|\|

*prarūḍhanavaśaṣpāḍhyā śakragopācitā mahī \|*  
*tathā mārakatīvāsītpadmarāgavibhūṣitā \|\| 37 \|\|*

Abounding in fresh green grass and dotted with sturdy herdsmen, the earth resembled an emerald field spread with reddest rubies. 37

## \|\| 5.6.38 \|\|

*ūhurunmārgavāhīni nimnagāṃbhāṃsi sarvataḥ \|*  
*manāṃsi durvinītānāṃ prāpya lakṣmīṃ navāmiva \|\| 38 \|\|*

The river waters burst their banks and flowed in all directions, like minds of fickle folk overwhelmed by newfound wealth. 38

## \|\| 5.6.39 \|\|

*na rejentaritaścandro nirmalo malinair ghanaiḥ \|*  
*sadvādivādo mūrkhaṇāṃ pragalbhābhir ivoktibhiḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

The unstained moon then lost its gleam, concealed by lowering clouds, just like the words of truthful men amid the din of fools. 39

## \|\| 5.6.40 \|\|

*nirguṇenāpi cāpena śakrasya gagane padam \|*  
*avāpyatāvivekasya nṛpasyaiva parigrahe \|\| 40 \|\|*

Indra’s bow, although unstrung, still found a place in heaven, like a worthless hanger-on in the court of a feckless king. 40

## \|\| 5.6.41 \|\|

*meghapṛṣṭhe balākānāṃ rarāja vimalā tatiḥ \|*  
*durvatte vṛttaceṣṭeva kulīnasyātiśaubhanā \|\| 41 \|\|*

Cranes shone in pure formations against the clouds, just as the ways of well-bred folk contrast with those of rogues. 41

## \|\| 5.6.42 \|\|

*na ba bandhāṃbare sthairyaṃ vidyudatyantacañcalā \|*  
*maitrīva pravare puṃsi durjanena prayojitā \|\| 42 \|\|*

Fickle lightning flashed inconstant in the sky, like the friendship of a scoundrel with a man of virtue. 42

## \|\| 5.6.43 \|\|

*mārgā babhūvur aspaṣṭās tṛṇaśaṣpacayāvṛtāḥ \|*  
*arthāntaramanuprāptāḥ prajaḍānāmivoktayaḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

Paths overgrown with mats of grass were difficult to follow, like the twisted arguments of fools. 43

## \|\| 5.6.44 \|\|

*unmattaśikhisāraṅge tasminkāle mahāvane \|*  
*kṛṣṇarāmau mudāyuktau gopālaiś ceratuḥ saha \|\| 44 \|\|*

During that season, in the mighty forest, home to frenzied bees and peacocks, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, filled with joy, roamed together with the other lads. 44

## \|\| 5.6.45 \|\|

*kvicidgobhiḥ samaṃ ramyaṃ goyatānaratāvubhau \|*  
*ceratuḥ kvacidatyarthaṃ śītavṛkṣatalāśritau \|\| 45 \|\|*

At times those two enjoyed a song while wandering happily with the cows; at others, when the days were cold, they’d shelter beneath the trees. 45

## \|\| 5.6.46 \|\|

*kvacitkadaṃbasrakcitrau mayūrasragvirājitau \|*  
*viliptau kvacidāsātāṃ vividhair giridhātubhiḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

At times they wore kadamba garlands, at others peacock feathers. Sometimes they daubed themselves with coloured minerals brought from distant mountains. 46

## \|\| 5.6.47 \|\|

*parṇaśayyāsu saṃsuptau kvacinnidrāntaraiṣiṇau \|*  
*kvacidgarjati jīmūte hāhākāraravākulau \|\| 47 \|\|*

Sometimes, feeling weary, they slept on beds of leaves. At others, on hearing a peal of thunder, they’d cry out in surprise. 47

## \|\| 5.6.48 \|\|

*gāyatāmanya gopānāṃ praśaṃsāparamau kvacit \|*  
*mayūrakekānugatau gopaveṇupravādakau \|\| 48 \|\|*

Sometimes they praised the cowherds’ songs, and played their rustic flutes accompanied by peacocks. 48

## \|\| 5.6.49 \|\|

*iti nānāvidhair bhāvair uttamaprītisaṃyutau \|*  
*krīḍantau tau vane tasmiñceratustuṣṭamānasau \|\| 49 \|\|*

In these varied states and filled with purest joy, the two boys roamed to their hearts’ content while playing in the forest. 49

## \|\| 5.6.50 \|\|

*vikāle ca samaṃ gobhir gopavṛndasamanvitau \|*  
*vihṛtyātha yathāyogaṃ vrajametya mahābalau \|\| 50 \|\|*

When dusk approached, these two strong lads returned to camp at leisure together with the herds and other boys. 50

## \|\| 5.6.51 \|\|

*gopaiḥ samānaiḥ sahitau krīḍantāvamarāviva \|*  
*evaṃ tāvūṣatustatra rāmakṛṣṇau mahādyutī \|\| 51 \|\|*

There, like a pair of deities, they romped amid the cowherd folk. 51

## Chapter 7 - Kṛṣṇa subdues the serpent Kāliya

## \|\| 5.7.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ekadā tu vinā rāmaṃ kṛṣṇo vṛndāvanaṃ yayau \|*  
*vicacāra vṛto gopair vanyapuṣpasragujjvalaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

One day, leaving Balarāma in the camp, Kṛṣṇa went to Vṛndāvana. Surrounded by the herding boys and wearing a splendent garland of forest flowers, he roamed about at will. 1

## \|\| 5.7.2 \|\|

*sa jagāmātha kālindīṃ lola kallolaśālinīm \|*  
*tīrasaṃlagnaphenaughair hasantīm iva sarvataḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Now, Kṛṣṇa came to the Yamunā, filled with swirling streams. Its banks lay hidden under drifts of foam, and the laughter of its currents seemed to mock the world around. 2

## \|\| 5.7.3 \|\|

*tasyāścatimahābhīmaṃ viṣāgniśritavāriṇam \|*  
*hradaṃ kālīyanāgasya dadarśativibhīṣaṇam \|\| 3 \|\|*

There, he beheld the frightful pool where the nāga Kāliya lurked, with flames and poison rising from its horrid depths. 3

## \|\| 5.7.4 \|\|

*viṣāgninā prasaratā dagdhatīraṃmahīruham \|*  
*vātāhatāṃbuvikṣepasparśadagdhavihaṅgamam \|\| 4 \|\|*

Mighty trees on the riverbank were scorched by fire and venom spewing from the pool. Its waters, whipped up by winds, touched passing birds and scalded them. 4

## \|\| 5.7.5 \|\|

*tamatīva mahā raudraṃ mṛtyuvakramivāparam \|*  
*vilokya cintayāmāsa bhagavānmadhusūdanaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Seeing that terrifying pool, gaping like a second maw of Death, Lord Madhusūdana reflected: 5

## \|\| 5.7.6 \|\|

*asminvasati duṣṭātmā kālīyo 'sau viṣāyudhaḥ \|*  
*yo mayā nirjitastyaktvā duṣṭo gacchetpayonidhim \|\| 6 \|\|*

‘This must be the lair of wicked Kāliya, whose weapon is his poison. Last time I beat this evil beast, it left the sea and disappeared. 6

## \|\| 5.7.7 \|\|

*teneyaṃ dūṣitā sarvā yamunā sāgaraṅgamā \|*  
*na naraigodhanaiś cāpi tṛṣārtair upabhujyate \|\| 7 \|\|*

He’s so defiled the Yamunā, consort queen of Sāgara, that neither thirsty man nor beast can drink from it. 7

## \|\| 5.7.8 \|\|

*tadasya nāgarājasya kartavyo nigraho mayā \|*  
*nistrāsāstu sukhaṃ yena careyurvrajavāsinaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Let me slay this nāga king, so residents of the cattle camp may roam here happily forever. 8

## \|\| 5.7.9 \|\|

*etadarthaṃ tu lokesminnavatāraḥ kṛto mayā \|*  
*yadeṣāmutpathasthānāṃ kāryā śāntirdurātmanām \|\| 9 \|\|*

I descended to the world of men to pacify such wicked beings that take the path of evil. 9

## \|\| 5.7.10 \|\|

*tadetaṃ nātidūrasthaṃ kadaṃbamuruśākhinam \|*  
*adirudya patiṣyāmi hrade 'sminnanilāśinaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

That kadamba tree with its strong limbs is not too far away. I’ll climb up it, then dive into the serpent’s pool.’ 10

## \|\| 5.7.11 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*itthaṃ vicintya baddhā ca gāḍhaṃ parikaraṃ tataḥ \|*  
*nipapāta hrade tatra nāgarājasya vegataḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

Parāśara:

With this thought, he tightened his waistband, then leapt at once into the watery lair of the nāga king. 11

## \|\| 5.7.12 \|\|

*tenātipatatā tatra kṣobhitaḥsa mahāhradaḥ \|*  
*atyarthaṃ dūrajātāṃstu samasiṃcanmahīruhān \|\| 12 \|\|*

That huge lake was set in motion by his dive, and its waters even splashed the trees standing at a distance. 12

## \|\| 5.7.13 \|\|

*te hi duṣṭaviṣajvālātaptāṃbupavanokṣitāḥ \|*  
*jajvaluḥ pādāpāḥsadyo jvālāvyāptadigantarāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Blasted by the wind and water heated by the energy of the serpent’s virulent poison, those trees burst into flame at once, and the conflagration spread to the horizon. 13

## \|\| 5.7.14 \|\|

*āsphoṭayāmāsa tadā kṛṣṇo nāgahrane bhujam \|*  
*tacchabdaśravaṇāccāśu nāgarājo 'bhyupāgamat \|\| 14 \|\|*

Now Kṛṣṇa was in the nāga’s pool, he clapped his hands. On hearing this, the serpent king then rushed towards him. 14

## \|\| 5.7.15 \|\|

*ātāmranayanaḥ kopādviṣajvālākulair mukhaiḥ \|*  
*vṛto mahāviṣaiś cānyair uragair anilāśanaiḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

His raging eyes gleamed like copper, his many hoods ablaze with venom, as other slithering poisonous snakes surrounded him. 15

## \|\| 5.7.16 \|\|

*nāgapatnyaś ca śataśo hārihāropaśobhitāḥ \|*  
*prakaṃpitatanukṣepacalatkuntasakāntayaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Hundreds of his nāga wives, adorned with gorgeous strings of pearls, their loveliness enhanced by the shaking of their earrings, swayed their trembling bodies. 16

## \|\| 5.7.17 \|\|

*tataḥ praveṣṭitaḥsarpaiḥ sa kṛṣṇe bhogabandhanaiḥ \|*  
*dadaṃśuste 'pi taṃ kṛṣṇaṃ viṣajvālākulair mukhaiḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

The serpents coiled themselves round Kṛṣṇa and struck him with their fangs that dripped with fiery poison. 17

## \|\| 5.7.18 \|\|

*taṃ tatra patitaṃ dṛṣṭvā sarpabhogaurnipīḍitam \|*  
*gopā vrajamupāgamya cukruśuḥ śokalālasāḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

When the other cowherds saw that he had dived into the pool and the serpents’ coils now held him, they were filled with terror and ran shouting back to camp. 18

## \|\| 5.7.19 \|\|

*gopā ūcuḥ*  
*eṣa mohaṃ gataḥ kṛṣṇo magnau vai kāliyahrade \|*  
*bhakṣyate nāgarājena tamāgacchata paśyata \|\| 19 \|\|*

The cowherds:

‘Kṛṣṇa’s mad! He’s jumped into Kāliya’s pool! The serpent king has eaten him! Come and see!’ 19

## \|\| 5.7.20 \|\|

*tacchutvā tatra te gopā vajrapā topamaṃ vacaḥ \|*  
*gopyaś ca tvarītā jagmuryaśodāpramukhā hradam \|\| 20 \|\|*

Hearing these cries, which struck them like a thunderbolt, the herding men and women, with Yaśodā in the lead, rushed down towards the pool. 20

## \|\| 5.7.21 \|\|

*hāhā kvāsāviti jano gopīnāmativihvalaḥ \|*  
*yaśodayā samaṃ bhrānto drutapraskhalitaṃ yayau \|\| 21 \|\|*

‘No, no! Where is he now?’ the herdsmen’s wives cried out in anguish. Together with Yaśodā, they stumbled forward in a panic. 21

## \|\| 5.7.22 \|\|

*nandagopaś ca gopāś ca rāmaścādbhutavikramaḥ \|*  
*tvaritaṃ yamunāṃ jagmuḥ kṛṣṇadarśanalālāsāḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Nanda, with the other herders and Balarāma of great prowess, rushed towards the Yamunā in the hope of seeing Kṛṣṇa. 22

## \|\| 5.7.23 \|\|

*dadṛśuścāpi te tatra sarparājavaśaṅgatam \|*  
*niṣprayatnīkṛtaṃ kṛṣṇaṃ sarpabhogaviveṣṭitam \|\| 23 \|\|*

There they found him, captive of the nāga king, wrapped within the serpents’ coils, yet Kṛṣṇa offered no resistance. 23

## \|\| 5.7.24 \|\|

*nandagopopi niśceṣṭo nyasya putramukhe dṛśam \|*  
*yaśodā ca mahābhāgā babhūva munisattama \|\| 24 \|\|*

The herdsman Nanda stood quite still and fixed his gaze on Kṛṣṇa’s face, best of brahmins, as did Yaśodā, that most fortunate of women. 24

## \|\| 5.7.25 \|\|

*gopyas tvanyā rudantyaś ca dadṛśuḥ śokakātarāḥ \|*  
*procuś ca keśavaṃ prītyā bhayakātaryagadgadam \|\| 25 \|\|*

The other herders’ wives looked on and wept in terror, crying out their love for Keśava, their voices choked with fear: 25

## \|\| 5.7.26 \|\|

*gopya ūcuḥ*  
*sarvā yaśodayā sārdha viśāmotra mahāhradam \|*  
*sarparājasya no gantumasmābhir yujyate vrajam \|\| 26 \|\|*

‘Let’s all jump in the nāga’s pool with Yaśodā. There’s no point in going back to camp. 26

## \|\| 5.7.27 \|\|

*divasaḥ so vinā sūryaṃ vinā candreṇa kā niśā \|*  
*vinā vṛṣeṇa kā gāvo vinā kṛṣṇena ko vrajaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

What’s the day without the sun? What’s night without the moon? What are cows without a bull? What’s the camp without our Kṛṣṇa? 27

## \|\| 5.7.28 \|\|

*vinākṛtā na yāsyāmaḥ kṛṣṇenānena gokulam \|*  
*aramyaṃ nātisevyaṃ ca vārihīnaṃ yathā saraḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

We won’t return to camp without him. Like a lake without its water, the forest has lost its charm. 28

## \|\| 5.7.29 \|\|

*yatra nendīvaraśyāmakāyakāntirayaṃ hariḥ \|*  
*tenāpi pāturvāsena ratirastīti vismayaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

Hari shines with loveliness like the petal of an azure lily. We’re amazed that anyone could savour life without him. 29

## \|\| 5.7.30 \|\|

*utphullapaṅkajadalaspaṣṭakāntivilocanam \|*  
*apaśyanto hariṃ dīnāḥ kathaṃ goṣṭhe bhaviṣyatha \|\| 30 \|\|*

If we poor creatures cannot see him, his eyes as bright and charming as the petal of a fully opened lotus, how will we survive in camp? 30

## \|\| 5.7.31 \|\|

*atyanta madhurālāpahṛtāśeṣamanoratham \|*  
*na vinā puṇḍarīkākṣaṃ yāsyāmo nandagokulam \|\| 31 \|\|*

We won’t go back to Nanda’s camp without that lotus-eyed boy who steals the treasure of our hearts with his sweet, sweet words. 31

## \|\| 5.7.32 \|\|

*bhogenāviṣṭitasyāpi sarparājasya paśyata \|*  
*smitaśobhimukhaṃ gopyaḥ kṛṣṇasyāsmadvilokane \|\| 32 \|\|*

See his face, you cowherd women. Even though the nāga king has wrapped him in his coils, he’s still smiling in our direction.’ 32

## \|\| 5.7.33 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*iti gopīvacaḥ śrutvā rauhiṇeyo mahābalaḥ \|*  
*gopāṃś ca trāsavidhurānvi lokya stimitekṣaṇān \|\| 33 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing the women’s words, Balarāma, sturdy son of Rohiṇī, looked with steady gaze upon the herdsmen, who were stiff with fear, 33

## \|\| 5.7.34 \|\|

*nandaṃ ca dīnamatyarthaṃ nyastadṛṣṭiṃ sutānane \|*  
*mūrchākulāṃ yaśodaṃ ca kṛṣṇamāhātmasaṃjñayā \|\| 34 \|\|*

And on Nanda, sunk in deepest misery while staring at his son, and on Yaśodā, who’d fallen in a swoon. Then, conscious of his brother’s actual greatness, he spoke these words to Kṛṣṇa. 34

## \|\| 5.7.35 \|\|

*kimidaṃ devadeveśa bhāvo 'yaṃ mānuṣastvayā \|*  
*vyajyatetyantamātmānaṃ kimanantaṃ na votsi yat \|\| 35 \|\|*

Balarāma:

Why, lordly god of gods, did you adopt this human form? Don’t you know your real self is endless and immortal? 35

## \|\| 5.7.36 \|\|

*tvameva jagato nābhir arāṇāmiva saṃśrayaḥ \|*  
*kartāpahartā pātā ca traulokyaṃ tvaṃ trayīmayaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

You are the navel of the world, as a hub is to the spokes. You are the threefold creator, preserver and destroyer of the triple realm. 36

## \|\| 5.7.37 \|\|

*sendrai rudrāgnivasubhir ādityair marudaśvibhiḥ \|*  
*cintyase tvamacintyātman samastaiś caiva yogibhiḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

The Ādityas, along with Indra, Rudra, the Aśvins, Vasus and the Winds and Fires, all contemplate you, whose true self is beyond conception, as do all the yogins. 37

## \|\| 5.7.38 \|\|

*jagatyarthaṃ jagannātha bhārāvataraṇecchayā \|*  
*avatīrṇo 'si martyeṣu tavāṃśaścāhamagrajaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

For the benefit of all, universal Lord, you descended here among these mortals, because you wished to ease the burden of the earth, and I, your elder brother, am but an aspect of you. 38

## \|\| 5.7.39 \|\|

*manuṣyalīlāṃ bhagavan bhajatā bhavatā surāḥ \|*  
*viḍambayantastvallīlāṃ sarva eva sahā sate \|\| 39 \|\|*

While you disport yourself as mortal, Lord, all the other deities imitate your pastimes. 39

## \|\| 5.7.40 \|\|

*avatārya bhavānpūrvaṃ gokule tu surāṅganāḥ \|*  
*krīḍārthamātmanaḥ paścādavartīrṇo 'si śāśvata \|\| 40 \|\|*

First you caused the goddesses to descend here to the cattle camp to join your games, and then you, eternal Lord, appeared. 40

## \|\| 5.7.41 \|\|

*atrāvatīrṇayoḥ kṛṣṇa gopā eva hi bāndhavāḥ \|*  
*gopyaś ca sīdataḥ kasmādetānbandhūnupekṣase \|\| 41 \|\|*

As we’ve manifested here, these herdsmen are our relatives, Kṛṣṇa, as are their despairing wives. Why disdain our kinfolk so? 41

## \|\| 5.7.42 \|\|

*darśito mānuṣo bhāvo darśitaṃ bālacāpalam \|*  
*tadayaṃ damyatāṃ kṛṣṇa duṣṭatmā daśanāyudhaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

You’ve shown the marks of a mortal being, and you’ve had your boyish fun. Now, Kṛṣṇa, you must destroy this wicked sharp-toothed snake. 42

## \|\| 5.7.43 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*iti saṃsmāritaḥ kṛṣṇaḥ smitabhinnoṣṭhasaṃpuṭaḥ \|*  
*āsphoṭya mocayāmāsa svadehaṃ bhogibandhanāt \|\| 43 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus reminded of his actual nature, Kṛṣṇa began to smile. He clapped his hands and freed himself from the serpents’ coils. 43

## \|\| 5.7.44 \|\|

*ānamya cāpi hastābhyāmubhābhyāṃ madhyamaṃ śiraḥ \|*  
*āruhyābhugnaśirasaḥ praṇanartoruvikramaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

Pressing down upon the centre of Kāliya’s hood with his two hands, then climbing on its lowered head, that valiant being began to dance upon it. 44

## \|\| 5.7.45 \|\|

*prāṇāḥ phaṇe 'bhavaṃścāsya kṛṣṇasyāṅghrinikuṭṭanaiḥ \|*  
*yatronnatiṃ ca kurute nanāmāsya tataḥ śiraḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

Wounds appeared on the serpent’s hood where Kṛṣṇa’s feet had struck it, but when Kāliya tried to raise his head, Kṛṣṇa forced it down again. 45

## \|\| 5.7.46 \|\|

*mūrchāmupāyayau bhrāntyā nāgaḥ kṛṣṇasya recakaiḥ \|*  
*daṇḍapātanipātena vavāma rudhiraṃ bahu \|\| 46 \|\|*

When Kṛṣṇa danced the recaka, the nāga swooned and swayed, and when he did the daṇḍapāta, the snake began to vomit blood. 46

## \|\| 5.7.47 \|\|

*taṃ vibhigraśirogrīvamāsyebhyasmutaśoṇitam \|*  
*vilokya karuṇaṃ jagmustatpatnyo madhusūdanam \|\| 47 \|\|*

Seeing his head and neck both broken as blood streamed from his jaws, Kāliya’s women came to Madhusūdana to beg for his protection. 47

## \|\| 5.7.48 \|\|

*nāgapatnya ūcuḥ*  
*jñāto 'si devadeveśa sarvajñastvamanuttamaḥ \|*  
*paraṃ jyotiracintyaṃ yattadaṃśaḥ parameśvaraḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

The nāga’s wives:

We know you, lordly god of gods. You are the peerless lord of all, the highest god, whose aspect is the finest light beyond imagination. 48

## \|\| 5.7.49 \|\|

*na samarthāḥ surāḥstotuṃ yamananyabhavaṃ vibhum \|*  
*svarūpavarṇanaṃ tasya kathaṃ yoṣitkariṣyati \|\| 49 \|\|*

Even the deities cannot fully praise the lord who arises from no other. How, then, could women like ourselves describe him? 49

## \|\| 5.7.50 \|\|

*yasyākhilamahīrvyomajalāgnipavanātmakam \|*  
*brahmāṇḍamalpakālpāṃśaḥ stoṣyāmastaṃ kathaṃ vayam \|\| 50 \|\|*

How can we fully praise him, when all the cosmic egg, comprising earth and sky, water, fire and wind, is but one small aspect of one part of him? 50

## \|\| 5.7.51 \|\|

*yamato na vidurnityaṃ yatsvarūpaṃ hi yoginaḥ \|*  
*paramārthamaṇoralpaṃ sthūlātsthūlaṃ natāḥ sma tam \|\| 51 \|\|*

We bow to him whose form is endless, the highest goal, smaller than the smallest, larger than the largest, whom feckless beings strive in vain to comprehend. 51

## \|\| 5.7.52 \|\|

*na yasya janmane dhātā yasya cāntāya nāntakaḥ \|*  
*sthitikartā na cānyosti yasya tasmai namaḥsadā \|\| 52 \|\|*

No-one brought about his birth, and none will bring him to an end. We bow to him as he’s the single author of the world. 52

## \|\| 5.7.53 \|\|

*kopaḥ svalpo 'pi te nāsti sthitipālanameva te \|*  
*kāraṇaṃ kāliya syāsya damane śruyatāṃ vacaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

You are not subject to the slightest anger and are protector of creation. You are the cause of Kāliya’s downfall, so listen now to what we say. 53

## \|\| 5.7.54 \|\|

*striyonukaṃpyāḥsādhūnāṃ mūḍhā dīnāś ca jantavaḥ \|*  
*yatastatosya dīnasya kṣamyatāṃ kṣamatāṃvara \|\| 54 \|\|*

Wise men should treat all women gently; even fools are kind to animals. Have mercy, therefore, on this miserable being, as you are the most merciful of all. 54

## \|\| 5.7.55 \|\|

*samasta jagadāvāro bhavānalpabalaḥ phaṇī \|*  
*tvatpādapīḍito jahyānmuhūrtārdhana jīvitam \|\| 55 \|\|*

You are the foundation of all the world, and this hooded snake is weak. In half a moment he’ll breathe his last, trampled by your feet. 55

## \|\| 5.7.56 \|\|

*kva pannago' lpavīryo 'yaṃ kva bhavānbhuvanāśrayaḥ \|*  
*prītidveṣau samotkṛṣṭagocarau bhavato 'vyaya \|\| 56 \|\|*

What’s this poor snake compared to you on whom the world relies? Love and hate are for one’s equals or one’s betters, eternal Lord. 56

## \|\| 5.7.57 \|\|

*tataḥ kuru jagatsvāminprasādamavasīdataḥ \|*  
*prāṇāṃstyajati nāgo 'yaṃ bhartṛbhikṣā pradīyatām \|\| 57 \|\|*

Show mercy to these desperate beings, master of the universe. This nāga’s on the verge of death. We’re begging for our husband. Please let him go! 57

## \|\| 5.7.58 \|\|

*bhuvaneśa jagannātha mahāpuruṣapūrvaja \|*  
*prāṇāṃstyajati nāgo 'yaṃ bhartṛbhikṣāṃ prayaccha naḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After they had spoken and, encouraged by their words, the serpent, his body broken, softly begged the lord to spare him. 58

## \|\| 5.7.59 \|\|

*vedāntavedya deveśa duṣṭadaityanibarhama \|*  
*prāṇāṃstyajati nāgo 'yaṃ bhartabhikṣā pradīyatām \|\| 59 \|\|*

Kāliya:

Eightfold sovereignty is yours, Lord, self-arisen and supreme. Stripped of my magnificence, how may I presume to praise you? 59

## \|\| 5.7.60 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityukte tābhir āśvasya klāntadehopi pannagaḥ \|*  
*prasīda devadeveti prāha vākyaṃ śanaiḥ śanaiḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

You are supreme and supreme’s beginning, and from you supreme arises. You are supreme beyond supreme. How may I presume to praise you? 60

## \|\| 5.7.61 \|\|

*kāliya uvāca*  
*tavāṣṭaguṇamaiśvaryaṃ nātha svābhāvikaṃ param \|*  
*nirastātiśayaṃ yasya tasya stoṣyāmi kitrvaham \|\| 61 \|\|*

From you arose Brahmā, Rudra, the Moon and Indra, the Winds and Aśvins, the Vasus and Ādityas. How may I presume to praise you? 61

## \|\| 5.7.62 \|\|

*tvaṃ parastvaṃ parasyādyaḥ paraṃ tvattaḥ parātmaka \|*  
*parasmātparamo yas tvaṃ tasya stoṣyāmi kinnv aham \|\| 62 \|\|*

This whole world is but one part of a part of you and is an aspect of just one aspect of your imaginings. How may I presume to praise you? 62

## \|\| 5.7.63 \|\|

*yasmādbahmāca rudraś ca candredramarudaśvinaḥ \|*  
*vasavaś ca sahādityais tasya stoṣyāmi kitrvaham \|\| 63 \|\|*

You whose form is both existent and nonexistent: neither Brahmā nor the other thirteen deities know your highest essence. How may I presume to praise you? 63

## \|\| 5.7.64 \|\|

*ekāvayavasūkṣmāṃśo yasyaitadakhilaṃ jagat \|*  
*kalpanāvayavasyāṃśastasya stoṣyāmi kitrvaham \|\| 64 \|\|*

Brahmā and other divine beings honour you with flowers and lotions from Indra’s heaven, Nandana. How may I presume to worship you? 64

## \|\| 5.7.65 \|\|

*sadasadrūpiṇo yasya brahmādyās tridaśeśvarāḥ \|*  
*paramārthaṃ na jānanti tasya stoṣyāmi kitrvaham \|\| 65 \|\|*

Indra, king of gods, always reveres your varied appearances, yet even he knows not your highest form. How may I presume to worship you? 65

## \|\| 5.7.66 \|\|

*brahmādyaurarcite yastu gandhapuṣpānulepanaiḥ \|*  
*nandanādisamudbhūtaiḥ sorcyate vā kathaṃ mayā \|\| 66 \|\|*

Ascetics who’ve withdrawn their senses from all external objects worship you with thought. How may I presume to worship you? 66

## \|\| 5.7.67 \|\|

*yasyāvatārarūpāṇi devarājaḥsadārcati \|*  
*na vetti paramaṃ rūpaṃ sor'cyate vā kathaṃ mayā \|\| 67 \|\|*

They create your image in their hearts and worship you in meditation with offerings of mental states and flowers. Lord, how may I presume to worship you? 67

## \|\| 5.7.68 \|\|

*viṣayebhyaḥ samāvṛttya sarvākṣāṇi ca yoginaḥ \|*  
*yamarcayanti dhyānena sor'cyate vā kathaṃ mayā \|\| 68 \|\|*

I’m unfit to honour or to praise you, blessed god of gods. As your mind is filled with tenderness alone, be merciful to me. 68

## \|\| 5.7.69 \|\|

*hṛdi saṃkalpya yadrūpaṃ dhyānenārcanti yoginaḥ \|*  
*bhāvapuṣpādinā nāthaḥ sorcyate vā kathaṃ mayā \|\| 69 \|\|*

I was born in this cruel class of serpents, Keśava. It’s my way, eternal Lord, I’ve done no wrong. 69

## \|\| 5.7.70 \|\|

*so 'haṃ te devadeveśa nārcanādau stutau na ca \|*  
*sāmarthyavān kṛpāmātramanovṛttiḥ prasīda me \|\| 70 \|\|*

This whole world was made by you, and you will absorb it. You are the one who fixed the nature of every class of being. 70

## \|\| 5.7.71 \|\|

*sarpajātiriyaṃ krurā yasyāṃ jātosmi keśava \|*  
*tatsvabhāvo 'yamatrāsti nāparādho mamācyuta \|\| 71 \|\|*

Just as I was made by you, Lord, in this class and form, I was yoked with this same nature and acted as I did. 71

## \|\| 5.7.72 \|\|

*sṛjyate bhavatā sarvaṃ tathā saṃhrīyate jagat \|*  
*jātirūpasvabhāvāś ca sṛjyante sṛ-jatā tvayā \|\| 72 \|\|*

If my conduct were any different, god of gods, then I’d deserve your punishment, just as you commanded. 72

## \|\| 5.7.73 \|\|

*yathāhaṃ bhavatā sṛṣṭo jātyā rūpeṇa ceśvara \|*  
*svabhāvena ca sādhutvaṃ tathedaṃ ceṣṭitaṃ mayā \|\| 73 \|\|*

I’d happily endure such trials as the universal lord might wreak on me, as I’d wish for nothing else from you. 73
