## \|\| 5.7.74 \|\|

*yadyanyathā pravarteyaṃ devadeva tato mayi \|*  
*nyāyo daṇḍanipāto vai tavaivavacanaṃ yathā \|\| 74 \|\|*

You’ve robbed me of my prowess and my venom. You’ve vanquished me, unfailing Lord. Now spare my life and I shall do your bidding. 74

## \|\| 5.7.75 \|\|

*tathāpyajñe jagatsvāmindaṇḍaṃ pātitavānmayi \|*  
*sa ślāghyo 'yaṃ paro daṇḍas tvatto me nānyato varaḥ \|\| 75 \|\|*

The Lord:

You may dwell no longer in the waters of the Yamunā, serpent. Go to the ocean and take your minions and your kinsfolk with you. 75

## \|\| 5.7.76 \|\|

*hatavīryo hataviṣo damito 'haṃ tvayācyuta \|*  
*jīvitaṃ dīyatāmekamājñāpaya karomi kim \|\| 76 \|\|*

Your enemy Garuḍa will not attack you, nāga, when he sees my footprints on your head. 76

## \|\| 5.7.77 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*nātra stheyantvayā sarpa kadācidyamunājale \|*  
*saputraparivārastvaṃ samudrasalilaṃ vraja \|\| 77 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, Lord Hari freed the serpent king. He bowed to Kṛṣṇa, then set off for the ocean. 77

## \|\| 5.7.78 \|\|

*matpadāni ca te sarpa dṛṣṭvā mūrdhani sāgare \|*  
*garuḍaḥ pannagaripustvayi na prahariṣyati \|\| 78 \|\|*

With his servants, kin and offspring and all his wives in train, the serpent left his pool behind, while all creation looked on. 78

## \|\| 5.7.79 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvā sarparājaṃ taṃ mumoca bhagavānhariḥ \|*  
*praṇamya so 'pi kṛṣṇāya jagāma payasāṃ nidhim \|\| 79 \|\|*

After Kāliya had departed, all the herdsmen hugged Govinda as if he had returned from death, anointing his head with teardrops streaming from their eyes. 79

## \|\| 5.7.80 \|\|

*paśyatāṃ sarvabhūtānāṃ sabhṛtyasutabāndhavaḥ \|*  
*samastabhāryāsahitaḥ parityajya svakaṃ hradam \|\| 80 \|\|*

Amazed to see the waters of the river clear again, the delighted herders all praised Kṛṣṇa, whose actions never tire him. 80

## \|\| 5.7.81 \|\|

*gate sarpe pariṣvajya mṛtaṃ punarivāgatam \|*  
*gopā mūrdhani hārdena siṣicur netrajair jalaiḥ \|\| 81 \|\|*

Eulogised by herding women and praised by herdsmen as they walked along, Kṛṣṇa, whose behaviour tends to kindness, approached the cattle camp. 81

## Chapter 8 - Balarāma defeats Dhenuka the Donkey-Demon

## \|\| 5.8.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*gāḥ pālayantau ca punaḥ sahitau balakeśavau \|*  
*bhramamāṇau vane tasminramyaṃ tālavanaṃ gatau \|\| 1 \|\|*

One day while tending cows together, Balarāma and Keśava, wandering in the forest, came to the pleasant grove of Tālavana. 1

## \|\| 5.8.2 \|\|

*tattu tālavanaṃ divyaṃ dhenuko nāma dānavaḥ \|*  
*mṛgamāṃsakṛtāhāraḥ sadādhyāste kharākṛtiḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

A Dānava named Dhenuka had occupied that grove and, taking on a donkey’s form, fed on flesh of man and beast. 2

## \|\| 5.8.3 \|\|

*tattu tālavanaṃ pakvaphalasaṃpatsamanvitam \|*  
*dṛṣṭvā spṛhānvitā gopā phalādāne 'bruvanvacaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Seeing Tālavana filled with masses of ripe coconuts, the hungry herdsmen urged the boys to fetch some down for them. 3

## \|\| 5.8.4 \|\|

*gopā ūcuḥ*  
*he rāma he kṛṣṇa sadā dhenukenaiṣa rakṣyate \|*  
*bhūpradeśo yatastasmātpakvānīmāni saṃti vai \|\| 4 \|\|*

The herdsmen:

Balarāma! Kṛṣṇa! Because this place is always watched by Dhenuka, the coconuts are ripe. 4

## \|\| 5.8.5 \|\|

*phalāni paśya tālānāṃ gandhāmoditadiṃśi vai \|*  
*vayametānyabhīpsāmaḥ pātyantāṃ yadi rocate \|\| 5 \|\|*

See the fruit on those palm trees that fill the air with scent? We’d love to get our hands on them. Throw some down for us, if you don’t mind. 5

## \|\| 5.8.6 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*iti gopakumārāṇāṃ śrutvā saṃkarṣaṇo vacaḥ \|*  
*etatkartavyamityuktvā pātayāmāsa tāni vai \|*  
*kṛṣṇaś ca pātayāmāsa bhuvitāni phalāni vai \|\| 6 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Having heard the herding boys’ request, Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa threw some coconuts to the ground for them. 6

## \|\| 5.8.7 \|\|

*phalānāṃ patatāṃ śabdamākarṇya sudurāsadaḥ \|*  
*ājagāma sa duṣṭātmā kopāddaiteyagardabhaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

But that wicked donkey-demon, dangerous to approach, heard the sound of falling fruit and rushed towards the spot. 7

## \|\| 5.8.8 \|\|

*padbhyāmubhābhyāṃ sa tadā paścimābhyāṃ balaṃ balī \|*  
*jaghānorasi tābhyāṃ ca sa ca tenābhyagṛhyata \|\| 8 \|\|*

With his hind legs, that mighty being kicked Balarāma in the chest, but the lad grabbed hold of them. 8

## \|\| 5.8.9 \|\|

*gṛhītvā bhrāmayāmāsa soṃ'bare gatajīvitam \|*  
*tasminneva sa cikṣepa vegena tṛṇarājani \|\| 9 \|\|*

Holding tight, he whirled the donkey in the air and spun the life right out of him. With one last heave, he threw the demon up a tree. 9

## \|\| 5.8.10 \|\|

*tataḥ phalānyanekāni tālāgrānnipatankharaḥ \|*  
*pṛthivyāṃ pātayāmāsa mahāvāto ghanāniva \|\| 10 \|\|*

Its body struck the palm and caused a shower of coconuts, just as strong winds drive the clouds along. 10

## \|\| 5.8.11 \|\|

*anyā natha sajātīyānāgatāndaityagardabhān \|*  
*kṛṣṇaścikṣepa tālāgre balabhadraś ca līlayā \|\| 11 \|\|*

Other donkey-demons, relatives of Dhenuka, then appeared, but Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa playfully threw them on the palm trees, too. 11

## \|\| 5.8.12 \|\|

*kṣaṇenālaṅkṛtā pṛthvī pakvais talaphalais tadā \|*  
*daitya gardabhadehaiś ca maitreya śuśubhe 'dhikam \|\| 12 \|\|*

Soon the ground was spread with fruit, Maitreya, and was resplendent with donkey-demon corpses. 12

## \|\| 5.8.13 \|\|

*tato gāvo nirābādhāstasmiṃstalavane dvija \|*  
*navaśaṣvaṃ sukhaṃ ceruryanna bhuktamabhūtpurā \|\| 13 \|\|*

From that day forward, brahmin, the cattle, safe and happy, grazed at Tālavana as they never had before. 13

## Chapter 9 - Balarāma destroys Pralamba the Cowherd-Demon

## \|\| 5.9.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tasminrāsabhadaiteye sānuge vinipātite \|*  
*saumyaṃ tadgopagopīnāṃ ramyaṃ tālavanaṃ babhau \|\| 1 \|\|*

Parāśara:

When that donkey-demon and his ilk were overcome, the pleasant Tālavana grove regained its splendour and was enjoyed by cattle, herding men and women. 1

## \|\| 5.9.2 \|\|

*tatastau jātaharṣau tu vasudevasutāvubhau \|*  
*hatvā dhenukadaiteyaṃ bhāṇḍīravaṭam āgatau \|\| 2 \|\|*

Having slain the Daitya Dhenuka, the sons of Vasudeva, filled with joy, next ventured to the banyan tree called Bhāṇḍīra. 2

## \|\| 5.9.3 \|\|

*kṣvelamānau pragāyantau vicinvantau ca pādapān \|*  
*cārayantau ca gā dūre vyāharantau ca nāmabhiḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

There they jumped and sang and explored the tree, while grazing their cattle near and far, calling to each beast by name. 3

## \|\| 5.9.4 \|\|

*niryogapāśaskandhau tau vanamālāvibhūṣitau \|*  
*śuśubhāte mahātmānau balāśṛṅgāvivarṣabhau \|\| 4 \|\|*

With spare lead ropes on their shoulders and garlands of forest flowers, those two great beings looked wonderful, like two young bulls with new-grown horns. 4

## \|\| 5.9.5 \|\|

*suvarṇāñjanacūrṇābhyāṃ tau tadā rūṣitāṃbarau \|*  
*mahendrāyudhasaṃyuktau śvetakṛṣṇāvivāṃbudau \|\| 5 \|\|*

Their clothes were dusted gold and kohl like a pair of clouds—one white, one black—with a rainbow spread between them. 5

## \|\| 5.9.6 \|\|

*ceraturlaukasiddhābhiḥ krīḍābhir itaretaram \|*  
*samastalokanāthānāṃ nāthabhūtau bhuvaṃ gatau \|\| 6 \|\|*

These two lords of all the gods, appearing on the earth, wandered here and there, enjoying common pastimes. 6

## \|\| 5.9.7 \|\|

*manuṣyadharmābhiratau mānayantau manuṣyatām \|*  
*tajjātiguṇayuktābhiḥ kriḍābhiśceraturvanam \|\| 7 \|\|*

Delighting in their mortal state and honouring their humanity, they roamed about the forest, indulging in the games that boys enjoy. 7

## \|\| 5.9.8 \|\|

*tatastvāndolikābhiś ca niyuddhaiś ca mahābalau \|*  
*vyāyāmaṃ cakratustatra kṣepaṇīyais tathāśmabhiḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

These two strong lads exercised by playing on swings, wrestling and heaving boulders. 8

## \|\| 5.9.9 \|\|

*tallipsurasurastatra hyubhayo ramamāṇayoḥ \|*  
*ājagāma pralaṃbākhyo gopaveṣatirohitaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

While they amused themselves like this, a demon named Pralamba, disguised as a herding boy, approached to seize the lads. 9

## \|\| 5.9.10 \|\|

*so 'vagāhata niḥśaṅkasteṣāṃ madhyamamānuṣaḥ \|*  
*mānuṣaṃ vapurāsthāya pralaṃbo dānavottamaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

That Pralamba, best of Dānavas, inhuman being in human form, fearlessly joined the boys. 10

## \|\| 5.9.11 \|\|

*tayośichdrāntaraprepsuraviṣahyamamanyata \|*  
*kṛṣṇaṃ tato rauhiṇeyaṃ hantuṃ cakre manoratham \|\| 11 \|\|*

He tried to separate the two, but, finding that impossible, decided to kill Kṛṣṇa first and then to kill his brother. 11

## \|\| 5.9.12 \|\|

*hariṇākrīḍanaṃ nāma bālakrīḍanakaṃ tataḥ \|*  
*prakurvanto hi te sarve dvaudvau yugapadutthitau \|\| 12 \|\|*

The lads arranged themselves in pairs to play a game called ‘Catch the deer’, in which one boy chased another. 12

## \|\| 5.9.13 \|\|

*śrīdāmnā saha govindaḥ pralaṃbena tathā balaḥ \|*  
*gopālauraparaiś cānye gopālāḥ pupluvustataḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Govinda took after Śrīdāman, Balarāma chased Pralamba and all the other herding lads joined in. 13

## \|\| 5.9.14 \|\|

*śrīdāmānaṃ tataḥ kṛṣṇaḥ pralaṃbaṃ rohiṇīsutaḥ \|*  
*jitavānkṛṣṇapakṣīyair gopair anye parājitāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa caught his quarry, as did the son of Rohiṇī, till all the ‘deer’ were caught by Kṛṣṇa’s team. 14

## \|\| 5.9.15 \|\|

*te vāhayantastvanyonyaṃ bhāṇḍīraṃ vaṭametya vai \|*  
*punarnivavṛtuḥsarve yeye tatra parijitāḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Then the losers had to bear their captors on their shoulders to the banyan tree and back. 15

## \|\| 5.9.16 \|\|

*saṃkarṣaṇaṃ tu skandhena śīghramutkṣipyadānavaḥ \|*  
*nabhaḥsthalaṃ jagāmāśu sacandra iva vāridaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

The Dānava quickly lifted Balarāma and put him on his shoulders. Pralamba then ran off, resembling a cloud surmounted by the moon. 16

## \|\| 5.9.17 \|\|

*asahanrauhiṇeyasya sa bhāraṃ dānavottamaḥ \|*  
*vavṛdhe sa mahākāyaḥ prāvṛṣīva balāhakaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Rohiṇī’s son was too heavy for that best of Dānavas, so the demon grew in size, like a cloud in the rainy season. 17

## \|\| 5.9.18 \|\|

*saṃkarṣaṇastu taṃ dṛṣṭvā dagdhaśailopamākṛtim \|*  
*sragdāmalaṃbābharaṇaṃ makuṭāṭopamastakam \|\| 18 \|\|*

Seeing Pralamba towering like a mountain peak burned black by fire, adorned with swaying garlands and a diadem on his head, 18

## \|\| 5.9.19 \|\|

*raudraṃ śakaṭacakrākṣaṃ pādanyāsa calatkṣitim \|*  
*abhītamanasā tena rakṣasā rohiṇīsutaḥ \|*  
*hriyamāmastataḥ kṛṣṇamidaṃ vacanamabravīt \|\| 19 \|\|*

His fearsome eyes as wide as wagon wheels and shaking the earth with every step, Balarāma called to Kṛṣṇa as he was borne away: 19

## \|\| 5.9.20 \|\|

*kṛṣṇākṛṣṇa hriyāmyeṣa parvatodagramūrtinā \|*  
*kenāpi paśya daityena gopālacchadmarūpiṇā \|\| 20 \|\|*

‘Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa! I’ve been captured by some Daitya in a cowherd’s guise. He’s as lofty as a mountain. Look! 20

## \|\| 5.9.21 \|\|

*yadatra sāṃprataṃ kāryaṃ mayā madhuniṣūdana \|*  
*tatkathyatāṃ prayātyeṣa durātmātitvarānvitaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

You killed the demon Madhu, now tell me what to do. This wicked creature’s running off so fast.’ 21

## \|\| 5.9.23 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca*  
*kimayaṃ mānuṣo bhāvo vyaktamevāvalaṃbyate \|*  
*sarvātman sarvaguhyānāṃ guhyaguhyātmanā tvayā \|\| 23 \|\|*

Parāśara: Knowing Rohiṇī’s son was strong and brave, great Govinda addressed him with a smile. 22 The Lord:

Why do you—the heart of all creation and hidden secret of every mystery—seem to cling to human form? 23

## \|\| 5.9.24 \|\|

*smarāśeṣajagadbījakāraṇaṃ kāraṇāgraja \|*  
*ātmānamekaṃ tadvacca jagatyekārṇave ca yat \|\| 24 \|\|*

Remember, Lord of all the world, that I, the universal cause, and you, my elder brother, are really one, just as everything is merged when the world becomes one ocean. 24

## \|\| 5.9.25 \|\|

*kiṃ na vetsi yathāhaṃ ca tvaṃ caikaṃ kāraṇaṃ bhuvaḥ \|*  
*bhārāvatāraṇārthāya martyalokamupāgatau \|\| 25 \|\|*

Don’t you know that you and I are the single universal cause, come down to earth to ease its burden? 25

## \|\| 5.9.26 \|\|

*nabhaśiśrasteṃbuvahāś ca keśāḥ pādau kṣitirvatrkramanantavahni \|*  
*somo manaste śvasitaṃ samīraṇo diśaścatasro 'vyaya bāhavaste \|\| 26 \|\|*

Your head is sky and your body water. Your feet are earth and your mouth is fire, everlasting being. Your mind is the moon and your breath is wind. The four directions are your arms, eternal god. 26

## \|\| 5.9.27 \|\|

*sahasrabatrkro bhagavānmahātmā sahasrahastāṅghriśarīrabhedaḥ \|*  
*sahasrapadmodbhavayonirādyaḥsahasraśastvāṃ munayo gṛṇanti \|\| 27 \|\|*

You are the great being with a thousand faces, hands, feet and bodies. You are the primal source from which a thousand Brahmās rise, and sages praise you in a thousand forms. 27

## \|\| 5.9.28 \|\|

*divyaṃ hi rūpaṃ tava vetti nānyo devair aśeṣair avatārarūpam \|*  
*tadarcyate vetsi na kiṃ yadante tvayyeva viśvaṃ layamabhyupaiti \|\| 28 \|\|*

No-one else can know your heavenly form, the appearance praised by all the gods. Don’t you know that, in the end, all things will be absorbed by you? 28

## \|\| 5.9.29 \|\|

*tvayā dhṛteyaṃ dharaṇī bibharti carācaraṃ viśvamanantamūrte \|*  
*kṛtādibhedairaja kālarūpo nimeṣapūrvo jagadetadatsi \|\| 29 \|\|*

This world, supported by you whose form is endless, bears all things moving and unmoving. In the form of Time, beginning with minutes and divided into Kṛta and other ages, you consume the world. 29

## \|\| 5.9.30 \|\|

*attaṃ yathā vaḍāvavahnināṃbu himasvarūpaṃ parigṛhya kāstam \|*  
*himācale bhānumatoṃśusaṃgājjalatvamabhyeti punastadeva \|\| 30 \|\|*

The fires that burn beneath the waves drive off the ocean’s water. This water falls as snow in the Himālaya and melts again beneath the solar rays. 30

## \|\| 5.9.31 \|\|

*evaṃ tvayā saṃharaṇe 'ttametajjagatsamastaṃ tvadadhīnakaṃ punaḥ \|*  
*tavaiva sargāya samudyatasya jagattvamabhyetyasukalpamīśa \|\| 31 \|\|*

In that same way, this world, absorbed by you at the time of dissolution, inevitably becomes the world again through your creative effort, Lord. 31

## \|\| 5.9.32 \|\|

*bhavānahaṃ ca viśvātmannekameva ca kāraṇam \|*  
*jagatosya jagatyarthe bhedenāvāṃ vyavasthitau \|\| 32 \|\|*

You and I, universal being, are the single cause of all this world and took on separate forms to benefit it. 32

## \|\| 5.9.33 \|\|

*tatsmaryatāmameyātmastvayātmā jahi dānavam \|*  
*mānuṣyamevāvalaṃbya bandhūnāṃ kriyatāṃ hitam \|\| 33 \|\|*

For this reason, boundless being, remember now your actual self and destroy this Dānava. Lay aside your mortal form and do your kin a favour. 33

## \|\| 5.9.34 \|\|

*śrī parāśara uvāca*  
*iti saṃsmārito vipra kṛṣṇena sumahātmanā \|*  
*vihasya pīḍayāmāsa pralaṃbaṃ balavānbalaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus reminded by great Kṛṣṇa, mighty Balarāma gave a laugh, brahmin, and began to squeeze Pralamba. 34

## \|\| 5.9.35 \|\|

*muṣṭinā sohananmūrdhni kopasaṃraktalocanaḥ \|*  
*tena cāsya prahāreṇa bahiryāte vilocane \|\| 35 \|\|*

His eyes were red with rage as he struck Pralamba on the head, and the demon’s eyes popped out. 35

## \|\| 5.9.36 \|\|

*sa niṣkāsitamastiṣko mukhācchoṇitamudvaman \|*  
*nipapāta mahīpṛṣṭhe datya varyo mamāra ca \|\| 36 \|\|*

With his skull cracked open and blood gushing from his mouth, that Daitya hero fell down dead. 36

## \|\| 5.9.37 \|\|

*pralaṃbaṃ nihataṃ dṛṣṭvā balenādbhutakarmaṇā \|*  
*prahṛṣṭāstuṣṭuvurgaupāḥsādhusādhviti cābruvan \|\| 37 \|\|*

Seeing Pralamba slain by Balarāma of wondrous deeds, the delighted herdsmen sang his praises. ‘Well done, well done!’ they cried. 37

## \|\| 5.9.38 \|\|

*saṃstūyamāno gopais tu rāmo daitye nipātite \|*  
*pralaṃbaṃ saha kṛṣṇena punargokulamāyayau \|\| 38 \|\|*

Now the demon Pralamba had been defeated, Balarāma, praised by herders, returned with Kṛṣṇa to the camp. 38

## Chapter 10 - Autumn in the cattle camp

## \|\| 5.10.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tayorviharatorevaṃ rāmakeśavayorvraje \|*  
*prāvṛḍ vyatītā vikasatsarojā cābhavaccharat \|\| 1 \|\|*

Parāśara:

While Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma thus whiled away the time in camp, the rainy season passed and autumn came with lotuses in bloom. 1

## \|\| 5.10.2 \|\|

*avāpustāpamatyarthaṃ śapharyaḥ palvalodake \|*  
*putrakṣetrādisaktena mamatvena yathā gṛhī \|\| 2 \|\|*

The fishes in the smaller ponds began to feel the heat, like selfish householders concerned about their sons, their fields and all the rest. 2

## \|\| 5.10.3 \|\|

*mayurā maunamātasthuḥ parityaktamadā vane \|*  
*asāratāṃ parijñāya saṃsārasyeva yoginaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Peacocks stood in silence in the forest, their passion spent, like ascetics who’ve realised the futility of life. 3

## \|\| 5.10.4 \|\|

*utsṛjya jalasarvasvaṃ vimalāḥsitamūrtayaḥ \|*  
*tatyajuścāṃbaraṃ meghā gṛhaṃ vijñānino yathā \|\| 4 \|\|*

Having shed their showers, pure white clouds then fled the sky, as enlightened beings set out from home. 4

## \|\| 5.10.5 \|\|

*śaratsūryāṃśutaptāni yayuḥ śoṣaṃ sarāṃsi ca \|*  
*bahvālaṃbamamatvena hṛdayānīva dehinām \|\| 5 \|\|*

Warmed by the rays of the autumn sun, the lakes dried up, like hearts of selfish beings beset by many cares. 5

## \|\| 5.10.6 \|\|

*kumudaiḥ śaradaṃbhāṃsi yogyatālakṣaṇaṃ yayuḥ \|*  
*avabodhair manāṃsīva samatvamamalātmanām \|\| 6 \|\|*

The autumnal pools proved fit for silver waterlilies, just as hearts of virtuous folk are ready for understanding. 6

## \|\| 5.10.7 \|\|

*tārakāvimale vyomni rarājākhaṇḍamaṇḍalaḥ \|*  
*candraścaramadehātmā yogī sādhukule yathā \|\| 7 \|\|*

The full moon shone in a star-filled cloudless sky, like an ascetic whose final birth is in a noble family. 7

## \|\| 5.10.8 \|\|

*śanakaiḥ śanakais tīraṃ tatyajuś ca jalāśayāḥ \|*  
*mamatvaṃ kṣetraputrādirūḍhamuccair yathā budhāḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

The water in the reservoirs receded from the banks, just as the wise inevitably withdraw from selfish attachment to wives and sons and others. 8

## \|\| 5.10.9 \|\|

*pūrvaṃ tyaktaiḥ saroṃbhobhir haṃsā yogaṃ punar yayuḥ \|*  
*kleśaiḥ kuyogino śeṣair antarāyahatā iva \|\| 9 \|\|*

Wild geese returned to lakes they’d previously deserted, just as troubles come again to haunt false ascetics who are beset with obstacles. 9

## \|\| 5.10.10 \|\|

*nibhṛto bhavadatyarthaṃ samudraḥstimitodakaḥ \|*  
*kramāvāptamahāyogo viścalātmā yathā yatiḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

The ocean’s tranquil waters grew even calmer, like the ascetic’s steady heart, which slowly reaches union with the lord. 10

## \|\| 5.10.11 \|\|

*sarvatrātiprasannāni salilāni tathābhavan \|*  
*jñāte sarvagate viṣṇau manāṃsīva sumedhasām \|\| 11 \|\|*

The waters everywhere grew clear, like wise minds realising Viṣṇu’s universal nature. 11

## \|\| 5.10.12 \|\|

*babhūva nirmalaṃ vyoma śaradā dhvastatoyadam \|*  
*yogāgnidagdhakleśaughaṃ yogināmiva mānasam \|\| 12 \|\|*

The sky grew bright when autumn drove away the clouds, like ascetics’ hearts when yogic fire has burned all their defilements. 12

## \|\| 5.10.13 \|\|

*sūryāṃśujanitaṃ tāpaṃ nitye tārāpatiḥ śamam \|*  
*ahaṃmānodbhavaṃ duḥkhaṃ vivekaḥ sumahāniva \|\| 13 \|\|*

The moon dispelled the sun’s hot rays, just as true discernment drives off suffering born of selfishness. 13

## \|\| 5.10.14 \|\|

*nabhasobdaṃ bhuvaḥ paṅkaṃ kāluṣyaṃ cāṃbhasaḥ śarat \|*  
*indriyāṇīndriyārthebhyaḥ pratyāhāra ivāharat \|\| 14 \|\|*

Autumn drew the clouds from the sky, the moisture from the soil and the turbidity from the water, just as the practice of withdrawal retrieves the senses from their objects. 14

## \|\| 5.10.15 \|\|

*prāṇāyāma ivāṃbhobhiḥ sarasāṃ kṛtapūrakaiḥ \|*  
*abhyasyate 'nudivasaṃ recakākuṃbhakādibhiḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

The waters rose, peaked and fell again as if the lakes controlled their breath each day: inhaling, holding and exhaling. 15

## \|\| 5.10.16 \|\|

*vimalāṃbaranakṣatre kāle cābhyāgate vraje \|*  
*dadarśedro mahāraṃbhāyodyatāṃstānvrajaukasaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

When the constellations reappeared in the cloudless sky above the cattle camp, Kṛṣṇa beheld the residents planning a great festival for Indra. 16

## \|\| 5.10.17 \|\|

*kṛṣṇastānutsukāndṛṣṭvā gopānutsavalālasān \|*  
*kautūhalādidaṃ vākyaṃ prāha vṛddhānmahāmatiḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Seeing the herdsmen keenly preparing for this event, and being curious, that wise being addressed these words to the elders: 17

## \|\| 5.10.18 \|\|

*ko 'yaṃ śakramaho nāma yena vo harṣa āgataḥ \|*  
*prāha taṃ nandagopaś ca pṛcchantamatisādaram \|\| 18 \|\|*

‘What’s this so-called festival of Indra that is causing such excitement?’ Nanda the herdsman gave this reply as Kṛṣṇa asked politely: 18

## \|\| 5.10.19 \|\|

*nandagopa uvāca*  
*meghānāṃ payasāṃ ceśo devarājaḥ śatakratuḥ \|*  
*tena saṃcoditā meghā varṣatyaṃhumayaṃ rasam \|\| 19 \|\|*

‘Indra is the lord of thunderclouds and rains, the king of gods and deity of a hundred sacrifices. Urged by him, the clouds release the essence of life in the form of showers. 19

## \|\| 5.10.20 \|\|

*tadvṛṣṭijanitaṃ sasyaṃ vayamanye ca dehinaḥ \|*  
*vartayāmopayuñjānāstarpayāmaś ca devatāḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

We and other living things subsist on crops brought forth by rain, and it’s we who feed the gods. 20

## \|\| 5.10.21 \|\|

*kṣīravatya imā gāvo vatsavatyaś ca nirvṛtāḥ \|*  
*tena saṃvardhitaiḥ sasyais tuṣṭāḥ puṣṭāḥ bhavanti vai \|\| 21 \|\|*

These dairy cattle and contented cows with calves are satisfied and nourished with the feed the rain provides. 21

## \|\| 5.10.22 \|\|

*nāsasyā nātṛṇā bhūmirna bubhukṣārdito janaḥ \|*  
*dṛśyate yatra dṛsyante vṛṣṭimanto balāhakāḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Wherever rain-filled clouds are seen, the earth will not be lacking grain or pasture, nor will people suffer hunger or other woes. 22

## \|\| 5.10.23 \|\|

*bhaumame tatpayo dugdhaṃ gobhiḥ sūryasya vāridaiḥ \|*  
*parjanyaḥsarvalokasyodbhavāya bhuvi varṣati \|\| 23 \|\|*

Indra, who gives the rain, draws up water from the earth with the sun’s own rays. Parjanya sends it down again as showers to benefit the world. 23

## \|\| 5.10.24 \|\|

*tasmātprāvṛṣi rājānaḥsarve śakraṃ mudā yutāḥ \|*  
*makhaiḥ sureśamarcanti vayamanye ca mānavāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

That’s why every joyful king worships the lord of gods with festivals in the rainy season, as do we and other people.’ 24

## \|\| 5.10.25 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*nandagopasya vacanaṃ śrutvetthaṃ śakrapūjane \|*  
*roṣāya tridaśendrasya prāhadāmodarastadā \|\| 25 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing the herdsman Nanda praising mighty Indra, Kṛṣṇa then said this to goad the king of thirteen gods: 25

## \|\| 5.10.26 \|\|

*na vayaṃ kṛṣikartāro vāṇijyājīvino na ca \|*  
*gāvosmaddaivataṃ tāta vayaṃ vanacarā yataḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

‘We don’t plough the earth, nor do we live by trade. Cattle are our deities, dear father, because we’re forest nomads. 26

## \|\| 5.10.27 \|\|

*ānvikṣikī trayī vārtā daṇḍanitis tathā parā \|*  
*vidyā catuṣṭayaṃ caitadvārtamātraṃ śṛṇuṣva me \|\| 27 \|\|*

Knowledge has four branches: logical, scriptural, political and practical. Listen while I describe the last: 27

## \|\| 5.10.28 \|\|

*kṛṣirvaṇijyā tadvacca tṛtīyaṃ paśupālanam \|*  
*vidyā hyekā mahābhāga vārtā vṛttitrayāśrayā \|\| 28 \|\|*

Farming, trade, with herding as the third—these three modes of life comprise the field of practical knowledge, fortunate man. 28

## \|\| 5.10.29 \|\|

*karṣakāṇāṃ kṛṣirvṛttiḥ paṇyaṃ vipaṇījīvinām \|*  
*asmākaṃ gauḥ parāvṛttirvārtābhedair iyaṃ tribhiḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

Agriculture is the life of farming people, as trade is that of merchants, but cattle are our main concern. Such are the three forms of this field of knowledge. 29

## \|\| 5.10.30 \|\|

*vidyayā yo yayā yuktastasya sā daivataṃ mahat \|*  
*saiva pūjyārca nīyā ca saiva tasyopakārikā \|\| 30 \|\|*

The branch of knowledge by which one lives should be one’s first divinity. That branch alone deserves our praise and honour, as only it will succour us. 30

## \|\| 5.10.31 \|\|

*yo yasya phalamaśranvai pūjayatyaparaṃ naraḥ \|*  
*iha ca pretya caivāsau na tadāpnoti śobhanam \|\| 31 \|\|*

One who reveres or benefits from a different branch, dear father, will find no prosperity in this world or in the life hereafter. 31

## \|\| 5.10.32 \|\|

*kṛṣyāntā prathitā sīmā mīmāntaṃ ca punarvanam \|*  
*vanāntā girayaḥsarve te cāsmākaṃ parā gatiḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

The margins stretch beyond the fields; beyond the margins lie the forests. Beyond the forests rise the mountains, the furthest limit of our realm. 32

## \|\| 5.10.33 \|\|

*na dvārabandhāvaraṇā na gṛhakṣetriṇas tathā \|*  
*sukhinastvakhile loke yathā vai cakracāriṇaḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

We don’t live behind closed doors, nor do we own fields or houses, but roam freely across the earth. 33

## \|\| 5.10.34 \|\|

*śrūyante girayaścaiva vanesminkāmarūpiṇaḥ \|*  
*tattadrūpaṃ samāsthāya ramante sveṣu sānuṣu \|\| 34 \|\|*

It’s said these mountains can choose any shape at will and, in such forms, disport themselves among the peaks and in this very woodland. 34

## \|\| 5.10.35 \|\|

*yadā caite prabādhyante teṣāṃ ye kānanaukasaḥ \|*  
*tadā siṃhādirūpais tānghātayanti mahīdharāḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

If forest-dwelling folk offend them, mountains take the form of lions or some other beast and kill them. 35

## \|\| 5.10.36 \|\|

*girīyajñastvayaṃ tasmādgoyajñaś ca pravartyatām \|*  
*kimasmākaṃ mahendreṇa gāvaḥ śailāś ca devatāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

That’s why we should sacrifice to mountains and to cattle. What does mighty Indra mean to us? Cattle and these ranges are our deities! 36

## \|\| 5.10.37 \|\|

*mantrayajñaparā viprāḥsīrayajñāś ca karṣakāḥ \|*  
*girigoyajñaśīlāś ca vayamadrivanāśrayāḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

Brahmins sacrifice with mantras, and farmers worship ploughs. It’s natural for folk like us who depend on highlands and forests to worship hills and cattle. 37

## \|\| 5.10.38 \|\|

*tasmādgovardhanaḥ śailo bhavadbhir vividhārhaṇaiḥ \|*  
*arcyatāṃ pūjyatāṃ medhyānpaśunhatvā vidhānataḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

For this reason, having sacrificed a fitting beast, you should praise and honour Mount Govardhana in various ways according to the precepts. 38

## \|\| 5.10.39 \|\|

*sarvaghoṣasya saṃdoho gṛhyatāṃ mā vicāryatām \|*  
*bhojyantāṃ tena vai viprās tathā ye cābhivāñchakāḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

Take the milk produced by this whole herd without delay and offer it to brahmins and others who desire it. 39

## \|\| 5.10.40 \|\|

*tatrārcite kṛte home bhojiteṣu dvijātiṣu \|*  
*śaratpuṣpakṛtāpīḍāḥ parigacchantu gogaṇāḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

When oblations have been made and brahmins fed, then decorate the herds of cows with wreaths of autumn flowers and circumambulate them. 40

## \|\| 5.10.41 \|\|

*etanmama mataṃ gopāḥsaṃprītyā kriyate yadi \|*  
*tataḥ kṛtā bhavetprītirgavāmadres tathā mama \|\| 41 \|\|*

That’s my suggestion, and, if you herdsmen do this gladly, you will please the cattle, mountains and myself.’ 41

## \|\| 5.10.42 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*iti tasya vacaḥ śrutvā vandādyāste vrajaukasaḥ \|*  
*prītyutphullamukhā gopāḥsādhusādhvityathābruvan \|\| 42 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, brahmin, Nanda and the other herdsmen in the camp cried, ‘Good idea, good idea!’, as their faces beamed with joy. 42

## \|\| 5.10.43 \|\|

*śobhanaṃ te mataṃ vatsa yadetadbhavatoditam \|*  
*tatkariṣyāmahe sarvaṃ giriyajñaḥ pravartyatām \|\| 43 \|\|*

‘My dear boy, you’ve made a wonderful suggestion. We’ll do all that you’ve put forward. Let’s conduct a mountain sacrifice!’ 43

## \|\| 5.10.44 \|\|

*tathā ca kṛtavantaste giriyajña vrajaukasaḥ \|*  
*dadhipāyasamāṃsādyair daduḥ śailabaliṃ tataḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

Parāśara:

And so the residents of the camp performed a mountain sacrifice and made an offering of yoghurt, milk and meat. 44

## \|\| 5.10.45 \|\|

*dvijāṃś ca bhojayāmāsusśātaśau'tha sahasraśaḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

They also offered food to brahmins by the hundreds and the thousands. 45

## \|\| 5.10.46 \|\|

*gāvaḥ śailaṃ tataścakurarcitāstāḥ pradakṣiṇam \|*  
*vṛṣabhāścātinardantaḥsatoyā jaladā iva \|\| 46 \|\|*

Next, they walked around the mountain and the cows that had been worshipped, while all the bulls began to bellow like rain-filled thunderclouds. 46

## \|\| 5.10.47 \|\|

*girimūrdhani kṛṣṇo 'pi śailohamiti mūrtimān \|*  
*bubhujennaṃ bahutaraṃ gopavaryāhṛtaṃ dvija \|\| 47 \|\|*

On the mountain summit, Kṛṣṇa himself declared, ‘I’m this mountain in bodily form’, brahmin, and joined the splendid feast offered by the herding women. 47

## \|\| 5.10.48 \|\|

*svenaiva kṛṣṇo rūpeṇa gopaiḥ saha gireḥ śiraḥ \|*  
*adhiruhyārcayāmāsa dvitīyāmātmanastanum \|\| 48 \|\|*

In that very form, Kṛṣṇa, accompanied by the herdsmen, ascended to the summit, where he worshipped his second self. 48

## \|\| 5.10.49 \|\|

*antardhānaṃ gate tasmingopā labdhvā tato varān \|*  
*kṛtvā girīmakhaṃ goṣṭhaṃ nijamabyāyayuḥ punaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

After he withdrew from sight, the herders then received great boons, and, when the festival was over, they all returned to the cattle camp. 49

## Chapter 11 - Kṛṣṇa raises Mount Govardhana

## \|\| 5.11.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*makhe pratihate śakro maitreyātiruṣānvitaḥ \|*  
*saṃvartakaṃ nāma gaṇaṃ toyadānāmathābratrīt \|\| 1 \|\|*

Mighty Indra, furious that his festival had been abandoned, Maitreya, addressed a host of rainclouds called Saṃvartakas. 1

## \|\| 5.11.2 \|\|

*bhobho medhā niśamyaitadvacanaṃ gadato mama \|*  
*ājñānantaramevāśu kriyatāmavicāritam \|\| 2 \|\|*

‘Come, come, you clouds, and listen to my words. You must follow my instructions without question or delay. 2

## \|\| 5.11.3 \|\|

*nandagopaḥsudurbuddhirgopair anyaiḥ sahāyavān \|*  
*kṛṣṇāśrayaṃ balādhmāto makhabhaṅgamacīkarat \|\| 3 \|\|*

The herder Nanda is a fool and, along with other herdsmen, has a bloated sense of his importance, protected as he is by Kṛṣṇa, and now he’s stopped my festival. 3

## \|\| 5.11.4 \|\|

*ājīvo yāḥ parasteṣāṃ gāvastasya ca kāraṇam \|*  
*tāgāvo vṛṣṭipātena pīḍyantāṃ vacanānmama \|\| 4 \|\|*

Those cattle are their treasured means of livelihood, and that’s what makes them herdsmen. I order you to strike their cattle with a tempest. 4

## \|\| 5.11.5 \|\|

*aham apyadriśṛṅgābhaṃ tuṅgam āruhya vāraṇam \|*  
*sāhāyyaṃ vaḥ kariṣyāmi vāyvambūtsargayojitam \|\| 5 \|\|*

Riding on my elephant, towering like a mountain peak, I’ll help by bringing wind and rain.’ 5

## \|\| 5.11.6 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityājñaptās tatas tena mumucus te balāhakāḥ \|*  
*vātavarṣaṃ mahābhīmamabhāvāya gavāṃ dvija \|\| 6 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus commanded by the king of gods, brahmin, the clouds released a dreadful storm to annihilate the cattle. 6

## \|\| 5.11.7 \|\|

*tataḥ kṣaṇena pṛthivī kakubhoṃbaram eva ca \|*  
*ekaṃ dhārāmahāsārapūreṇanābhavanmune \|\| 7 \|\|*

In an instant, heaven, earth and all between, sage, were beset by a mighty deluge and became as one. 7

## \|\| 5.11.8 \|\|

*vidyullatākaṣāghātatrastair iva ghanair ghanam \|*  
*nādāpūritadikcakrair dhārāsāramapātyata \|\| 8 \|\|*

The clouds roared out as if they feared a whipping from the lightning bolts, filling all directions with their thunder and hurling down their torrents. 8

## \|\| 5.11.9 \|\|

*andhakārīkṛte loke varṣadbhir aniśaṃ ghanaiḥ \|*  
*adhaścordhvaṃ ca tiryak ca jagadāpyamivābhavat \|\| 9 \|\|*

The earth grew dark as endless showers fell. Above, below and on all sides, this whole world was inundated. 9

## \|\| 5.11.10 \|\|

*gāvastu tena patatā varṣavātena vāginā \|*  
*dhūtāḥ prāṇāñ jahuḥ sannatrikasakthisirodharāḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Stricken by the sudden gale that fell on them, the cattle’s hips and legs and necks gave way, and some of them then breathed their last. 10

## \|\| 5.11.11 \|\|

*kroḍena vatsān ākramya tasthur anyā mahāmune \|*  
*gāvo vivatsāś ca kṛtā vāripūreṇa cāparāḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

Others stood in anger with their calves beneath their bellies, sage, yet others lost their young when swept away by floods. 11

## \|\| 5.11.12 \|\|

*vatsāś ca dīnavadanā vātakaṃpitakandharāḥ \|*  
*trāhitrāhītyalpasabdāḥ kṛṣṇamūcurivāturāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Miserable calves, bellowing piteously and shivering in the wind, seemed to softly cry to Kṛṣṇa, ‘Save me, save me!’ 12

## \|\| 5.11.13 \|\|

*tatastadgokulaṃ sarvaṃ gogopīgopasaṃkulam \|*  
*atīvārtaṃ harirdaṣṭvā maitreyācintayattadā \|\| 13 \|\|*

Seeing the cattle and the herding men and women of the camp in chaos, Maitreya, Hari was deeply troubled and reflected: 13

## \|\| 5.11.14 \|\|

*etatkṛtaṃ mahendreṇa sakhabhaṅgavirodhinā \|*  
*tadetadakhilaṃ goṣṭhaṃ trātavyamadhunā māya \|\| 14 \|\|*

‘This must be Indra’s doing. He’s irked because his festival was abandoned. Now it’s up to me to save them all. 14

## \|\| 5.11.15 \|\|

*imam adrimahaṃ dhairyād utpāṭyoruśikhāghanam \|*  
*dhārayiṣyāmi goṣṭhasya pṛthucchatramivopari \|\| 15 \|\|*

I’ll raise this mountain steadily, a mass of solid rock, and hold it above the cattle pen like a huge umbrella.’ 15

## \|\| 5.11.16 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*iti kṛtvāmatiṃ kṛṣṇo govardhanamahīdharam \|*  
*utpāṭyaikakareṇeva dhārayāmāsa līlayā \|\| 16 \|\|*

Parāśara:

With this thought, Lord Kṛṣṇa raised Mount Govardhana with just one finger, holding it aloft with ease. 16

## \|\| 5.11.17 \|\|

*gopāṃścaha hasañchauriḥsamutpāṭitabhūdharaḥ \|*  
*viśadhvamatratvaritāḥ kṛtaṃ varṣanivāraṇam \|\| 17 \|\|*

The lord of all the world, with the mountain above his head, said to the herdsmen: ‘Come to the shelter I’ve provided. 17

## \|\| 5.11.18 \|\|

*sunivāteṣu deśeṣu yathā joṣamihāsyatām \|*  
*praviśyatāṃ na bhetavyaṃ girīpātācca nirbhayaiḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Sit here in comfort. It’s not windy. Come in. Don’t worry. There’s no chance the hill will fall.’ 18

## \|\| 5.11.19 \|\|

*ityuktāstena te gopā viviśurgodhanaiḥ saha \|*  
*śakaṭāropitair bhāḍair gopyaścāsārapīḍitāḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

Hearing this, the herders sheltered with their cattle and their laden wagons, as well as all the women who’d been drenched. 19

## \|\| 5.11.20 \|\|

*kṛṣṇopi taṃ dadhāraiva śailamatyantaniścalam \|*  
*vrajaukavāsibhir harṣavismitākṣair nirikṣitaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa held that mountain steadily while all the cattle camp looked on; their eyes were filled with pleasure and amazement. 20

## \|\| 5.11.21 \|\|

*gopagopījanair hṛṣṭaiḥ prītivistāritekṣaṇaiḥ \|*  
*saṃstūyamānacaritaḥ kṛṣṇaḥ śailamadhārayat \|\| 21 \|\|*

The joyful herding men and women, thrilled and wide-eyed with affection, praised Kṛṣṇa’s efforts as he held that mountain high. 21

## \|\| 5.11.22 \|\|

*saptarātraṃ mahāmedhā vavarṣurnandagokule \|*  
*indreṇa coditā vipra gopānāṃ nāśakāriṇā \|\| 22 \|\|*

For seven nights, those giant clouds rained down on Nanda’s cattle camp to annihilate the herdsmen, brahmin—all at Indra’s urging. 22

## \|\| 5.11.23 \|\|

*tato dhṛte mahāśaile paritrāte ca gokule \|*  
*mithyāpratijño balabhidvārayāmāsa tānghanān \|\| 23 \|\|*

But as the camp was safe beneath the lifted mountain, Indra, destroyer of the demon Bala, failing to fulfil his promise, then told the clouds to cease. 23

## \|\| 5.11.24 \|\|

*vyabhre nabhasi devendre vitathātmavacasyatha \|*  
*niṣkramya gokulaṃ hṛṣṭaṃ svasthānaṃ punarāgamat \|\| 24 \|\|*

When the sky grew clear again and Indra’s threat was proven hollow, the whole delighted cattle camp left the shelter of the mountain and returned to their locale. 24

## \|\| 5.11.25 \|\|

*mumoca kṛṣṇopi tadā govardhanamahācalam \|*  
*svasthāne vismitamukhair dṛṣṭas tais tu vrajaukasaiḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa then placed Govardhana on its old foundations, as the residents of the camp looked on with wonder in their eyes. 25

## Chapter 12 - Indra praises Kṛṣṇa

## \|\| 5.12.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*dhṛte govardhane śaile paritrāte ca gokule \|*  
*rojayāmāsa kṛṣṇasyadarśanaṃ pākaśāsanaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

After Kṛṣṇa raised the hill and saved the cattle camp, Indra, subduer of the demon Pāka, now desired to see him. 1

## \|\| 5.12.2 \|\|

*so 'dhiruhya mahānāgam airāvatam amitrajit \|*  
*govardhanagirau kṛṣṇaṃ dadarśa tridaśeśvaraḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Mounted on Airāvata, his mighty elephant, the lord of thirteen deities and conqueror of his foe found Kṛṣṇa on Govardhana. 2

## \|\| 5.12.3 \|\|

*cārayantaṃ mahāvīryaṃ gāstu gopavapurdharam \|*  
*kṛtsnasya jagato gopaṃ vṛtaṃ gopakumārakaiḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

There, mighty Kṛṣṇa, herdsman of the universe in the form of a common herding boy, grazed his cows alongside other lads. 3

## \|\| 5.12.4 \|\|

*garuḍaṃ ca dadarśoccair antardhānagataṃ dvija \|*  
*kṛtacchāyaṃ harermūrdhni pakṣābhyāṃ pakṣipuṅgavam \|\| 4 \|\|*

Far above, Indra also saw Garuḍa, chief of birds, invisible to mortals, brahmin, shading Hari’s head beneath his wings. 4

## \|\| 5.12.5 \|\|

*avaruhya sa nāgendrādekānte madhusūdanam \|*  
*śakraḥsasmitamāhedaṃ prītivistāritekṣaṇaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Mighty Indra descended from the regal elephant and, standing to one side, smiled as he spoke to Kṛṣṇa, slayer of the demon Madhu, with affection in his eyes. 5

## \|\| 5.12.6 \|\|

*indra uvāca*  
*kṛṣṇakṛṣṇa śṛṇuṣvedaṃ yadartha mahāmāgataḥ \|*  
*tvatsamīpaṃ mahābāho naitac cintyaṃ tvāyānyathā \|\| 6 \|\|*

Indra:

Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa! Hear why I have come to you. Otherwise, blessed being, you’ll never guess the reason. 6

## \|\| 5.12.7 \|\|

*bhārāvatāraṇārthāya pṛthivyāḥ pṛthivītale \|*  
*avatīrṇo 'khilādhāra tvameva parameśvara \|\| 7 \|\|*

You alone, foundation of the universe, are lord supreme, come down to earth to ease its burden. 7

## \|\| 5.12.8 \|\|

*makhabhaṅgavirodhena mayā gokulānāśakāḥ \|*  
*samādiṣṭā mahāmeghāstaiś cedaṃ kadanaṃ kṛtam \|\| 8 \|\|*

Because my festival was stopped, I sent those clouds to inundate the camp. This calamity was wrought by them. 8

## \|\| 5.12.9 \|\|

*trātāstāś ca tvāyā gāvaḥsamutpāṭya mahīdharam \|*  
*tenāhaṃ toṣito vīrakarmaṇātyadbhutena te \|\| 9 \|\|*

You raised the mighty mountain up and rescued all the cattle. I was thrilled by your marvellous undertaking, hero. 9

## \|\| 5.12.10 \|\|

*sādhinaṃ kṛṣṇa devānāmahaṃ manye prayojanam \|*  
*tvayāyamadripravaraḥ kareṇaikenayaddhṛtaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

I believe you satisfied the gods’ intention, Kṛṣṇa, as you held aloft this best of mountains with just one hand. 10

## \|\| 5.12.11 \|\|

*gobhiś ca coditaḥ kṛṣṇa tvatsakāśamihāgataḥ \|*  
*tvayā trātābhir atyarthaṃ yuṣmatsatkārakāraṇāt \|\| 11 \|\|*

Urged by the very cows you rescued, Kṛṣṇa, I’m here because I want to offer friendly service. 11

## \|\| 5.12.12 \|\|

*sa tvāṃ kṛṣṇābhiṣekṣyāmi gāvaṃ vākyapracoditaḥ \|*  
*upendratve gavamidro govindastvaṃ bhaviṣyasi \|\| 12 \|\|*

At the cattle’s own suggestion, I’ll consecrate you, Kṛṣṇa, as Upendra, my younger brother. As lord of cattle, you’ll be known as Govinda, ‘Finder of the Cows’. 12

## \|\| 5.12.13 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*athopavāhyādādāya ghaṇṭām airāvatād gajāt \|*  
*abhiṣekaṃ tayā cakre pavitrajalapūrṇayā \|\| 13 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Taking a vessel of purifying water from the back of Airāvata, Indra then performed the consecration. 13

## \|\| 5.12.14 \|\|

*kriyamāṇe 'bhiṣeke tu gāvaḥ kṛṣṇasya tatkṣaṇāt \|*  
*prasnavodbhūtadugdhārdrāṃ sadyaścakrurvasundharām \|\| 14 \|\|*

As Kṛṣṇa was being anointed, the cattle flooded all the world with streams of wondrous milk. 14

## \|\| 5.12.15 \|\|

*abhiṣicya gavāṃ vākyādupendraṃ vai janārdanam \|*  
*prītyā sapraśrayaṃ vākyaṃ punarāha śacīpatiḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

After Indra, king of gods and Śacī’s lord, had consecrated Janārdana at the cows’ behest, he spoke with tenderness to Kṛṣṇa, who was bowing with respect: 15

## \|\| 5.12.16 \|\|

*gavāmetatkṛtaṃ vākyaṃ tathānyadapi me śṛṇu \|*  
*yadbavīmi mahābhāga bhārāvataraṇecchayā \|\| 16 \|\|*

‘I’ve done as the cows suggested. Now hear what else I have to say, fortunate being, as I also wish to ease the burden of the earth. 16

## \|\| 5.12.17 \|\|

*mamāṃśaḥ puruṣavyāghra pṛthivyāṃ pṛthivīdhara \|*  
*avatīrṇor'junonāma saṃrakṣyo bhavatā sadā \|\| 17 \|\|*

An aspect of myself has come into the world, you tiger of a man. His name is Arjuna, and you must always shield him. 17

## \|\| 5.12.18 \|\|

*bhārāvataraṇe sāhyaṃ sa te vīraḥ kariṣyati \|*  
*saṃrakṣaṇīyo bhavatā yathātmā madhusūdana \|\| 18 \|\|*

He will help you ease the earthly burden, Madhusūdana. Guard this hero as you would yourself.’ 18

## \|\| 5.12.19 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*jānāmi bhārate vaṃśe jātaṃ pārthaṃ tavāṃśataḥ \|*  
*tamahaṃ pālayiṣyāmi yāvatsthāsyami bhūtale \|\| 19 \|\|*

The Lord:

I know your aspect has been born as Arjuna in the clan of Bhārata, and I’ll protect him for as long as I remain on earth. 19

## \|\| 5.12.20 \|\|

*yāvanmahītale śakra sthāsyāmyahamarindama \|*  
*na tāvadarjunaṃ kaściddevendra yudhi jeṣyati \|\| 20 \|\|*

While I’m in this world, mighty Indra, none shall vanquish him in battle, victorious king of gods. 20

## \|\| 5.12.21 \|\|

*kaṃso nāma mahābāhurdaityo 'riṣṭastathāsuraḥ \|*  
*keśī kuvalayāpīḍo narakādyās tathā pare \|\| 21 \|\|*

A powerful Daitya known as Kaṃsa, one called Ariṣṭa and others such as Keśin, Kuvalayāpīḍa and Naraka— 21

## \|\| 5.12.22 \|\|

*hateṣu teṣu devendra bhaviṣyati mahāhavaḥ \|*  
*tatra viddhi sahasrākṣa bhārāvataraṇaṃ kṛtam \|\| 22 \|\|*

When all these demons have been slain, king of gods with a thousand eyes, there will be a mighty conflict, during which, you’ll understand, the burden of the earth will be relieved. 22

## \|\| 5.12.23 \|\|

*sa tvaṃ gaccha na saṃtāpaṃ putrārthe kartumarhasi \|*  
*nārjunasya ripuḥ kaścinmamāgre prabhaviṣyati \|\| 23 \|\|*

Go now, worry not about your son. No enemy will prevail while I stand before him. 23

## \|\| 5.12.24 \|\|

*arjunārthe tvahaṃ sarvānyudhiṣṭhirapurogamān \|*  
*nivṛtte bhārate yuddhe kuntyai dāsyāmyavikṣatām \|\| 24 \|\|*

For the sake of Arjuna, when the Bhārata war is ended, I’ll restore to Kuntī all her sons unharmed, with Yudhiṣṭhira at their head. 24

## \|\| 5.12.25 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktaḥ saṃpariṣvajya devārājo janārdanam \|*  
*āruhyaurāvataṃ nāgaṃ punareva divaṃ yayau \|\| 25 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, Indra, king of gods, embraced Janārdana, mounted his elephant Airāvata and ascended to heaven. 25

## \|\| 5.12.26 \|\|

*kṛṣṇo hi sahito gobhir gopālaiś ca punarvrajam \|*  
*ājagāmātha gopīnāṃ dṛṣṭipūtena vartmanā \|\| 26 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa, together with the cows and other herdsmen, then returned to camp along a path rendered pure by the herding women’s gaze. 26

## Chapter 13 - Kṛṣṇa delights the herding women

## \|\| 5.13.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*gate śakre tu gopālāḥ kṛṣṇamakliṣṭakāriṇam \|*  
*ūcuḥ prītyā dhṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tena govardhanācalam \|\| 1 \|\|*

After mighty Indra had departed, and having witnessed Kṛṣṇa, untouched by deeds, holding Govardhana aloft, the herdsmen addressed him lovingly: 1

## \|\| 5.13.2 \|\|

*vayamasmānmahābhāga bhagavanmahato bhayāt \|*  
*gāvaś ca bhavatā trātā giridhāraṇakarmaṇā \|\| 2 \|\|*

‘When you lifted up the mountain, fortunate being, you saved us and our cattle from disaster. 2

## \|\| 5.13.3 \|\|

*bālakrīḍeyamatulā gopālatvaṃ jugupsitam \|*  
*divyaṃ ca bhavataḥ karma kimetattāta kathyatām \|\| 3 \|\|*

You made it look like child’s play, but it was no deed a cowherd boy could do. It was a supernatural feat! You must explain all this to us, dear boy. 3

## \|\| 5.13.4 \|\|

*kālīyo damitastoye dhenuko vinipātitaḥ \|*  
*dhṛto govardhanaścāyaṃ śaṅkitāni manāṃsi naḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Kāliya was killed in the lake, Pralamba was cast down and Mount Govardhana was raised. Our minds are filled with consternation. 4

## \|\| 5.13.5 \|\|

*satyaṃ satyaṃ hareḥ pādau śapāmo 'mita vikrama \|*  
*yathāvadvīryamālokya na tvāṃ manyāmahe naram \|\| 5 \|\|*

Truly, truly, we swear an oath at Hari’s feet, since we witnessed your prowess, we no longer consider you, whose courage is immeasurable, to be an ordinary mortal. 5

## \|\| 5.13.6 \|\|

*prītiḥsastrīkumārasya vrajasya tvayi keśava \|*  
*karma cedamaśakyaṃ yatsamastais tridaśerapi \|\| 6 \|\|*

This whole camp, including all the women and the children, love you, Keśava. Even the thirteen deities couldn’t do what you have done. 6

## \|\| 5.13.7 \|\|

*bālatvaṃ cātivīryatvaṃ janma cāsmāsu śobhanam \|*  
*cintyamānamameyātmañchaṅkāṃ kṛṣṇa prayacchati \|\| 7 \|\|*

When we consider your youth, great strength and glorious birth among us, it makes us worry, Kṛṣṇa. You’re beyond all estimation. 7

## \|\| 5.13.8 \|\|

*devo vā dānavo vā tvaṃ yakṣo gandharva eva vā \|*  
*kimasmākaṃ vicāreṇa bāndhavo 'si namostu te \|\| 8 \|\|*

Are you a deity, a Dānava, a yakṣa or a gandharva? Why ponder further? You’re our kinsman and we bow down to you.’ 8

## \|\| 5.13.9 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*kṣaṇaṃ bhūtvā tvasau tūṣṇīṃ kiñcitpraṇayakopavān \|*  
*ity evam uktas tair gopaiḥ kṛṣṇo 'py āha mahāmatiḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After the herdsmen had spoken thus, Kṛṣṇa, slightly peeved, great sage, was silent for a moment, then gave them this reply. 9

## \|\| 5.13.10 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*matsaṃbandhena vo gopā yadi lajjā na jāyate \|*  
*ślāghyo vāhaṃ tataḥ kiṃ vo vicāreṇa prayojanam \|\| 10 \|\|*

The Lord:

If you herding folk are not ashamed to be my kin, and if my deeds are worthy of your praise, then what’s the use of further contemplation? 10

## \|\| 5.13.11 \|\|

*yadi vo 'sti mayi prītiḥ ślāghyo 'haṃ bhavatāṃ yadi \|*  
*tadātmabandhusadṛśī buddhirvaḥ kriyātāṃ mayi \|\| 11 \|\|*

If you love me and I seem worthy to you, then regard me as the same as other relatives. 11

## \|\| 5.13.12 \|\|

*nāhaṃ devo na gāndharvo na yakṣo na ca dānavaḥ \|*  
*ahaṃ vo bāndhavo jāto naitaccintyamito 'nyathā \|\| 12 \|\|*

I’m no deity, no gandharva, no yakṣa or Dānava. I was born your kinsman. Please don’t think of me in any other way. 12

## \|\| 5.13.13 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*iti śrutvā harervākyaṃ baddhamaunāstato vanam \|*  
*yayurgopā mahābhāga tasmanpraṇayakopini \|\| 13 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Having heard these words of Hari and sensing his displeasure, fortunate sage, the herdsmen all fell silent, then set out for the forest. 13

## \|\| 5.13.14 \|\|

*kṛṣṇastu vimalaṃ vyoma śaraccandrasya candrikām \|*  
*tadā kumudinīṃ phullāmāmoditadigantarām \|\| 14 \|\|*

Seeing the clear night sky, the splendour of the autumn moon, the lotus forest in full bloom, perfuming the air in all directions, 14

## \|\| 5.13.15 \|\|

*vanarājīṃ tathā kūjandbhṛṅgamālāmanoharām \|*  
*vilokya saha gopībhir manaścakre ratiṃ prati \|\| 15 \|\|*

And wooded paths adorned with wreaths of humming bees, Kṛṣṇa set his heart on making love with the herding women. 15

## \|\| 5.13.16 \|\|

*vinā rāmeṇa madhuramatīva vanitāpriyam \|*  
*jagau kalapadaṃ śauristāramandrakṛtakramam \|\| 16 \|\|*

In the absence of his brother, Kṛṣṇa began to sing those sweet romantic songs that women love to hear. 16

## \|\| 5.13.17 \|\|

*ramyaṃ gītadhvaniṃ śrutvā saṃtyajyāvasathāṃstadā \|*  
*ājagmustvaritā gopyo yatrāste madhusūdanaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Hearing the seductive sound of Kṛṣṇa’s voice, the herding girls all left their homes and hastened to Madhu’s subduer. 17

## \|\| 5.13.18 \|\|

*śanaiḥ śanair jagau gopī kācittasya layānugam \|*  
*dattāvadhānā kācicca tameva manasāsmarat \|\| 18 \|\|*

One woman softly joined his melody; another, with fixed attention, drew him to her mind. 18

## \|\| 5.13.19 \|\|

*kācitkṛṣṇetikṛṣṇeti proktvā lajjāmupāyayau \|*  
*yayau ca kācitpremāndhā tatpārśvamavilaṃbitam \|\| 19 \|\|*

One cried out ‘Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa!’, but then was overcome with shyness. Another, blind with love, brashly sidled up to him. 19

## \|\| 5.13.20 \|\|

*kāciccāvasathasyānte sthitvā dṛṣṭvā bahirgurum \|*  
*tanmayatvena govindaṃ dadhyau mīlitalocanā \|\| 20 \|\|*

One saw her elders outside the house, so, staying indoors, she closed her eyes and contemplated Govinda, as if she were as one with him. 20

## \|\| 5.13.21 \|\|

*taccittavimalāhlādakṣīṇapuṇyacayā tathā \|*  
*tadaprāptimahāduḥkhavilīnāśeṣapātakā \|\| 21 \|\|*

The bliss she felt in doing so released her from the faults that she’d acquired; her anguish at not seeing Kṛṣṇa freed her from her sins. 21

## \|\| 5.13.22 \|\|

*cintayatī jagatsūtiṃ parabrahmasvarūpiṇam \|*  
*nirucchvāsatayā muktiṃ gatānyā gopakanyakā \|\| 22 \|\|*

Contemplating the creator of the world in the form of the highest Absolute, another of the herders’ girls won liberation just because she sighed so deeply. 22

## \|\| 5.13.23 \|\|

*gopīparivṛto rātriṃ śaraccandramanoramām \|*  
*mānayāmāsa govindo rāsāraṃbharasotsukaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Surrounded by the herding women, Govinda, eager to experience the joy of dancing in a ring, paid homage to the night rendered lovely by the autumn moon. 23

## \|\| 5.13.24 \|\|

*gopyaś ca vṛndaśaḥ kṛṣṇaceṣṭāsvāyattamūrtayaḥ \|*  
*anyadeśaṃ gate kṛṣṇe cerurvṛndāvanāntaram \|\| 24 \|\|*

When Kṛṣṇa had slipped away, groups of women, acting out his movements with their bodies, roamed about the forest of Vṛndavāna. 24

## \|\| 5.13.25 \|\|

*kṛṣṇe nibaddhahṛdayā idamūcuḥ parasparam \|\| 25 \|\|*

‘I’m Kṛṣṇa, so elegantly am I walking. Watch my gait!’ said one, ‘and listen to me sing his song.’ 25

## \|\| 5.13.26 \|\|

*kṛṣṇo 'hameṣa lalitaṃ vrajāmyālokyatāṃ gatiḥ \|*  
*anyā bravīnti kṛṣṇasya mama gītirniśamyatām \|\| 26 \|\|*

‘Wicked Kāliya, stay where you are, for I am Kṛṣṇa!’ another cried, waving her arms while acting out his encounter with that serpent. 26

## \|\| 5.13.27 \|\|

*duṣṭakāliya tiṣṭhātra kṛṣṇohamiti cāparā \|*  
*bāhumāsphoṭya kṛṣṇasya līlayā sarvamādade \|\| 27 \|\|*

Another said, ‘Come, herdsmen, don’t be frightened. You stay here. Don’t fear the tempest. I’m holding up Govardhana.’ 27

## \|\| 5.13.28 \|\|

*anyā bravīti bho gopā niḥśaṅkaiḥ sthīyatāmiti \|*  
*alaṃ vṛṣṭibhayenātra dhṛto govardhano mayā \|\| 28 \|\|*

‘I’ve defeated Dhenuka, now graze your cattle where you wish,’ said another herding girl while aping Kṛṣṇa’s deeds. 28

## \|\| 5.13.29 \|\|

*dhenuko 'yaṃ mayākṣipto vicarantu yathecchayā \|*  
*gāvo bravīti caivānyā kṛṣṇalīlānusāriṇī \|\| 29 \|\|*

Thus, the herding women, intent on Kṛṣṇa’s various acts, roamed together through Vṛndavāna’s lovely woodlands. 29

## \|\| 5.13.30 \|\|

*evaṃ nānāprakārāsu kṛṣṇaceṣṭa su tāstadā \|*  
*gopyo vyagrāḥsamaṃ cerū ramyaṃ vṛndāvanāntaram \|\| 30 \|\|*

One of the foremost herding girls looked at the ground, then cried aloud, as the hairs on every limb stood up and her lotus eyes grew wide: 30

## \|\| 5.13.31 \|\|

*vilokyaikā bhuvaṃ prāha gopī gopavarāṅganā \|*  
*pulakāñcitasarvāṅgī vikāsinayanotpalā \|\| 31 \|\|*

‘See this line of footprints marked with banner, thunderbolt, goad and lotus? They were made by Kṛṣṇa, his paces rendered lovelier by his games. 31

## \|\| 5.13.32 \|\|

*dhvajabajrāṅkuśābjāṅkarekhāvantyāli paśyata \|*  
*padānyetāni kṛṣṇasya līlālalitagāminaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

Some lucky girl, drunk with passion, has gone with him. Her steps were short and stumbling. 32

## \|\| 5.13.33 \|\|

*kāpi tena samāyātā kṛtapuṇyā madālasā \|*  
*padāni tasyāścaitāni ghanānyalpatanūni ca \|\| 33 \|\|*

Here’s where great Dāmodara picked some flowers while standing on his toes. 33

## \|\| 5.13.34 \|\|

*puṣpāpacayamatroccaiś cakre dāmodare dhruvam \|*  
*yenāgrākrāntamātrāṇi padānyatra mahātmanaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

She was seated over there when Kṛṣṇa placed the garland on her. In another life she must have worshiped Viṣṇu with all her heart. 34

## \|\| 5.13.35 \|\|

*atropaviśya vai tena kācitpuṣpair alaṅkṛtā \|*  
*anyajanmani sarvātmā viṣṇur abhyarcitas tayā \|\| 35 \|\|*

Once honoured with the garland, she grew proud. Look! The herder Nanda’s son deserted her and set off by another path. 35

## \|\| 5.13.36 \|\|

*puṣpabandhanasaṃmānakṛtamānāmapāsya tām \|*  
*nandagopasuto yāto mārgeṇānena paśyata \|\| 36 \|\|*

Here a different girl gave up the chase because her hips were heavy. She hurried after him and left behind deep footprints. 36

## \|\| 5.13.37 \|\|

*anuyātainamatrānyā nitaṃbabharamantharā \|*  
*yā gantavye drutaṃ yāti nimnapādāgrasaṃsthitiḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

This girl placed her fingers in his hand while walking, friend. The mingling of their footprints shows her movements were uncertain. 37

## \|\| 5.13.38 \|\|

*hastanyastāgrahasteyaṃ tena yāti tathā sakhī \|*  
*anāyattapadanyāsā lakṣyate padapaddhatiḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

But as he touched her only with his hand, that cad then let her down. Her footprints show she turned back slowly, disappointed. 38

## \|\| 5.13.39 \|\|

*hastasaṃsparśamātreṇa dhūrtenaiṣā vimānitā \|*  
*nairāśyānmandagāminyā nivṛttaṃ lakṣyate padam \|\| 39 \|\|*

Surely, he had said to her, “I’ll be back beside you shortly”, for here lie Kṛṣṇa’s hurried footprints. 39

## \|\| 5.13.40 \|\|

*nūnamuktā tvarāmīti punareṣyāmitentikam \|*  
*tena kṛṣṇena yenaiṣā tvaritā padapaddhatiḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

As Kṛṣṇa went into this thicket, we can see his tracks no more. We should go back, as the moonlight doesn’t reach this far.’ 40

## \|\| 5.13.41 \|\|

*praviṣṭo gahanaṃ kṛṣṇaḥ padamatra na lakṣyate \|*  
*nivartadhvaṃ śaśāṅkasya naitaddī dhitigocare \|\| 41 \|\|*

The herding women then turned away, despairing of the sight of Kṛṣṇa, but when they reached the Yamunā, they sang about his exploits. 41

## \|\| 5.13.42 \|\|

*nivṛttāstāstadā gopyo nirāśāḥ kṛṣṇadarśane \|*  
*yamunātīramāsādya jagustaccaritaṃ tathā \|\| 42 \|\|*

It’s then they saw him drawing near—protector of the worlds, untouched by deeds—his face a full-blown lotus. 42

## \|\| 5.13.43 \|\|

*tato dadṛśurāyāntaṃ vikāsimukhapaṅkajam \|*  
*gopyas trailokyagoptāraṃ kṛṣṇamakliṣṭaceṣṭitam \|\| 43 \|\|*

One girl was overcome with joy at seeing Govinda there. ‘Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa,’ was all that she could say. 43

## \|\| 5.13.44 \|\|

*kācidālokya govindamāyāntamatiharṣitā \|*  
*kṛṣṇākṛṣṇeti kṛṣṇeti prāha nānyadudīrayat \|\| 44 \|\|*

Another knit her brows and, seeing Hari’s handsome forehead, drank his lotus features with eyes as black as bees. 44

## \|\| 5.13.45 \|\|

*kācidbhrūbhaṅguraṃ kṛtvā lalāṭaphalakaṃ harim \|*  
*vilokya netrabhṛṅgābhyāṃ papau tanmukhapaṅkajam \|\| 45 \|\|*

One, having glanced at Govinda, closed her eyes and, contemplating him, appeared to be absorbed in yoga. 45

## \|\| 5.13.46 \|\|

*kācidālokya govindaṃ nimīlitavilocanā \|*  
*tasyaiva rūpaṃ dhyāyantī yogārūḍheva sā babhau \|\| 46 \|\|*

Then Kṛṣṇa, Madhu’s scion, discreetly led some girls with loving words, some with looks and raised eyebrows and others with his hand. 46

## \|\| 5.13.47 \|\|

*tataḥ kāñcit priyālāpaiḥ kāñcid bhrūbhaṅgavīkṣitaiḥ \|*  
*ninye 'nunayamanyāṃ ca karasparśena mādhavaḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

Noble Hari politely pleased those herding women assembled for the circle dance, gladdening their hearts. 47

## \|\| 5.13.48 \|\|

*tābhiḥ prasannacittābhir gopībhiḥ saha sādaram \|*  
*rarāsa rāsagoṣṭhībhir udāracarito hariḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

But the circle couldn’t be completed because the girls, unwilling to leave the side of Kṛṣṇa, were all standing on the spot. 48

## \|\| 5.13.49 \|\|

*rāsamaṇḍalabandhopi kṛṣṇapārśvamanujjhatā \|*  
*gopījanena naivābhūdekasthānasthirātmanā \|\| 49 \|\|*

Hari closed the eyes of every girl with his fingertips and, leading them by the hand, arranged them in a ring. 49

## \|\| 5.13.50 \|\|

*hastena gṛhya caikaikāṃ gopīnāṃ rāsamaṇḍale \|*  
*cakāra tatkarasparśanimīlitadṛśaṃ hariḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

Then the dance began with the jingling of their swaying bracelets, followed by the sound of songs with lyrics suited to the season. 50

## \|\| 5.13.51 \|\|

*tataḥ pravavṛte rāsaścaladvalayanisvanaḥ \|*  
*anuyātaśaratkāvyagoyagītiranukramāt \|\| 51 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa sang about the autumn moon, the moonlight and lotus pools, but the herding girls could only call his name. 51

## \|\| 5.13.52 \|\|

*kṛṣṇaḥ śaraccandramasaṃ kaumudīṃ kumudākaram \|*  
*jagau gopījanastveka kṛṣṇanāma punaḥpunaḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

Tiring of the circle dance, one herding girl, her bracelets tinkling, laid her slender arm on Madhu’s slayer’s shoulder. 52

## \|\| 5.13.53 \|\|

*parivṛttiśrameṇaikā caladvalayalāpinīm \|*  
*dadau bāhulatāṃ skandhe gopī madhunighātinaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

Another of the herding girls, slyly pretending to sing his praises, grabbed him by one arm and kissed him. 53

## \|\| 5.13.54 \|\|

*kācitpravilasadbāhuṃ parirabhya cucuṃba tam \|*  
*gopīgītastutivyājānnipuṇā madhusūdanam \|\| 54 \|\|*

Beads of sweat from Hari’s limbs, falling on the cowgirls’ faces, caused the down upon their cheeks to rise, just as rain brings forth the crops. 54

## \|\| 5.13.55 \|\|

*gopīkapolasaṃśleṣamabhigamya harerbhujau \|*  
*pulakodgamasasyāya svedāṃbughanatāṃ gatau \|\| 55 \|\|*

While Kṛṣṇa sang the song of the circle dance in a clear, clear voice, the women cried out twice as loud, ‘Well sung, Kṛṣṇa! Well sung!’ 55

## \|\| 5.13.56 \|\|

*rāsageyaṃ jagau kṛṣṇo yāvattārataradhvaniḥ \|*  
*sādhu kṛṣṇeti kṛṣṇeti tāvattā dviguṇaṃ jaguḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

When he led, they followed; when he turned, they moved to face him. When he danced to left or right, the women matched his every step. 56

## \|\| 5.13.57 \|\|

*gatenugamanaṃ cakrurvalanaṃ saṃmukhaṃ yayuḥ \|*  
*pratilomānulomābhyāṃ bhejurgaupāṅganā harim \|\| 57 \|\|*

While Madhu’s slayer played among the herding girls, one moment seemed ten million years without him. 57

## \|\| 5.13.58 \|\|

*sa tathā saha gopībhī rarāsa madhurūdanaḥ \|*  
*yathābdakoṭipratimaḥ śraṇastena vinābhavat \|\| 58 \|\|*

Even though their husbands, fathers and brothers forbade it, that night the girls enjoyed themselves with Kṛṣṇa; they all loved making love. 58

## \|\| 5.13.59 \|\|

*tā vāryamāṇāḥ patibhiḥ pitṛbhir bhrātṛbhis tathā \|*  
*kṛṣṇaṃ gopāṅganā rātrau ramayanti ratipriyāḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

Madhusūdana, a being beyond all limits and descriptions, enjoyed himself without restraint in youthful guise among the cowgirls on those nights. 59

## \|\| 5.13.60 \|\|

*so 'pi kaiśorakavayo mānayanmadhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*reme tābhir ameyātmā kṣapāsukṣapitāhitaḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

In his universal form, the lord pervaded them, their husbands and all creation, just as air is everywhere. 60

## \|\| 5.13.61 \|\|

*tadbhartṛṣu tathā tāsu sarvabhūteṣu ceśvaraḥ \|*  
*ātmasvarūparūpo 'sau vyāpī vāyuriva sthitaḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

This whole world consists of fire and space, earth and air and water. In that same way, he is all-pervading and ubiquitous. 61

## Chapter 14 - Kṛṣṇa slays Ariṣṭa the Bull-Demon

## \|\| 5.14.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara vāca*  
*pradoṣāgro kadācittu rāsāsakte janārdane \|*  
*trāsayansamado gauṣṭhamariṣṭasamupāgamat \|\| 1 \|\|*

As Janārdana enjoyed the circle dance one night, the furious bull-demon Ariṣṭa came to terrorise the camp. 1

## \|\| 5.14.2 \|\|

*sa toyatoyadacchāyastīkṣṇaśṛṅgorkalocanaḥ \|*  
*khurāgrapātair atyarthaṃ dārayandhāraṇītalam \|\| 2 \|\|*

As dark as rain-filled thunderclouds, his horns were sharp, his eyes ablaze like the sun itself and, with his hooves, he rent the earth. 2

## \|\| 5.14.3 \|\|

*lelihānaḥsaniṣpeṣaṃ jihvayoṣṭhau punaḥpunaḥ \|*  
*saṃraṃbhāviddhalāṅgūlaḥ kaṭhinaskandhabandhanaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

He smacked his tongue against his lips and flicked his tail in fury, his shoulders strongly knit together. 3

## \|\| 5.14.4 \|\|

*udagrakakudābhogapramāṇo duratikramaḥ \|*  
*viṇmū6traliptapṛṣṭhāṅgo gavamudvegakārakaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

The great hump upon his back was tall beyond any measure, his hind limbs were covered with muck as he terrorised the cows. 4

## \|\| 5.14.5 \|\|

*pralaṃbakaṇṭho 'timukhastarukhātāṅkitānanaḥ \|*  
*pātayansa gāvaṃ garbhandaityo vṛṣabharūpadhṛk \|\| 5 \|\|*

His dewlap hung below his mighty jaw and his face was scarred from butting trees. In bovine form, this Daitya caused the cows to cast the young ones from their wombs. 5

## \|\| 5.14.6 \|\|

*sūdayaṃstāpasānugro vanānaṭati yaḥsadā \|\| 6 \|\|*

The peerless beast laid waste to forests with his energy as he roamed. 6

## \|\| 5.14.7 \|\|

*tatastamatighorākṣamavekṣyātibhayāturāḥ \|*  
*gopā gopastriyaścaiva kṛṣṇakṛṣṇeti cukruśuḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Beholding this creature with such terrifying eyes, the herders and their womenfolk, overcome with fear, cried, ‘Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!’ 7

## \|\| 5.14.8 \|\|

*siṃhanādaṃ tataś cakre talaśabdaṃ ca keśavaḥ \|*  
*tacchabda śravaṇāccāsau dāmodaramupāyayau \|\| 8 \|\|*

Keśava gave his lion’s roar and clapped his hands. Hearing this, the beast raced forward to face Dāmodara. 8

## \|\| 5.14.9 \|\|

*agranyastaviṣāṇāgraḥ kṛṣṇakukṣikṛtekṣaṇaḥ \|*  
*abhyadhāvata duṣṭātmā kṛṣṇaṃ vṛṣabhadānavaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

The wicked bull-demon lowered his horns, fixed his eyes on Kṛṣṇa’s belly and charged towards him. 9

## \|\| 5.14.10 \|\|

*āyāntaṃ daityavṛṣabhaṃ dṛṣṭvā kṛṣṇo mahābalaḥ \|*  
*na cacāla tadāsthānādavajñāsmitalīlayā \|\| 10 \|\|*

Seeing the Daitya bull approaching, mighty Kṛṣṇa stood his ground and smiled at him with playful disrespect. 10

## \|\| 5.14.11 \|\|

*āsannaṃ caiva jagrāha grāhavanmadhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*jaghāna jānunā kukṣau viṣāṇagrahaṇācalam \|\| 11 \|\|*

Madhu’s slayer, like a crocodile, seized the bull as he drew near and, holding him steady by the horns, kneed him in the abdomen. 11

## \|\| 5.14.12 \|\|

*tasya darpabalaṃ bhaṅktvā gṛhītasya viṣāṇayoḥ \|*  
*apīḍayadariṣṭasya kaṇṭhaṃ klinnamivāṃbaram \|\| 12 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa pinned him down, snapped the pride and strength of his two horns, then wrung Ariṣṭa’s neck like some wet rag. 12

## \|\| 5.14.13 \|\|

*utpāṭya śṛṅgamekaṃ tu tenaivātāḍayattataḥ \|*  
*mamāra sa mahādaityau mukhācchoṇitamudvaman \|\| 13 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa then tore out one horn and beat the Daitya with it. The bull fell down and died at once, blood gushing from his mouth. 13

## \|\| 5.14.14 \|\|

*tuṣṭuvur nihate tasmin daitye gopā janārdanam \|*  
*jaṃbhe hate sahasrākṣaṃ purā devagaṇā yathā \|\| 14 \|\|*

When this Daitya had been slain, the herdsmen all praised Janārdana, as in former times the hosts of gods praised Indra of a thousand eyes for killing the demon Jambha. 14

## Chapter 15 - Kaṃsa sends Akrūra to the cattle camp

## \|\| 5.15.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*kakudmati hate 'riṣṭe dhenuke vinipātite \|*  
*pralaṃbe nidhanaṃ nīte dhṛte govardhanācale \|\| 1 \|\|*

After Ariṣṭa the hump-backed bull was slain, Dhenuka overthrown, Pralamba led to death and Mount Govardhana raised, 1

## \|\| 5.15.2 \|\|

*damite kāliye nāge bhagnetuṅgadrumadvaye \|*  
*hatāyāṃ pūtanāyāṃ ca śakaṭe parivartite \|\| 2 \|\|*

The nāga Kāliya vanquished, the tall trees felled, Pūtanā killed and the wagon overturned, 2

## \|\| 5.15.3 \|\|

*kaṃsāya nāradaḥ prāha yathāvṛttamanukramāt \|*  
*yaśodādevakīgarbaparivṛttadyaśeṣataḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Nārada informed Kaṃsa of all of these events in order, and that the infants of Yaśodā and Devakī had been exchanged. 3

## \|\| 5.15.4 \|\|

*śrutvā tatsakalaṃ kaṃso nāradāddevadarśanāt \|*  
*vasudevaṃ prati tadā kopaṃ cakre sudurmatiḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Hearing this from Nārada, the sage with divine perception, wicked Kaṃsa was furious with Vasudeva. 4

## \|\| 5.15.5 \|\|

*sotikopādupālabhya sarvayādavasaṃsadi \|*  
*jagarja yādavāṃścaiva kāryaṃ caitadacintayat \|\| 5 \|\|*

At a meeting of the Yādava clan, filled with fury, he threatened and reproached the clansmen, then pondered his next move: 5

## \|\| 5.15.6 \|\|

*yāvanna balamārūḍhau rāmakṛṣṇau subālakau \|*  
*tāvadeva mayā vadhyāvasādhyau rūḍhayauvanau \|\| 6 \|\|*

‘I’ll kill Balarāma and his brother while they’re young and weak, because it won’t be possible when they’ve come of age. 6

## \|\| 5.15.7 \|\|

*cāṇūrotra mahāvīryo muṣṭikaś ca mahābalaḥ \|*  
*etābhyāṃ mallayuddhena mārayiṣyāma durmatī \|\| 7 \|\|*

Mighty Cāṇūra and powerful Muṣṭika are here. I’ll have them kill those two young fools in a wrestling match. 7

## \|\| 5.15.8 \|\|

*dhanurmahamahāyogavyājenānīya tau vrajāt \|*  
*tathā tathā yatiṣyāmi yāsyete saṃkṣayaṃ yathā \|\| 8 \|\|*

Under the pretext of some great tournament, I’ll invite them from their cattle camp, then I’ll do what’s needed to send them to their doom. 8

## \|\| 5.15.9 \|\|

*śvaphalpatanayaṃ śūramakrūraṃ yadupuṅgavam \|*  
*tayorānayanārthāya preṣayiṣyāmi gokulam \|\| 9 \|\|*

I’ll dispatch Śvaphalka’s son Akrūra, that bull among the Yādavas, to their camp to fetch them here. 9

## \|\| 5.15.10 \|\|

*vṛndā vanacaraṃ ghoramādekṣyāmi ca keśinam \|*  
*tatraivāsāvatibalastāvubhau ghātayiṣyati \|\| 10 \|\|*

Then I’ll send an order to Keśin, the terrible beast who roams Vṛndāvana. He’s strong enough to kill the two boys there. 10

## \|\| 5.15.11 \|\|

*gajaḥ kuvalāyāpīḍo matsakāśamihāgatau \|*  
*ghātayiṣyati vā goṣau vasudevasutāvubhau \|\| 11 \|\|*

If those sons of Vasudeva make it here alive, the elephant Kuvalayāpīḍa will trample them to death.’ 11

## \|\| 5.15.12 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityālocya sa duṣṭātmā kaṃso rāmajanārdanau \|*  
*hantuṃ kṛtam artivīrāv akrūraṃ vākyam avṛbravīt \|\| 12 \|\|*

Parāśara:

With this thought, wicked Kaṃsa resolved to kill Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa. He then addressed heroic Akrūra: 12

## \|\| 5.15.13 \|\|

*kaṃsa uvāca*  
*bho bho dānapate vākyaṃ kriyatāṃ prītaye mama \|*  
*itaḥ syandanamāruhya gamyatāṃ nandagokulam \|\| 13 \|\|*

Kaṃsa:

Come, my generous man, and do as I command. You’ll be doing me a favour. Take a chariot and ride to Nanda’s cattle camp. 13

## \|\| 5.15.14 \|\|

*vasudevasutau tatra viṣṇor aṃśasamudbhavau \|*  
*nāśāya kila saṃbhūtau mama duṣṭau pravardhataḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Both of Vasudeva’s evil sons, Viṣṇu’s aspects born for my destruction, are maturing there. 14

## \|\| 5.15.15 \|\|

*dhanurmaho mamāpyatra caturdaśyāṃ bhaviṣyati \|*  
*āneyau bhavatā gatvā mallayuddhāya tatra tau \|\| 15 \|\|*

On the fourteenth day this month, I’ll hold a tournament here. Go to the camp and invite them to a wrestling match. 15

## \|\| 5.15.16 \|\|

*cāṇūramuṣṭikau mallau niyuddhakuśalau mama \|*  
*tābhyāṃ sahānayoryuddhaṃ sarvaloko 'tra paśyatu \|\| 16 \|\|*

My two wrestlers, Cāṇūra and Muṣṭika, are skilled in combat. I want everyone to watch the two boys fight with them. 16

## \|\| 5.15.17 \|\|

*gajaḥ kuvalayāpīḍo mahāmātrapracoditaḥ \|*  
*sa vā haniṣyate pāpau vasudevātmajau śiśu \|\| 17 \|\|*

If they fail, the elephant Kuvalayāpīḍa, urged forward by his driver, will crush Vasudeva’s two young wicked sons. 17

## \|\| 5.15.18 \|\|

*tau hatvā vasudevaṃ ca nandagopaṃ ca durmatim \|*  
*haniṣye pitaraṃ cainamugrasenaṃ sudurmatim \|\| 18 \|\|*

I’ll kill them first, then I’ll get rid of Vasudeva and that wicked-minded herder Nanda, as well as Ugrasena, my own father, who’s just as bad. 18

## \|\| 5.15.19 \|\|

*tataḥsamastagopānāṃ godhanānyakhilānyaham \|*  
*vittaṃ cāpahariṣyāmi duṣṭānāṃ madvadhaiṣiṇām \|\| 19 \|\|*

Next I’ll seize the herders’ cattle and all their other property. Those upstarts want me dead. 19

## \|\| 5.15.20 \|\|

*tvāmṛte yādavāścaite dviṣo dānapate mama \|*  
*eteṣāṃ ca vadhāyāhaṃ yatiṣye 'nukramāttataḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

Except for you, my generous man, all these evil Yādavas detest me, so I’ll kill them one by one. 20

## \|\| 5.15.21 \|\|

*tadā niṣkaṇṭakaṃ sarvaṃ rājyametadayādavam \|*  
*prasādhiṣye tvayā tasmānmatprītyai vīra gamyatām \|\| 21 \|\|*

With your assistance, I’ll then rule this whole domain, rid of the prickly Yādavas. Your love for me will take you there, brave man. 21

## \|\| 5.15.22 \|\|

*yathā ca māhiṣaṃ sarpirdadhicāpyupahārya vai \|*  
*gopāḥsamānayantvāśu tathā vācyāstvayā ca te \|\| 22 \|\|*

And tell the herdsmen to bring me all their yoghurt and buffalo butter. 22

## \|\| 5.15.23 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityājñaptastadākrūromahābhāgavato dvaja \|*  
*prītimānabhavatkṛṣṇaṃ śvodrakṣyāmīti satvaraḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Parāśara:

On hearing this command, Akrūra was filled at once with love for Kṛṣṇa, brahmin, knowing that the next day he would see him. 23

## \|\| 5.15.24 \|\|

*tathetyaktvā ca rājānaṃ rathamāruhya śobhanam \|*  
*niścakrāma tataḥ puryā mathurāyā madhupriyaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Akrūra, assenting to the king’s request, mounted a splendid chariot. Then that friend of Madhu set out from the city of Mathurā. 24

## Chapter 16 - Kṛṣṇa slays Keśin the Horse-Demon

## \|\| 5.16.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*keśī cāpi balodagraḥ kaṃsadūtaḥ pracoditaḥ \|*  
*kṛṣṇasya nidhanākāṅkṣī vṛndāvanamupāgamat \|\| 1 \|\|*

At the urging of a messenger sent by Kaṃsa, mighty Keśin approached Vṛndāvana with the aim of killing Kṛṣṇa. 1

## \|\| 5.16.2 \|\|

*sa khurakṣatabhūpṛṣṭhaḥsaṭākṣepadhutāṃbudaḥ \|*  
*drutavikrāntacandrārkamārgo gopānupādravat \|\| 2 \|\|*

Rending the surface of the earth with his hooves, scattering the clouds with a flick of his mane, at each leap, he transcended the orbits of the sun and moon as he charged towards the herdsmen. 2

## \|\| 5.16.3 \|\|

*tasya deṣitaśabdena gopālā daityavājinaḥ \|*  
*gopyaś ca bhayasaṃvignā govindaṃ śaraṇaṃ yayuḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

At the sound of the neighing of that horse-demon, the herders and their womenfolk, filled with terror, fled to Govinda for protection. 3

## \|\| 5.16.4 \|\|

*trāhitrāhiti govindaḥśrutvāteṣāṃ tato vacaḥ \|*  
*satoyajaladadhvānagaṃbhīramidamuktavān \|\| 4 \|\|*

Hearing their cries for help, Govinda reassured them with a voice as deep as thunder from a rain-filled cloud. 4

## \|\| 5.16.5 \|\|

*alaṃ trāsena gopālāḥ keśinaḥ kiṃ bhayāturaiḥ \|*  
*bhavidbhir gopajātī yair vīravīryaṃ vilopyate \|\| 5 \|\|*

The Lord:

‘Have no fear of Keśin, herdsmen. Have you cattle-folk, overcome with terror, lost your valour? 5

## \|\| 5.16.6 \|\|

*kimanonālpasāreṇa haṣitāṭopakāriṇā \|*  
*daiteyabalavāhyena valgatā duṣṭavājinā \|\| 6 \|\|*

Enough of this weakling who relies on whinnies, a steed with a Daitya’s strength, a wicked prancing pony! 6

## \|\| 5.16.7 \|\|

*ehyehi duṣṭa kṛṣṇo 'haṃ pūṣṇastviva pinākadhṛt \|*  
*pātayiṣyāmi daśanānvadanādakhilāṃstava \|\| 7 \|\|*

Come here, you wretch. I’m Kṛṣṇa and I’ll knock the teeth right out of your jaw, just as Pināka-wielding Śiva did to Pūṣan.’ 7

## \|\| 5.16.8 \|\|

*ityuktvāsphoṭya govindaḥ keśinaḥsaṃmukhaṃ yayau \|*  
*vivṛtāsyaś ca so 'py enaṃ daiteyāśva upādravat \|\| 8 \|\|*

So saying, Govinda clapped his hands and advanced on Keśin. The horse-demon charged at him, his mouth agape. 8

## \|\| 5.16.9 \|\|

*bāhumābhogitaṃ kṛtvā makhe tasya janārdanaḥ \|*  
*praveśayāmāsa tadā keśino duṣṭavājinaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Janārdana bent his arm and thrust it down the throat of Keśin, that evil steed. 9

## \|\| 5.16.10 \|\|

*keśino vadanaṃ tena viśatā kṛṣṇabāhunā \|*  
*śātitā daśanāḥ petuḥ sitābbrāvayavā iva \|\| 10 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa’s arm, now deep inside the horse’s mouth, smashed his teeth, which tumbled out like wisps of snowy cloud. 10

## \|\| 5.16.11 \|\|

*kṛṣṇasya vavṛdhe bāhuḥ keśidehagato dvija \|*  
*vināśāya yathā vyādhirāsaṃbhūterupekṣitaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

Inside Keśin’s body, Kṛṣṇa’s arm began to grow, brahmin, like a plague that’s in its early stages, in order to destroy the demon. 11

## \|\| 5.16.12 \|\|

*vipāṭitoṣṭho bahulaṃ saphenaṃ rudhiraṃ vaman \|*  
*sokṣiṇī vivṛte cakre viśiṣṭe muktabandhane \|\| 12 \|\|*

Keśin’s lips were split in two, foaming blood gushed from his mouth, his eyes rolled back and both popped out, and then his ligaments gave way. 12

## \|\| 5.16.13 \|\|

*jadhāna dharaṇīṃ pādaiḥ śakṛnmūtraṃ samutsṛjan \|*  
*svedārdragātrasśāntaś ca niryatnaḥso 'bhavattadā \|\| 13 \|\|*

Shitting and pissing, he pawed at the earth with his hooves. His limbs were drenched with sweat and, at last, when quite exhausted, he gave up the fight. 13

## \|\| 5.16.14 \|\|

*vyāditāsyamahāṃrandhraḥso 'suraḥ kṛṣṇabāhunā \|*  
*nipātito dvidhā bhūmau vaidyutena yathā drumaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

That terrible demon, his mouth rent open by Kṛṣṇa’s arm, fell to the ground like a tree that’s struck by lightning. 14

## \|\| 5.16.15 \|\|

*dvipāde pṛṣṭhapucchārdha śravaṇaikākṣināsike \|*  
*keśinaste dvidhā bhūte śakale dve virejatuḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Each half of Keśin’s body, torn in two, had one pair of legs, half a back, half a tail, one ear, one eye and a single nostril. 15

## \|\| 5.16.16 \|\|

*hatvā tu keśinaṃ kṛṣṇo gopālair muditair vṛtaḥ \|*  
*anāyastatanuḥsvastho hasaṃstatraiva tasthivān \|\| 16 \|\|*

After Kṛṣṇa killed the demon, the delighted herdsmen gathered round him as he stood there smiling, unwearied and unscathed. 16

## \|\| 5.16.17 \|\|

*tato gopyaś ca gopāś ca hate keśini vismitāḥ \|*  
*tuṣṭuvuḥ puṇḍarīkākṣamanurāgamanoramam \|\| 17 \|\|*

The herding men and women, amazed at Keśin’s death, praised lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa, whose affection brought them pleasure. 17

## \|\| 5.16.18 \|\|

*athāhāntarhito vipra nārado jalade sthitaḥ \|*  
*keśinaṃ nihataṃ dṛṣṭvā harṣanirbharamānasaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Then spoke the brahmin Nārada, unseen while riding on a cloud. Having seen the death of Keśin, his heart was filled with pleasure. 18

## \|\| 5.16.19 \|\|

*sādhusādhu jagannātha līlayaiva yadacyuta \|*  
*nihato 'yaṃ tvayā keśī kleśadastridivaukāsām \|\| 19 \|\|*

‘It’s excellent, excellent indeed, that you, eternal lord of all, slew this Keśin so easily. He brought such woe to denizens of the threefold heavens. 19

## \|\| 5.16.20 \|\|

*yuddhotsukohamatyarthaṃ naravājimahāhavam \|*  
*abhūtapūrvamanyatra draṣṭuṃ svargādihāgataḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

I always love a spectacle, especially a contest between man and horse. Such a match has never taken place before, so I came from heaven just to see it. 20

## \|\| 5.16.21 \|\|

*karmāṇya'trāvatāre te kṛtāni madhusūdana \|*  
*yāni tair vismitaṃ cetastoṣametena me gatam \|\| 21 \|\|*

I’m amazed by the feats that you’ve performed since coming down to earth, Madhu’s slayer, but this one gave me greatest satisfaction. 21

## \|\| 5.16.22 \|\|

*turaṅgasyāsya śakropi kṛṣṇa devāś ca bibhyati \|*  
*dhutakesārajālasya hreṣato 'bhrāvalokinaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Even Indra and the other gods all feared this beast, Kṛṣṇa, when he shook his mane, neighed and looked down from the clouds. 22

## \|\| 5.16.23 \|\|

*yasmāttvayaiṣa duṣṭātmā hataḥ keśī janārdana \|*  
*tasmātkeśavanāmnā tvaṃ loke khyāto bhaviṣyasi \|\| 23 \|\|*

Because you slew this wicked Keśin, Janārdana, you’ll be called by the name of Keśava throughout the world. 23

## \|\| 5.16.24 \|\|

*svastyastu te gamiṣyāmi kaṃsayuddhe 'dhunā punaḥ \|*  
*paraśvo 'ha sameṣyāmi tvayā keśiniṣūdana \|\| 24 \|\|*

May all be well with you. I’ll be on my way, but we’ll meet again when you battle Kaṃsa in two days’ time, Keśin’s slayer! 24

## \|\| 5.16.25 \|\|

*ugrasenasute kaṃse sānuge vinipātite \|*  
*bhārāvatārakartā tvaṃ pṛthivyā pṛthivīdhara \|\| 25 \|\|*

When Kaṃsa son of Ugrasena and his ilk have all been killed, you, foundation of the universe, will ease the burden of the earth. 25

## \|\| 5.16.26 \|\|

*tatrāneka prakārāṇi yuddhāni pṛthivīkṣitām \|*  
*draṣṭavyāni mayā yuṣmatpraṇītāni janārdana \|\| 26 \|\|*

I’ll witness many varied contests, Janārdana, between you and other kings. 26

## \|\| 5.16.27 \|\|

*so 'haṃ yāsyāmi govinda devakāryaṃ mahatkṛtam \|*  
*tvayaiva viditaṃ sarvaṃ svasti te 'stu vrajāmyaham \|\| 27 \|\|*

I’ll be going, Govinda. Your great deeds befit the gods, and I am pleased with you. All the best. I really must be off.’ 27

## \|\| 5.16.28 \|\|

*nārade tu gate kṛṣṇaḥ saha gopaiḥ sabhājitaḥ \|*  
*viveśa gokulaṃ gopīnetrapānaikabhājanam \|\| 28 \|\|*

After Nārada had departed, Kṛṣṇa, unsurprised, returned to camp with the other herders—sole object of the cowgirls’ gaze. 28

## Chapter 17 - Akrūra arrives at the cattle camp

## \|\| 5.17.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*akrūro 'pi viniṣkramya syandanenāśugāminā \|*  
*kṛṣṇasaṃdarśanākāṅkṣī prayayau nandagokulam \|\| 1 \|\|*

Akrūra set off on his swift chariot for Nanda’s camp, eager for the sight of Kṛṣṇa. 1

## \|\| 5.17.2 \|\|

*cintayāmāsa cākrūro nāsti dhanyataro mayā \|*  
*yo 'ham aṃśāvatīrṇasya mukhaṃ drakṣyāmi cakriṇaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

‘No-one is luckier than I,’ he thought, ‘as I’ll behold this being’s face, an aspect of discus-wielding Viṣṇu come to earth. 2

## \|\| 5.17.3 \|\|

*adya me saphalaṃ janma suprabhātābhavanniśā \|*  
*yadunnidrābhapatrākṣaṃ viṣṇordrakṣyāmyahaṃ mukham \|\| 3 \|\|*

Today, my life is now worthwhile and night has dawned as day, because I’ll see the face of Viṣṇu, whose eyes are like a full-blown lotus petal. 3

## \|\| 5.17.4 \|\|

*pāpaṃ harati yatpuṃsāṃ smṛtaṃ saṃkalpanāmayam \|*  
*tatpuṇḍarīkanayanaṃ viṣṇordrakṣyāmyahaṃ mukham \|\| 4 \|\|*

I’ll behold the face of Viṣṇu, who dispels the sins of those who call the lotus-eyed being to their hearts. 4

## \|\| 5.17.5 \|\|

*vinirjagmuryato vedā vedāṅgānyakhilāni ca \|*  
*drakṣyāmi tatparaṃ dhāma dhāmrāṃ bhagavato mukham \|\| 5 \|\|*

I’ll behold his blessed face, supreme abode of all the gods, from which the Vedas, Vedāṅgas and their supplements arose. 5

## \|\| 5.17.6 \|\|

*yajñeṣu yajñapuruṣaḥ puruṣaiḥ puruṣottamaḥ \|*  
*ijyate yo 'khilādhārastaṃ drakṣyāmi jagatpatim \|\| 6 \|\|*

I’ll behold the universal lord, who is praised by men as the spirit of every sacrifice, the highest spirit and foundation of the world. 6

## \|\| 5.17.7 \|\|

*iṣṭvā yamindro yajñānāṃ śatenāmararājatām \|*  
*avāpa tamanantādimahaṃ drakṣyāmi keśavam \|\| 7 \|\|*

I’ll behold Keśava, who has no start or finish, and to whom Indra made a hundred sacrifices and thereby came to rule the gods. 7

## \|\| 5.17.8 \|\|

*na brahma nendrarudrāśvivasvādityamarudgaṇāḥ \|*  
*yasya svarūpaṃ jānanti pratyakṣaṃ yāti me hariḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Hari, whose true form is known to neither Brahmā, Indra, Rudra, the Aśvins, Vasus, Ādityas, nor the host of Maruts, will touch my body. 8

## \|\| 5.17.9 \|\|

*sarvātmā sarvavit sarvaḥ sarvabhūteṣv avasthitaḥ \|*  
*yohya'cintyo 'vyayo vyāpī sa vakṣyati mayā saha \|\| 9 \|\|*

He who is at the heart of all, knows all, is all, abides in all and is extensive, unchanging and pervasive will speak to me. 9

## \|\| 5.17.10 \|\|

*matsya kūrmavarāhāśvasiṃharūpādibhiḥ sthitim \|*  
*cakāra jagato yo 'jaḥ jagato yo 'jaḥ so 'dya māṃ pralapiṣyati \|\| 10 \|\|*

The birthless being, who took the form of fish, tortoise, boar, horse, lion and the others to preserve the world, will speak to me today. 10

## \|\| 5.17.11 \|\|

*sāṃprataṃ ca jagatsvāmī kāryamātmahṛdi sthitam \|*  
*kartuṃ manuṣyatāṃ prāptaḥsvecchādehadhṛdavyayaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

And now the everlasting master of the universe, who adopts all forms at will, has taken on a mortal body to undertake the duty closest to his heart. 11

## \|\| 5.17.12 \|\|

*yonantaḥ pṛthivīṃ dhatte śekharasthitisaṃsthitām \|*  
*so 'vatīrṇo jagatyarthe māma krūreti vakṣyati \|\| 12 \|\|*

That eternal being, who placed the universe upon his crest, has come to earth to benefit the world and will call on me by name. 12

## \|\| 5.17.13 \|\|

*pitṛputrasuhṛdbhrātṛmātṛbandhumayīmimām \|*  
*yanmāyāṃ nālamuttartuṃ jagattasmai namonamaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

I bow to him who appears in forms of father, son, friend, brother, mother and kin—an illusion all the world cannot dispel. 13

## \|\| 5.17.14 \|\|

*taratyavidyāṃ vitatāṃ hṛdi yasminniveśite \|*  
*yogamāyāmameyāya tasmai vidyātmane namaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

When Viṣṇu fills his heart, the ascetic abandons deepest ignorance and delusion. I bow to the immeasurable being, the heart of knowledge. 14

## \|\| 5.17.15 \|\|

*yajvabhir yajñapuruṣo vāsudevaś ca sāttvataiḥ \|*  
*vedāntavedibhir viṣṇuḥ procyate yo natosmi tam \|\| 15 \|\|*

I bow to him whom sacrificers call the spirit of the sacrifice; whom devotees call Vāsudeva. Those who know the Vedāntas call him Viṣṇu. 15

## \|\| 5.17.16 \|\|

*yathā yatra jagaddhāmni dhātaryetatpratiṣṭhitam \|*  
*sadasattena satyena mayyasau yātu saumyātām \|\| 16 \|\|*

Just as all the world abides in its creator as a refuge, by that same truth, may the existent and nonexistent being be merciful to me. 16

## \|\| 5.17.17 \|\|

*smṛte sakalakalyāṇabhājanaṃ yatra jāyate \|*  
*puruṣastamajaṃ nityaṃ vrajāmi śaraṇaṃ harim \|\| 17 \|\|*

I take refuge in the birthless, everlasting Hari, the spirit in whom every joy arises when remembered.’ 17

## \|\| 5.17.18 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*itthaṃ saṃcintayanviṣṇuṃ bhaktinamrātmamānasaḥ \|*  
*akrūro gokulaṃ prāptaḥ kiñcitsūrye virājati \|\| 18 \|\|*

Parāśara:

With these thoughts of Viṣṇu, and bowing in devotion with his heart and soul, Akrūra reached the cattle camp just as the sun was setting. 18

## \|\| 5.17.19 \|\|

*sa dadarśa tadā kṛṣṇamādāvādohane gavām \|*  
*vatsamadhyāgataṃ phullanī lotpaladalacchavim \|\| 19 \|\|*
