There he beheld Kṛṣṇa, as the cows were being milked, wandering among the calves, as beautiful as the petal of an azure waterlily in full bloom. 19

## \|\| 5.17.20 \|\|

*praphullapadmapatrākṣaṃ śrīvatsāṃkitavakṣasam \|*  
*pralaṃbabāhumāyāmatuṅgorasthalamunnasam \|\| 20 \|\|*

His eyes were unstained lotus petals, his bosom displayed Śrīvatsa, his arms were long, his chest was broad and muscular and his nose was high. 20

## \|\| 5.17.21 \|\|

*savilāsasmitādhāraṃ bibhrāṇaṃ mukhapaṅkajam \|*  
*juṅgaraktanakhaṃ padybhāṃ dharaṇyāṃ supratiḥṣitam \|\| 21 \|\|*

A pleasant smile graced his handsome features, his fingernails were long and rosy, his feet were planted firmly on the ground. 21

## \|\| 5.17.22 \|\|

*bibhrāṇaṃ vāsasī pīte vanyapuṣpavibhūṣitam \|*  
*seṃdunīlācalābhaṃ taṃ sitāṃbhojāvataṃsakam \|\| 22 \|\|*

He wore two yellow garments and adorned himself with forest flowers. His arms resembled smooth dark vines, and he wore a circlet of silvery waterlilies. 22

## \|\| 5.17.23 \|\|

*haṃsakundendudhavalaṃ nīlāṃbaradharaṃ dvija \|*  
*tasyānu balabhadraṃ ca dadarśa yadunandanam \|\| 23 \|\|*

Akrūra, delighter of the Yadus, saw Balarāma, snowy as a wild goose, jasmine flowers or the moon, brahmin. Clad in blue, he stood at Kṛṣṇa’s side. 23

## \|\| 5.17.24 \|\|

*prāṃśumuttuṅgabāhvaṃsaṃ vikāsi mukhapaṅkajam \|*  
*meghamālāparivṛtaṃ kailāsādrimivāparam \|\| 24 \|\|*

His arms were long and his shoulders broad, his face like a lotus in full bloom. Balarāma was a second Kailāsa wreathed in clouds. 24

## \|\| 5.17.25 \|\|

*tau dṛṣṭvā vikasadvaktrasarojaḥ sa mahāmatiḥ \|*  
*pulakāñcitasarvāṅgastadākrūro 'jagaddhātari bhavanmune \|\| 25 \|\|*

Seeing these two, wise Akrūra, whose face was like an open lily, spoke, while the hairs on his whole body bristled with excitement, sage: 25

## \|\| 5.17.26 \|\|

*tadetatparamaṃ dhāma tadetatparamaṃ padam \|*  
*bhagavadvāsudevāṃśo dvidhā yo 'yaṃ vyavasthitaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

‘This is that supreme abode. This is that highest state. This is the twofold manifestation of Lord Vāsudeva. 26

## \|\| 5.17.27 \|\|

*sāphalyamakṣṇoryugametadatra dṛṣṭe jagaddhātari yātamuccaiḥ \|*  
*apyaṅgametadbhagavatprasādāttadaṅgasaṃge phalavanmama syāt \|\| 27 \|\|*

Now that they’ve beheld the foundation of the world, my two eyes are here fulfilled in the highest measure. Would not this body of mine be satisfied, if, by the mercy of the lord, he were to touch me? 27

## \|\| 5.17.28 \|\|

*apyeṣa pṛṣṭhe mama hastapadmaṃ kariṣyati śrīmadanantamūrtiḥ \|*  
*yasyāṅgulisparśahatākhilāghair avāpyate siddhirapāstadoṣā \|\| 28 \|\|*

Would not this being who appears in endless glorious forms place his lotus-hand upon my back? The touch of his fingers removes all stains and imparts unending perfection. 28

## \|\| 5.17.29 \|\|

*yenāgnividyudraviraśmimālākarālamatyugramapeta caktram \|*  
*cakraṃ ghnatā daityapaterhṛtāni daityāṅganānāṃ nayanāñjanāni \|\| 29 \|\|*

That hand laid down his cruel and dreadful discus, wreathed with the brilliance of lightning, fire and sun combined, which destroyed the forces of the Daitya lord and robbed the makeup from their women’s eyes. 29

## \|\| 5.17.30 \|\|

*yatrāṃbu vinyasya balirmanojñānavāpa bhogānvasudhātavalasthaḥ \|*  
*tathāmaratvaṃ tridaśādhipatvaṃ manvantaraṃ pūrṇamapetaśatrum \|\| 30 \|\|*

Having made an offering of water to that hand, Bali experienced exquisite pleasures in this world, as well as immortality and sovereignty over thirteen gods, unchallenged, for a whole Manvantara. 30

## \|\| 5.17.31 \|\|

*apyeṣa māṃ kaṃsaparigraheṇa doṣāspadībhūtamadoṣaduṣṭam \|*  
*kartāmānopahataṃ dhigastu tajjanma yatsādhubahiṣkṛtasya \|\| 31 \|\|*

But won’t this being regard me as an object of contempt, stained with infamy, on account of my relationship with Kaṃsa, even though I’m blameless? A curse on the life of virtuous men unfairly treated! 31

## \|\| 5.17.32 \|\|

*jñānātmakasyāmalasattvarāśerapetadoṣasya sadā sphuṭasya \|*  
*kiṃ vā jagatyatra samastapuṃsāmajñātamasyāsti hṛdi sthitasya \|\| 32 \|\|*

And yet, what in this world is unknown to him who is knowledge itself, the totality of pure truth, untouched by faults, always manifest and who dwells in all men’s minds? 32

## \|\| 5.17.33 \|\|

*tasmādahaṃ bhaktivinamracetā vrajāmi sarveśvamīśvarāṇām \|*  
*aṃśāvatāraṃ puruṣottamasya hyanādimadhyāntamajasya viṣṇoḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

With a heart that’s filled with faith, I’ll approach the universal lord of lords, Viṣṇu’s aspect come to earth, the highest spirit who has no start, no middle or conclusion.’ 33

## Chapter 18 - Kṛṣṇa sets out for Mathurā

## \|\| 5.18.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*cintayannitigovindamupagamya sa yādavaḥ \|*  
*akrurosmīti caraṇau nanāma śirasā hareḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Parāśara:

With these thoughts, the Yādava approached Govinda. ‘I am Akrūra,’ said he, and bowed his head at Hari’s feet. 1

## \|\| 5.18.2 \|\|

*so 'pyenaṃ dhvajavajrābjakṛtacihnena pāṇinā \|*  
*saṃspṛśyākṛṣya ca prītya sugāḍhaṃ pariṣasvaje \|\| 2 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa touched Akrūra with his hand marked with banner, thunderbolt and lotus, drew him close with love and hugged him warmly. 2

## \|\| 5.18.3 \|\|

*kṛtasaṃvandanau tena yathāvadbalakeśavau \|*  
*tataḥ pravīṣṭau saṃhṛṣṭau tamādāyātmamandiram \|\| 3 \|\|*

Akrūra duly honoured Balarāma and Keśava. The happy youths then led him to their own abode. 3

## \|\| 5.18.4 \|\|

*saha tābhyāṃ tadākrūraḥ kṛtasaṃvandanādikaḥ \|*  
*bhuktabhojyo yathānyāyyamācacakṣe tatastayoḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Accompanied by the pair, Akrūra received their reverence and accepted food to eat. He then duly spoke with them. 4

## \|\| 5.18.5 \|\|

*yathā nirbhatsitastena kaṃsenānakadundubhiḥ \|*  
*yathā ca devakī devī dānavena durātmanā \|\| 5 \|\|*

Akrūra told them how their father, Vasudeva, and Princess Devakī had been abused by Kaṃsa, the wicked Dānava, 5

## \|\| 5.18.6 \|\|

*ugrasene yathā kaṃsaḥsa durātmā ca vartate \|*  
*yaṃ caivārthaṃ samuddiśya kaṃsena tu visarjitaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

And how that evil being had mistreated his own father, Ugrasena. Akrūra then explained the reason Kaṃsa sent him. 6

## \|\| 5.18.7 \|\|

*tatsarvaṃ vistarācchutvā bhagavāndevakīsutaḥ \|*  
*uvācākhilamapyetajjñātaṃ dānapate mayā \|\| 7 \|\|*

Having listened carefully to his report, the lord, Keśin’s slayer, said, ‘All this is known to me already, you generous man. 7

## \|\| 5.18.8 \|\|

*kariṣye tanmāhabhāga yadatraupayikaṃ matam \|*  
*vicintyaṃ nānyathaitatte viddhi kaṃsaṃ hataṃ mayā \|\| 8 \|\|*

I’ll do what I regard as fitting in this case, you fortunate being. Don’t think it could be otherwise. Understand that Kaṃsa is already slain by me. 8

## \|\| 5.18.9 \|\|

*ahaṃ rāmaś ca mathurāṃ śvo yāsyāvaḥsaha tvayā \|*  
*gopavṛddhāś ca yāsyanti hyādāyopāyanaṃ bahu \|\| 9 \|\|*

I’ll go with you and Balarāma to Mathurā tomorrow, and the elder herdsmen will follow us with many offerings. 9

## \|\| 5.18.10 \|\|

*niśeyaṃ nīyatāṃ vīra na cintāṃ kartumarhasi \|*  
*trirātrābhyantare kaṃsaṃ nihanaṣyāmi sānugam \|\| 10 \|\|*

Spend the night here, hero. Do not worry. Within three days, I’ll vanquish Kaṃsa and his ilk.’ 10

## \|\| 5.18.11 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*samādiśya tato gopānakrūro 'pi ca keśavaḥ \|*  
*suṣvāpa balabhadraś ca nandagopagṛhe tataḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Having told the other herdsmen of this plan, Akrūra, Keśava and Balarāma retired to sleep in the home of the herdsman Nanda. 11

## \|\| 5.18.12 \|\|

*tataḥ prabhāte vimale kṛṣṇarāmau mahādyutī \|*  
*akrūreṇa samaṃ gantumudyatau mathurāṃ purīm \|\| 12 \|\|*

In bright morning light the following day, wise Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, accompanied by Akrūra, prepared to leave for Mathurā. 12

## \|\| 5.18.13 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā gopījanaḥsāsraḥ ślathadvalayabāhukaḥ \|*  
*niśaśvasātiduḥkhārtaḥ prāha parasparam \|\| 13 \|\|*

Seeing this, the tearful herding women, their bracelets slipping from their arms, with deep sighs and wracked with pain, cried to one another: 13

## \|\| 5.18.14 \|\|

*mathurāṃ prāpya govindaḥ kathaṃ gokulameṣyati \|*  
*nagarastrīkalālāpamadhu śrotreṇa pāsyati \|\| 14 \|\|*

‘Once Govinda reaches Mathurā, why would he return to camp? His ears will love the city women’s sweet and artful talk.’ 14

## \|\| 5.18.15 \|\|

*vilāsivākyapāneṣu nāgarīṇāṃ kṛtāspadam \|*  
*cittamasya kathaṃ bhūyo grāmyagopīṣu yāsyati \|\| 15 \|\|*

‘Once he’s heard the brilliant conversation of the ladies in the town, will his mind return to rustic cowgirls?’ 15

## \|\| 5.18.16 \|\|

*sāraṃ samastagoṣṭhasya vidhinā haratā harim \|*  
*prahṛtaṃ gopayoṣitsu nirghṛṇena durātmanā \|\| 16 \|\|*

‘Robbing us of Hari, the pride of all the camp, accursed Fate has dealt us herding women a cruel blow.’ 16

## \|\| 5.18.17 \|\|

*bhāvargabhasmitaṃ vākyaṃ vilāsalalitā gatiḥ \|*  
*nāgarīṇāmatīvaitatkaṭākṣekṣitameva ca \|\| 17 \|\|*

‘The city women’s banter is graced with telling smiles, their gait is elegant and charming, and they always make those amorous sidelong glances.’ 17

## \|\| 5.18.18 \|\|

*grāmyo harirayaṃ tāsāṃ vilāsanigaḍair yutaḥ \|*  
*bhavatīnāṃpunaḥ pārśvaṃ kayā yuktyā sameṣyati \|\| 18 \|\|*

‘Once this Hari, a simple rustic youth, is ensnared by their elegance, what reason would he have to return to us?’ 18

## \|\| 5.18.19 \|\|

*eṣaiṣa rathamāruhya mathurāṃ yāti keśavaḥ \|*  
*krūreṇākrūrakeṇātra nirghṛṇena pratāritaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

‘This—this Keśava has climbed into a chariot to go to Mathurā, deceived by cruel and desperate Akrūra.’ 19

## \|\| 5.18.20 \|\|

*kiṃ na vetti nṛśaṃsoyamanurāgaparaṃ janam \|*  
*yenaivamakṣṇorāhlādaṃ nayatyanyatra no harim \|\| 20 \|\|*

‘That callous being is carrying off our Hari, who brings such pleasure to our eyes. Does he not know how much we love him?’ 20

## \|\| 5.18.21 \|\|

*eṣa rāmeṇa sahitaḥ prayātyatyantanirghṛṇaḥ \|*  
*rathamāruhya govindastvaryatāmasya vāraṇe \|\| 21 \|\|*

‘It’s heartless of Govinda to mount this carriage and leave with Balarāma. Quick, let’s stop him!’ 21

## \|\| 5.18.22 \|\|

*gurūṇāmagrato vaktuṃ viṃ bravītha na naḥ kṣamam \|*  
*guravaḥkiṃ kariṣyanti dagdhānāṃ virahāgninā \|\| 22 \|\|*

‘We should tell the old folk first.’ ‘What would you say?’ ‘We can’t do that.’ ‘What can elders do for us, burning with the pain of separation?’ 22

## \|\| 5.18.23 \|\|

*nadaṅgopamikhā gopā gantumete samudyatāḥ \|*  
*nodyamaṃ kurute kaścidgovindavinivartane \|\| 23 \|\|*

‘The herdsmen led by Nanda are preparing to depart. Not one of them is trying to stop Govinda.’ 23

## \|\| 5.18.24 \|\|

*suprabhātādya rajanī mathurāvāsayoṣitām \|*  
*pāsyantyacyutavaktrābjaṃ yāsāṃ netrālipaṅktayaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

‘This day dawns bright for the womenfolk of Mathurā, as their eyes as black as any row of bees may imbibe the lotus-face of that eternal being.’ 24

## \|\| 5.18.25 \|\|

*dhanyāste pathi ye kṛṣṇamito yāntyanivāritāḥ \|*  
*udvahiṣyanti paśyantaḥ svadehaṃ pulakāñcitam \|\| 25 \|\|*

‘Fortunate are those who follow Kṛṣṇa unhindered on the path. Beholding him, their bodies will bristle with delight.’ 25

## \|\| 5.18.26 \|\|

*mathurānagarīpauranayanānāṃ mahotsavaḥ \|*  
*govindāvayavair dṛṣṭair atīvādya bhaviṣyati \|\| 26 \|\|*

‘The sight of Govinda’s limbs today will provide a feast for the eyes of Mathurā’s inhabitants.’ 26

## \|\| 5.18.27 \|\|

*ko nu svapraḥsabhāgyābhir dṛṣṭastābhir adhokṣajam \|*  
*vistārikāṃ tinayanā yā drakṣyantyanivāritāḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

‘What dreams will greet those lucky girls when they rest their lovely almond eyes on Kṛṣṇa unimpeded?’ 27

## \|\| 5.18.28 \|\|

*aho gopījanasyāsya darśayitvā mahānidhim \|*  
*utkṛttānyatra netrāṇi vidhinā karuṇātmanā \|\| 28 \|\|*

‘Sadly, having shown this greatest treasure to the gaze of us cowherd women, hard-hearted Brahmā, arranger of the universe, deprives us of this sight again.’ 28

## \|\| 5.18.29 \|\|

*anurāgeṇa śaithilyamasmāsu vrajite harauḥ \|*  
*śaithilyamupayāntyāśu kareṣu valayānyapi \|\| 29 \|\|*

‘Just as Hari’s love for us is cooler now that he is leaving, our bangles are slipping from our arms.’ 29

## \|\| 5.18.30 \|\|

*akrūraḥ krūrahṛdayaḥ śīvraṃ prerayate hayān \|*  
*eva mārtāsu yoṣitsu kṛpā kasya na jāyate \|\| 30 \|\|*

‘As cruel Akrūra urges the horses forward, does no-one feel compassion for us women in distress?’ 30

## \|\| 5.18.31 \|\|

*eṣa kṛṣṇarathasyoccaiś cakrareṇur nirīkṣyatām \|*  
*dūrībhūto hariryena so 'pi reṇurnalakṣyate \|\| 31 \|\|*

‘See the dust thrown up by Kṛṣṇa’s chariot wheels.’ ‘We can’t even see the dust. He must be far away.’ 31

## \|\| 5.18.32 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityevamatihārdena gopījananirīkṣitaḥ \|*  
*tatyāja vrajabhūbhāgaṃ saha rāmeṇa keśavaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

Parāśara: So Keśava and Balarāma left the region of the camp, while the broken-hearted herding girls looked on. 32

## \|\| 5.18.33 \|\|

*gacchanto javanāśvena rathena yamunātaṭam \|*  
*praptamadhyāhnasamaye rāmākrūrajanārdanāḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

Proceeding in the chariot drawn by swiftest horses, Balarāma, Akrūra and Janārdana reached the Yamunā’s banks at noon. 33

## \|\| 5.18.34 \|\|

*athāha kuṣmamakrūro bhavadybhāṃ tāvadāsyatām \|*  
*yāvatkaromi kālindyā āhnikārhaṇamaṃbhasi \|\| 34 \|\|*

There Akrūra said to Kṛṣṇa, ‘You two wait while I perform my daily ritual for the river in its waters.’ 34

## \|\| 5.18.35 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tathetyuktastatasnātaḥsvācāntaḥsa mahāmatiḥ \|*  
*dadhyau brahma paraṃ vipra graviṣṭo yamunājale \|\| 35 \|\|*

With their agreement, wise Akrūra bathed and rinsed his mouth, then waded into the Yamunā, brahmin, where he meditated on the highest Absolute. 35

## \|\| 5.18.36 \|\|

*phaṇāsahasramālāḍhyaṃ balabhadraṃ dadarśa saḥ \|*  
*kundamālāṅgamunnidrapadmapatrāyatekṣaṇam \|\| 36 \|\|*

Below the surface of the waters, Akrūra beheld Balarāma in the form of Śeṣa, circled by a thousand shining hoods, his body white as jasmine and his ruddy eyes like petals of a newly wakened lotus. 36

## \|\| 5.18.37 \|\|

*vṛtaṃ vāsukiraṃbhādyair mahadbhiḥ pavanā śibhiḥ \|*  
*saṃstūyamānamudgandhivanamālāvibhūṣitam \|\| 37 \|\|*

Flanked by Vāsuki, Rambha and other powerful serpents, he wore a chaplet of forest flowers, while gandharvas sang his praises. 37

## \|\| 5.18.38 \|\|

*dadhānamasite vastre cārurūpāvataṃsakam \|*  
*cārukuṇḍalinaṃ bhāntamantarjalatale sthitam \|\| 38 \|\|*

Clad in two dark garments, a necklace of lovely waterlilies and a pair of gorgeous earrings, he appeared beneath the waters, drunk with joy. 38

## \|\| 5.18.39 \|\|

*tasyotsaṃge ghanaśyāmamātāmrāyatalocanam \|*  
*caturbāhumudārāṅgaṃ cakrādyāyudhabhūṣaṇam \|\| 39 \|\|*

Reclining on the serpent’s lap, Akrūra beheld Viṣṇu, dark as thunderclouds, his almond eyes like copper and his noble four-armed form resplendent with his discus and other weapons. 39

## \|\| 5.18.40 \|\|

*pīte vasānaṃ vasane citramālyopaśobhitam \|*  
*śakracāpata-ḍinmālāvicitramiva toyadam \|\| 40 \|\|*

Clad in golden garments and decked with varied garlands, he resembled a cloud adorned with Indra’s bow and a diadem of lightning. 40

## \|\| 5.18.41 \|\|

*śrīvatsavakṣasaṃ cāru sphuranmakarakuṇḍalam \|*  
*dadarśa kṛṣṇamakliṣṭaṃ puṇḍarīkāvataṃsakam \|\| 41 \|\|*

With Śrīvatsa on his chest, Kṛṣṇa shone with splendid bracelets, crown and lotus wreaths, as he appeared to be at rest. 41

## \|\| 5.18.42 \|\|

*sanandanādyair munibhiḥ saddhayogair akalmaṣaiḥ \|*  
*saṃcintyamānaṃ tatrasthair nāsāgranyastalocanaiḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

Sanandana and other sages, accomplished in yoga and free from faults, their eyes fixed on their noses, stood about him contemplating. 42

## \|\| 5.18.43 \|\|

*balakṛṣṇau tathākrūraḥ pratyabhijñāya vismitaḥ \|*  
*acintayadrathācchīghraṃ kathamatrāgatāviti \|\| 43 \|\|*

Seeing Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa in these forms, Akrūra was astonished and wondered how they got there from the chariot so quickly. 43

## \|\| 5.18.44 \|\|

*vivakṣostaṃbhayāmāsa vācaṃ tasya janārdanaḥ \|*  
*tato niṣkramya salilādrathamabhyāgataḥ punaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

Before he was able to say a word, Janārdana silenced him, so Akrūra left the river and went back to the carriage. 44

## \|\| 5.18.45 \|\|

*dadarśa tatra caivobhau rathasyopari niṣṭhitau \|*  
*rāmakṛṣṇau yathāpūrvaṃ manuṣyavapuṣānvitau \|\| 45 \|\|*

There he beheld Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa in mortal form still seated in the chariot as before. 45

## \|\| 5.18.46 \|\|

*nimagnaś ca punas toye dadarśa ca tathaiva tau \|*  
*saṃstūyamānau gandharvair munisiddhamahoragaiḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

Akrūra plunged back in the river, where he saw them underwater, still worshipped by the gandharvas, sages, siddhas and mighty serpents. 46

## \|\| 5.18.47 \|\|

*tato vijñātasadbhāvaḥsa tu dānapatistadā \|*  
*tuṣṭāva sarva vijñānamayamacyutamīśvaram \|\| 47 \|\|*

Realising the actual nature of the everlasting lord, all knowledge’s embodiment, generous Akrūra eulogised him: 47

## \|\| 5.18.48 \|\|

*akrūra uvāca*  
*sanmātrarūpiṇetintyamahimre paramātmane \|*  
*vyāpine naikarūpaikasvarūpāya namo namaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

Akrūra:

I bow to you, whose form is existence alone, whose greatness is beyond conception, the all-pervasive highest spirit, whose forms are one and many. 48

## \|\| 5.18.49 \|\|

*sarvarūpāya te 'cintyahavirbhūtāya te namaḥ \|*  
*namo vijñānapārāya parāya prakṛteḥ prabhoḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

I bow to you, being beyond all contemplation, in the form of truth and essence of the sacrifice. I bow to you, Lord, whose form is inconceivable, and who lies beyond primeval nature. 49

## \|\| 5.18.50 \|\|

*bhūtātmā cendriyātmā ca pradhānātmā tathā bhavān \|*  
*ātmā ca paramātmā ca tvamekaḥ pañcadhā sthitaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

You are at the heart of all creation, the senses and primal matter. You are the Self and the highest Self. You are one in fivefold form. 50

## \|\| 5.18.51 \|\|

*prasīda sarva sarvātmna kṣarākṣaramayeśvara \|*  
*brahmaviṣṇuśivākhyābhiḥ kalpanābhir udīritaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

Take pity on me, universal heart of all, the deity who embodies both the permanent and the transient, whether you are addressed as forms of Brahmā, Viṣṇu or Śiva. 51

## \|\| 5.18.52 \|\|

*anākhyeyasvarūpātmannanākhyeyaprayojana \|*  
*anākhyena bhidhānaṃ tvāṃ natosmi parameśvara \|\| 52 \|\|*

Your true form cannot be named, nor can your purpose be described. Your true name cannot be spoken. I bow to you, almighty Lord. 52

## \|\| 5.18.53 \|\|

*na yatra nātha vidyante nāmajātyādikalpanāḥ \|*  
*tadbrahma paramaṃ nityamavikāri bhavānajaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

You, Lord, have no name, no birth or other attributes. You are tat—‘that’, the highest Absolute, eternal, unchanging and unborn. 53

## \|\| 5.18.54 \|\|

*na kalpanāmṛterthasya sarvasyādhigamo yataḥ \|*  
*tataḥ kṛṣṇacyutānantaviṣṇusaṃjñābhiriḍyate \|\| 54 \|\|*

Because we cannot reach our goals without conceptions, you are worshipped with the names of Kṛṣṇa, Acyuta, Viṣṇu and Ananta. 54

## \|\| 5.18.55 \|\|

*sarvārthastvamaja vikalpanābhir etair vādyair bhavati hi yair anantaviśvam \|*  
*viśvātmā tvamiti vikārahīnametatsarvasminna hi bhavatosti kiñcidanyat \|\| 55 \|\|*

You are every object, birthless deity, of these conceptions. You are the beginning, all the world and everything. The heart of all, you are beyond all changes and existence. There’s a part of you in all this world. 55

## \|\| 5.18.56 \|\|

*tvaṃ brahmā paśupatiryamā vidhātā dhātā tvaṃ tridaśapatiḥsamīraṇo 'gniḥ \|*  
*toyeśo dhanapatiṃratakastvameko bhinnārthair jagadabhipāsiśaktibhedaiḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

You are Brahmā, Śiva, lord of beasts, and Aryaman, arranger, disposer, Indra lord of thirteen gods, Wind and Fire. You are Varuṇa, lord of waters, and Kubera, god of wealth. You alone are Death who brings an end to all. For varied goals, you protect the world with all these different powers. 56

## \|\| 5.18.57 \|\|

*viśvaṃ bhavānsṛjati sūryagabhastirūpo viśveśa te guṇamayoyamataḥ prapañcaḥ \|*  
*rūpaṃ paraṃ saditi vācakamakṣaraṃ yajjñānātmane sadasate praṇato 'smi tasmai \|\| 57 \|\|*

In the form of a solar ray, you brought forth the universe. This whole world consists of your own qualities, birthless deity. Your highest form is signified by the everlasting syllable sat—‘existence’. I bow to that being at the heart of knowledge, who exists and is yet beyond existence. 57

## \|\| 5.18.58 \|\|

*oṃ namo vāsudevāya namaḥsaṃkarṣaṇāya ca \|*  
*pradyumnāya namastubhyamaniruddhāya te namaḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

Oṃ! I bow to you as Vāsudeva and as Balarāma. I bow to you as Pradyumna and as Aniruddha. 58

## Chapter 19 - Kṛṣṇa slays the washerman and blesses the garland-maker

## \|\| 5.19.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evamantarjale viṣṇumabhiṣṭūya sa yādavaḥ \|*  
*arcayāmāsa sarveśaṃ dhūpapuṣpair manomayaiḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Having thus praised Viṣṇu appearing in the waters, Akrūra, scion of the Yādavas, made a mental offering to the universal lord with flowers and with incense. 1

## \|\| 5.19.2 \|\|

*parityaktānyaviṣayo manastatra niveśya saḥ \|*  
*brahmabhūte ciraṃ sthitvā virarāma samādhinaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Free from all distractions, his mind set on the deity, he tarried long in the presence of the Absolute, then drew his meditation to a close. 2

## \|\| 5.19.3 \|\|

*kṛtakṛtyamivātmānaṃ manyamāmau mahāmatiḥ \|*  
*ājagāma rathaṃ bhūyo nirgamya yamunāṃbhasaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*  
*dadarśa rāmakṛṣṇau ca yathāpurvamavasthitau \|*  
*vismitākṣastadākrūrastaṃ ca kṛṣṇo 'bhyabhāṣata \|\| 5.19.4 \|\|*

Having done the necessary, that prudent individual rose from the waters of the river and walked back to the chariot. 3

## \|\| 5.19.4 \|\|

*sanskrit not found \|\| 4 \|\|*

Seeing Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa seated as before, his eyes filled with astonishment. Kṛṣṇa then said to him, 4

## \|\| 5.19.5 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca*  
*nūnaṃ te dṛṣṭamāścaryamakrūra yamunājale \|*  
*vismayotphullanayano bhavānsaṃlakṣyate yataḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

‘You must have seen some prodigy in the waters of the Yamunā, Akrūra, since your eyes are wide with wonder.’ 5

## \|\| 5.19.6 \|\|

*akrūra uvāca*  
*antarjale yadāścaryaṃ dṛṣṭaṃ tatra mayācyuta \|*  
*tadatrāpi hi paśyāmi mūrtimatpurataḥ sthitam \|\| 6 \|\|*

Akrūra:

The prodigy in the waters that I beheld, eternal Lord, I now see standing here before me in mortal form. 6

## \|\| 5.19.7 \|\|

*jagadetanmahāścaryarūpaṃ yasya mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*tenāścaryapareṇāhaṃ bhavatā kṛṣṇa saṃgataḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Now I’ve met you, Kṛṣṇa, the greatest prodigy of all, the mighty being whose wondrous form fills all the world. 7

## \|\| 5.19.8 \|\|

*tatkimetena mathurāṃ yāsyāmo madhusūdana \|*  
*bibhemi kaṃsāddhigjanma paripiṇḍopajīvinām \|\| 8 \|\|*

But enough of this. Let’s proceed to Mathurā, Madhusūdana. But I’m afraid of Kaṃsa, as the life of one who lives on charity is cursed. 8

## \|\| 5.19.9 \|\|

*ityuktvā codayāmāsa sa hayān vātaraṃhasaḥ \|*  
*saṃprāptaścāpi sāyāhne so 'krūro mathurāṃ purīm \|\| 9 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, Akrūra urged the horses forward, faster than the wind, and they reached the city late that very night. 9

## \|\| 5.19.10 \|\|

*vilokya mathurāṃ kṛṣṇaṃ rāmaṃ cāha sa yādavaḥ \|*  
*padybhāṃ yātaṃ mahāvīrau rathenaiko viśāmyaham \|\| 10 \|\|*

Seeing Mathurā, the Yādava said to them, ‘You two brave boys proceed on foot. I’ll ride the chariot alone. 10

## \|\| 5.19.11 \|\|

*gantavyaṃ vasudevasya no bhavadybhāṃ tathā gṛham \|*  
*yuvayorhi kṛte vṛddhaḥsa kaṃsena nirasyate \|\| 11 \|\|*

But don’t approach the home of Vasudeva, as Kaṃsa banished that old man on your account.’ 11

## \|\| 5.19.12 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvā praviveśātha so 'krūro mathurāṃ purīm \|*  
*praviṣṭau rāmakṛṣṇau ca rājamārgamupāgatau \|\| 12 \|\|*

Parāśara:

With these words, Akrūra entered Mathurā, while Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa followed him on foot along the royal road. 12

## \|\| 5.19.13 \|\|

*strībhir naraiś ca sānandaṃ lotanair abhivīkṣitau \|*  
*jagmaturlīlayā vīrau mattau bālagajāviva \|\| 13 \|\|*

Men and women, filled with joy, beheld the heroes as they strode along with ease, entering the city like a pair of youthful elephants. 13

## \|\| 5.19.14 \|\|

*bhramamāṇau tato dṛṣṭvā rajakaṃ raṅgakārakam \|*  
*ayocetāṃ sūrūpāṇi vāsāṃsi rucirāṇi tau \|\| 14 \|\|*

While wandering, they came upon a washerman dyeing clothes and, with a smile, they asked for something nice to wear. 14

## \|\| 5.19.15 \|\|

*kaṃsasya rajakaḥ so 'tha prasādārūḍhavismayaḥ \|*  
*bahūnyākṣepavākyāni prāhoccai rāmakeśavau \|\| 15 \|\|*

The startled washerman was arrogant because of Kaṃsa’s favour and loudly cursed the lads. 15

## \|\| 5.19.16 \|\|

*tatastalaprahāreṇa kṛṣṇastasya durātmanaḥ \|*  
*pātayāmāsa roṣeṇa rajakasya śiro bhuvi \|\| 16 \|\|*

With a single blow, Kṛṣṇa, enraged, struck off that wicked dyer’s head, which fell upon the ground. 16

## \|\| 5.19.17 \|\|

*hatvā'dāya ca vastrāṇi pītanīlāṃbarau tataḥ \|*  
*kṛṣṇarāmau mudā yuktau mālākāragṛhaṃ gatau \|\| 17 \|\|*

Having slain him, the two boys helped themselves to clothes. Kṛṣṇa in yellow and Balarāma in blue, filled with joy, then approached a garland-maker’s home. 17

## \|\| 5.19.18 \|\|

*vikāsinetrayugalo mālākārotivismitaḥ \|*  
*etau kasya sutau yātau maitreyācintayattadā \|\| 18 \|\|*

That person’s eyes were widened with astonishment as he wondered whose sons they were and whence they came, Maitreya. 18

## \|\| 5.19.19 \|\|

*pītanīlāṃbaradharau tau dṛṣṭvātimanoharau \|*  
*sa tarkayāmāsa tadā bhuvaṃ devāvupāgatau \|\| 19 \|\|*

Seeing the handsome lads in blue and yellow, he thought that they were deities come down to earth. 19

## \|\| 5.19.20 \|\|

*vikāsimukhapadmābhyāṃ tābhyāṃ puṣpāṇi yācitaḥ \|*  
*bhivaṃ viṣṭabhya hastābhyāṃ pasparśa śirasā mahīm \|\| 20 \|\|*

When the boys, whose faces shone like blooming lotuses, asked him for some flowers, the garland-maker placed his hands upon the ground and pressed his forehead to the earth. 20

## \|\| 5.19.21 \|\|

*prasādaparamau nāthau mama gehamupāgatau \|*  
*dhanyo 'hamarcayiṣyāmītyāha tau mālyajīvanaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

‘I’m fortunate that you two gracious masters have visited my home. I worship you,’ said the florist to the boys. 21

## \|\| 5.19.22 \|\|

*tataḥ prahṛṣṭavadanastayoḥ puṣpaṇi kāmataḥ \|*  
*cārūṇyetānyathaitāni pradadau sa pralobhayan \|\| 22 \|\|*

Then, with a cheery smile, to please the boys, he gave them each the finest flowers that they chose. 22

## \|\| 5.19.23 \|\|

*punaḥpunaḥ praṇamyobhau mālākāro narottamau \|*  
*dadau puṣpāṇi cārūṇi gandhavantyamalāni ca \|\| 23 \|\|*

Again and again, the garland-maker bowed down to those two outstanding beings and gave them garlands, fresh and fragrant. 23

## \|\| 5.19.24 \|\|

*mālākārāya kṛṣṇo pi prasannaḥ pradadau varān \|*  
*śrīstvāṃ matsaṃśrayā bhadra na kadācittyajiṣyati \|\| 24 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa was pleased with the florist and granted him a boon: ‘Prosperity, which flows from me, will never leave you, my good man. 24

## \|\| 5.19.25 \|\|

*balahānirna te saumya dhanahānirathāpi vā \|*  
*yāvaddināni tāvacca na naśiṣyati saṃtatiḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Nor, my friend, will you lack strength or prosperity. As long as one day follows another, your lineage will endure. 25

## \|\| 5.19.26 \|\|

*bhuktvā ca vipulānbhogāṃstvamante matprasādataḥ \|*  
*mamānusmaraṇaṃ prāpyadivyaṃ lokamavāpsyasi \|\| 26 \|\|*

After you’ve indulged in every pleasure and your end is nigh, by my mercy, recalling me, you’ll attain a heavenly realm. 26

## \|\| 5.19.27 \|\|

*dharme manaś ca te bhadra sārvakālaṃ bhaviṣyati \|*  
*yuṣmatsaṃtatijātānāṃ dhīrghamāyurbhaviṣyati \|\| 27 \|\|*

Your mind will always dwell on virtue, good fellow, and all your offspring will enjoy long lives. 27

## \|\| 5.19.28 \|\|

*nopasargādikaṃ doṣaṃ yuṣmatsaṃtatisaṃbhavaḥ \|*  
*avāpsyati mahābhāgā yāvatsūryo bhaviṣyati \|\| 28 \|\|*

Those born in your family will not endure disease or other woes, you lucky man, as long as the sun will shine.’ 28

## \|\| 5.19.29 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*atyuktvā tadgṛhātkṛṣṇo baladevasahāyavān \|*  
*nirjagāma muniśreṣṭha mālākāreṇa pūjitaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, Kṛṣṇa, worshipped by the garland-maker, best of sages, left his house with Balarāma. 29

## Chapter 20 - Kṛṣṇa in Mathurā; The wrestling bout; Kaṃsa’s demise

## \|\| 5.20.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*rājamārge tataḥ kṛṣṇaḥsānulepanabhājanām \|*  
*dadarśa kubjāmāyāntīṃ navayovanagocarām \|\| 1 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Approaching on the royal road, Kṛṣṇa then saw a young woman with a crooked spine bearing a jar of massage oil. 1

## \|\| 5.20.2 \|\|

*tāmāha lalitaṃ kṛṣṇaḥ kasyedamanulepanam \|*  
*bhavatyā nīyate satyaṃ vadendīvaralocane \|\| 2 \|\|*

‘Who are you carrying this lotion for, young girl with lotus eyes? Tell me truly,’ Kṛṣṇa asked in fun. 2

## \|\| 5.20.3 \|\|

*sakāmeneva sā proktā sānurāgā hariṃ prati \|*  
*prāha sā lalitaṃ kubjā taddarśanabalātkṛtā \|\| 3 \|\|*

Thus addressed affectionately, the girl warmed to Hari at first sight, and light-heartedly replied, 3

## \|\| 5.20.4 \|\|

*kānta kasmānna jānāsi kaṃsena viniyojitām \|*  
*naikavakreti vikhyātāmanulepanakarmaṇi \|\| 4 \|\|*

‘Don’t you know, you handsome man? I’m called Naikavakrā. I’m employed by Kaṃsa as his masseuse. 4

## \|\| 5.20.5 \|\|

*nānyapiṣṭaṃ hi kaṃsasya prītaye hyanulepanam \|*  
*bhavāmyahamatīvāsya prasādadhanabhājanam \|\| 5 \|\|*

He doesn’t like the lotion mixed by anybody else, and I’ve made a lot of money thanks to him.’ 5

## \|\| 5.20.6 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca*  
*sugandhametadrājārhaṃ ruciraṃ rucirānane \|*  
*āvayorgātra sadṛśaṃ dīyatāmanulepanam \|\| 6 \|\|*

Glorious Kṛṣṇa:

This fine and fragrant lotion suits the king, you pretty girl. But it’s also good for us, so give us some. 6

## \|\| 5.20.7 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*śrutvaitadāha sā kubjā gṛhyatāmiti sādaram \|*  
*anulepanaṃ ca pradadau gātrayogyamathobhayoḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the girl replied, ‘Help yourself’, and respectfully gave them enough for two. 7

## \|\| 5.20.8 \|\|

*bhakticchedānuliptāṅgau tatastau puruṣarṣabhau \|*  
*seṃdracāpau vyarājetāṃ sitakṛṣṇāvivāṃbudau \|\| 8 \|\|*

Having then applied the oil, these two bulls of men, whose bodies bore the marks of gods, glowed like rain-filled clouds—one white, one black—both graced with rainbows. 8

## \|\| 5.20.9 \|\|

*tatastāṃ cubuke śaurirullāpana vidhānavit \|*  
*utpāṭya tolayāmāsa vdyaṅgulenāgrapāṇinā \|\| 9 \|\|*

Śūra’s scion Kṛṣṇa, master of flattery, then took the girl by the chin and lifted her with his two fingertips. 9

## \|\| 5.20.10 \|\|

*cakarṣa padybhāṃ ca tadā ṛjutvaṃ keśavo 'nayat \|*  
*tataḥsā ṛjutāṃ prāptāyoṣitamabhavadvarā \|\| 10 \|\|*

Pressing down her feet with his own, Keśava drew her up, thus stretching out her spine. As soon as her back was straight again, she became the handsomest of women. 10

## \|\| 5.20.11 \|\|

*vilāsalilitaṃ prāha premagarbhabharālasam \|*  
*vastre pragṛhya govindaṃ mama gehaṃ vrajeti vai \|\| 11 \|\|*

With amorous flirtations and languor born of love, she took hold of Govinda’s robe and said, ‘Come back to my place.’ 11

## \|\| 5.20.12 \|\|

*evamuktastayā śaurī rāmasyālokya cānanam \|*  
*prahasya kubjāṃ tāmāha naikavakrāmaninditām \|\| 12 \|\|*

‘Some other time,’ replied Hari with a smile, and sent her on her way. Seeing the look on Balarāma’s face, Kṛṣṇa laughed aloud. 12

## \|\| 5.20.13 \|\|

*āyāsye bhavatīgehamiti tāṃ prahasanhariḥ \|*  
*visasarja jahāsoccai rāmasyālokya cānanam \|\| 13 \|\|*

With marks of godhood on their bodies and wearing garments of blue and gold, still adorned with finest garlands, the two young men next reached the hall of archery. 13

## \|\| 5.20.14 \|\|

*bhaktibhedānuniptāṅgau nīlapītāṃbarau tu tau \|*  
*dhanuḥśālāṃ tato yātau citramālyopasobhitau \|\| 14 \|\|*

There they asked the guards about a mighty bow called Āyogava, the ‘Iron Cow’. On being told about it, Kṛṣṇa then raised the bow and drew it to its full extent. 14

## \|\| 5.20.15 \|\|

*āyāgaṃ taddhanūratnaṃ tābhyāṃ pṛṣṭais tu rakṣibhiḥ \|*  
*ākhyāte sahasā kṛṣṇo gṛhītvā pūrayaddhanuḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

On account of his great strength, when he did so, the bow snapped in two and that retort filled all of Mathurā. 15

## \|\| 5.20.16 \|\|

*tataḥ pūrayatā tena bhajyamānaṃ balāddhanuḥ \|*  
*cakāra sumahacchabdaṃ mathurā yena pūritā \|\| 16 \|\|*

The guards reproached the boys for what they’d done, but ignoring them, the two youths left the hall behind. 16

## \|\| 5.20.17 \|\|

*anuyuktau tatastau tu bhagre dhanuṣi rakṣibhiḥ \|*  
*rakṣisainyaṃ nihatyobhau niṣkrāntau kārmukālayāt \|\| 17 \|\|*

When Kaṃsa heard that Akrūra had returned and learned the bow was broken, he summoned his two wrestlers, Cāṇūra and Muṣṭika. 17

## \|\| 5.20.18 \|\|

*akrūrāgamavṛttāntamupalabhya mahaddhanuḥ \|*  
*bhagnaṃ śrutvā ca kaṃso 'pi prāha cāṇūramuṣṭikau \|\| 18 \|\|*

Kaṃsa:

‘The cattle-herder’s sons are here. I want them killed in a match before my eyes, as they’re my mortal enemies. 18

## \|\| 5.20.19 \|\|

*kaṃsa uvāca*  
*gopāladārakau prāptau bhavadybhāṃ tu mamāgrataḥ \|*  
*mallayuddhena hantavyau mama prāṇaharau di tau \|\| 19 \|\|*

I’d be pleased if you can kill them in a contest. I’ll give you anything you want, you mighty wrestlers, but not otherwise. 19

## \|\| 5.20.20 \|\|

*niyuddhe tadvināśena bhavadbhyāṃ toṣito hy aham \|*  
*dāsyāmyabhimatānkāmānnānyathaitau mahābalau \|\| 20 \|\|*

Destroy those enemies of mine, by any means foul or fair, and, when they’re dead, I’ll share this realm with you.’ 20

## \|\| 5.20.21 \|\|

*nyāyatonyāyato vāpi bhavadybhāṃ tau mamāhitau \|*  
*hantāvyau tadvadhādrājyaṃ sāmānyaṃ vāṃ bhaviṣyati \|\| 21 \|\|*

After he’d given this order to the wrestlers, he summoned his elephant-driver and shouted this instruction, ‘Take the elephant to the entrance of the wrestling arena. 21

## \|\| 5.20.22 \|\|

*ityādiśya sa tau mallau tataścāhūya hastipam \|*  
*provācoccais tvayā mallasamājadvāri kuñjaraḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

When the two herding boys reach the gate for the contest, I want Kuvalayāpīḍa to trample them to death.’ 22

## \|\| 5.20.23 \|\|

*sthāpyaḥ kuvalayāpīḍastena tau gopadārakau \|*  
*ghātanīyau niyuddhāya raṅgadvāramupāgatau \|\| 23 \|\|*

Having given this command, and seeing all the seating was in place, Kaṃsa, whose death was near at hand, now waited for the sun to rise. 23

## \|\| 5.20.24 \|\|

*tamapyājñāpya dṛṣṭvā ca sarvānmañcānupākṛtān \|*  
*āsannamaraṇaḥ kaṃsaḥ sūryodayamudaikṣata \|\| 24 \|\|*

The residents of the city filled the stands, and the king and his ministers took the dais reserved for them. 24

## \|\| 5.20.25 \|\|

*tataḥ samastamañceṣu nāgaraḥsa tadā janaḥ \|*  
*rājamañceṣu cārūḍhāḥsaha bhṛtyair narādhipāḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Kaṃsa sent the referees to the middle of the arena, while he himself sat high on the viewing platform. 25

## \|\| 5.20.26 \|\|

*mallaprāśrikavargaś ca raṅgamadhyasamīpagaḥ \|*  
*kṛtaḥ kaṃsena kaṃso 'pi tuṅgamañce vyavasthitaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

Separate seating was arranged for palace women, for courtesans and townsmen’s wives. 26

## \|\| 5.20.27 \|\|

*antaḥpurāṇāṃ mañcāś ca tathānye parikalpitāḥ \|*  
*anye ca vāramukhyānāmanye nāgarayoṣitām \|\| 27 \|\|*

Nanda and the herders were given other seats, while Akrūra and Vasudeva sat at the end of the benches. 27

## \|\| 5.20.28 \|\|

*nandagopādayo gopā mañceṣv anyeṣv avasthitāḥ \|*  
*akrūravasudevau ca mañcaprānte vyavasthitau \|\| 28 \|\|*

Among the townsmen’s wives sat Devakī, who yearned for Kṛṣṇa: ‘Even if he’s killed, I’ll see my son’s fair face again.’ 28

## \|\| 5.20.29 \|\|

*nāgarīyoṣitāṃ madhye devakīputragardhinī \|*  
*antakāle 'pi putrasya drakṣyāmīti mukhaṃ sthitā \|\| 29 \|\|*

When the music first began and Cāṇūra leapt up, the crowd let out a roar as Muṣṭika clapped his hands. 29

## \|\| 5.20.30 \|\|

*vādyamāneṣu turyeṣu cāṇūre cātivalgati \|*  
*hāhākārapare loke hyāsphoṭayati muṣṭike \|\| 30 \|\|*

The mahout drove Kuvalayāpīḍa towards the boys, but they slew the beast. With limbs now smeared in musth and blood, they armed themselves with tusks. 30

## \|\| 5.20.31 \|\|

*īṣaddhasaṃtau tau vīrau balabhadrajanārdanau \|*  
*gopaveṣadhārau vālau raṅgadvāramupāgatau \|\| 31 \|\|*

Balarāma and Janārdana then strode into that arena, looking round with proud and jaunty glances, like a pair of lions amid a herd of deer. 31

## \|\| 5.20.32 \|\|

*tataḥ kuvalayāpīḍo mahāmātrapracoditaḥ \|*  
*abhyadhāvata vegena hantuṃ gopakumārakau \|\| 32 \|\|*

The astonished crowds in all the stalls at once let out a mighty cry: ‘That’s Kṛṣṇa, and that’s Balarāma!’ 32

## \|\| 5.20.33 \|\|

*hāhākāro mahāñjajñe raṅgamadhye dvijottama \|*  
*baladevo 'nujaṃ dṛṣṭvā vacanaṃ cedam abravīt \|\| 33 \|\|*

The onlookers:

‘He’s the one who killed that fearsome night-ranging Pūtanā, overturned the wagon and snapped the arjuna trees. 33

## \|\| 5.20.34 \|\|

*hantavyo hi mahābhāga nāgo 'yaṃ śatrucoditaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

He’s the boy who danced on the serpent Kāliya and held up Mount Govardhana for seven nights. 34

## \|\| 5.20.35 \|\|

*ityuktaḥso 'grajenātha baladevena vai dvija \|*  
*siṃhanādaṃ tataścakre mādhavaḥ paravīrahā \|\| 35 \|\|*

He’s the great being who easily slew wicked Ariṣṭa, Dhenuka and Keśin. Look! He’s Acyuta, the imperishable deity. 35

## \|\| 5.20.36 \|\|

*kareṇa karamakṛṣya tasya keśiniṣūdanaḥ \|*  
*bhramayāmāsa taṃ śairir airāvatasamaṃ bale \|\| 36 \|\|*

And that’s strong-armed Balarāma, his elder brother, proceeding with ease in front of him, delighting the hearts and eyes of all the girls. 36

## \|\| 5.20.37 \|\|

*īśopi sarvajagatāṃ bālalīlānusārataḥ \|*  
*krīḍitvā suciraṃ kṛṣṇaḥ kadirantapadāntare \|\| 37 \|\|*

The wise who know the gist of ancient texts say the herdsman Kṛṣṇa will raise the declining clan of Yādava once more. 37

## \|\| 5.20.38 \|\|

*utpāṭya vāmadantaṃ tu dakṣiṇenaiva pāṇinā \|*  
*tāḍayāmāsa yantāraṃ tasyāsīcchatadhā śiraḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

He’s Viṣṇu’s aspect, the ultimate origin of all creation, descended to the world to ease the burden of the earth.’ 38

## \|\| 5.20.39 \|\|

*dakṣiṇa dantamutpāṭya balabhadro 'pi tatkṣaṇāt \|*  
*sa roṣastena pārśvasthān gajapālānapothayat \|\| 39 \|\|*

When people spoke of Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa in this way, Devakī’s bosom swelled with pride and her two breasts flowed with milk brought forth by love. 39

## \|\| 5.20.40 \|\|

*tatastūtplutya vegena rauhiṇeyo mahābalaḥ \|*  
*jaghāna vāmapādena mastake hastinaṃ ruṣā \|\| 40 \|\|*

For Vasudeva, seeing the faces of his sons was cause for celebration and, shrugging off the years, he grew young once more. 40

## \|\| 5.20.41 \|\|

*sa papāta hatastena balabhadreṇa līlayā \|*  
*sahasrākṣeṇa vajreṇa tāḍitaḥ parvato yathā \|\| 41 \|\|*

The palace women couldn’t draw their eyes from Kṛṣṇa, nor could the crowd of townsmen’s wives: 41

## \|\| 5.20.42 \|\|

*hatvā kuvalayāpīḍaṃ hastyārohapracoditam \|*  
*madāsṛganuliptāṅgau hastidantavarāyudhau \|\| 42 \|\|*

‘Look at Kṛṣṇa, friends, whose eyes are red, whose face is flecked with beads of sweat from the effort of fighting off the elephant. 42

## \|\| 5.20.43 \|\|

*mṛgamadhye yathā siṃhau garvalīlāvalokinau \|*  
*praviṣṭau sumahāraṅgaṃ balabhadrajanārdanau \|\| 43 \|\|*

It’s like a blooming autumn lotus, bejewelled with drops of dew. Give your present life some meaning and look at him!’ 43

## \|\| 5.20.44 \|\|

*hāhākāro mahāñjajñe mahāraṅge tvanantaram \|*  
*kṛṣṇo 'yaṃ balabhadbhoyamiti lokasya vismayaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

‘Look, my dear, at that young man’s broad chest marked with Śrīvatsa, and those two arms with which he crushed his enemies.’ 44

## \|\| 5.20.45 \|\|

*so 'yaṃ yena hatā ghorā pūtanā bālaghātinī \|*  
*kṣiptaṃ tu śakaṭaṃ yena bhagnau tu yamalārjunau \|\| 45 \|\|*

‘Can’t you see Balarāma approaching, dressed in blue, his face as white as jasmine, the moon or fibres of a lotus stalk?’ 45

## \|\| 5.20.46 \|\|

*soyaṃ yaḥ kāliyaṃ nāgaṃ mamardāruhya vālakaḥ \|*  
*dhṛto govardhano yena saptarātraṃ mahāgiriḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

‘See, my friend, how Balarāma laughs in the face of prancing Muṣṭika and Cāṇūra.’ 46

## \|\| 5.20.47 \|\|

*ariṣṭo dhenukaḥ keśī līlayaiva mahātmanā \|*  
*nihatā yena durvṛttā dṛśyatāmeṣa so 'cyutaḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

‘Look, friends, Hari approaches Cāṇūra for a fight. Are no elders here to referee the match? 47

## \|\| 5.20.48 \|\|

*ayaṃ cāsya mahābāhurbalabhadro 'grato 'grajaḥ \|*  
*prayāti līlayā yoṣinmanonayananandanaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

On one side is Hari, whose tender form is on the verge of youth, while on the other is this mighty demon, with a muscled body, hard as diamonds.’ 48

## \|\| 5.20.49 \|\|

*ayaṃ sa kathyate prājñaiḥ purāṇārthaviśāradaiḥ \|*  
*gopālo yādavaṃ vaṃśaṃ magnamabhyaddhariṣyati \|\| 49 \|\|*

‘These two lovely lads are already on the field, and the demon wrestlers led by Cāṇūra will show no mercy. 49

## \|\| 5.20.50 \|\|

*ayaṃ hi sarvalokasya viṣṇorakhilajanmanaḥ \|*  
*avatīrṇo mahīmaṃśo nūnaṃ bhāraharo bhuvaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

The judges of the spectacle have made a big mistake in permitting referees to oversee this match between an adult and a child.’ 50

## \|\| 5.20.51 \|\|

*ityevaṃ varṇite paurai rāme kṛṣṇe ca tatkṣaṇāt \|*  
*urastatāpa devakyāḥ snehasnutapayodharam \|\| 51 \|\|*

Parāśara:

While the palace women and townsmen’s wives were saying this, Lord Hari tightened his waistband and jumped upon the ground, causing it to tremble. 51

## \|\| 5.20.52 \|\|

*mahotsavam ivāsādya putrānanavilokanāt \|*  
*yuvave vasudevo 'bhūdvihāyābhyāgatāṃ jarām \|\| 52 \|\|*

When Balarāma clapped his hands and leapt about in play, it was a miracle the earth didn’t crack beneath his feet. 52

## \|\| 5.20.53 \|\|

*vistāritākṣiyugalo rājāntaḥpurayoṣitām \|*  
*nāgārastrīsamūhaś ca draṣṭuṃ na virarāma tam \|\| 53 \|\|*

Then Kṛṣṇa, whose prowess was immeasurable, fought Cāṇūra, while the demon Muṣṭika, himself a skilful wrestler, battled Balarāma. 53

## \|\| 5.20.54 \|\|

*sakhyaḥ paśyata kṛṣṇasya mukhamatyaruṇekṣaṇam \|*  
*gajayuddhakṛtāyāsasvedāṃbukaṇikacitam \|\| 54 \|\|*

Hari and Cāṇūra fought together, each seizing then releasing the other, with throws and blows of elbows, fists and forearms. 54

## \|\| 5.20.55 \|\|

*vikāsiśaradaṃbhojamapaśyāmajalokṣitam \|*  
*paribhūyaṃ sthitaṃ janma saphalaṃ kriyatāṃ dṛśaḥ \|\| 55 \|\|*

Dire indeed was the bout between them, kicking one another, their bodies intertwined. 55

## \|\| 5.20.56 \|\|

*śrīvatsāṃkaṃ mahaddhāma bālasyaitadvilokyatām \|*  
*vipakṣakṣapaṇaṃ vakṣo bhujayugyaṃ ca bhāmīni \|\| 56 \|\|*

That terrible hand-to-hand combat was vicious and, as the excited crowd looked on, both life and strength were jeopardised. 56

## \|\| 5.20.57 \|\|

*kiṃ na paśyasi dugdhendumṛṇāladhavalākṛtim \|*  
*balabhadramimaṃ nīlaparidhānamupagatam \|\| 57 \|\|*

The longer Cāṇūra fought with Hari, the more his initial strength began to ebb. 57

## \|\| 5.20.58 \|\|

*valgatāmuṣṭikenaiva cāṇūreṇa tathā sakhi \|*  
*krīḍatobalabhadrasya harerhāsyaṃ vilokyatām \|\| 58 \|\|*

But Kṛṣṇa, the essence of the world, wrestled him as if in play. The wreath on Cāṇūra’s head began to slip as weariness set in, yet still he fought. 58

## \|\| 5.20.59 \|\|

*sakhyaḥ paśyata cāṇūraṃ niyuddhārthamayaṃ hariḥ \|*  
*samupaiti na saṃtyatra kiṃ vṛddhā muktakārimaḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

Seeing Cāṇūra weaken as Kṛṣṇa grew ever stronger, furious Kaṃsa brought the music to a stop. 59

## \|\| 5.20.60 \|\|

*kva yauvanonmukhībhūtamukumāratanurhariḥ \|*  
*kva vajrakaṭhinābhogaśarīro 'yaṃ mahāsuraḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

The instant that the drums and other instruments fell silent, all kinds of heavenly music sounded in the sky. 60

## \|\| 5.20.61 \|\|

*imau sulalitair aṅgair vartete navayauvanau \|*  
*daiteyamallāścāṇūrapramukhāstvatidāruṇāḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

‘Victory to Govinda! Defeat the demon Cāṇūra, Keśava!’ cried the joyous gods unseen. 61

## \|\| 5.20.62 \|\|

*niyuddhaprāśnikānāṃ tu mahāneṣa vyatikramaḥ \|*  
*yadbālabalinor yuddhaṃ madhyasthaiḥ samupekṣyate \|\| 62 \|\|*

Having toyed with Cāṇūra for a while, Madhu’s subduer raised him in the air, spun him round and prepared to end his life. 62

## \|\| 5.20.63 \|\|

*śrīpāraśara uvāca*  
*itthaṃ purastrīlokasva vadataścālayanbhuvam \|*  
*vavalga baddhakakṣyontarjanasya bhagavānhariḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

Victorious Kṛṣṇa whirled the demon wrestler round a hundred times then dashed him to the ground, and Cāṇūra’s life force ascended to the sky. 63

## \|\| 5.20.64 \|\|

*balabhadro 'pi cāsphoṭya vavalgalalitaṃ tathā \|*  
*padepade tathā bhūmiryanna śīrṇā tadadbhutam \|\| 64 \|\|*

The demon’s body, cast down by Kṛṣṇa, broke into a hundred pieces and made the earth a red and bloody mire. 64

## \|\| 5.20.65 \|\|

*cāṇūreṇa tataḥ kṛṣṇo yayudhe 'mitavikramaḥ \|*  
*niyuddhakuśalo daityau balabhadreṇa muṣṭikaḥ \|\| 65 \|\|*

Balarāma, at that same time, fought the mighty demon wrestler Muṣṭika, just as Hari wrestled Cāṇūra. 65

## \|\| 5.20.66 \|\|

*sannipātāvadhūtais tu cāṇūreṇa samaṃ hariḥ \|*  
*prakṣepaṇair muṣṭibhiś ca kīlavajranipātanaiḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

Balarāma struck his rival on the head, kneed him in the chest, threw him to the ground, then crushed the life right out of him. 66

## \|\| 5.20.67 \|\|

*pādoddhūtaiḥ pramṛṣṭaiś ca tayoryuddham abhūn mahat \|\| 67 \|\|*

Next, with a blow of his left hand, Kṛṣṇa struck mighty Tośalaka, king of wrestlers, felling him to the ground. 67

## \|\| 5.20.68 \|\|

*aśastramatighoraṃ tat tayor yuddhaṃ sudāruṇam \|*  
*balaprāṇaviniṣpādyaṃ samājotsavasannidhau \|\| 68 \|\|*

When Cāṇūra had been slain, Muṣṭika overthrown and Tośalaka put to death, all the other wrestlers fled. 68

## \|\| 5.20.69 \|\|

*yāvadyāvacca cāṇūro yuyudhe hariṇā saha \|*  
*prāmahānimavāpāgryāṃ tāvattavallavāllavam \|\| 69 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma then pulled other boys their age on to the field, where they danced for joy. 69

## \|\| 5.20.70 \|\|

*kṛṣṇopi yuyudhe tena līlayaiva jaganmayaḥ \|*  
*khedāccālayatā kopānnijakesaraśekharam \|\| 70 \|\|*

Kaṃsa’s eyes were red with rage as he shouted to his lackeys, ‘Take those herding boys away where the public cannot see them, using force if necessary. 70

## \|\| 5.20.71 \|\|

*balakṣayaṃ vivṛddhiṃ ca dṛṣṭvā cāṇūrakṛṣṇayoḥ \|*  
*vārayāmāsa tūryāṇi kaṃsaḥ kopaparāyaṇaḥ \|\| 71 \|\|*

Bring that wicked Nanda here in iron chains and put Vasudeva to death—a fate too good for that old man. 71

## \|\| 5.20.72 \|\|

*mṛdaṅgādiṣu tūryeṣu pratiṣiddheṣu tatkṣaṇāt \|*  
*khe saṃgatānyavādyanta devatūryāṇyanekaśaḥ \|\| 72 \|\|*

Confiscate the cows and any other property belonging to the herders who are dancing here with Kṛṣṇa.’ 72

## \|\| 5.20.73 \|\|

*jaya govinda cāṇūraṃ jahi keśava dānavam \|*  
*antardhānagatā devāstamūcuratiharṣitāḥ \|\| 73 \|\|*

As Kaṃsa was issuing these commands, Madhu’s subduer gave a laugh, leapt up on the dais and smartly seized the king. 73

## \|\| 5.20.74 \|\|

*cāṇūreṇa ciraṃ kālaṃ pīḍitvā madhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*utpāṭya bhramayāmāsa tadvadhāya kṛto dyamaḥ \|\| 74 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa knocked the crown from Kaṃsa’s head, dragged him by the hair, then threw him to the ground and jumped on top of him. 74

## \|\| 5.20.75 \|\|

*bhrāmayitvā śataguṇaṃ daityamallamamitrajit \|*  
*bhūbhāvātphoṭayāmāsa gagane gatajīvitam \|\| 75 \|\|*

When Kṛṣṇa leapt on Kaṃsa with the weight of the universe, Ugrasena’s royal son gave up the ghost. 75

## \|\| 5.20.76 \|\|

*bhūmāvāsphoṭitastenacāṇūraḥ śatadhābhavat \|*  
*raktasrāvamahāpaṅkāṃ cakāra ca tadā bhavam \|\| 76 \|\|*

Mighty Madhusūdana then took Kaṃsa’s body by the hair and dragged it to the middle of the field. 76

## \|\| 5.20.77 \|\|

*baladevo 'pi tatkālaṃ muṣṭikena mahābalaḥ \|*  
*yayudhe daityamallena cāṇūreṇa yathā hariḥ \|\| 77 \|\|*  
*so 'pyenaṃ muṣṭinā mūrdhni vakṣasyāhatya jānunā \|*  
*pātayitvā dharāpṛṣṭhe niṣpipeṣa gatāyuṣam \|\| 5.20.78 \|\|*

Because of its great weight, the corpse, when drawn along by Kṛṣṇa, left behind a channel as if cut by the current of a mighty river. 77

## \|\| 5.20.78 \|\|

*sanskrit not found \|\| 78 \|\|*

When Kṛṣṇa first seized Kaṃsa, his brother Sunāman rushed forward angrily, but was dispatched with ease by Balarāma. 78

## \|\| 5.20.79 \|\|

*kṛṣṇa stośalakaṃ bhūyo mallarājaṃ mahābalam \|*  
*vāmamuṣṭiprahāreṇa pātayāmāsa bhūtale \|\| 79 \|\|*

Seeing the king of Mathurā killed with such contempt by Kṛṣṇa, everyone in the arena let out a cry of anguish. 79

## \|\| 5.20.80 \|\|

*cāṇūre nahate malle muṣṭeke vinipātite \|*  
*nīte kṣayaṃ tośalake sarve mallāḥ pradudruvuḥ \|\| 80 \|\|*

Strong-armed Kṛṣṇa, accompanied by Balarāma, immediately touched the feet of Vasudeva and of Devakī. 80

## \|\| 5.20.81 \|\|

*vavalgatustato raṅge kṛṣṇasaṃkarṣaṇāvubhau \|*  
*samānavayaso gopānbalādākṛṣyaharṣitau \|\| 81 \|\|*

Lifting up Janārdana, his parents recalled what he had said to them at birth and bowed before him. 81

## \|\| 5.20.82 \|\|

*kaṃso 'pi koparaktākṣaḥ prāhoccair vyāyatānnarān \|*  
*gopāvetau samājaughānniṣkrāmyetāṃ balāditaḥ \|\| 82 \|\|*

Vasudeva:

Lord, be merciful. You grant the wishes of the gods when they’re despondent, Lord. So, too, through your mercy for us, Keśava, you became upholder of the world. 82

## \|\| 5.20.83 \|\|

*nando 'pi gṛhyatāṃ pāpo nirgalair āyasair iha \|*  
*avṛddhārhaima daṇḍena vasudevo 'pi badhyatām \|\| 83 \|\|*

Because the blessed lord appeared for the destruction of the wicked in my home, our family has been purified. 83

## \|\| 5.20.84 \|\|

*valganti gopāḥ kṛṣṇena ye ceme sahitāḥ purāḥ \|*  
*gāvo nigṛhya tāmeṣāṃ yaccāsti vasu kiñcana \|\| 84 \|\|*

You are at the heart of every creature and abide in them. Both past and future emanate from you, the heart of everything. 84

## \|\| 5.20.85 \|\|

*evamājñāpayānaṃ tu prahasya madhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*utplutyāruhya taṃ mañcaṃ kaṃsaṃ jagrāha vegataḥ \|\| 85 \|\|*

You are always honoured with the sacrifice and embody all the deities, eternal being. You alone are the sacrifice and sacrificer, highest lord of sacrifices. 85

## \|\| 5.20.86 \|\|

*keśeṣvā kṛṣya vigalatkirīṭamavanītale \|*  
*saṃ kaṃsaṃ pātayāmāsa tasyopari papāta ca \|\| 86 \|\|*

My mind was playing tricks when Devakī and I loved you like a son—a grave misjudgement. 86

## \|\| 5.20.87 \|\|

*aśeṣajagadādhāraguruṇā patatopari \|*  
*kṛṣṇena tyājitaḥ prāṇānugrasenātmajo nṛpaḥ \|\| 87 \|\|*

How improper that my mortal tongue might call you, creator of all creatures with neither start nor finish, ‘son’. 87

## \|\| 5.20.88 \|\|

*mṛtasya keśeṣu tadā gṛhītvā madhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*cakarṣa dehaṃ kaṃsasya raṅgamadhye mahābalaḥ \|\| 88 \|\|*

How can it be fitting, other than by illusion, that he from whom this world arises, the universal lord, was born to us? 88

## \|\| 5.20.89 \|\|

*gauravemātipatatā parighātena kṛṣyatā \|*  
*kṛtā kaṃsasya dehena vegeneva mahāṃbhasaḥ \|\| 89 \|\|*

How can he, in whom this world of moving and unmoving things abides, first lying in a womb, then be born to mortals? 89

## \|\| 5.20.90 \|\|

*kaṃse gṛhīte kṛṣṇena tadbhrātābhyāgato ruṣā \|*  
*sunāmā bala bhadreṇa līlayaiva nipātitaḥ \|\| 90 \|\|*

Be merciful, highest Lord. Protect the world by manifesting aspects of yourself. You’re no son of mine. Why delude this whole world, from Brahmā down to the merest tree, Lord, when it comes from you, heart of the highest spirit? 90

## \|\| 5.20.91 \|\|

*tato hāhākṛtaṃ sarvamāsīttadraṅgamaṇḍalam \|*  
*avajñayā hataṃ dṛṣṭvā kṛṣṇena mathureśvaram \|\| 91 \|\|*

Because my eyes were clouded by illusion, I regarded you as my own son. Fearing Kaṃsa, and overcome by dread, I took you to the safety of the cattle camp where you grew up. Lord, I no longer claim you as my own. 91

## \|\| 5.20.92 \|\|

*kṛṣṇe 'pi vasudevasya pādau jagrāha satvaraḥ \|*  
*devakyāś ca mahābāhurbaladevasahāyavān \|\| 92 \|\|*

The deeds of yours we witnessed could not be done by Rudra, the Maruts, the Aśvins or by Indra, for you are Viṣṇu, Lord, come for the benefit of the worlds. Now we understand this, our delusions are dispelled. 92

## Chapter 21 - Ugrasena is reinstated

## \|\| 5.21.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tau samutpannavijñānau bhagavatkarmadarśanāt \|*  
*devakī vasudevau kudṛṣṭvā māyāṃ punarhariḥ \|*  
*mohāya yaducakrasya vitatāna sa vaiṣṇavīm \|\| 1 \|\|*

Now that Devakī and Vasudeva’s insight had been wakened, having seen the exploits of the lord, Hari covered them again with his illusion to deceive the Yadu clan. 1

## \|\| 5.21.2 \|\|

*uvāca cāṃba he tāta cirādutkaṇṭhitena me \|*  
*bhavantau kaṃsabhītena dṛṣṭau saṃkarṣaṇena ca \|\| 2 \|\|*

‘Dearest mother, dearest father,’ said he, ‘Balarāma and I have always looked on you with love, but we were fearful of Kaṃsa. 2

## \|\| 5.21.3 \|\|

*kurvatāṃ yāti yaḥ kālo mātāpitrorapūjanam \|*  
*tatkhaṇḍamāyuṣo vyarthamasādhūnāṃ hi jāyate \|\| 3 \|\|*

Decent folk regard the lives of those who live without respect for parents as deficient and in vain. 3

## \|\| 5.21.4 \|\|

*gurudevadvijātīnāṃ mātāpitroś ca pūjanam \|*  
*kurvatāṃ saphalaḥ kālo dehināṃ tāta jāyate \|\| 4 \|\|*

But, father, the lives of those who respect their guru, gods, the twice-born orders and their parents are fulfilled. 4

## \|\| 5.21.5 \|\|

*tatkṣantavyamidaṃ sarvamatikramakṛtaṃ pitaḥ \|*  
*kaṃsavīryapratāpābhyāmāvayoḥ paravaśyayoḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Forgive us, therefore, for all that we have done in error, father, as we were subject to Kaṃsa’s will on account of his temper and his power.’ 5

## \|\| 5.21.6 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvātha praṇamyobhau yaduvṛddhānanukramāt \|*  
*yathāvadabhipūjyātha cakratuḥ pauramānanam \|\| 6 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, both Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma duly honoured the Yadu elders according to their station, then paid respects to all the other townsfolk. 6

## \|\| 5.21.7 \|\|

*kaṃsapatnyastataḥ kaṃsaṃ parivārya hataṃ bhuvi \|*  
*vilepurmātaraścāsya duḥkhaśokapariplutāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Kaṃsa’s wives, surrounding his corpse upon the ground, bewailed him, and pain and grief overwhelmed his mothers. 7

## \|\| 5.21.8 \|\|

*bahuprakāramasvasthāḥ paścāttāpāturo hariḥ \|*  
*tāḥsamāśvāsayāmāsa svayamasrāvilekṣamaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Hari, filled with deep regret, sought to comfort them, as tears were streaming from his eyes. 8

## \|\| 5.21.9 \|\|

*ugrasenaṃ tato bandhāmumoca madhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*abhyasiṃcattadaivainaṃ nijarājye hatātmajam \|\| 9 \|\|*

Madhu’s subduer then set Ugrasena free, and, now that his son Kaṃsa was no more, he was anointed sovereign of the realm again by Kṛṣṇa. 9

## \|\| 5.21.10 \|\|

*rājyobhiṣiktaḥ kṛṣṇena yadusiṃhaḥsutasya saḥ \|*  
*cakāra pretakāryāṇi ye cānye tatra ghātitāḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

After Kṛṣṇa had enthroned him, the lion of the Yadu clan then held funerals for his own son and the others who’d been slain. 10

## \|\| 5.21.11 \|\|

*kṛtaurdhadaihikaṃ cainaṃ siṃhāsanagataṃ hariḥ \|*  
*uvācājñāpaya vibho yatkāryamaviśaṅkitaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

When the rites had been completed and Ugrasena returned to his lion throne, Hari said to him, ‘Your majesty, surely you should tell us what to do. 11

## \|\| 5.21.12 \|\|

*yayātiśāpādvaṃśoyamarājyārhepi sāṃpratam \|*  
*mayi bhṛtye sthite devanājñāpayatu kiṃ nṛpaiḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Because of Yayāti’s curse, this clan deserves no sovereignty, but while I’m at your service, you may rule the gods, not to mention other kings.’ 12

## \|\| 5.21.13 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvā so 'smaradvāyumājagāma ca tatkṣaṇāt \|*  
*uvāca cainaṃ bhagavānkeśavaḥ kāryamānuṣaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After he had spoken, Kṛṣṇa summoned Vāyu with a thought, and the wind god came at once. Lord Keśava in human guise addressed him: 13

## \|\| 5.21.14 \|\|

*gacchedaṃ brūhi vāyo tvamalaṃ garvoṇa vāsava \|*  
*dīyatāmugrasenāya sudharmā bhavatā sabhā \|\| 14 \|\|*

‘Go to Indra, Vāyu, and tell that chief of Vasus to set aside his pride. He should give his assembly hall that’s known as Sudharmā to Ugrasena. 14

## \|\| 5.21.15 \|\|

*kṛṣṇo bravīti rājārhametadratnamanuttamam \|*  
*sūdharmākhyasabhāyuktamasyāṃ yadubhir āsitum \|\| 15 \|\|*

Tell him Kṛṣṇa says Sudharmā is a peerless gem that suits a king. It’s fitting that the hall becomes the Yadu clan’s.’ 15

## \|\| 5.21.16 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktaḥ pavano gatvā sarvamāha śacīpatim \|*  
*dadau so 'pi sudharmākhyāṃ sabhāṃ vāyoḥ purandaraḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the wind god went to Indra, Śacī’s husband, and relayed to him the message. Accordingly, Indra, destroyer of his rivals’ cities, gave the hall to Vāyu. 16

## \|\| 5.21.17 \|\|

*vāyunā cāhṛtāṃ divyāṃ sabhāṃ te yadupuṅgavāḥ \|*  
*bubhujuḥsarvaratnāḍhyāṃ govindabhujasaṃśrayāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Vāyu then delivered it, sparkling with jewels of every kind, to the Yadu heroes, who enjoyed it, thanks to the might of Govinda’s arms. 17

## \|\| 5.21.18 \|\|

*viditākhilavijñānau sarvajñānamayāvapi \|*  
*śiṣyācāryakramaṃ vīrau khyāpayantau sadūttamau \|\| 18 \|\|*

Those two peerless heroes of the Yādavas, who grasped each branch of knowledge and possessed all wisdom, wished to demonstrate the bond between the teacher and his pupil. 18

## \|\| 5.21.19 \|\|

*tataḥsāṃdīpaniṃ kāśyamavantīpuravāsinam \|*  
*vidyārthaṃ jagmaturbālau kṛtopanayanakramau \|\| 19 \|\|*

Accordingly, Balarāma and Janārdana called on Sāndīpani—born in Kāśī, but now residing in the city of Avantī—to learn the use of weapons. 19

## \|\| 5.21.20 \|\|

*vedābhyāsakṛtaprīti saṃkarṣaṇajanārdanau \|*  
*tasya śiṣyatvamabhyetya guruvṛttipurau hi tau \|*  
*darśayāñcakratur vīrā vācāramakhile jane \|\| 20 \|\|*

Those two heroes became disciples and, intent upon the service of their guru, displayed the finest conduct in the presence of everyone. 20

## \|\| 5.21.21 \|\|

*sarahasyaṃ dhanurvedaṃ sasaṃgrahamadhīyatām \|*  
*ahorātracatuḥṣaṣṭyā tadadbhutamabhūddvūja \|\| 21 \|\|*

Brahmin, it was a marvel that they mastered secret weapons, the science of archery and its use in battle in four and sixty days. 21

## \|\| 5.21.22 \|\|

*sāṃdīpanirasaṃbhāvyaṃ tayoḥ karmātimānuṣam \|*  
*vicintya tau tadā mene prāptau candradivākarau \|\| 22 \|\|*

Sāndīpani, who thought such superhuman feats impossible, felt as if the sun and moon had come to him. 22

## \|\| 5.21.23 \|\|

*sāṃgāṃś ca caturo vedānsarvaśāstrāṇi caiva hi \|*  
*astragrāmamaśeṣaṃ ca proktamātramavāpya tau \|\| 23 \|\|*

As they mastered every class of weapon as soon as they’d been taught, they asked what fee their guru might expect. 23

## \|\| 5.21.24 \|\|

*ūcuturvriyatāṃ yā te dātavyā gurudakṣimā \|\| 24 \|\|*

That wise man, having seen their supernatural feats, asked for his son who’d drowned at Prabhāsa in the ocean. 24

## \|\| 5.21.25 \|\|

*so 'py atīndriyamālokya tayoḥ karma mahāmati \|*  
*ayācata sṛtaṃ putraṃ prabhāse lavaṇārṇave \|\| 25 \|\|*

The heroes took up their weapons, but the mighty Ocean, greeting them hospitably with water for their feet, said, ‘It wasn’t I who took the son of Sāndīpani. 25

## \|\| 5.21.26 \|\|

*gṛhītāstrau tatastau tu sārghyahasto mahodadhiḥ \|*  
*uvāca na mayā putro hṛtaḥsāṃdīpaneriti \|\| 26 \|\|*

A Daitya named Pañcajana, appearing as a conch, has seized the boy. He still resides below my waters, demon slayer.’ 26

## \|\| 5.21.27 \|\|

*daityaḥ pañcajano nāma śaṅkharūpaḥ sa bālakam \|*  
*jagrāha yosti salile mamaivā surasūdana \|\| 27 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, Kṛṣṇa dived into the ocean, slew Pañcajana and seized his wondrous shell, as hard as bone. 27

## \|\| 5.21.28 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvontarjalaṃ gatvā hatvā pañcajanaṃ ca tam \|*  
*kaṣṇo jagrāha tasyāsthiprabhavaṃ śaṅkhamuttamam \|\| 28 \|\|*

(When that conch is sounded, Daityas lose their strength, the ardour of the gods increases and evil is destroyed.) 28

## \|\| 5.21.29 \|\|

*yasya nādena daityānāṃ balahānirajāyata \|*  
*devānāṃ vavṛdhe tejo yātyadharmaś ca saṃkṣayam \|\| 29 \|\|*

Hari blew a note on Pāñcajana, then, entering the city of the dead, accompanied by mighty Balarāma, defeated Yama, the Sun’s own son. 29

## \|\| 5.21.30 \|\|

*taṃ pāñcajanyamāpūrya gatvā yamapuraṃ hariḥ \|*  
*baladevaś ca balavāñjitvā vaivasvataṃ yamam \|\| 30 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, foremost of mighty warriors, saved the boy from that place of torment and gave him to his father in his original body. 30

## \|\| 5.21.31 \|\|

*taṃ bālaṃ yātanāsaṃstha yathāpūrvaśarīriṇam \|*  
*pitre pradattavānkṛṣṇo balaś ca balināṃ varaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

To the delight of Mathurā’s men and women, they returned to that very city, now ruled by Ugrasena. 31

## Chapter 22 - Kṛṣṇa battles Jarāsandha

## \|\| 5.22.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*jarāsaṃdhamute kasa upayeme mahābalaḥ \|*  
*astiṃ prāptiṃ ca maitreya cayorbhartṛhaṇaṃ harim \|\| 1 \|\|*

Mighty Kaṃsa married two of Jarāsandha’s daughters, Maitreya: Asti and Prāpti. 1

## \|\| 5.22.2 \|\|

*mahā balaparīvāro magadhādhipatirbalī \|*  
*hantumabhyāyayau kopājjarāsaṃdhaḥsayādavam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Jarāsandha, the powerful king of Magadha, was furious when Hari slew his daughters’ husband, and set out with a mighty force to slay that scion of Yadu. 2

## \|\| 5.22.3 \|\|

*upetya mathurāṃ so 'tha rurodha magadheśvaraḥ \|*  
*akṣauhiṇībhiḥ sainyasya trayoviṃśatibhir vṛtaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Reaching Mathurā, the lord of Magadha besieged the city with a force of twenty-three divisions. 3

## \|\| 5.22.4 \|\|

*niṣkramyālpaparīvārāvubhau rāmajanārdanau \|*  
*yuyudhāte samaṃ tasya balinau balisainikaiḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Powerful Balarāma and Janārdana set out with a retinue to confront the force of Jarāsandha. 4

## \|\| 5.22.5 \|\|

*tato rāmaś ca kṛṣṇaś ca matiṃ cakraturañjasā \|*  
*āyudhānāṃ purāṇānāmādāne munisattama \|\| 5 \|\|*

Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa made the wise decision to deploy their ancient weapons, best of sages. 5

## \|\| 5.22.6 \|\|

*anantaraṃ hareḥ śārṅgaṃ tūṇī cākṣayasāyakau \|*  
*ākāśādāgatau vipra tathā kaumodakī gadā \|\| 6 \|\|*

That instant, Hari’s bow, Śārṅga, and two inexhaustible quivers descended from the heavens, brahmin, as did his mace, Kaumodakī. 6

## \|\| 5.22.7 \|\|

*halaṃ ca balabhadrasya gaganādāgataṃ mahat \|*  
*manaso 'bhimataṃ vipra sānandaṃ musalaṃ tathā \|\| 7 \|\|*

As soon as Balarāma called his blazing plough to mind, it, too, descended from the sky, brahmin, with his mace, Saunanda. 7

## \|\| 5.22.8 \|\|

*tato yuddhe parājitya sasainyaṃ sagadhādhipam \|*  
*purīṃ viviśaturvīrāvubhau rāmajanārdanau \|\| 8 \|\|*

The pair of heroes overcame the king of Magadha and his army in that battle, then returned to Mathurā. 8

## \|\| 5.22.9 \|\|

*jite tasminsudurvṛtte jarāsaṃdhe mahā mune \|*  
*jīvamāne gate kṛṣṇastenāmanyata nājitam \|\| 9 \|\|*

Wicked Jarāsandha had been vanquished, sage, but as he had escaped alive, Kṛṣṇa did not consider him defeated. 9

## \|\| 5.22.10 \|\|

*punarapyājagāmātha jarāsaṃdho balānvitaḥ \|*  
*jitaś ca rāmakṛṣṇābhyāmapakrānto dvijottama \|\| 10 \|\|*

In fact, Jarāsandha returned with another force, best of brahmins, but was overcome again by Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa and escaped once more. 10

## \|\| 5.22.11 \|\|

*daśa cāṣṭau ca saṃgramānevamatyantadurmadaḥ \|*  
*yadubhir māgadho rājā cakre kṛṣṇapurogamaiḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

The doughty king of Magadha fought eighteen such battles with the Yadus led by Kṛṣṇa. 11

## \|\| 5.22.12 \|\|

*sarveṣveteṣu yuddheṣu yādavaiḥ sa parājitaḥ \|*  
*apakrāñco jarāsaṃdhaḥsvalpasainyair balādhikaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

In each encounter, Jarāsandha was beaten by a smaller force of Yādavas, even though outnumbering them, but he always managed to escape. 12

## \|\| 5.22.13 \|\|

*na tadbalaṃ yādavānāṃ vijitaṃ yadanekaśaḥ \|*  
*tat tu saṃnidhimāhātmyaṃ viṣṇor aṃśasya cakriṇaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

The army of the Yādavas, unconquered by Jarāsandha in every clash, relied on Kṛṣṇa’s greatness—an aspect of discus-wielding Viṣṇu. 13

## \|\| 5.22.14 \|\|

*manuṣyadharmaśīlasya līlā sā jagatīpateḥ \|*  
*astrāṇyanekarūpāṇi yadarātiṣu muñcati \|\| 14 \|\|*

To launch his varied weapons at a foe was just a game for the universal lord in mortal form. 14

## \|\| 5.22.15 \|\|

*manasaiva jagatsṛṣṭiṃ saṃhāraṃ ca karoti yaḥ \|*  
*tasyāpipakṣakṣapaṇe kiyānudyamavistaraḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

How little effort is the conquest of an enemy for one who brings about creation and destruction of the universe through thought alone? 15

## \|\| 5.22.16 \|\|

*tathāpi yo manuṣyāṇāṃ dharmastamanuvartate \|*  
*kurvanbalavatā saṃdhiṃ hīnair yuddhaṃ karotyasau \|\| 16 \|\|*

Nevertheless, he followed the usual practices of mortals, such as allying with the strong and waging war against the weak. 16

## \|\| 5.22.17 \|\|

*sāma copapradānaṃ ca tathā bhedaṃ ca darśayan \|*  
*karoti daṇḍapātaṃ ca kvacideva palāyanam \|\| 17 \|\|*

He also practised conciliation, bribery, punishment and subversion, and sometimes even took to flight. 17

## \|\| 5.22.18 \|\|

*manuṣyadehināṃ ceṣṭāmityevamanuvartate \|*  
*līlā jagatpatestasya cchandataḥ parivartate \|\| 18 \|\|*

Adopting all these strategies of mortal men, the universal lord engaged in games like these at will. 18

## Chapter 23 - Kṛṣṇa leads the Yādavas to Dvārakā

## \|\| 5.23.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*gārgyaṃ goṣṭhyāṃ dvijaṃ śyālaḥṣaṇḍa ityuktavāndvija \|*  
*yadūnāṃ sannidhau sarve jahasuryādavāstadā \|\| 1 \|\|*

Parāśara:

At a public gathering one day, in the presence of the Yadu clan, the brahmin Gārgya’s brother-in-law accused the man of impotence, and everybody mocked him, brahmin. 1

## \|\| 5.23.2 \|\|

*tataḥ kopaparītātmā dakṣiṇāpathametya saḥ \|*  
*sutamicchaṃstapastepe yaducakrabhayāvaham \|\| 2 \|\|*

Gārgya was filled with rage and journeyed to the southern lands, where, desiring a son who could wreak revenge on the Yādavas, he undertook austerities. 2

## \|\| 5.23.3 \|\|

*ārādhayanmahādevaṃ lohacūrṇamabhakṣayat \|*  
*dadau varaṃ ca tuṣṭo 'smai varṣe tu dvādaśe haraḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

To propitiate Lord Śiva, he lived on iron filings for a dozen years. That great god, being gratified, then granted him a boon. 3

## \|\| 5.23.4 \|\|

*saṃtoṣayāmāsa ca taṃ yavaneśo hyanātmajaḥ \|*  
*tadyoṣitsaṃgamāccāsya putro 'bhūdalisannibhaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

The king of Yavanas, who also had no children, received Gārgya hospitably. The latter had intercourse with the king’s own consort and she gave birth to a son as black as a bee. 4

## \|\| 5.23.5 \|\|

*taṃ kālayavanaṃ nāma rāye sve yavaneśvaraḥ \|*  
*abhiṣicya vanaṃ yāto vajragrakaṭhinorasam \|\| 5 \|\|*

Kālayavana or ‘Black Yavana’ was that child’s name, and his chest was as hard as the tip of a thunderbolt. The Yavana lord anointed his son as king of the realm and then retired to the forest. 5

## \|\| 5.23.6 \|\|

*sa tu vīryamadonmattaḥ pṛthivyāṃ balino nṛpān \|*  
*apṛcchannāradastasmai kathayāmāsa yādavān \|\| 6 \|\|*

Maddened by pride in his own prowess, Kālayavana asked Nārada which were the mightiest kings on earth. ‘The Yādavas,’ was his reply. 6

## \|\| 5.23.7 \|\|

*mlecchakoṭisahasrāṇāṃ sahasraiḥ so 'bhisaṃvṛtaḥ \|*  
*gajāśvarathasaṃpannaiś cakāra paramodyamam \|\| 7 \|\|*

Flanked by barbarians in the thousands of millions, in train with elephants, horses and chariots, and sparing no effort, 7

## \|\| 5.23.8 \|\|

*prayayau sa vyavacchinnaṃ chinnayāno dinedine \|*  
*yādavānprati sāmarṣo maitreya mathurāṃ purīm \|\| 8 \|\|*

He advanced each day on Mathurā—tiring his transport, but not himself—indignant on the Yādavas’s account, Maitreya. 8

## \|\| 5.23.9 \|\|

*kṛṣṇo 'pi cintayāmāsa kṣapitaṃ yādavaṃ balam \|*  
*yavanena raṇe gamyaṃ māgadhasya bhaviṣyati \|\| 9 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa feared that if the Yādava force were weakened by the Yavanas in battle, they might then be defeated by the Magadha king. 9

## \|\| 5.23.10 \|\|

*māgadhasya balaṃ kṣīṇaṃ sa kālayavano balī \|*  
*hantaitadevamāyātaṃ yadūnāṃ vyasanaṃ dvidhā \|\| 10 \|\|*

But if the Yādavas were reduced by the Māgadhas, then the powerful Kālayavana might defeat them. Thus, the Yādavas faced a twofold threat. 10

## \|\| 5.23.11 \|\|

*tasmāddurgaṃ kariṣyāmi yadūnāmaridurjayam \|*  
*striyo 'pi yatra yuddheyuḥ kiṃ punarvṛṣṇipuṅgavāḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

‘I’ll build a stronghold for the Yadus that’s difficult for their enemies to attack. It will be defensible by womenfolk alone, to say nothing of the heroic Vṛṣṇi tribe. 11

## \|\| 5.23.12 \|\|

*mayi matte pramatte vā supte pravasite 'pi vā \|*  
*yādavābhibhavaṃ duṣṭā mā kurvaṃstvarayodhikāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

If I’m careless, asleep, abroad or drunk, even a mighty horde of wicked enemies won’t be able to defeat them.’ 12

## \|\| 5.23.13 \|\|

*iti saṃcintya govindo yojanānāṃ mahodadhim \|*  
*yayāce dvādaśapurīṃ dvārakāṃ tatra nirmame \|\| 13 \|\|*

With this in mind, Govinda asked the Ocean for twelve leagues of land, on which he built the city known as Dvārakā. 13

## \|\| 5.23.14 \|\|

*mahodyānāṃ mahāvaprāṃ taṭākaśataśobhitām \|*  
*prāsādagṛhasaṃbādhāmindrasyevāmarāvatīm \|\| 14 \|\|*

With extensive parks and lofty ramparts, it shone with a hundred reservoirs. Filled with residences and courtyards, it resembled Indra’s city Amarāvatī. 14

## \|\| 5.23.15 \|\|

*mathurāvāsinaṃ lokaṃ tatrānīya janārdanaḥ \|*  
*āsanne kālayavane mathurāṃ ca svayaṃ yayau \|\| 15 \|\|*

Janārdana led the residents of Mathurā to Dvārakā, then returned to Mathurā, just as Kālayavana drew near. 15

## \|\| 5.23.16 \|\|

*bahirāvāsite sainye mathurāyā nirāyudhaḥ \|*  
*nirjagāma ca govindo dadarśa yavanaś ca tam \|\| 16 \|\|*

When the latter’s army was camped outside the city, Govinda came forth unarmed and saw the Yavana king. 16

## \|\| 5.23.17 \|\|

*sa jñātvā vāsudevaṃ taṃ bāhupraharaṇaṃ nṛpaḥ \|*  
*anuyāto mahāyogī cetobhiḥ prāpyate na yaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

The strong-armed monarch recognised Vāsudeva and pursued him, but Kṛṣṇa travelled faster than the greatest yogins’ thoughts. 17

## \|\| 5.23.18 \|\|

*tenānuyātaḥ kṛṣṇo 'pi praviveśa mahāguhām \|*  
*yatra śete mahāvīryo mucukundo nareśvaraḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

With the Yavana in pursuit, Kṛṣṇa entered a deep cavern where Mucukunda, that powerful hero and king of men, was sleeping. 18

## \|\| 5.23.19 \|\|

*so 'pi praviṣṭo yavano dṛṣṭvā śayyāgataṃ nṛpam \|*  
*pādena tāḍayāmāsa matvā kṛṣṇaṃ sudurmatiḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

The wicked Yavana entered the cave and saw the sleeping figure. Mistaking him for Kṛṣṇa, he gave the man a kick. 19

## \|\| 5.23.20 \|\|

*utthāya mucukundo 'pi dadarśa yavanaṃ nṛpaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

As soon as Mucukunda set eyes on him, the Yavana was burned by fire born of fury, Maitreya, and was reduced at once to ashes. 20

## \|\| 5.23.21 \|\|

*dṛṣṭamātraś ca tenāsau jajvāla yavanogninā \|*  
*tatkrodhajena maitreya bhasmībhūtaś ca tatkṣaṇāt \|\| 21 \|\|*

(Long ago, Mucukunda had helped the gods defeat the mighty demigods in battle and, being fatigued, he asked the gods for the boon of a long repose. 21

## \|\| 5.23.22 \|\|

*sa hi devāsure yuddhe gato hatvā mahāsurān \|*  
*nidrārtaḥsumahākālaṃ nidrāṃ vavre varaṃ surān \|\| 22 \|\|*

The gods then promised that anyone who disturbed his rest would be burned at once to ashes by fire from his body.) 22

## \|\| 5.23.23 \|\|

*proktaś ca devaiḥ saṃsuptaṃ yastvāmutthāpayiṣyati \|*  
*dehajenāgninā sadyaḥsa tu bhasmībhaviṣyati \|\| 23 \|\|*

After the wicked king was consumed by flames, Mucukunda beheld Madhu’s subduer and asked him who he was. ‘I was born in the Lunar Dynasty, as the son of Vasudeva, in the lineage of Yadu,’ he replied. 23

## \|\| 5.23.24 \|\|

*evaṃ dagdhvā sa taṃ pāpaṃ dṛṣṭvā ca madhusūdanam \|*  
*kastvamityāha so 'pyāha jātohaṃ śaśinaḥ kule \|*  
*vasudevasya tanayo yadorvaṃśasamudbavaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Mucukunda then recalled an ancient prophesy of Garga. 24

## \|\| 5.23.25 \|\|

*mucukundo 'pi tatrāsau vṛddhagargyāvaco 'smarat \|\| 25 \|\|*

With that recollection, he threw himself upon the ground and addressed these words to Hari, the universal lord of all: ‘I know that you’re an aspect of Viṣṇu, highest Lord. 25

## \|\| 5.23.26 \|\|

*saṃsmṛtya praṇipatyainaṃ sarvaṃ sarveśvaraṃ harim \|*  
*prāha jñāto bhavān viṣṇor aṃśastvaṃ parameśvara \|\| 26 \|\|*

Long ago, Garga foretold that Hari would be born in Yadu’s lineage at the end of the twenty-eighth Dvāpara age. 26

## \|\| 5.23.27 \|\|

*purā gārgyeṇa kathitamaṣṭāviṃśatime yuge \|*  
*dvāparānte harerjanma yaduvaṃśe bhaviṣyati \|\| 27 \|\|*

You are the one who’s come, no doubt, for the benefit of all mortal beings. 27

## \|\| 5.23.28 \|\|

*sa tvaṃ prāpto na saṃdeho martyānāmupakārakṛt \|\| 28 \|\|*

That’s why I can’t withstand your brilliance. That’s why your voice is deeper than the rumbling of a rain-filled thundercloud. That’s why the earth subsides when pressed beneath your feet. 28

## \|\| 5.23.29 \|\|

*tathāhi sumahattejo nālaṃ soḍhumahaṃ tava \|*  
*tathāhi sajalāṃbhodanādadhīrataraṃ tava \|*  
*vākyaṃ namati caivorvī yuṣmatpādaprapīḍitā \|\| 29 \|\|*

In the great war between the gods and demigods, the mighty warriors of the Daitya army could not withstand my ardour, but even I’m unable to withstand yours. 29

## \|\| 5.23.30 \|\|

*devāsuramahāyuddhe daityasainyamahābhaṭāḥ \|*  
*na sehurmama te jaste tvattejo na sahāmyaham \|\| 30 \|\|*

You alone are the highest refuge for every being who has come into this world. Be merciful, ease the pain we suffer and deliver us from evil. 30

## \|\| 5.23.31 \|\|

*saṃsārapatitasyaiko jantostvaṃ śaraṇaṃ param \|*  
*prasīda tvaṃ prapannārtihara nāśaya me 'śubham \|\| 31 \|\|*

You are the oceans, mountains and the rivers. You are forests, earth and sky, wind and fire and water. You are mind. 31

## \|\| 5.23.32 \|\|

*tvaṃ payo nidhayaḥ śailasaritastvaṃ vanāni ca \|*  
*medinī gaganaṃ vāyurāpognistvaṃ tathā manaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

You are intellect, the primal substance of the universe. You are breath and lord of life, the spirit and that which lies beyond, all-pervading, unchanging and unborn. 32

## \|\| 5.23.33 \|\|

*buddhiravyākṛtaprāṇāḥ prāṇeśastvaṃ tathā pumān \|*  
*puṃsaḥ parataraṃ yacca vyāpya janma vikāravat \|\| 33 \|\|*

You transcend sensations such as sound, undecaying, immeasurable, imperishable, ageless and indestructible. You are that which is the Absolute, without a start or finish. 33

## \|\| 5.23.34 \|\|

*śabdādihīnamajaramameyaṃ kṣayavarjitam \|*  
*avṛddhināśaṃ tadbrahma tvamādyantavivarjitam \|\| 34 \|\|*

From you arise immortals, ancestors, yakṣas, gandharvas, kiṃnaras, siddhas and apsarases. From you arise humanity, birds and animals. 34

## \|\| 5.23.35 \|\|

*tvatto 'marāḥsapitaro yakṣagandharvakinnarāḥ \|*  
*siddhāścāpsarasastvatto manuṣyāḥ paśavaḥ khagāḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

From you arise the serpents and other wild creatures, all the plants that grow upon the earth, all that has existed and all that’s yet to come and everything in this world, both moving and unmoving. 35

## \|\| 5.23.36 \|\|

*sarīsṛpā mṛgāḥsarve tvattaḥsarve mahīruhāḥ \|*  
*yacca bhūtaṃ bhaviṣyaṃ ca kiñcidatra carācaram \|\| 36 \|\|*

Formed and formless, gross and subtle—all this is you, creator of the world. Nothing lies beyond you. 36

## \|\| 5.23.37 \|\|

*mūrtāmūrtaṃ tathā cāpi sthūlaṃ sūkṣmataraṃ tathā \|*  
*tatsarvaṃ tvaṃ jagatkartā nāsti kiñcittvā yā vinā \|\| 37 \|\|*

Always wandering in the cycle of existence, Lord, overwhelmed by threefold suffering, I cannot find release. 37

## \|\| 5.23.38 \|\|

*mayā saṃsāracakre 'sminbhramatā bhagavaṃstadā \|*  
*tāpatrayābhibhūtena na prāptā nirvṛtiḥ kvicit \|\| 38 \|\|*  
*duḥkhānyeva sukhānīti mṛgatṛṣṇājalāśayāḥ \|*  
*mayā nātha gṛhītāni tāni tāpāya me 'bhavan \|\| 5.23.39 \|\|*

Mistaking pain for pleasure, like thirsty deer confused by a mirage, I grasp at indulgences, Lord, but they only bring me further pain. 38

## \|\| 5.23.39 \|\|

*sanskrit not found \|\| 39 \|\|*

A kingdom, lands, power, wealth, allies, children, wives, servants, pleasant sounds and other sensations, Lord— 39

## \|\| 5.23.40 \|\|

*rājyamurvī balaṃ kośo mitrapakṣastathātmajāḥ \|*  
*bhāryā bhṛtya jano ye ca śabdādya viṣayāḥ prabho \|\| 40 \|\|*

I acquired all these, thinking they would bring me joy, changeless deity. But in the end, Lord, they only brought me misery. 40

## \|\| 5.23.41 \|\|

*sukhabuddhyā mayā sarvaṃ gṛhītamidamavyayam \|*  
*pariṇāme tadeveśa tāpātmakamabhūnmama \|\| 41 \|\|*

Even hosts of gods in heaven wanted help from me, Lord. Whence comes eternal liberation? 41

## \|\| 5.23.42 \|\|

*devalokagatiṃ prāpto nātha devagaṇo 'pi hi \|*  
*mattaḥsāhāyyakāmobhūcchāśvatī kutra nirvṛtiḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

Without worshipping you, the cause of the existence of all the worlds, highest Lord, who can achieve lasting freedom? 42

## \|\| 5.23.43 \|\|

*tvāmanārādhya jagatāṃ sarveṣāṃ prabhavāspadam \|*  
*śāśvatī prāpyate kena parameśvara nirvṛtiḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

Their minds are clouded by your illusory power and, having felt the pain of birth, old age and death and so on, people then face Yama. 43

## \|\| 5.23.44 \|\|

*tvanmāyāmūḍhamanaso janmamṛtyujarādikān \|*  
*avāpya tāpānpaśyanti pretarājamanantaram \|\| 44 \|\|*

They meet with terrifying tortures in the realms of hell as wages for their former deeds, because they failed to recognise your actual form. 44

## \|\| 5.23.45 \|\|

*tato nijakriyāsūtinarakeṣvatidāruṇam \|*  
*prāpnuvanti narā duḥkhamasvarūpavidastava \|\| 45 \|\|*

Hooked on sensual pleasures and beguiled by your illusion, highest Lord, I stagger round the pit of pride created by my sense of self. 45

## \|\| 5.23.46 \|\|

*ahamatyantaviṣayī mohitastava māyayā \|*  
*mamatvagarvagartāntarbhramāmi parameśvara \|\| 46 \|\|*

I come to you for refuge—limitless, praiseworthy Lord, the highest state beyond which there’s nought. My heart is wearied by the travails of life and I yearn for nirvāṇa, the perfect rest.’ 46

## Chapter 24 - Balarāma returns to the cattle camp

## \|\| 5.24.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*itthaṃstutastadā tena mucukundena dhīmatā \|*  
*prāheśaḥ sarvabhūtānāmanādinidhano hariḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Thus eulogised by prudent Mucukunda, Lord Hari, beginningless master of all creation, replied, 1

## \|\| 5.24.2 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*yathābhivāñchitāndivyāngacchalokānnarādhipa \|*  
*avyāhataparaiśvaryo matprasādopabṛṃhitaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

‘You’ll proceed to the heavenly realms you wish for, king. Your sovereignty will not be challenged by another, and you’ll be nourished by my mercy. 2

## \|\| 5.24.3 \|\|

*bhuktvā divyanmahā bhogānbhaviṣyasi mahākule \|*  
*jātismaro matprasādāttatomokṣamavāpsyasi \|\| 3 \|\|*

After you’ve tasted the delights of heaven, you’ll be born in a noble family and, remembering your former lives, you’ll win liberation through my grace.’ 3

## \|\| 5.24.4 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktaḥ praṇipatyeśaṃ jagatāmacyutaṃ nṛpaḥ \|*  
*guhāmukhādviniṣkrāntataḥsa dadarśālpakānnarān \|\| 4 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the king bowed down before the unfailing universal lord. When Mucukunda emerged from the mouth of the cave, he noticed that the people were much shorter than they were before. 4

## \|\| 5.24.5 \|\|

*tataḥ kaliyugaṃ matvā prāptaṃ taptuṃ nṛpastapaḥ \|*  
*naranārāyaṇasthānaṃ prayayau gandhamādanam \|\| 5 \|\|*

Realising that the age of Kali had begun, the king set out for Mount Gandhamādana to undertake austerities in the ashram of Nara and Nārāyaṇa. 5

## \|\| 5.24.6 \|\|

*kṛṣṇopi ghātayitvārimupāyena hi tadvalam \|*  
*jagrāha mathurāmetya hastyaśvasyandanojjvalam \|\| 6 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa, meanwhile, destroyed his rival with a stratagem, then returned to Mathurā, where he seized his rival’s army with its splendid elephants, chariots and steeds. 6

## \|\| 5.24.7 \|\|

*ānīya cograsenāya dvāravatvāṃ nyavedayat \|*  
*parābhibhavaniḥ śaṅkaṃ babhūva ca yadoḥ kulam \|\| 7 \|\|*

He led all these to Ugrasena in Dvārakā, and there informed the Yadu clan that they need no longer fear defeat. 7

## \|\| 5.24.8 \|\|

*baladevo 'pi maitreya praśāntākhilavigrahaḥ \|*  
*jñātidarśanasotkaṇṭhaḥ prayayau nandagokulam \|\| 8 \|\|*

Now that the war was over, Balarāma wished to see his family again, Maitreya, and so set out for Nanda’s cattle camp. 8

## \|\| 5.24.9 \|\|

*tato gopāś ca gopyaś ca yathā pūrvam amitrajit \|*  
*tathaivābhyavadatpramṇā bahumānapuraḥsaram \|\| 9 \|\|*

The victorious warrior met the herdsmen and their women as before with love and great respect. 9

## \|\| 5.24.10 \|\|

*sa kaiścit saṃpariṣvaktaḥ kāṃścicca pariṣasvaje \|*  
*hāsyaṃ cakre samaṃ kaiścid gopair gopījanais tathā \|\| 10 \|\|*
