Some hugged him and he hugged others, while he and the herding people laughed together. 10

## \|\| 5.24.11 \|\|

*priyāṇyanekānyavadan gopāstatra halāyudham \|*  
*gopyaś ca premakupitāḥ procuḥserṣyamathāparāḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

The herdsmen had many kind words for Balarāma, the wielder of the plough, but some of the women spoke sulkily because of their affection, while others expressed their envy. 11

## \|\| 5.24.12 \|\|

*gopyaḥ papracchuraparā nāgarījanavallabham \|*  
*kaccidāste sukhaṃ kṛṣṇaścalapremalavātmakaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Yet other herding women said, ‘I hope the darling of the city girls is happy. But Kṛṣṇa’s fickle love will only last for half a second.’ 12

## \|\| 5.24.13 \|\|

*asmacceṣṭāmapahasanna kaccitpurayoṣitām \|*  
*saubhāgyamāna madhikaṃ karoti kṣaṇasauhṛdaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

‘I hope he doesn’t mock our manners with the women of Mathurā. He’ll make them feel so special, but he’ll only love them for a minute.’ 13

## \|\| 5.24.14 \|\|

*kaccitsmarati naḥ kṛṣṇo gītānugamanaṃ kalam \|*  
*apyasau mātaraṃ draṣṭu sakṛdapyāgamiṣyati \|\| 14 \|\|*

‘I hope that Kṛṣṇa remembers how we used to sing so softly with him. Will he come back just once to see his mother?’ 14

## \|\| 5.24.15 \|\|

*atha vā kiṃ tadālāpaiḥ kriyantāmaparāḥ kathāḥ \|*  
*yasyāsmabhir vinā tena vināsmākaṃ bhaviṣyati \|\| 15 \|\|*

‘Why bother mentioning him? It’s a different story now we live without him, and he no longer lives with us.’ 15

## \|\| 5.24.16 \|\|

*pitā mātā tathā bhrātā bhartā bandhujanaś ca kim \|*  
*saṃtyaktastatkṛtesmābhir akṛtajñadhvajo hi saḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

‘Didn’t we leave our fathers, mothers, brothers, husbands and relations to be with him? That man is the standard-bearer of ingratitude.’ 16

## \|\| 5.24.17 \|\|

*tathāpi kaccidālāpamihāgamanasaṃśrayam \|*  
*karoti kṛṣṇo vaktavyaṃ bhavatā rāma nānṛtam \|\| 17 \|\|*

‘I hope that Kṛṣṇa spoke of coming back. Tell us truly, Balarāma.’ 17

## \|\| 5.24.18 \|\|

*dāmodaro 'sau govindaḥ purastrīsaktamānasaḥ \|*  
*apetaprītirasmāsu durdarśaḥ pratibhāti naḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

‘Govinda is still Dāmodara, the toddler with the cord tied round his middle.’ ‘The city girls will set their hearts on him. He doesn’t love us anymore. I doubt we’ll see him further.’ 18

## \|\| 5.24.19 \|\|

*āmantritaś ca kṛṣṇeti punardāmodareti ca \|*  
*jahasuḥsasvaraṃ gopyo hariṇā hṛtacetasaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The herding women even called Balarāma ‘Kṛṣṇa’ and ‘Dāmodara’ as they wept aloud, since Hari stole their hearts. 19

## \|\| 5.24.20 \|\|

*saṃdeśaiḥ sāmamadhuraiḥ premagarbhair agarvitaiḥ \|*  
*rāmeṇāśvāsitā gopyaḥ kṛṣṇasyātimanoharaiḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

Balarāma comforted them with kind, sweet, affectionate, modest and charming messages from Kṛṣṇa. 20

## \|\| 5.24.21 \|\|

*gopaiś ca pūrvavadrāmaḥ parihāsamanoharāḥ \|*  
*kathāścakāra reme ca saha tair vrajabhūmiṣu \|\| 21 \|\|*

He also swapped amusing tales of interest with the herdsmen as before and enjoyed himself in their company in the lands around the camp. 21

## Chapter 25 - Balarāma diverts the Yamunā River

## \|\| 5.25.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*vane vicaratas tasya saha gopair mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*mānuṣacchadmarūpasya śeṣasya dharaṇīdhṛtaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

While the mighty serpent Śeṣa, upholder of the world, in the mortal form of Balarāma roamed the forest with the herding men, 1

## \|\| 5.25.2 \|\|

*niṣpādito rukāryasya kāryoṇorvīpracāriṇaḥ \|*  
*upabhogārthamatyarthaṃ varuṇaḥ prāha vāruṇīm \|\| 2 \|\|*

And achieved great deeds through his actions as he wandered through the world, Varuṇa, most anxious for amusement, addressed his consort: 2

## \|\| 5.25.3 \|\|

*abhīṣṭā sarvadā yasya madire tvaṃ mahaujasaḥ \|*  
*anantasyopabhogāya tasya gaccha mude śubhe \|\| 3 \|\|*

‘You, Madirā, liquor goddess, pleasant and delightful, are always agreeable to mighty Śeṣa, so go now and amuse him.’ 3

## \|\| 5.25.4 \|\|

*ityuktā vāruṇī tena sannidhānamathākaret \|*  
*vṛndāvanasamutpannakadaṃ batarukoṭare \|\| 4 \|\|*

Thus addressed, Varuṇa’s consort hid in the hollow of a kadamba tree that stood in the forest at Vṛndāvana. 4

## \|\| 5.25.5 \|\|

*vicaran baladevo 'pi madirāgandhamuttamam \|*  
*āghrāya madirātarṣamavāpātha varānanaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Balarāma, wandering nearby, caught a tempting whiff of liquor and his old hankering for wine returned. 5

## \|\| 5.25.6 \|\|

*tataḥ kadaṃbātsahasāmadyadhārāṃ sa lāṅgalī \|*  
*patantīṃ vīkṣya maitreya prayayau paramāṃ mudam \|\| 6 \|\|*

When the bearer of the plough saw the stream of liquor trickling from the tree, Maitreya, he was as happy as can be. 6

## \|\| 5.25.7 \|\|

*papau ca gopagopībhiḥ samupeto mudānvitaḥ \|*  
*pragīyamāno lalītaṃ gītavādyaviśāradaiḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

The herding men and women gathered round while Balarāma drank his fill, and those who were skilled in song and music entertained him sweetly. 7

## \|\| 5.25.8 \|\|

*sa mattotyantagharmāṃbhaḥkaṇikāmauktikojjvalaḥ \|*  
*āgaccha yamune snātumicchāmītyāha vihvalaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Drunk and moody, Balarāma, agleam with pearls of perspiration, said to the river, ‘Come here, Yamunā. I want a bath.’ 8

## \|\| 5.25.9 \|\|

*tasya vācaṃ nadī sā tu mattoktāmavamatya vai \|*  
*nājagāma tataḥ ktruddho halaṃ jagrāha lāṅgalī \|\| 9 \|\|*

Because he was drunk, the river ignored him, but when she failed to do his bidding, furious Balarāma grabbed his plough. 9

## \|\| 5.25.10 \|\|

*gṛhītvā tāṃ halāntena cakarṣa madavihvalaḥ \|*  
*pāpe nāyāsi nāyāsi gamyatāmicchayānyataḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Addled by wine, he caught the riverbank with his weapon and dragged her towards him, saying, ‘You wicked river! You won’t come? You won’t come? Now try to get away!’ 10

## \|\| 5.25.11 \|\|

*sākṛṣṭā sahasā tena mārgaṃ saṃtyajya nimragā \|*  
*yatrāste balabhadro 'sau plāvayāmāsa tadvanam \|\| 11 \|\|*

Dragged with force by Balarāma, the river left her usual course and flowed into the forest where he sat. 11

## \|\| 5.25.12 \|\|

*śorīriṇī tadābhyetya trāsavihvalalocanā \|*  
*prasīdetyabravīdrāmaṃ muñca māṃ musalāyudha \|\| 12 \|\|*

Appearing to him in human form, Yamunā came to Balarāma with terror in her eyes and begged the wielder of the plough to be merciful and let her go. 12

## \|\| 5.25.13 \|\|

*tatastasyāḥ suvacanamākarṇya sa halā yudhaḥ \|*  
*so 'bravīdavajānāsi mama śauryabale nadi \|*  
*so 'haṃ tvāṃ halapātena nayiṣyāmi sahasradhā \|\| 13 \|\|*

‘You disrespect my courage and my strength, you river, so I’ll dash you into a thousand pieces with my plough!’ 13

## \|\| 5.25.14 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktayāti saṃtrāsāttayā nadya prasāditaḥ \|*  
*bhūbhāge plāvite tasminmumoca yamunāṃ balaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Yamunā was terrified by these threats, but convinced Balarāma to show her mercy and, after the river gave her waters to all the land, he released her. 14

## \|\| 5.25.15 \|\|

*tataḥ snātasya vai kāntir ajāyata mahātmanaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Mighty Balarāma took a bath, and Lakṣmī, goddess of beauty, then appeared with a lovely azure waterlily as an ornament for one of his ears and an earring for the other. 15

## \|\| 5.25.16 \|\|

*avataṃsotpalaṃ cāru gṛhītvaikaṃ ca kuṇḍalam \|*  
*varuṇaprahitāṃ cāsmai mālāmamlānapaṅkajām \|*  
*samudrābhe tathā vastre nīle lakṣmīrayacchata \|\| 16 \|\|*

She also gave him gifts from Varuṇa: a garland of fresh lotuses and a pair of sea-blue garments. 16

## \|\| 5.25.17 \|\|

*kṛtāvataṃsaḥ sa tadā cārukuṃḍalabhūṣitaḥ \|*  
*nīlāṃbaradharaḥsragvī śuśubhe kāntisaṃyutaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

With the lily and the earring, clad in blue and wearing flowers, Balarāma shone like loveliness itself. 17

## \|\| 5.25.18 \|\|

*itthaṃ vibhūṣito reme tatra rāmas tathā vraje \|*  
*māsadvayena yātaś ca sa punardvārakāṃ purīm \|\| 18 \|\|*

Thus adorned, he amused himself in the cattle camp for the next two months, then returned to Dvārakā. 18

## \|\| 5.25.19 \|\|

*revatīṃ nāma tanayāṃ raivatasya mahīpateḥ \|*  
*upayeme balastasyāṃ jajñāte niśitolmukau \|\| 19 \|\|*

There he married Revatī, daughter of King Raivata, who bore him two sons, Niśaṭha and Ulmuka. 19

## Chapter 26 - Kṛṣṇa abducts Rukmiṇī

## \|\| 5.26.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*bhīṣmakaḥ kuṇḍine rājā vidarbhaviṣaye 'bhavat \|*  
*rukmītasyabhavatputro rukmiṇī ca varānanā \|\| 1 \|\|*

Bhīṣmaka, king of the Vidarbha lands, who lived in Kuṇḍina, had a son named Rukmin and a beautiful daughter, Rukmiṇī. 1

## \|\| 5.26.2 \|\|

*rukmiṇīṃ cakame kṛṣṇaḥsā ca taṃ cāruhīsini \|*  
*na dadau yācate caināṃ rukmī dveṣeṇa cakriṇe \|\| 2 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa fell in love with Rukmiṇī, and that sweetly smiling maiden loved him in return. But her brother loathed the discus-wielder and wouldn’t let her go to Kṛṣṇa as requested. 2

## \|\| 5.26.3 \|\|

*dadau ca śiśupālāya jarāsandhapracoditaḥ \|*  
*bhīṣmako rukmiṇā sārdha rukmiṇīmuruvikramaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Brave Bhīṣmaka, urged on by Jarāsandha and with Rukmin’s agreement, gave Rukmiṇī instead to Śiśupāla. 3

## \|\| 5.26.4 \|\|

*vivāhāryaṃ tataḥ sarve jarāsaṃdhamukhā nṛpāḥ \|*  
*bhīṣmakasya puraṃ jagmuḥ śiśupālapriyaiṣiṇaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

All the monarchs led by Jarāsandha, desiring Śiśupāla’s felicity, assembled in Bhīṣmaka’s capital for his daughter’s wedding. 4

## \|\| 5.26.5 \|\|

*kṛṣṇo 'pi balabhadrādyair yadubhiḥ parivāritaḥ \|*  
*prayayau kuṇḍinaṃ draṣṭuṃ vivāhaṃ caidyabhūbhṛtaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa also came to Kuṇḍina to see the nuptials of the Cedi king, surrounded by a host of Yādavas including Balarāma. 5

## \|\| 5.26.6 \|\|

*śvo bhāvini vivāhe tu tāṃ kanyāṃ hṛtavānhariḥ \|*  
*vipakṣabhāram āsajya rāmādiṣv atha bandhuṣu \|\| 6 \|\|*

But the day before the wedding, Hari carried the girl away, leaving Balarāma and his other kin to fight against their enemies. 6

## \|\| 5.26.7 \|\|

*tataś ca paiṇḍrakaśrśrīmāndantavakro vidūrathaḥ \|*  
*śiśupālajarāsaṃdhaśālvādyāś ca mahībhṛtaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Pauṇḍraka, Śrīmant, Dantavakra, Vidūratha, Śiśupāla, Jarāsandha, Śālva and the other kings were furious. 7

## \|\| 5.26.8 \|\|

*kupitāste hariṃ hantuṃ cakrurudyogamuttamam \|*  
*nirjitāś ca samāgamya rāmādyoryadupuṅgavaiḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

They made every effort to conquer Hari but were overcome in battle by Balarāma and the other Yadu heroes. 8

## \|\| 5.26.9 \|\|

*kuṇḍinaṃ na pravekṣyāmi hyahatvā yudhi keśavam \|*  
*kṛtvā pratijñāṃ rukmī ca hantuṃ kṛṣṇamanudrutaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Rukmin swore he’d not return to Kuṇḍina until he’d vanquished Keśava and set off in pursuit to do so. 9

## \|\| 5.26.10 \|\|

*hatvā balaṃ sanāgāśvaṃ pattisyandanasaṃkulam \|*  
*nirjitaḥ pātitaścorvyāṃ līlayaiva sa cakriṇā \|\| 10 \|\|*

But the bearer of the discus had no trouble in defeating Rukmin’s army with its elephants, horses, infantry and chariots; then he struck Rukmin to the ground. 10

## \|\| 5.26.11 \|\|

*nirjitya rukmiṇaṃ samyagupayeme ca rukmiṇīm \|*  
*rākṣasena vivāhena saṃprāptāṃ madhusudanaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

After Madhu’s subduer had completely vanquished Rukmin, he married Rukmiṇī, whom he’d forcefully abducted. 11

## \|\| 5.26.12 \|\|

*tasyāṃ jajñe ca pradyumno madanāṃśaḥsavīryavān \|*  
*jahāra śaṃbaro yaṃ vai yo jaghāna ca śaṃbaram \|\| 12 \|\|*

She bore him the hero Pradyumna, an aspect of Madana, god of love. The demon Śambara abducted Pradyumna, but Pradyumna slew him in the end. 12

## Chapter 27 - Kṛṣṇa’s son Pradyumna is taken by a demon

## \|\| 5.27.1 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*saṃbareṇahṛto vīraḥ pradyumnaḥ sa kathaṃ mune \|*  
*śaṃbaraḥ sa mahāvīryaḥ pradyumnena kathaṃ hataḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

How was this hero Pradyumna taken by Śambara, sage, and how did Pradyumna kill that fearless being? 1

## \|\| 5.27.2 \|\|

*yastenāpahṛtaḥ pūrvaṃ sa kathaṃ vijaghāna tam \|*  
*etadvistarataḥ śrotumicchāmi saralaṃ guro \|\| 2 \|\|*

Śambara snatched Pradyumna from his nursery, sage, when the child was only six days old, because that demon, as terrible as Death, believed the infant would one day slay him. 2

## \|\| 5.27.3 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ṣaṣṭhehni jātamātraṃ tu pradyumnaṃ sūtikāgṛhāt \|*  
*mamaiṣa hanteti mune hṛtavānkālaśaṃbaraḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

After Śambara seized Pradyumna, he threw him into the frightful salty sea, the crocodiles’ abode, with its terrifying monsters and whirlpools whipped up by waves. 3

## \|\| 5.27.4 \|\|

*hṛtvā cikṣepa caivainaṃ grāhogre lavaṇārṇave \|*  
*kallolajanitāvarte sughore makārālaye \|\| 4 \|\|*

As he fell into the water, the child was swallowed by a fish, but, warmed by the fire in the fish’s belly, the baby didn’t die. 4

## \|\| 5.27.5 \|\|

*pātitaṃ tatra caivaiko matsyo jagrāha bālakam \|*  
*na mamāra ca tasyāpi jaṭharāgnipradīpitaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

That fish, along with its kin and others, brahmin, were caught in time and delivered to the demon. 5

## \|\| 5.27.6 \|\|

*matsyabandhaiś ca matsyo 'sau matsyair anyaiḥ saha dvaja \|*  
*ghātito 'sūravaryāya śaṃbarāya niveditaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Śambara’s blameless wife, Māyāvatī, was mistress of all his household and supervised the cooks. 6

## \|\| 5.27.7 \|\|

*tasya māyā vatī nāma patnī sarvagṛheśvarī \|*  
*kārayāmāsa sūdānāmādhipatyamaninditā \|\| 7 \|\|*

When the fish’s belly was opened, she beheld the lovely baby boy, like a fresh green shoot on the once charred tree of love. 7

## \|\| 5.27.8 \|\|

*dārite matsyajaṭhare sā dadarśātiśobhanam \|*  
*kumāraṃ manmathatarordagdhasya prathamāṅkuram \|\| 8 \|\|*

That slender woman was filled with wonder about the child’s identity and how he came to be there, till Nārada enlightened her: 8

## \|\| 5.27.9 \|\|

*ko 'yaṃ kathamayaṃ matsyajaṭhare praviveśitaḥ \|*  
*ityevaṃ kaunupāviṣṭāṃ nanvīṃ prāhātha nāradaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

‘This is the son of Viṣṇu, creator and destroyer of the universe, taken from his nursery by the demon Śambara. 9

## \|\| 5.27.10 \|\|

*ayaṃ samastajagataḥ sthitisaṃhārakāriṇaḥ \|*  
*śaṃbareṇa hṛto viṣṇostanayaḥ sūtikāgṛhāt \|\| 10 \|\|*

He was cast into the ocean and swallowed by this fish. Now this treasure of humanity is in your care, and you, fair-browed and fearless woman, must nurture him.’ 10

## \|\| 5.27.11 \|\|

*kṣiptaḥsamudre matsyena nigīrṇaste gṛhaṃ gataḥ \|*  
*nararatnamidaṃ subhru visrabdhā paripālaya \|\| 11 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After Nārada gave Māyāvatī this explanation, she cared for the child from the time he was young with deep affection, beguiled by his wondrous beauty. 11

## \|\| 5.27.12 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*nāradenaivam uktā sā pālayāmāsa taṃ śiśum \|*  
*bālyādevātirāgeṇarūpātiśayamohitā \|\| 12 \|\|*

When he acquired the handsomeness of youth, great sage, Māyāvatī, her gait as stately as a she-elephant’s, began to desire him. 12

## \|\| 5.27.13 \|\|

*sa yadā yauvanābhogabhūṣito 'bhūnmahāmate \|*  
*sābhilāṣā tadā sāpi babhūva gajagāmini \|\| 13 \|\|*

Blind with love, she conferred all her illusionary powers on Pradyumna, sage, her heart and eyes set on him alone. 13

## \|\| 5.27.14 \|\|

*māyāvatī dadau tasmai māyāḥ sarvā mahāmune \|*  
*pradyumnāyānurāgāndhā tatryastahṛdayekṣaṇā \|\| 14 \|\|*

Pradyumna, son of Kṛṣṇa, rebuked that lotus-eyed woman who lusted after him, ‘You’ve abandoned normal motherly affection. Why are you acting differently with me?’ 14

## \|\| 5.27.15 \|\|

*prasājjantīṃ tu tāṃ prāha sa kārṣṇiḥ kamalebhaṇām \|*  
*mātṛtvamapahāyādya kimevaṃ vartase 'nyathā \|\| 15 \|\|*

‘You’re not my son,’ she said, ‘but are the son of Viṣṇu. Śambara, as terrible as Death, abducted you. 15

## \|\| 5.27.16 \|\|

*sā tasmai kathayāmāsana putrastvaṃ mameti vai \|*  
*tanayaṃ tvāmayaṃ viṣṇorhṛtavānkālaśaṃbaraḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

He threw you in the ocean and I saved you from the belly of a fish. Your natural mother weeps for you because she loves you deeply.’ 16

## \|\| 5.27.17 \|\|

*kṣiptaḥ samudre matsyasya saṃprāpto jaṭharānmayā \|*  
*sā hi roditite mātā kāntādyāpyativatsalā \|\| 17 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, mighty Pradyumna challenged Śambara to battle and, with fury in his heart, waged war against his foe. 17

## \|\| 5.27.18 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktaḥ śaṃbaraṃ yuddhe pradyumnaḥ sa samāhvayat \|*  
*krodhākulīkṛtamanā yuyudhe ca mahā balaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Madhu’s scion destroyed the Daitya’s army, leaving nothing, and seven times foiled the phantoms conjured by that demon. 18

## \|\| 5.27.19 \|\|

*hatvā sainyamaśeṣaṃ tu tasya daityasya yādavaḥ \|*  
*saptamāyā vyatikramya māyāṃ prayuyuje 'ṣṭamīm \|\| 19 \|\|*

He tamed the eighth and sent it back to kill the Daitya Śambara, as terrible as Death. With the aid of that same being, a form of Māyāvatī, Pradyumna rose up in the air and flew to the residence of Kṛṣṇa, his natural father. 19

## \|\| 5.27.20 \|\|

*tayā jaghāna taṃ daityaṃ māyayā kālaśaṃbaram \|*  
*utpattya ca tayā sārdhamājagāma pituḥ puram \|\| 20 \|\|*

When the women saw Pradyumna descending to the inner chambers with Māyāvatī, they mistook him for his father. 20

## \|\| 5.27.21 \|\|

*antaḥpure nipatitaṃ māyāvatyā samanvitam \|*  
*taṃ dṛṣṭvā kṛṣṇa saṃkalpā babhūvuḥ kṛṣṇayoṣitaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

The eyes of blameless Rukmiṇī were filled with tears as she said tenderly, ‘Any woman would be glad to have a son like you on the threshold of maturity. 21

## \|\| 5.27.22 \|\|

*rukmiṇī sābhavatpremṇā sāsradṛṣṭiraninditā \|*  
*dhanyāyā khalv ayaṃ putro vartate navayauvane \|\| 22 \|\|*

Pradyumna, my own son, would be about your age, if he were still alive. Some lucky mother has you as an ornament, dear boy. 22

## \|\| 5.27.23 \|\|

*asminvayasi putro me pradyumno yadi jīvati \|*  
*sabhāgyā jananī vatsa sā tvāyā kā vibhīṣitā \|\| 23 \|\|*

And yet, such is the love I feel for you and such is your own beauty, my dear, you must be Hari’s son.’ 23

## \|\| 5.27.24 \|\|

*atha vā yādṛsaḥ sneho mama yādṛgvapustava \|*  
*harerapatyaṃ suvyaktaṃ bhavānvatsa bhaviṣyati \|\| 24 \|\|*

Parāśara:

At that very moment, Nārada appeared, accompanied by Kṛṣṇa, and addressed the princess Rukmiṇī, resident of the inner chambers, gladdening her heart: 24

## \|\| 5.27.25 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*etasminnantare prāptaḥsaha kṛṣṇena nāradaḥ \|*  
*antaḥpuracarāṃ devīṃ rukmiṇīṃ prāha harṣayan \|\| 25 \|\|*

‘This is indeed your son, you fair-browed woman. He was taken as a child from his nursery by Śambara, but Pradyumna slew that demon and now he has returned. 25

## \|\| 5.27.26 \|\|

*eṣa te tanayaḥ subhru hatvā śaṃbaramāgataḥ \|*  
*hṛto yenābhavadbālo bhavatyāḥsūtikāgṛhāt \|\| 26 \|\|*

This good woman is Māyāvatī, the wife of your own son, not Śambara. Listen while I explain: 26

## \|\| 5.27.27 \|\|

*iyaṃ māyāvatī bhāryā tanayasyāsya te satī \|*  
*śaṃbarasya na bhāryeyaṃ śrūyatāmatra kāraṇam \|\| 27 \|\|*

After Manmatha, the god of love, had been destroyed, the goddess of beauty, Rati, intent on seeing him reborn, took illusory form and enchanted Śambara. 27

## \|\| 5.27.28 \|\|

*manmathe tu gate nāśaṃ tadudbhavaparayaṇā \|*  
*śaṃbaraṃ mohayāmāsa māyārūpeṇa rūpiṇī \|\| 28 \|\|*

She appeared to the Daitya as a seductive phantom, her eyes as intoxicating as wine, and they enjoyed themselves in many ways together. 28

## \|\| 5.27.29 \|\|

*vihārādyupabhogeṣu rūpaṃ māyāmayaṃ śubham \|*  
*darśayāmāsa daityasya tasyeyaṃ madirekṣaṇā \|\| 29 \|\|*

The god of love has been reborn as your son Pradyumna, and Māyāvatī is his beloved Rati. Have no doubt that she’s your splendid daughter-in-law.’ 29

## \|\| 5.27.30 \|\|

*kāmo 'vatīrṇaḥ putraste tasyeyaṃ dayitā ratiḥ \|*  
*viśaṅkā nātra kartavyā snuṣeyaṃ tava śobhane \|\| 30 \|\|*

Both Rukmiṇī and Keśava were filled with joy, and all the city cried in jubilation. 30

## \|\| 5.27.31 \|\|

*tato harṣasamāviṣṭau rukmiṇī keśavautadā \|*  
*nagarī ca samastā sā sādhusādhvityabhāṣata \|\| 31 \|\|*

The denizens of Dvārakā were all amazed to see her reunited with her long-lost son. 31

## Chapter 28 - Pradyumna’s son Aniruddha; Balarāma slays Rukmin

## \|\| 5.28.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*cārudeṣṇaṃ sudeṣṇaṃ ca cārudehaṃ ca vīryavān \|*  
*suṣeṇaṃ cāruguptaṃ ca bhadracāruṃ tathā param \|\| 1 \|\|*

Cārudeṣṇa, Sudeṣṇa, brave Cārudeha, Suṣeṇa, Cārugupta, Bhadracāru, 1

## \|\| 5.28.2 \|\|

*cāruvindaṃ sucāruṃ ca cāruṃ cabalināṃ varam \|*  
*rukmiṇyajanayatputrānkanyāṃ cārumatīṃ tathā \|\| 2 \|\|*

Cāruvinda, Sucāru and Cāru, strongest of the strong, were the sons of Rukmiṇī. She also bore a daughter, Cārumatī. 2

## \|\| 5.28.3 \|\|

*anyāś ca bhāryāḥ kṛṣṇasya babhūvuḥ sapta śobhanāḥ \|*  
*kālidī mitravindā ca satyā cāgnajitī tathā \|\| 3 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa had seven other splendid wives: Kālindī, Mitravindā, Nagnajit’s daughter Satyā, 3

## \|\| 5.28.4 \|\|

*devī jāmbavatī cāpi rohiṇī kāmarūpiṇī \|*  
*madrarājasutā cānyā suśīlā śīlamaṇḍanā \|\| 4 \|\|*

Divine Jāmbavatī, shape-changing Rohiṇī, the king of Madra’s decorous daughter Suśīlā and Satrājita’s daughter Satyabhāmā. 4

## \|\| 5.28.5 \|\|

*satrājitī satyabhāmā lakṣmaṇā cāruhāsinī \|*  
*ṣoḍaśāsan sahasrāṇi strīṇāmanyāni cakriṇaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

The discus-wielder also wedded sweetly smiling Lakṣmaṇā and sixteen thousand other women. 5

## \|\| 5.28.6 \|\|

*pradyumna'pi mahāvīryo rukmiṇastanayāṃ śubhām \|*  
*svayaṃbare tāṃ jagrāha sā ca taṃ tanayaṃ hareḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Mighty Pradyumna fell for Rukmin’s lovely daughter at her svayaṃvara, the tournament for suitors, and she chose that son of Hari as her husband. 6

## \|\| 5.28.7 \|\|

*tasyāmasyābhaktaputro mahābalaparākramaḥ \|*  
*aniruddho raṇe ruddhavīryodadhirarindamaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

She and Pradyumna had a son named Aniruddha, brave and strong, a sea of courage when met in battle, a destroyer of his enemies. 7

## \|\| 5.28.8 \|\|

*tasyāpi rukmiṇaḥ pautrīṃ varayāmāsa keśavaḥ \|*  
*dauhitrāya dadau rakmī tāṃ spardhannapi cakriṇā \|\| 8 \|\|*

Keśava chose Rukmin’s granddaughter for his grandson Aniruddha, even though Rukmin advised against it. 8

## \|\| 5.28.9 \|\|

*tasyā vivāhe rāmādyā yādavā hariṇā saha \|*  
*kalyāṇārthaṃ tataḥsarve ye cānye bhūbhṛtas tathā \|*  
*rukmiṇī nagaraṃ jagmurnāmnā bhojakaṭaṃ dvija \|\| 9 \|\|*

On the occasion of their wedding, Hari, Balarāma and the other Yādavas came to Rukmin’s city, brahmin, Bhojakaṭa by name. 9

## \|\| 5.28.10 \|\|

*vivāhe tatra nirvṛtte pradyumnasya mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*kaliṅgarājapramukhā rukmiṇaṃ vākyamabruvan \|\| 10 \|\|*

After the marriage of Pradyumna’s powerful son, several monarchs led by the king of Kaliṅga gave this advice to Rukmin: 10

## \|\| 5.28.11 \|\|

*anakṣajño halī dyute tathāsya vyasanaṃ mahat \|*  
*tajjayāmo balaṃ kasmāddyutenainaṃ mahābalam \|\| 11 \|\|*

‘Plough-wielding Balarāma is a gambling addict but doesn’t have much skill. Why don’t we beat him in a game of dice, your majesty?’ 11

## \|\| 5.28.12 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tatheti tānāha nṛpānrukmī balamadānvitaḥ \|*  
*sabhāyāṃ saha rāmeṇa cakre dyūtaṃ ca vai tadā \|\| 12 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Mighty Rukmin agreed to this, and a match with Balarāma was held in the assembly hall. 12

## \|\| 5.28.13 \|\|

*sahasramekaṃ niṣkāṇāṃ rukmiṇā vijito balaḥ \|*  
*dvitīye 'pipaṇe cānyatsahasraṃ rukmiṇā jitaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

In the first round, Rukmin won a thousand pieces of gold from Balarāma and, in the second, another thousand. 13

## \|\| 5.28.14 \|\|

*tato daśasahasrāṇi niṣkāṇāṃ paṇamādade \|*  
*balabhadro 'jayattāni rukmī dyutavidāṃ varaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Balarāma then staked ten thousand pieces, but Rukmin, an expert gambler, won that as well. 14

## \|\| 5.28.15 \|\|

*tato jahāsa svanavatkaliṅgādhipatirdhija \|*  
*dantān vidarśayan mūḍho rukmī cāha madoddhataḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

The king of Kaliṅga laughed so loud that you could see his teeth, brahmin, and unwise Rukmin, puffed up with excitement, said, 15

## \|\| 5.28.16 \|\|

*avidyo 'yaṃ mayā dyūte balabhadraḥ parājitaḥ \|*  
*bhudhaivākṣāvalepāndho yo 'vamene 'kṣakovidān \|\| 16 \|\|*

‘I’ve beaten foolish Balarāma in this match. Blind with pride in gambling, he vainly insulted all the experts.’ 16

## \|\| 5.28.17 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā kaliṅgarājānaṃ prakāśadaśanānanam \|*  
*rikmiṇaṃ cāpi durvākyaṃ kopaṃ cakre halāyudhaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

When the wielder of the plough saw the monarch’s teeth and heard Rukmin’s slander, he was filled with rage. 17

## \|\| 5.28.18 \|\|

*tataḥ kopaparītātmā niṣkakoṭiṃ samādade \|*  
*glahaṃ jagrāha rukmī ca tadarthekṣānapātayat \|\| 18 \|\|*

Balarāma, mad with fury, then staked ten million pieces, and Rukmin rolled the dice. 18

## \|\| 5.28.19 \|\|

*ajayadbaladevastaṃ prāhoccair vijitaṃ mayā \|*  
*mayeti rukmī prāhoccair alīkokter alaṃ bala \|\| 19 \|\|*

Balarāma won the throw and called, ‘It’s mine!’, but Rukmin cried aloud, ‘No, it’s mine, Balarāma. Don’t lie! 19

## \|\| 5.28.20 \|\|

*tvayokto 'yaṃ glahaḥsatyaṃ na mayaiṣonumoditaḥ \|*  
*evaṃ tvayā cedvijitaṃ vijitaṃ na mayā katham \|\| 20 \|\|*

It’s true you staked the bet, but I did not accept it. If you won like this, then am I not a winner, too?’ 20

## \|\| 5.28.21 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*athāntarikṣe vāgucceḥ prāha gaṃbhīranādinī \|*  
*baladevasya taṃ kopaṃ vardhayantī mahātmanaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

Parāśara:

At that moment, a deep voice sounded in the heavens, further stirring mighty Balarāma’s wrath: 21

## \|\| 5.28.22 \|\|

*jitaṃ balena dharmeṇa rukmiṇā bhāṣitaṃ mṛṣā \|*  
*anuktvāpi vacaḥ kiñcit kṛta bhavati karmaṇā \|\| 22 \|\|*

‘Balarāma won this fairly. What Rukmin said is wrong. Even though he didn’t accept the challenge with his words, he accepted with his actions.’ 22

## \|\| 5.28.23 \|\|

*tato balaḥ samutthāya kopasaṃrakkalocanaḥ \|*  
*jaghānāṣṭapadenaiva rikmiṇaṃ sa mahābalaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

That mighty warrior Balarāma leapt up; his eyes were red with rage. He struck Rukmin with the game-board, killing him. 23

## \|\| 5.28.24 \|\|

*kaliṅgarājaṃ cādāya visphurantaṃ balādbalaḥ \|*  
*babhañja dantānkupito yaiḥ prakāśair jahāsa saḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Furious Balarāma then seized the trembling king of Kaliṅga and smashed the very teeth that he’d revealed before. 24

## \|\| 5.28.25 \|\|

*ākṛṣya ca mahāstaṃbhaṃ jātarūpamayaṃ balaḥ \|*  
*jaghāna tānye tatpakṣe bhūbhṛtaḥ kupito bhṛśam \|\| 25 \|\|*

He grabbed a massive golden pillar and slew the other royals who sided with the Kaliṅgan king. 25

## \|\| 5.28.26 \|\|

*tato hāhākṛtaṃ sarvaṃ palāyana paraṃ dvija \|*  
*tadrājamaṇḍalaṃ bhītaṃ babhūva kupite bale \|\| 26 \|\|*

All the monarchs cried aloud, brahmin, as they tried to flee from Balarāma’s fury. 26

## \|\| 5.28.27 \|\|

*balena nihataṃ dṛṣṭvā rukmiṇaṃ madhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*novāca kiñcinmaitreya rukmiṇī balayorbhayāt \|\| 27 \|\|*

When Madhu’s subduer heard that Balarāma had slain Rukmin, Maitreya, he said nothing as he feared both Rukmiṇī and his brother. 27

## \|\| 5.28.28 \|\|

*tatoniruddhamādāya kṛtadāraṃ dvijottama \|*  
*dvārakāmājagāmātha yaducakraṃ ca keśavaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Keśava then led newly married Aniruddha and the Yadu clansmen back to Dvārakā, best of brahmins. 28

## Chapter 29 - Kṛṣṇa slays the demon Naraka and retrieves Aditi’s earrings

## \|\| 5.29.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*dvāravatyāṃ sthite kṛṣṇe śakrastribhuvaneśvaraḥ \|*  
*ājagāmātha maitreya mattairāvatapṛṣṭhagaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Maitreya, in Dvārakā, mighty Indra, lord of threefold worlds, came to Kṛṣṇa, Śūra’s grandson, riding on his raging elephant Airāvata. 1

## \|\| 5.29.2 \|\|

*praviśya dvārakāṃ so 'tha sametya hariṇā tataḥ \|*  
*kathayāmāsa daityasya narakasya vicoṣṭitam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Entering the city, he met Hari and told him of the Daitya Naraka’s wicked deeds: 2

## \|\| 5.29.3 \|\|

*tvayānāthena devānāṃ manaṣyatvepi tiṣṭhatā \|*  
*praśamaṃ sarvaduḥkhāni nītāni madhusūdana \|\| 3 \|\|*

‘Subduer of Madhu and leader of the gods, even though you’ve appeared in human form, you bring all suffering to an end. 3

## \|\| 5.29.4 \|\|

*tapasvivyamanārthāya soriṣṭo dhenukas tathā \|*  
*pravṛtto yas tathā keśīte sarve nihatāstvayā \|\| 4 \|\|*

You slew Ariṣṭa, Dhenuka and Keśin and all those who tried to harm ascetics. 4

## \|\| 5.29.5 \|\|

*kaṃsaḥ kuvalayāpīḍaḥ pūtanā bālaghātinī \|*  
*nāśaṃ nītāstvayā sarve ye 'nye jagadupadravāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Kaṃsa, Kuvalayāpīḍa, child-killing Pūtanā and all the other blights on earth were led to their doom by you. 5

## \|\| 5.29.6 \|\|

*yuṣmaddordaḍasaṃbhūtiparitrāte jagattraye \|*  
*yajvayajñāṃśasaṃprāptyā tṛptiṃ yānti divaukasaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

When the threefold worlds are protected by your prowess and your wisdom, the denizens of heaven are nourished by the portions of the sacrifice received from worshippers. 6

## \|\| 5.29.7 \|\|

*so 'haṃ sāṃpratamāyāto yannimittaṃ janārdana \|*  
*tacchutvā tatpratīkāraprayatnaṃ kartumarhasi \|\| 7 \|\|*

When you’ve heard the reason for my present visit, Janārdana, you must try to help me. 7

## \|\| 5.29.8 \|\|

*bhaumo 'yaṃ narako nāma prāgjyotiṣapureśvaraḥ \|*  
*karoti sarvabhūtānām upaghātam arindama \|\| 8 \|\|*

The son of Earth named Naraka, lord of the city of Prāgjyotiṣa, is wreaking havoc on all creation, slayer of your enemies. 8

## \|\| 5.29.9 \|\|

*devasiddasurādīnāṃ nṛpāṇāṃ ca najārdana \|*  
*hṛtvā tu so 'suraḥ kanyā rurudhe nijamandire \|\| 9 \|\|*

This demon carried off the daughters of the gods and siddhas, demigods and kings and locked them in his palace. 9

## \|\| 5.29.10 \|\|

*chatraṃ yatsalilasrāvi tajjahāra pracetasaḥ \|*  
*madaṃrasya tathā śṛṅgaṃ hṛtavānmaṇiparvatam \|\| 10 \|\|*

He stole wise Varuṇa’s umbrella from which the waters flow, and carried off the gem-laden peak of Mount Mandara. 10

## \|\| 5.29.11 \|\|

*amṛtastrāviṇī divye manmātuḥ kṛṣṇakuñjale \|*  
*jahāra so 'suro dityā vāñchatyairāvataṃ gajam \|\| 11 \|\|*

That demon stole the heavenly earrings, source of nectar, from my mother, Aditi, Kṛṣṇa, and now he wants my elephant Airāvata. 11

## \|\| 5.29.12 \|\|

*durnītametadgovindamayā tasya niveditam \|*  
*yadatra pratikartavyaṃ tatsvayaṃ parimṛśyatām \|\| 12 \|\|*

Now I’ve told you of his mischief, Govinda, you must think of retribution.’ 12

## \|\| 5.29.13 \|\|

*śrīparāśasa uvāca*  
*itiśrutvā smitaṃ kṛtvā bhagavāndevakīsutaḥ \|*  
*gṛhītvā vāsavaṃ haste samuttasthau varāsanāt \|\| 13 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the lord, son of Devakī, smiled, took Indra by the hand and rose from his splendid throne. 13

## \|\| 5.29.14 \|\|

*saṃcintyāgatamāruhya garuḍaṃ gaganecaram \|*  
*satyabhāmāṃ samāropya yayau prāgjyotiṣaṃ puram \|\| 14 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa mounted sky-ranging Garuḍa, whom he summoned with just a thought, and, helping Satyabhāmā up, flew to the city of Prāgjyotiṣa. 14

## \|\| 5.29.15 \|\|

*āruhyaurāvataṃ nāgaṃ śakro 'pi tridivaṃ yayau \|*  
*tatau jagāma kṛṣṇaś ca paśyatāṃ dvārakaukasām \|\| 15 \|\|*

Mighty Indra then set out on Airāvata for the thirteen gods’ abode, Maitreya, while the residents of Dvārakā looked on. 15

## \|\| 5.29.16 \|\|

*prāgjyotiṣapurasyāpi samantācchatayojanam \|*  
*ācitā mauravaiḥ pāśaiḥ kṣurāntair bhūr dvijottama \|\| 16 \|\|*

The land around Prāgjyotiṣa for a hundred leagues was filled with razor-nooses laid by the demon Muru, best of brahmins. 16

## \|\| 5.29.17 \|\|

*tāñciccheda hariḥ pāśānkṣiptvā cakraṃ sudarśanam \|*  
*tato muraḥsamuttasthau taṃ jaghāna ca keśavaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

With one blow of his discus, Sudarśana, Hari cut them all. Muru then sprang forward, but Keśava killed him, too. 17

## \|\| 5.29.18 \|\|

*murasya tanayānsapta sahasrāṃstāṃstato hariḥ \|*  
*cakradhārāgninirdagdhāṃścakāra śalabhāniva \|\| 18 \|\|*

With his flaming weapon, Hari burned Muru’s seven thousand sons like so many moths. 18

## \|\| 5.29.19 \|\|

*hatvāsuraṃ hayagrīvaṃ tathā pañcajanaṃ dvija \|*  
*prāgjyotiṣapuraṃ dhīmāṃstvārāvānsamupādravat \|\| 19 \|\|*

After he had slain the demons Muru, Hayagrīva and Pañcajana, brahmin, wise Hari swiftly marched on Prāgjyotiṣa. 19

## \|\| 5.29.20 \|\|

*narakeṇāsya tatrābhūnmahāsainyena saṃyugam \|*  
*kṛṣṇasya yatra govindo yatra govindo jaghne daityānsahasraśaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

There he battled Naraka and his great army, and Govinda slew the Daityas by the thousands. 20

## \|\| 5.29.21 \|\|

*śastrāstravarṣaṃ muñcantaṃ taṃ bhaumaṃ narakaṃ balī \|*  
*kṣiptvā cakraṃ dvidhā cakre cakrī daiteyacakrahā \|\| 21 \|\|*

The Earth’s son Naraka sent forth showers of missiles and other weapons, but the mighty discus-wielder and destroyer of demon hordes cast Sudarśana at the Daitya, cutting him in two. 21

## \|\| 5.29.22 \|\|

*hate tu narake bhūmirgṛhītvāditikuṇḍale \|*  
*upatasthe jagannāthaṃ vākyaṃ cedamathābravīt \|\| 22 \|\|*

When Naraka had been slain, the Earth brought forth Aditi’s earrings. She approached the universal lord and said: 22

## \|\| 5.29.23 \|\|

*pṛthvyuvāca*  
*yadāhamuddhṛtā nātha tvayā sūkaramūrtinā \|*  
*tvatsparśasaṃbhavaḥ putrastadāyaṃ mayyajāyata \|\| 23 \|\|*

‘After you lifted me, Lord, in the form of a boar, I produced this son, arising from your touch. 23

## \|\| 5.29.24 \|\|

*so 'yaṃ tvayaiva datto me tvayaiba vinipātitaḥ \|*  
*gṛhāma kuṇḍale ceme pālayāsya ca saṃtatim \|\| 24 \|\|*

You presented him to me, but now you’ve killed him, so take these earrings and guard his children. 24

## \|\| 5.29.25 \|\|

*bhārāvataraṇārthāya mamaiva bhagavānimam \|*  
*aṃśena lokamāyātaḥ prasādasumukhaḥ prabho \|\| 25 \|\|*

Your aspect, Lord, beneficent and handsome, has come into this world to ease my burden. 25

## \|\| 5.29.26 \|\|

*tvaṃ kartā ca vikartā ca saṃhartā prabhavo 'pyayaḥ \|*  
*jagatāntvaṃ jagadrūpaḥstūyate 'cyuta kiṃ tava \|\| 26 \|\|*

You are creator, transformer and destroyer, the origin and dissolution of the world. You are the world’s own form. What’s praise to you, eternal Lord? 26

## \|\| 5.29.27 \|\|

*vyāptivyāpyaṃ kriyākartā kāryaṃ ca bhagavānyathā \|*  
*sarvabhūtātmabhūtasya stūyate tava kiṃ tathā \|\| 27 \|\|*

You, Lord, are pervader and pervaded; action, act and agent. You are at the heart of all creation. What, then, is praise to you? 27

## \|\| 5.29.28 \|\|

*paramātmā ca bhūtātmā tvamātmā cāpyayo bhavān \|*  
*yathātathā stutirnātha kimarthaṃ te pravartate \|\| 28 \|\|*

As you are the highest spirit, the very spirit, creation’s heart, eternal Lord, what, then, is praise to you? 28

## \|\| 5.29.29 \|\|

*prasīda sarvabhūtātmannarakeṇa tu yatkṛtam \|*  
*tatkṣamyatāmadoṣāya tvatsutastvannipātitaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

Be merciful, heart of all creation. Pardon Naraka for his deeds. He died, but may your son be free from sin.’ 29

## \|\| 5.29.30 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tatheti coktvā dharaṇīṃ bhagavān bhūtabhāvanaḥ \|*  
*ratnāni narakāvāsājjagrāha munisattama \|\| 30 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The lord, cause of creation, granted all the Earth’s requests, best of sages, and recovered various treasures from the realm of Naraka. 30

## \|\| 5.29.31 \|\|

*kanyāpure sa kanyānāṃ ṣoḍaḥśātulavikramaḥ \|*  
*śatādhikāni dadṛśe sahasrāṇi mahāmune \|\| 31 \|\|*

In the women’s quarters, Kṛṣṇa of unequalled prowess found sixteen thousand girls, great sage, and one hundred more. 31

## \|\| 5.29.32 \|\|

*caturdaṃṣṭrāngajāṃścāgryān ṣaṭsahasrāṃś ca dṛṣṭavān \|*  
*kāṃbhojānāṃ tathāśvānāṃ niyutānyekaviṃśatim \|\| 32 \|\|*

He also saw six thousand awesome four-tusked elephants and twenty-one million Kambojan steeds. 32

## \|\| 5.29.33 \|\|

*tāḥ kanyāstāṃs tathā nāgāṃstānaśvān dvārakāṃ purīm \|*  
*prāpayāmāsa govindaḥsadyo narakakiṅkaraiḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

Govinda sent the girls, the elephants and the horses to Dvārakā at once, along with Naraka’s servants. 33

## \|\| 5.29.34 \|\|

*dadṛśe vāruṇaṃ cchatraṃ tathaiva maṇiparvatam \|*  
*āropayāmāsa harirgaruḍepatageśvare \|\| 34 \|\|*

Hari also found the umbrella of Varuṇa and the jewelled peak of Mount Mandara and placed them on Garuḍa. 34

## \|\| 5.29.35 \|\|

*āruhya ca svayaṃ kṛṣṇaḥ satyabhāmāsahāyavān \|*  
*adityāḥ kuṇḍale dānuṃ jagāma tridaśālayam \|\| 35 \|\|*

Mounting the king of birds himself, Kṛṣṇa then flew with Satyabhāmā to the thirteen gods’ abode to return Aditi’s earrings. 35

## Chapter 30 - Aditi praises Kṛṣṇa

## \|\| 5.30.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*garuḍo vāruṇaṃ chatraṃ tathaiva maṇiparvatam \|*  
*sabhāryaṃ ca hṛṣīkeśaṃ līlayaiva vahanyayau \|\| 1 \|\|*

Garuḍa flew easily with Hṛṣīkeśa and his consort, the umbrella of Varuṇa and the jewelled mountain peak. 1

## \|\| 5.30.2 \|\|

*tataḥ śakhamupādhmāsītsvargadvāragato hariḥ \|*  
*upatasthus tathā devāḥsārghyahastā janarjanam \|\| 2 \|\|*

When Hari reached the gates of heaven, he blew his conch. The deities then welcomed him with bowls of water to wash his feet. 2

## \|\| 5.30.3 \|\|

*sa devair arcitaḥ kṛṣṇo devamāturniveśanam \|*  
*sitābhraśikharākāraṃ praviśya dadṛśe 'ditim \|\| 3 \|\|*

Honoured by the gods, Kṛṣṇa entered their mother’s residence, resembling the summit of a snowy cloud, and there beheld Aditi. 3

## \|\| 5.30.4 \|\|

*sa tāṃ praṇamya śakreṇa saha te kuṇḍalottame \|*  
*dadau narakanāśaṃ ca śaśaṃsāsyai janārdanaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

In company with mighty Indra, Janārdana bowed to her, handed her the splendid earrings and informed her of the death of Naraka. 4

## \|\| 5.30.5 \|\|

*tataḥ prītājaganmātā dhātāraṃ jagatāṃ harim \|*  
*tuṣṭavāditiravyagrā kṛtvā tatpravaṇaṃ manaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Aditi, mother of the world, was pleased with Hari, foundation of the universe, and, setting her heart on him, lauded him without distraction: 5

## \|\| 5.30.6 \|\|

*aditiruvāca*  
*namaste puṇḍarīkākṣa bhaktānāmabhayaṅkara \|*  
*sanātanātman sarvātman bhūtātman bhūtabhāvana \|\| 6 \|\|*

Aditi:

I bow to you, lotus-eyed protector of devotees, eternal, universal, essence of creation and origin of the world. 6

## \|\| 5.30.7 \|\|

*praṇetarmanaso buddherindriyāṇāṃ guṇātmaka \|*  
*triguṇātīta nirdvadva śuddhasattva hṛdi sthita \|\| 7 \|\|*

Author of the intellect, the mind and senses, you are the heart of all three qualities, yet lie beyond them. You are pure and free from all dualities, abiding in every heart. 7

## \|\| 5.30.8 \|\|

*sitadīrghādiniḥ śeṣakalpanāparivarjita \|*  
*janmādibhir asaṃspṛṣṭa svapnādiparivarjita \|\| 8 \|\|*

You are beyond all conceptions such as appearance and dimension, untouched by birth and other processes, free from sleep and other limitations. 8

## \|\| 5.30.9 \|\|

*saṃdhyārātriraho bhūmirgaganaṃ vāyuraṃbu ca \|*  
*hutāśano mano buddhirbhūtādistvaṃ tathācyuta \|\| 9 \|\|*

You are twilight, day and night, earth and sky, wind and water, fire and mind, intellect and all the rest, Acyuta. 9

## \|\| 5.30.10 \|\|

*sargasthitivināśānāṃ kartā kartṛpatirbhavān \|*  
*brahmaviṣṇuśivākhyābhir ātmamūrtibhir īśvara \|\| 10 \|\|*

You are the agent of creation, preservation and destruction, and the master of creators, in three forms known as Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva, Lord. 10

## \|\| 5.30.11 \|\|

*devā daityās tathā yakṣā rākṣasāḥ siddhapannagāḥ \|*  
*kūṣmāṇḍāś ca pisācāś ca gandharvā manujās tathā \|\| 11 \|\|*

The deities, yakṣas, Daityas, rākṣasas, siddhas, nāgas, kūṣmāṇḍas, piśācas, gandharvas and humans, 11

## \|\| 5.30.12 \|\|

*paśavaś ca mṛgāścaiva pataṅgāś ca sarīsṛpāḥ \|*  
*vṛkṣagulmalatābahvyaḥsamastāstṛṇajātayaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Wild and domestic animals, birds, insects, snakes, trees, shrubs, vines, creepers and every kind of herb— 12

## \|\| 5.30.13 \|\|

*sthūlā madhyās tathā sūkṣmāḥsūkṣmātsūkṣmatarāś ca ye \|*  
*dehabhedā bhavān sarve ye kecitpurgalāśrayāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

You are every individual entity, large, medium and small, and smaller than the smallest, as well as everything that takes a concrete form. 13

## \|\| 5.30.14 \|\|

*māyā taveyamajñātaparamārthātimohinī \|*  
*anātmanyātmavijñānaṃ yayā mūḍho niruddhyate \|\| 14 \|\|*

This, your illusion, beguiles all those who do not know your true condition. The deluded person is restrained by this, misplacing recognition of the spirit on to something that it’s not. 14

## \|\| 5.30.15 \|\|

*asve svamiti bhāvotra yatpuṃsāmupajāyate \|*  
*ahaṃ mameti bhāvo yatprāyeṇaivābhijāyate \|*  
*saṃsāramātur māyāyās tavaitannātha ceṣṭitam \|\| 15 \|\|*

Conceptions such as ‘I’ and ‘mine’ that arise in folk from this illusion, Lord, which is itself the mother of the world, are all your doing. 15

## \|\| 5.30.16 \|\|

*yaiḥ svadharmaparair nātha narair ārādhito bhavān \|*  
*te tarantyakhilāmetāṃ māyāmātmavimuktaye \|\| 16 \|\|*

Those men intent on their own duties while praising you, Lord, transcend all this illusion and liberate themselves. 16

## \|\| 5.30.17 \|\|

*brahmādyāḥsakalā devā manuṣyāḥ paśavas tathā \|*  
*viṣṇumāyā mahāvartamohāndhatamasāvṛtāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Brahmā and the other gods, humankind and animals are all enveloped by this darkness and blinded by this error in the whirlpool of Vaiṣṇava illusion. 17

## \|\| 5.30.18 \|\|

*ārādhya tvāmabhīpsaṃte kāmānātmabhavakṣayam \|*  
*yadete puruṣā māyā saiveyaṃ bhagavaṃstava \|\| 18 \|\|*

Having worshipped you, some desire to fulfil their wishes and bring their own existence to an end—this is simply your illusion, Lord. 18

## \|\| 5.30.19 \|\|

*mayā tvaṃ putrakāminyā vairipakṣajayāya ca \|*  
*ārādhito na mokṣāya māyāvilasitaṃ hi tat \|\| 19 \|\|*

Others worship you as they want a son or desire to conquer enemies, but not for liberation—this, too, is just the play of your illusion. 19

## \|\| 5.30.20 \|\|

*kaupīnācchādanaprāyā vāñchā kalpadrumādapi \|*  
*jāyate yadapuṇyānāṃ soparādhaḥ svadoṣajaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

Like asking for a humble loincloth from a wish-fulfilling tree, to do so is an error made by those who have no merit; it arises from their own deficiencies. 20

## \|\| 5.30.21 \|\|

*tatprasīdākhilajaganmāyāmohakarāvyaya \|*  
*ajñānaṃ jñānasadbhāvabhūtaṃ bhūteśa nāśaya \|\| 21 \|\|*

Be merciful, therefore, eternal cause of this illusion that seduces all the world, Lord of creation, and destroy that ignorance masquerading as true knowledge. 21

## \|\| 5.30.22 \|\|

*namaste cakrahastāya śārṅgahastāya te namaḥ \|*  
*nandahastāya te viṣṇo śaṅkhahastāya te namaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

I bow to you, wielder of the discus. I bow to you, bearer of Śārṅga. I bow to you, Viṣṇu, holding mace and conch. 22

## \|\| 5.30.23 \|\|

*etatpaśyāmi te rūpaṃ sthūlacihnopalakṣitam \|*  
*na jānāmi paraṃ yatte prasīda parameśvara \|\| 23 \|\|*

I see your form that’s marked with signs, but your highest form still lies beyond my comprehension. Show us your mercy, highest Lord. 23

## \|\| 5.30.24 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*adityaivaṃ stuto viṣṇuḥ prahasyāha surāraṇim \|*  
*mātā devi tvamasmākaṃ prasīda varadā bhava \|\| 24 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus praised by the deities’ mother, Aditi, Viṣṇu laughed and said, ‘Mother goddess, may you be merciful to us and grant a boon.’ 24

## \|\| 5.30.25 \|\|

*adidiruvāca*  
*evamastu yathecchā te tvamaśeṣaiḥ surāsuraiḥ \|*  
*ajeyaḥ puruṣavyāghra sartyaloke bhaviṣyasi \|\| 25 \|\|*

Aditi:

So be it. As you wish. You will never be defeated by god or demon in the mortal world, you tiger of a man. 25

## \|\| 5.30.26 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tataḥ kṛṣṇasya patnī ca śakreṇa sahitāditim \|*  
*satyabhāmā praṇamyāha prasīdeti punaḥ punaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Satyabhāmā, with Śacī, wife of mighty Indra, at her side, bowed at once to Aditi and asked repeatedly for her blessing. 26

## \|\| 5.30.27 \|\|

*aditiruvāca*  
*matpa3sādānna te subhru jarā vairūpyameva vā \|*  
*bhaviṣyatyanavadyāṅgi susthiraṃ navayauvanam \|\| 27 \|\|*

Aditi:

By my mercy, fair-browed woman, you’ll be never old or ugly, always retaining your flawless form. 27

## \|\| 5.30.28 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*adityā tu kṛtānujño devarājo janārdanam \|*  
*yathāvatpūjayāmāsa bahumānapuraḥsaram \|\| 28 \|\|*

Parāśara:

With the permission of Aditi, Indra, king of gods, first honoured Janārdana, then duly praised him. 28

## \|\| 5.30.29 \|\|

*śacī ca satyabhāmāyai pārijātasya puṣpakam \|*  
*na dadau mānuṣīṃ matvā svayaṃ puṣpair alaṅkṛtā \|\| 29 \|\|*

Then Kṛṣṇa, accompanied by Satyabhāmā, visited the other lovely pleasure gardens of Nandana and the deities, best of sages. 29

## \|\| 5.30.30 \|\|

*tato dadarśa kṛṣṇo 'pi satyabhāmāsahāyavān \|*  
*davodyānāni hṛdyāni nandanādīni sattama \|\| 30 \|\|*

There, Keśava, universal lord and Keśin’s slayer, beheld the Pārijāta tree, richly scented, bearing flowery masses, bestowing pleasant coolness and resplendent with reddish sprouts. 30

## \|\| 5.30.31 \|\|

*dadarśa ca sugandhāḍhyaṃ mañjarīpuñjadhāriṇam \|*  
*nityāhlādakaraṃ tāmrabālapallavaśobhitam \|\| 31 \|\|*

The Pārijāta, which first appeared when the ocean was churned for nectar, had bark that shone like gold. 31

## \|\| 5.30.32 \|\|

*mathyamāne 'mṛte jātaṃ jātarūpopamatvacam \|*  
*pārijātaṃ jagannāthaḥ keśavaḥ keśisūdanaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

When Satyabhāmā saw it, best of brahmins, she said to Govinda, ‘Kṛṣṇa, why don’t we take it back to Dvārakā? 32

## \|\| 5.30.33 \|\|

*tutoṣa paramaprītyā tarurājamanuttamam \|*  
*taṃ dṛṣṭvā prāha govindaṃ satyabhāmā dvijottama \|*  
*kasmānna dvārakāmeṣa nīyate kṛṣṇa pādapaḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

If what you say to me is true and you really love me, let’s take it for the pleasure grove at my residence. 33

## \|\| 5.30.34 \|\|

*yadi cettvadvacaḥ satyaṃ tvamatyarthaṃ priyeti me \|*  
*madgehaniṣkuṭārthāya tadayaṃ nīyatāṃ taruḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa, you’ve often said with fondness, “Neither Jāmbavatī nor Rukmiṇī are as dear to me as you are, Satyā.” 34

## \|\| 5.30.35 \|\|

*na me jāṃbavatī tādṛgabhīṣṭā na ca rukmiṇī \|*  
*satye yathā tvamityuktaṃ tvayā kṛṣṇāsakṛtpriyam \|\| 35 \|\|*

If that’s true, Govinda, not mere flattery on your part, then this Pārijāta tree should become a feature in my residence. 35

## \|\| 5.30.36 \|\|

*satyaṃ tadyadi govinda nopacārakṛtaṃ mama \|*  
*tadastu pārijāto 'yaṃ mama gehavibhūṣaṇam \|\| 36 \|\|*

With its flowers in my hair, I want to shine amid the other queens.’ 36

## \|\| 5.30.37 \|\|

*bibhratī pārijātasya keśapakṣeṇa mañjarīm \|*  
*sapatnīnāmahaṃ madhye śobheyamiti kāmaye \|\| 37 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, Hari smiled at Satyabhāmā and put the Pārijāta on Garuḍa’s back. But the keepers of the grove admonished him: 37

## \|\| 5.30.38 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktaḥsa prahasyaināṃ pārijātaṃ garutmati \|*  
*āropayāmāsa haristamūcurvanarakṣiṇaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

‘Excuse me, sir, but that belongs to Śacī, consort of the king of gods. You can’t just take the Pārijāta tree, Govinda. 38

## \|\| 5.30.39 \|\|

*bho śacī devarājasya mahiṣī tatparīgraham \|*  
*pārijātaṃ na govinda hartumarhasi pādapam \|\| 39 \|\|*

Brought forth for decorative flowers for Śacī when the gods first churned the ocean, it cannot be removed! 39

## \|\| 5.30.40 \|\|

*utpanno devarājāya dattaḥso 'pi dadau punaḥ \|*  
*mahiṣyai sumahābhāga devyai śacyai kurūhalāt \|\| 40 \|\|*

It belongs to Śacī, whose face the king of gods delights to see. It’s folly to desire it. Who could ever take it? 40

## \|\| 5.30.41 \|\|

*śacīvibhūṣaṇārthāya devair amṛtamanthane \|*  
*utpādito 'yaṃ na kṣemī gṛhītvainaṃ gamiṣyasi \|\| 41 \|\|*

The king of gods will surely want revenge for this, Kṛṣṇa, and the other gods will side with mighty Indra with his thunderbolt in hand. 41

## \|\| 5.30.42 \|\|

*devarājo mukhaprekṣī yasyāstasyāḥ parigraham \|*  
*maiḍhyātprārthayase kṣemī gṛhītvainaṃ hi ko vrajet \|\| 42 \|\|*

Don’t provoke these deities, Acyuta. The wise do not commend an act that leads to bitterness.’ 42

## \|\| 5.30.43 \|\|

*avaśyamasya devendro niṣkṛtiṃ kṛṣṇa yāsyati \|*  
*vajrodyatakaraṃ śakramanuyāsyanti cāmarāḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After they had spoken, furious Satyabhāmā then replied, ‘What does Śacī have to do with the Pārijāta tree? And who’s this Indra, king of gods? 43

## \|\| 5.30.44 \|\|

*tadalaṃ sakalair devair vigraheṇa tavācyuta \|*  
*vipākakaṭu yatkarma tanna śaṃsaṃti paṇḍitāḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

The tree was brought forth at the churning of the nectar as the property in common for all the worlds, you gods, so why should Indra keep it for himself? 44

## \|\| 5.30.45 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityukte tair uvācaitān satyabhāmātikopinī \|*  
*kā śacī pārijātasya ko vā śakraḥsurādhipaḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

Like the nectar, moon and Śrī, you keepers of the grove, this tree belongs to all the peoples of the world. 45

## \|\| 5.30.46 \|\|

*sāmānyaḥsarvalokasya yadyeṣo 'mṛtamanthane \|*  
*samutpannastaruḥ kasmādoko gṛhṇāti vāsavaḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

Śacī’s very proud of her husband’s mighty arms, and so she wants to keep it. Tell her she need no longer look for it, as Satyā’s had it taken somewhere else. 46

## \|\| 5.30.47 \|\|

*yathā surā yathaivenduryathā śrīrvanarakṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*sāmānyaḥsarvalokasya pārijātas tathā drumaḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

Go at once and give this weighty message to Puloman’s daughter, and say to her that Satyabhāmā sent it: 47

## \|\| 5.30.48 \|\|

*bhartṛ bahumahāgarvādruṇaddhyenamatho śacī \|*  
*tatkathyatāmalaṃ kṣāntyā satvā hārayati drumam \|\| 48 \|\|*

“If your husband loves you and obeys you, then let him try to stop my husband carrying the tree away. 48

## \|\| 5.30.49 \|\|

*kathyatāṃ ca drutaṃ gatvā paulomyā vacanaṃ mama \|*  
*satyabhāmā vadatyetaditigarvoddhatākṣaram \|\| 49 \|\|*

I know your husband, Indra, and I know he’s lord of thirteen gods. All the same, I, a mortal woman, am taking this Pārijāta away from you.”’ 49

## \|\| 5.30.50 \|\|

*yadi tvaṃ dayitā bharturyadi vaśyaḥ patistava \|*  
*madbharturharato vṛkṣaṃ tatkāraya nivāraṇam \|\| 50 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the keepers went to Śacī and delivered Satyā’s message. Śacī spurred into action her husband, that lord of thirteen deities. 50

## \|\| 5.30.51 \|\|

*jānāmi te patiṃ śakraṃ jānāmi tridaśeśvaram \|*  
*pārijātaṃ tathāpyenaṃ mānuṣī hārayāmi te \|\| 51 \|\|*

Accordingly, he set out to battle Hari for the tree, brahmin, flanked by armies of all the gods. 51

## \|\| 5.30.52 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktā rakṣiṇo gatvā śacyāḥ procuryathoditam \|*  
*śrutvā cotsāhayāmāsa śacī cakraṃ surādhipam \|\| 52 \|\|*

The thirteen deities, armed with finest weapons, such as bludgeons, swords, clubs and spears, prepared themselves, while mighty Indra stood with thunderbolt in hand. 52

## \|\| 5.30.53 \|\|

*tataḥsamastadevānāṃ sainyaiḥ parivṛto harim \|*  
*prayayau pārijātārthamindroyoddhuṃ dvijottama \|\| 53 \|\|*

When Govinda saw Indra mounted on the king of elephants and advancing with his retinue of deities to do battle, 53

## \|\| 5.30.54 \|\|

*tataḥ parighanistriṃśagadāśūlavarāyudhāḥ \|*  
*babhūvustridasāḥsajjāḥ śakre vajrakare sthite \|\| 54 \|\|*

He blew his conch, filling all directions with its sound, and loosed a flight of arrows numbering tens of millions. 54

## \|\| 5.30.55 \|\|

*tato nirikṣya govindo nāgarājopari sthitam \|*  
*śakraṃ devaparivāraṃ yuddhāya samupasthitam \|\| 55 \|\|*

Seeing each direction and the sky itself beset with arrows by the hundreds, all thirteen gods unleashed their varied weaponry and missiles. 55

## \|\| 5.30.56 \|\|

*cakāra śaṅkhanirghoṣaṃ diśaḥ śabdena pūrayan \|*  
*mumoca śarasaṃghātān sahasrāyutaśaḥ śitān \|\| 56 \|\|*

But Madhu’s subduer, lord of worlds, easily reduced to a thousand fragments each weapon and each missile the deities released. 56

## \|\| 5.30.57 \|\|

*tato diśo nabhaścaiva dṛṣṭvā śaraśataiścitam \|*  
*mumucustridaśāḥsarve hyastraśastrāmyanekaśaḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

Garuḍa, serpents’ devourer, dragged aside the noose that Varuṇa, king of oceans, cast, and, like the body of a tender snake, ripped it with his bill. 57

## \|\| 5.30.58 \|\|

*ekaikamastraṃ śastraṃ ca daivair muktaṃ sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*ciccheda līlayaiveśo jagatāṃ madhusūdanaḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

With a blow of his mace, Lord Kṛṣṇa, son of Devakī, broke Yama’s staff and knocked it to the ground. 58

## \|\| 5.30.59 \|\|

*pāśaṃ salilarājasya samākṛṣyīragātpunaḥ \|*  
*cakāra khaṇḍaśaścañcvā bālapannagadehavat \|\| 59 \|\|*

Next, the lord, Śūra’s scion, shattered Kubera’s weapon, Śibikā, with his discus and, with a glance of just one eye, robbed the Sun of brilliance. 59

## \|\| 5.30.60 \|\|

*yamena prahitaṃ daṇḍaṃ gadāvikṣepakhaṇḍitam \|*  
*pṛthivyāṃ pātayāmāsa bhagavān devakīsutaḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

He cut Agni, god of fire, into hundreds with his arrows, and caused the Vasus to flee in all directions. He felled the Rudras to the ground, their lance blades shattered by his discus. 60

## \|\| 5.30.61 \|\|

*śibikāṃ ca dhaneśasya cakreṇa tilaśo vibhuḥ \|*  
*cakāra śaurirarkaṃ ca dṛṣṛdṛṣṭahataujasam \|\| 61 \|\|*

With arrows loosed from his bow, Śārṅga, he scattered the Sādhyas, Viśvas, Maruts and gandharvas in the air, like fluff that blows from cottonwood trees. 61

## \|\| 5.30.62 \|\|

*nīto 'gniḥ śītatāṃ bāṇair drāvitā vasavo diśaḥ \|*  
*cakravicchinnaśulāgrā rudrā bhuvi nipātitāḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

Garuḍa tore the deities with his bill, beat them with his wings and ripped them with his blade-like talons. 62

## \|\| 5.30.63 \|\|

*sādhyā viśve 'tha maruto gandharvāścaiva sā yakaiḥ \|*  
*śārṅgiṇā preritair astā vyomni śālmalitūlavat \|\| 63 \|\|*

The king of gods and Madhu’s slayer showered arrows on each other by the thousand, like a pair of rain clouds shedding torrents. 63

## \|\| 5.30.64 \|\|

*garutmānapi tuṇḍena pakṣābhyāṃ ca nakhāṅkuraiḥ \|*  
*bhakṣayaṃstāḍayan devān dārayaṃś ca cacāra vai \|\| 64 \|\|*

Garuḍa took on Airāvata in that battle, and mighty Indra, with all the gods, engaged Janārdana. 64

## \|\| 5.30.65 \|\|

*tataḥ śatasahasreṇa devendramadhusūdanau \|*  
*parasparaṃ vavarṣāte dhārābhir iva toyadau \|\| 65 \|\|*

When all their arrows, missiles and other weapons had been cut down, the Vasus’ leader, Indra, swiftly seized his thunderbolt as Kṛṣṇa raised his discus. 65

## \|\| 5.30.66 \|\|

*airāvatena garuḍo yuyudhe tatra saṃkule \|*  
*devaiḥ samastair yuyudhe śakreṇa ca janārdanaḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

Seeing the king of gods and Janārdana with their weapons, best of brahmins, the threefold worlds cried out in anguish. 66

## \|\| 5.30.67 \|\|

*bhinneṣvaśeṣabāṇeṣu śastreṣvastreṣu ca tvaran \|*  
*jagrāha vāsavo vajraṃ kṛṣṇaścakraṃ sudarśanam \|\| 67 \|\|*

But Lord Hari caught the thunderbolt hurled by Indra and, holding back his discus, called on Indra to desist. 67

## \|\| 5.30.68 \|\|

*tato hāhākṛtaṃ sarvaṃ trailokyaṃ dvijasattama \|*  
*vajracakrakarau dṛṣṭvā devārājajanārdanau \|\| 68 \|\|*

Now the king of gods had lost his weapon, his elephant was wounded by Garuḍa and he was on the point of fleeing, Satyabhāmā said to Indra, that heroic god: 68

## \|\| 5.30.69 \|\|

*kṣiptaṃ vajramathendreṇa jagrāha bhagavānhariḥ \|*  
*na mumoca tadā cakraṃ śakraṃ tiṣṭheti cābravīt \|\| 69 \|\|*

‘It ill befits you, lord of all three worlds and Śacī’s husband, to run away. May Śacī approach you with a wreath of Pārijāta flowers. 69

## \|\| 5.30.70 \|\|

*praṇaṣṭavajraṃ devendraṃ garuḍakṣatavāhanam \|*  
*satyabhāmābravīdvīraṃ palāyanaparāyaṇam \|\| 70 \|\|*

What’s the good of your heavenly kingdom if you can’t see Śacī with such a garland, and if she doesn’t love you as before? 70

## \|\| 5.30.71 \|\|

*trailokyeśa na te yuktaṃ śacībhartuḥ palāyanam \|*  
*pārijātasragābhogā tvāmupasthāsyate śacī \|\| 71 \|\|*

Let’s stop this battle, Indra. Don’t be ashamed. Keep this Pārijāta tree and tell the deities not to worry. 71

## \|\| 5.30.72 \|\|

*kīdṛśaṃ devarājyaṃ te pārijātisragujjvalām \|*  
*apakyeto yathāpūrvaṃ graṇayābhyāgatāṃ śacīm \|\| 72 \|\|*

Śacī’s proud of her husband and didn’t show me courtesy or look on me hospitably when I came to visit her. 72

## \|\| 5.30.73 \|\|

*alaṃ śakra prayāsena na vrīḍāṃ gantumarhasi \|*  
*nīyatāṃ pārijātoyaṃ devāḥsaṃtu gatavyathāḥ \|\| 73 \|\|*

As a woman, I’m no deep thinker. My husband’s reputation is my only care. That’s why, Indra, I began this war with you. 73

## \|\| 5.30.74 \|\|

*parigarvāvalepena bahumānapuraḥsaram \|*  
*na dadarśa gṛhaṃ yātāmupacāreṇa māṃ śacī \|\| 74 \|\|*

I’ve had enough of this Pārijāta. It’s someone else’s property, yet we tried to take it. Śacī’s proud of her good looks, and what woman isn’t proud of her own husband?’ 74

## \|\| 5.30.75 \|\|

*strītvādagurucittāhaṃ svabhartṛślāghanāparā \|*  
*tataḥ kṛtavatī śakra bhavatā saha vigraham \|\| 75 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the king of gods turned and said, ‘Enough of these upsetting words, my ardent friend. 75

## \|\| 5.30.76 \|\|

*tadala pārījātena parasvena hṛtena me \|*  
*rūpeṇa garvitā sā tu bhartrā kā strī na garvitā \|\| 76 \|\|*

I feel no shame in being beaten by the god of universal form, the agent of creation, destruction and preservation of all the world, 76

## \|\| 5.30.77 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityukto vai nivavṛte devarājastayā dvija \|*  
*prāha caināmalaṃ caṇḍi sakhyuḥ khe doktivistaraiḥ \|\| 77 \|\|*

In whom this universe abides, who has no middle or beginning, from whom the world arises, the universal being who’ll bring it to an end. How can one who is defeated by the cause of the origin, dissolution and preservation of the world feel shame, good woman? 77

## \|\| 5.30.78 \|\|

*indra uvāca*  
*na cāpi sargasaṃhārasthitikartākhilasya yaḥ \|*  
*jitasya tena me vrīḍā jāyate viśvarūpiṇā \|\| 78 \|\|*

He takes the form of the origin of the world, as subtle as an atom, which only those who understand all things can understand, and no-one else. Who could defeat the birthless, uncreated, eternal lord, who appears in mortal form of his own accord to benefit the world?’ 78

## Chapter 31 - Kṛṣṇa plants the Pārijāta tree in Dvārakā

## \|\| 5.31.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*saṃstuto bhagavānitthaṃ devarājena keśavaḥ \|*  
*prahasya bhāvagaṃbhīramuvācendraṃ dvijottama \|\| 1 \|\|*

Lord Keśava, thus praised by Indra, best of brahmins, smiled and made this profound reply: 1

## \|\| 5.31.2 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca*  
*devarājo bhavānindro vayaṃ martyā jagatpate \|*  
*kṣantavyaṃ bhavataivedam aparādhaṃ kṛtaṃ mama \|\| 2 \|\|*

The Lord:

You are Indra, king of gods, and we’re mere mortals, universal lord. Forgive us our transgression. 2

## \|\| 5.31.3 \|\|

*pārijātataruścāyaṃ nīyatāmucitāspadam \|*  
*gṛhītoyaṃ mayā śakra satyāvacanakāraṇāt \|\| 3 \|\|*

The Pārijāta should be returned to its proper place. I only took it, mighty Indra, because Satyā told me to. 3

## \|\| 5.31.4 \|\|

*vajraṃ cedaṃ gṛhāma tvaṃ yadatra prahitaṃ tvayā \|*  
*tabaivaitatpraharaṇaṃ śakra vairividāraṇam \|\| 4 \|\|*

Here’s the thunderbolt I took when you hurled it at me. Please accept your rival-slaying weapon, mighty god. 4

## \|\| 5.31.5 \|\|

*indra uvāca*  
*vimohayasi māmīśa martyo 'hamiti kiṃ vadan \|*  
*jānīmastvāṃ bhagavato na tu sūkṣmavido vayam \|\| 5 \|\|*

Indra:

Why do you deceive me, Lord, claiming to be mortal? We’re fortunate that we recognise you, even though we cannot grasp your subtlety. 5

## \|\| 5.31.6 \|\|

*yo 'si so 'si jagattraṇapravṛttau nātha saṃsthitaḥ \|*  
*jagataḥ śalyaniṣkarṣaṃ karoṣyamurasūdana \|\| 6 \|\|*

You are who you are, Lord, bent on the task of universal liberation. You, demon slayer, remove all thorns from the flesh of the world. 6

## \|\| 5.31.7 \|\|

*nīyatāṃ pārījāto 'yaṃ kṛṣṇa dvāravatīṃpurīm \|*  
*martyaloke tvayā tyakte nāyaṃ saṃsthāsyate bhuvi \|\| 7 \|\|*

Take this Pārijāta to the city of Dvārakā, Kṛṣṇa, where it should remain till you leave the mortal realm. 7

## \|\| 5.31.8 \|\|

*devadeva jagannātha kṛṣṇa viṣṇo mahābhuja \|*  
*śaṅkhacakragadāpāṇe kṣamasvaitad vyatikramam \|\| 8 \|\|*

Parāśara:

‘So be it,’ said Hari to the king of gods. He then returned to earth, while attendant siddhas, gandharvas and sages sang his praises. 8

## \|\| 5.31.9 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tathetyuktvā ca devendramājagāma bhuvaṃ hariḥ \|*  
*prasaktaiḥ siddhagandharvaiḥ stūyamānaḥ surarṣibhiḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Arriving over Dvārakā, Kṛṣṇa blew his conch, brahmin, delighting the city’s residents. 9

## \|\| 5.31.10 \|\|

*tataḥ śaṅkhamupādhmāya dvārakopari saṃsthitaḥ \|*  
*harṣamutpādayāmāsa dvārakāvāsināṃ dvaja \|\| 10 \|\|*

He alighted from Garuḍa and, with Satyabhāmā’s help, planted the mighty Pārijāta in her pleasure garden. 10

## \|\| 5.31.11 \|\|

*avatīryātha garuḍātsatyabhāmā sahāyavān \|*  
*niṣkuṭe sthāpayāmāsa pārijātaṃ mahātarum \|\| 11 \|\|*

All who approached that tree recalled their previous existences, and the scent of its flowers perfumed the world for three leagues all around. 11

## \|\| 5.31.12 \|\|

*yam abhyetya janaḥ sarvo jātiṃ smarati paurvikīm \|*  
*vāsyate yasya puṣpotthagandhenorvī triyojanam \|\| 12 \|\|*

Then all the Yādavas, showing their faces to the tree, beheld the original heavenly forms that each of them embodied. 12

## \|\| 5.31.13 \|\|

*tataste yādavāḥsarve dehavandhānamānuṣān \|*  
*dadṛśuḥ pādape tasmin kurvanto makhadarśanam \|\| 13 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa then took possession of the booty that he’d seized from Naraka: elephants, horses and other valuables, as well as women, all carried thither by the demon’s servants. 13

## \|\| 5.31.14 \|\|

*kiṅkaraiḥ samupānītaṃ hastyaśvādi tato dhanam \|*  
*vibhajya pradadau kṛṣṇo bāndhavānāṃ mahāmatiḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

At an auspicious season, Janārdana married all the girls whom Naraka had abducted from elsewhere. 14

## \|\| 5.31.15 \|\|

*kanyāś ca kṛṣṇe jagrāha narakasya parigrahān \|\| 15 \|\|*

At one and the same moment, Govinda duly took the hand of every girl in marriage with proper rites, great sage, each in her own residence. 15

## \|\| 5.31.16 \|\|

*tataḥ kāle śubhe prāpte upayeme janārdanaḥ \|*  
*tāḥ kanyā narakeṇāsansarvato yāḥsamāhṛtāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

The lord then manifested one form of himself for each of the sixteen thousand and one hundred women. 16

## \|\| 5.31.17 \|\|

*ekasminneva govindaḥ kāle tāsāṃ mahāmune \|*  
*jagrāha vidhivatvāṇīnpṛthaggeheṣu dharmataḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Every girl believed that she alone had married Madhu’s subduer. 17

## \|\| 5.31.18 \|\|

*ṣoḍaśastrīsahasrāṇi śatamekaṃ tatodhikam \|*  
*tāvanti cakrerūpāṇi bhagavān madhusūdanaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Keśava, creator of the world, spent the nights in all of their abodes, brahmin, as he may take on many forms at will. 18

## Chapter 32 - Uṣā dreams of Aniruddha

## \|\| 5.32.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*pradyumnādyā hareḥ putrā rukmiṇyāṃ kvathitāstava \|*  
*bhānubhaumerikādyaṃś ca satyabhāmā vyajāyata \|\| 1 \|\|*

I’ve mentioned Pradyumna and the other sons of Rukmiṇī and Hari. Satyabhāmā bore Bhānu and Bhaimarika. 1

## \|\| 5.32.2 \|\|

*dīptimattāmrapakṣādya rohiṇyāṃ kathitā hareḥ \|*  
*babhūvurjāmbavatyāṃ ca sāṃbādyā bāhuśālinaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Rohiṇī’s sons with Hari included Dīptimanta and Prayakṣa. Jāmbavatī had Sāmba and others who relied on their own strong arms. 2

## \|\| 5.32.3 \|\|

*tanayā bhadravindādyā nāgnajityāṃ mahābalāḥ \|*  
*saṃgrāmajitpradhānāstu śaibyāyāṃ ca hareḥsutāḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Nāgnajitī’s mighty sons included Bhadravinda. Chief among Śaibyā’s sons was Saṃgrāmajit. 3

## \|\| 5.32.4 \|\|

*vṛkādyāś ca sutā mādryāṃ gātravatpramukhān sutān \|*  
*avāpa lakṣmāṇā putrānkālindyāś ca śrutādayaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Mādrī had Vṛka and other sons. First among the sons of Lakṣmaṇā was Gātravant. Kālindī had Śruta and others. 4

## \|\| 5.32.5 \|\|

*anyāsāṃ caiva bhāryāṇāṃ samutpannāni cakriṇaḥ \|*  
*aṣṭāyutāni putrāṇāṃ sahasrāṇī śataṃ tathā \|\| 5 \|\|*

The bearer of the discus had one hundred and eighty thousand sons with his other wives. 5

## \|\| 5.32.6 \|\|

*pradyumnaḥ prathamasteṣāṃ sarveṣāṃ rukmiṇīsutaḥ \|*  
*pradyumnādaniruddho 'bhūdvajrastasmādajāyata \|\| 6 \|\|*

Rukmiṇī’s son Pradyumna was the eldest of them all. His son was Aniruddha. His son was Vajra. 6

## \|\| 5.32.7 \|\|

*aniruddho raṇe 'ruddho baleḥ pautrīṃ mahābalaḥ \|*  
*uṣāṃbāṇasya tanayāmupayeme dvijottama \|\| 7 \|\|*

Mighty Aniruddha, victorious in battle, won Uṣā, daughter of Bāṇa and granddaughter of Bali, best of brahmins. 7

## \|\| 5.32.8 \|\|

*yatra yuddhamabhūddhoraṃ hariśaṅkarayormahat \|*  
*chinnaṃ sahasraṃ bāhūnāṃ yatra bāṇasya cākriṇā \|\| 8 \|\|*

On that occasion, there was a great and terrible contest between Hari and Śiva, in which Hari severed Bāṇa’s thousand arms with his discus. 8

## \|\| 5.32.9 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*kathaṃ yuddamabhūdbrahyannuṣārthe harakṛṣṇayoḥ \|*  
*kathaṃ kṣayaṃ ca bāṇasya bāhūnāṃ kṛtavānhariḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

How did this battle over Uṣā come about, brahmin, and how did Hari cut off Bāṇa’s limbs? 9

## \|\| 5.32.10 \|\|

*etatsarvaṃ mahābhāga mamākhyātuṃ tvamarhasi \|*  
*mahatkautūhalaṃ jātaṃ kathāṃ śrotumimāṃ hareḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Please describe all this to me, fortunate sage, as I’m very keen to hear this narrative of Hari. 10

## \|\| 5.32.11 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*uṣā bāṇasutā vipra pārvatīṃ saha śaṃbhunā \|*  
*krīḍantīmupalakṣyoccaiḥ spṛhāṃ cakre tadāśrayām \|\| 11 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Bāṇa’s daughter Uṣā saw Pārvatī and Śiva making love one day, brahmin, and conceived a great desire to do the same. 11

## \|\| 5.32.12 \|\|

*tataḥsakalacittajñā gaurī tāmāha bhāminīm \|*  
*alamatyarthatāpena bhartrā tvamapi raṃsyase \|\| 12 \|\|*

Fair Pārvatī, who could read the minds of all, said to that lovely woman, ‘Why torment yourself? Just find a husband.’ 12

## \|\| 5.32.13 \|\|

*ityuktā sā tayā cakre kadeti matimātmanaḥ \|*  
*ko vā bhartā mametyāha punastāmāha pārvatī \|\| 13 \|\|*

Hearing this, Uṣā wondered when this might happen and who might be her husband. Pārvatī continued: 13

## \|\| 5.32.14 \|\|

*pārvatyuvāca*  
*vaiśākhaśukladvādaśyāṃ svapne yobhibhavaṃ tava \|*  
*kariṣyati sa te bhartā rājaputri bhaviṣyati \|\| 14 \|\|*

‘The man who sweeps you away in a dream on the twelfth night of the bright fortnight in the month of Vaiśākha, princess, will be your husband.’ 14

## \|\| 5.32.15 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tasyāṃ tithābuṣāsvapne yathā devyā samīritam \|*  
*tathaivā bhibhavaṃ cakre kaścidrāgaṃ ca tatra sā \|\| 15 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Just as the goddess had foretold, a man appeared in a dream that night and Uṣā fell in love with him. 15

## \|\| 5.32.16 \|\|

*tataḥ prabuddhā puruṣamapaśyantī samutmukā \|*  
*kva gatesīti nirlajjā maitreyoktavatī sakhim \|\| 16 \|\|*

But when she woke, she couldn’t see him anymore, Maitreya, and within hearing of her companion anxiously cried out, ‘Where have you gone?’ 16

## \|\| 5.32.17 \|\|

*bāṇasya mantrī kuṃbhāṇḍaṃ citrarekhā ca tatsutā \|*  
*tasyāḥsakhyabhavatsā ca prāha koyaṃ tvayocyate \|\| 17 \|\|*

Now, this companion, Citralekhā, daughter of Bāna’s minister Kumbhāṇḍa, asked Uṣā whom she was addressing. 17

## \|\| 5.32.18 \|\|

*yadā lajjākulā nāsyai kathayāmāsa sā sakhī \|*  
*tadā viśvāsamānīya sarvamevābhyavādayat \|\| 18 \|\|*

Thrown into confusion, Uṣā at first said nothing, but after Citralekhā reassured her, she explained it all. 18

## \|\| 5.32.19 \|\|

*viditārthāṃ tu tāmāha punaścoṣā yathoditam \|*  
*devyā tathaiva tatprāptau yo hyupāyaḥ kuruṣva tam \|\| 19 \|\|*

Now that Citralekhā understood the matter, Uṣā asked her to find a way to fulfil the goddess’s prediction. 19

## \|\| 5.32.20 \|\|

*citralekhovāca*  
*durvijñeyamidaṃ vaktuṃ prāptuṃ vāpi na sakyate \|*  
*tathāpi kiñcatkartavyamupakāraṃ priye tava \|\| 20 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Accordingly, Citralekhā painted on a canvas the portraits of the foremost deities, Daityas, gandharvas and men, and showed them to her friend. 20

## \|\| 5.32.21 \|\|

*saptāṣṭadinaparyantaṃ tāvatkālaḥ pratīkṣyatām \|*  
*ityuktvābhyantaraṃ gatvā upāyaṃ tamathākarot \|\| 21 \|\|*

Uṣā passed over the gandharvas, nāgas, gods and demons, but her eyes were drawn to the men of the Andhaka and Vṛṣṇi clans. 21

## \|\| 5.32.22 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tataḥ pade surāndaityangandharvāṃś ca pradhānataḥ \|*  
*manuṣyāṃś ca vilikhyāsyai citralekhā vyadarśayat \|\| 22 \|\|*

Seeing Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, that fair-browed woman was overcome with shyness, brahmin, and, at the sight of Pradyumna, she modestly dropped her gaze. 22

## \|\| 5.32.23 \|\|

*apāsya sā tu gandharvāṃs tathoragasurāsurān \|*  
*manuṣyeṣu dadau dṛṣṭiṃ teṣv apy andhakavṛṣṇiṣu \|\| 23 \|\|*

The moment she saw Pradyumna’s lovely son, brahmin, that wanton woman cast off all restraint. 23

## \|\| 5.32.24 \|\|

*kṛṣṇarāmau vilokyāsīt subhrūr lajjājaḍeva sā \|*  
*pradyumnadarśane vrīḍedṛṣṭiṃ ninyenyato dvija \|\| 24 \|\|*

‘That’s him!’ she cried, ‘That’s him!’ Citralekhā reassured her friend and, as she possessed the yogic power of flight, she set off for Dvārakā. 24

## Chapter 33 - Aniruddha is bound by Bāṇa and freed by Kṛṣṇa

## \|\| 5.33.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*bāṇopi praṇipatyāgre maitreyāha trilocanam \|*  
*deva bāhusahasreṇa nirviṇṇosmyāhavaṃ vinā \|\| 1 \|\|*

Some time earlier, the Daitya Bāṇa bowed to three-eyed Śiva, Maitreya, and spoke these words to him, ‘Lord, in the absence of a war, my thousand arms are an embarrassment. 1

## \|\| 5.33.2 \|\|

*kaccinmamaiṣāṃ bāhūnāṃ sāphalyajanako raṇaḥ \|*  
*bhaviṣyati vinā yuddhaṃ bhārāya mama kiṃ bhujaiḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

I hope there’ll be a battle to afford these limbs some use, as, without a fight, they’re just a burden. What purpose do they serve?’ 2

## \|\| 5.33.3 \|\|

*śrīśaṅkara uvāca*  
*mayūradhvajabhaṅgaste yadā bāṇā bhaviṣyati \|*  
*piśitāśijanānandaṃ prapsyase tvaṃ tadā raṇam \|\| 3 \|\|*

Śiva:

When your peacock banner breaks, Bāṇa, you shall have a war to delight flesh-eating ghouls. 3

## \|\| 5.33.4 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tataḥ praṇamya varadaṃ śaṃbhumabhyāgato gṛham \|*  
*sabhagnaṃ dhvajamālokya hṛṣṭo harṣaṃ punaryayau \|\| 4 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Filled with glee, Bāṇa bowed to Śiva and went back home, where, seeing his broken banner, his excitement was redoubled. 4

## \|\| 5.33.5 \|\|

*etasminneva kāle tu yogavidyābalena tam \|*  
*aniruddhamathāninye citralekhā varāpsarāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

At that very time, the exquisite apsaras Citralekhā, employing the power of her yogic knowledge, returned from Dvārakā, bringing Aniruddha with her. 5

## \|\| 5.33.6 \|\|

*kanyāntaḥpuramabhyetya ramamāṇaṃ sahoṣayā \|*  
*vijñāya rakṣiṇo gatvā śaśaṃsurdaityabhūpate \|\| 6 \|\|*

When the palace guards found Aniruddha making love with Uṣā in the inner chambers, they went to the Daitya king to report the matter. 6

## \|\| 5.33.7 \|\|

*vyādiṣṭaṃ kiṅkarāṇāṃ tu sainyaṃ tena mahātmanā \|*  
*jaghāna parighaṃ ghoramādāya paravīrahā \|\| 7 \|\|*

That mighty being sent his servant army, but Aniruddha, destroyer of his doughty foes, seized an iron bar and killed them. 7

## \|\| 5.33.8 \|\|

*hateṣu teṣu bāṇopi rathasthastadvadhodyataḥ \|*  
*yudhyamāno yathāśakti yaduvīreṇa nirjitaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

After they had been subdued, Bāṇa himself, mounted on his chariot, resolved to kill the interloper. Bāṇa battled Aniruddha with all his might, but was driven back by his rival’s heroism. 8

## \|\| 5.33.9 \|\|

*māyayā yuyudhe tena sa tadā mantricoditaḥ \|*  
*tatastaṃ pannagāstreṇa babandha yadunandanam \|\| 9 \|\|*

At the urging of his ministers, he set on Aniruddha a second time and, deploying his magic powers, bound the Yadus’ hero with a serpent-weapon. 9

## \|\| 5.33.10 \|\|

*dvāravatyāṃ kva yāto 'sāvaniruddheti jalpatām \|*  
*yadūnāmācacakṣe taṃ baddhaṃ bāṇena nāradaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

The Yādavas in Dvārakā asked where Aniruddha was, and Nārada informed them that he was Bāṇa’s captive. 10

## \|\| 5.33.11 \|\|

*taṃ śośitapuraṃ nītaṃ śrutvā vidyāvidagdhayā \|*  
*yoṣitā pratyayaṃ jagmuryādavā nāmarair iti \|\| 11 \|\|*

When they heard that he’d been taken to the city of Śoṇita by a woman with yogic powers, they were reassured he hadn’t been abducted by the gods. 11

## \|\| 5.33.12 \|\|

*tato garuḍamāruhya smṛtamātrāgataṃ hariḥ \|*  
*balapradyumnasahito bāṇasya prayayau puram \|\| 12 \|\|*

Hari, mounted on Garuḍa, whom he summoned with a single thought, advanced on Bāṇa’s city with Balarāma and Pradyumna. 12

## \|\| 5.33.13 \|\|

*purapraveśe pramathair yuddhamāsīnmahātmanaḥ \|*  
*yayau bāṇapurābhyāśaṃ nītvā tānsaṃkṣayaṃ hariḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Coming to that city, Hari battled the Pramathas, Śiva’s demon minions, and, having overcome them all, he entered Bāṇa’s capital. 13

## \|\| 5.33.14 \|\|

*tatastripādastriśirā jvaro māheśvaro mahān \|*  
*bāṇarakṣārthamabhyetya yuyudhe śārṅgadhanvanā \|\| 14 \|\|*

There, a mighty Fever, in the form of a three-legged, three-headed emanation of Śiva, fought valiantly with Hari, Śārṅga’s wielder, in defence of Bāṇa. 14

## \|\| 5.33.15 \|\|

*tadbhasmasparśasaṃbhūtatāpaḥ kṛṣṇāṅgasaṃgamāt \|*  
*avāpa baladevopi śramamāmīlitekṣaṇaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Scorched by contact with the Fever’s embers, Balarāma closed his eyes and hid behind his brother. 15

## \|\| 5.33.16 \|\|

*tataḥsa yuddhyamānastu saha devena śārṅgiṇā \|*  
*vaiṣṇavena jvareṇāśu kṛṣṇadehānnirākṛtaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

While fighting the divine wielder of Śārṅga, the Śaiva Fever was quickly driven off by a Vaiṣṇava Fever that rose from Kṛṣṇa’s body. 16

## \|\| 5.33.17 \|\|

*nārāyaṇabhujāghātapāripījanavihvalam \|*  
*taṃ vīkṣya kṣamyatāmasyetyāha devaḥ pitāmahaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

When divine Brahmā, grandsire of the world, saw the Śaiva Fever enduring the blows of Nārāyaṇa’s arms, he begged the god to pardon him. 17

## \|\| 5.33.18 \|\|

*tataś ca kṣāntameveti proktvā taṃ vaiṣṇavaṃ jvaram \|*  
*ātmanyevalayaṃ ninye bhagavānmadhusūdanaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Lord Madhusūdana duly forgave the Śaiva Fever and withdrew the Vaiṣṇava sickness to himself. 18

## \|\| 5.33.19 \|\|

*jvara uvāca*  
*mama tvāya samaṃ yuddhaṃ ye smarīṣyanti mānavāḥ \|*  
*vijvarāste bhaviṣyantī tyuktvā cainaṃ yayau jvaraḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

‘Those who recall the battle I fought with you will never suffer our disease,’ the Śaiva Fever declared to Kṛṣṇa as he left. 19

## \|\| 5.33.20 \|\|

*tatognīnbhagavānpañca jitvā nītvā tathā kṣayam \|*  
*dānavānāṃ balaṃ kṛṣṇaścūrṇayāmāsa līlayā \|\| 20 \|\|*

Lord Viṣṇu then overcame and crushed the fivefold Fires and broke with ease the army of the Dānavas. 20

## \|\| 5.33.21 \|\|

*tataḥsamastasainyena daiteyānāṃ baleḥsutaḥ \|*  
*yuyudhe śaṅkaraścaiva kārttikeyaś ca śauriṇā \|\| 21 \|\|*

All the Daitya forces, Bāṇa, son of Bali, Śiva and his scion Kārttikeya then fought with Kṛṣṇa, Śūra’s offspring. 21

## \|\| 5.33.22 \|\|

*hariśaṅkarayoryuddhamatīvāsītsudāruṇam \|*  
*cukṣubhuḥsa kalā lokāḥ śastrāstrāṃśupratāpitiḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

The battle Hari fought with Śiva was terrible indeed, and all the worlds were trembling as the deities’ flaming weapons scorched them. 22

## \|\| 5.33.23 \|\|

*pralayoyamaśeṣasya jagato nūnamāgataḥ \|*  
*menire tridaśāstatra vartamāne mahāraṇe \|\| 23 \|\|*

As that awful conflict raged, the thirteen gods all feared the world would surely be destroyed. 23

## \|\| 5.33.24 \|\|

*jṛṃbha kāstreṇa govindo jṛṃbhayāmāsa śaṅkaram \|*  
*tataḥ praṇemurdaiteyāḥ pramathāś ca samantataḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

With the weapon known as Jṛmbhaṇa, Govinda made Śiva yawn, then destroyed the Daityas and Pramathas on all sides. 24

## \|\| 5.33.25 \|\|

*jṛṃbhābhibhūtasyu haro rathopastha upāviśat \|*  
*na śaśāka tato yoddhuṃ kṛṣṇenākliṣṭakarmaṇā \|\| 25 \|\|*

Yawning uncontrollably, Śiva sank down on the chariot seat, unable to fight Kṛṣṇa, the being untouched by deeds. 25

## \|\| 5.33.26 \|\|

*garujakṣatavāhaś ca pradyumnāstreṇa pīḍitaḥ \|*  
*kṛṣṇahuṅkāranirdhūtaśaktiścāpayayau guhaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

Kārttikeya’s mount was downed by Garuḍa, as Pradyumna’s missiles pierced him. His strength undone by Kṛṣṇa’s thundering bow, Kārttikeya fled. 26

## \|\| 5.33.27 \|\|

*jṛṃbhite śaṅkare naṣṭe daityasainye guhe jite \|*  
*nīte pramathasainye ca saṃkṣayaṃ śārṅgadhanvanā \|\| 27 \|\|*

With Śiva yawning, the Daitya army vanquished, Kārttikeya beaten and the Pramatha forces destroyed by Śārṅga’s wielder, 27

## \|\| 5.33.28 \|\|

*nandinā saṃgṛhītāśvamadhirūḍho mahāratham \|*  
*bāṇastatrāyayau yoddhuṃ kṛṣṇakārṣṇibalaiḥ saha \|\| 28 \|\|*

Bāṇa mounted his great chariot, to which Śiva’s bull, Nandīśa, had been harnessed in place of steeds, and advanced to fight with Kṛṣṇa, his son Pradyumna and Balarāma. 28

## \|\| 5.33.29 \|\|

*balabhadro mahāvīryo bāṇasainyamanekadhā \|*  
*vivyādha bāṇaiḥ prabhraśya dharmataścāpalāyata \|\| 29 \|\|*

The mighty hero Balarāma tormented Bāṇa’s army with his arrows repeatedly and, having stripped it of its honour, forced it to retreat. 29

## \|\| 5.33.30 \|\|

*ākṛṣya lāṅgalāgreṇa mukalenāśu tāḍitam \|*  
*balaṃ balena dadṛśe bāṇo bāṇaiś ca cakriṇā \|\| 30 \|\|*

Bāṇa saw his army dragged away on the tip of Balarāma’s plough and beaten with his hammer, while discus-wielding Kṛṣṇa showered it with arrows. 30

## \|\| 5.33.31 \|\|

*tataḥ kṛṣṇena bāṇasya yuddhamāsītsudāruṇam \|\| 31 \|\|*

Thus, the battle between Kṛṣṇa and Bāṇa unfolded on all sides. 31

## \|\| 5.33.32 \|\|

*samasyatoriṣūndīptānkāyatrāṇavibhedinaḥ \|*  
*kṛṣṇaściccheda bāṇais tān bāṇena prahitāñchitān \|*  
*vivyādha keśavaṃ bāṇo bāṇaṃ vivyādha cakradhṛk \|\| 32 \|\|*

Each warrior split the other’s brilliant armour-piercing arrows with missiles of his own. Bāṇa split each of Keśava’s, and the bearer of the discus did the same to Bāṇa’s. 32

## \|\| 5.33.33 \|\|

*mumucāte tathāstrāṇi bāṇakṛṣṇau jigīṣayā \|*  
*parasparakṣatikarau lāghavādaniśaṃ dvija \|\| 33 \|\|*

Bāṇa and Kṛṣṇa, both impulsive warriors, striving for the upper hand, intent upon destruction, brahmin, then loosed their shafts at one another. 33

## \|\| 5.33.34 \|\|

*bhidyamāneṣvaśeṣeṣu śareṣvastre ca sīdati \|*  
*prācuryeṇa tato bāṇaṃ hantuṃ cakre harirmanaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

When all their arrows had been cut down and their missiles almost spent, Hari turned his mind in full to Bāṇa’s annihilation. 34

## \|\| 5.33.35 \|\|

*tatorkaśatasaṃghātatejasā sadṛśadyuti \|*  
*jagrāha daityacakrārirhariścakraṃ sudarśanam \|\| 35 \|\|*

Hari, bane of the Daitya host, raised his discus, Sudarśana, whose brilliance equals that of a hundred blazing suns. 35

## \|\| 5.33.36 \|\|

*muñcato bāṇanāśāya tataścakraṃ madhudviṣaḥ \|*  
*nagnā daiteyavidyābhūtkoṭarī purato hareḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

That enemy of Madhu cast his discus to fell Bāṇa, but the naked demoness Koṭavī, conjured by the Daitya’s secret knowledge, appeared in front of Kṛṣṇa. 36

## \|\| 5.33.37 \|\|

*tāmagrato harirdṛṣṭvā mīlitākṣaḥsudarśanam \|*  
*mumoca bāṇamuddiśya cchettuṃ bāhuvanaṃ ripoḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

Seeing her before him, unblinking Kṛṣṇa loosed his discus to fell his rival Bāṇa’s forest of arms. 37

## \|\| 5.33.38 \|\|

*krameṇa tattu bāhūnāṃ bāṇasyācyutacoditam \|*  
*chedaṃ cakre 'surāpāstaśastrau ghakṣapaṇādṛtam \|\| 38 \|\|*

Cast by Acyuta and famed for its destruction of the demons’ serried weapons, it severed one by one the limbs of Bāṇa. 38

## \|\| 5.33.39 \|\|

*chinne bāhuvane tattu karasthaṃ madhusūtanaḥ \|*  
*mumukṣurbāṇanāśāya vijñātastripuradviṣā \|\| 39 \|\|*

When Bāṇa’s mass of arms had all been amputated, Śiva, assailant of Tripura city, knew that Madhusūdana would launch the discus in his hand for Bāṇa’s final doom. 39

## \|\| 5.33.40 \|\|

*samupetyāha govindaṃ sāmapūrvamumāpatiḥ \|*  
*vilokya bāmaṃ dādaḍacchedāsṛksrāvavarṣiṇam \|\| 40 \|\|*

Seeing Bāṇa bleeding freely from the wounds where his limbs were missing, Umā’s husband came with words to soothe Govinda: 40

## \|\| 5.33.41 \|\|

*śaṅkara uvāca*  
*kṛṣṇakṛṣṇa jagannātha jāne tvāṃ puruṣottamam \|*  
*pareśaṃ paramātmānamanādinidhanaṃ harim \|\| 41 \|\|*

Śiva:

Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa, universal Lord! I know you as the highest spirit, highest lord, highest Self and highest being with neither start nor finish. 41

## \|\| 5.33.42 \|\|

*devatiryaṅmanuṣyeṣu śarīragrahaṇātmikā \|*  
*līleyaṃ sarvabhūtasya tava ceṣṭopalakṣaṇā \|\| 42 \|\|*

Appearing in this bodily form among the gods and beasts and mortals is just a game for you, universal being, and is but a minor mark of your activity. 42

## \|\| 5.33.43 \|\|

*tatprasīdābhayaṃ dattaṃ bāṇasyāsya mayā prabho \|*  
*tattvāya nānṛtaṃ kāryaṃ yanmayā vyāhṛtaṃ vacaḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

Be merciful, therefore. I’ve vouched for Bāṇa’s safety, Lord. Don’t let my word prove hollow. 43

## \|\| 5.33.44 \|\|

*asmatsaṃśrayadṛptoyaṃ nāparādhī tavāvyaya \|*  
*mayā dattavaro daityastatastvāṃ kṣamayāmyaham \|\| 44 \|\|*

He grew up under my protection and hasn’t wronged you, eternal god. I granted him a boon, now I beg you to forgive him. 44

## \|\| 5.33.45 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktaḥ prāha govidaḥ śulapāṇimumāpatim \|*  
*prasannapadano bhūtvā gatāmarṣo 'suraṃ prati \|\| 45 \|\|*

Parāśara:
