Hearing this, Govinda’s visage softened. He shed his anger with the Daitya and spoke to Umā’s husband, who held a trident in his hand: 45

## \|\| 5.33.46 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*yuṣmaddattavaro bāṇo jīvatāmeṣa śaṅkara \|*  
*tvadvākyagauravādetanmayā cakraṃ nivartitam \|\| 46 \|\|*

The Lord:

As Bāṇa received a boon from you, Śiva, I’ll spare him and, because I respect your words, I’ll recall my discus. 46

## \|\| 5.33.47 \|\|

*tvayā yadabhayaṃ dattaṃ taddattamakhilaṃ mayā \|*  
*matto 'vibhinnamātmānaṃ draṣṭumarhasi śaṅkara \|\| 47 \|\|*

I’ll uphold in full the guarantee you gave him, Śiva. Regard yourself as being one with me. 47

## \|\| 5.33.48 \|\|

*yohaṃ sa tvaṃ jagaccedaṃ sadevāsuramānuṣam \|*  
*matto nānyadaśeṣaṃ yattattvañjñātumihārhasi \|\| 48 \|\|*

I’m all the world, including mortals, gods and demigods, and so are you. Only those confused by ignorance see a difference between us. 48

## \|\| 5.33.49 \|\|

*avidyāmohitātmānaḥ puruṣā bhinnadarśinaḥ \|*  
*vadanti bhedaṃ paśyanti cāvayorantaraṃ hara \|\| 49 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, Kṛṣṇa proceeded to the place where Pradyumna’s son was held. There, the serpents binding Aniruddha were blasted by Garuḍa’s breath and fled. 49

## \|\| 5.33.50 \|\|

*prasanno 'haṃ gamiṣyāmi tvaṃ gaccha vṛṣabhadhvaja \|\| 50 \|\|*

Placing Aniruddha and his wife on Garuḍa’s back, Balarāma, Kṛṣṇa’s son Pradyumna and Dāmodara himself returned to the city of Dvārakā. 50

## Chapter 34 - Kṛṣṇa burns Vārāṇasī

## \|\| 5.34.1 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*cakre karma mahacchaurirbībhraṇo mānuṣīṃ tanum \|*  
*jigāya śakraṃ śarvaṃ ca sarvāndevāṃś ca līlayā \|\| 1 \|\|*

Performing mighty deeds in mortal form, Śūra’s scion defeated Indra, Śiva and all the other gods with ease. 1

## \|\| 5.34.2 \|\|

*yac cānyad akarot karma divyaceṣṭavighātakṛt \|*  
*tatkathyatāṃ mahābhāga paraṃ kautūhalaṃ hi me \|\| 2 \|\|*

Tell me what other deeds were done by that being capable of supernatural feats, fortunate sage, as I’m very curious. 2

## \|\| 5.34.3 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*gadato mama viprarṣe śrūyatāmidamādarāt \|*  
*narāvatāre kṛṣṇena dagdhā vārāṇasī yathā \|\| 3 \|\|*

Listen closely, brahmin sage, while I recount how Kṛṣṇa in mortal form burned the city of Vārāṇasī. 3

## \|\| 5.34.4 \|\|

*paiṇḍrako vāsudevastu vāsudevo 'bhavadbhuvi \|*  
*avatīrmastvamityukto janair ajñānamohitaiḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

There was once a man named Pauṇḍraka Vāsudeva. Folk misled by ignorance told him he was the actual Vāsudeva, Viṣṇu’s avatāra in this world. 4

## \|\| 5.34.5 \|\|

*sa mene vāsudevohamavatīrṇo mahītale \|*  
*naṣṭasmṛtistataḥsarvaṃ viṣṇucihnamacīkarat \|\| 5 \|\|*

He began to think he was indeed that being come down to earth. Forgetting his own true nature, he made himself a set of Vaiṣṇava insignia. 5

## \|\| 5.34.6 \|\|

*dūtaṃ ca preṣayāmāsa kṛṣṇāya sumahātmane \|*  
*tyaktvā cakrādikaṃ cihnaṃ madīyaṃ nāma cātmanaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

He sent an emissary to mighty Kṛṣṇa, saying, ‘Give up your discus and other emblems, and renounce the name of Vāsudeva, 6

## \|\| 5.34.7 \|\|

*vāsudevātmakaṃ mūḍha tyaktvā sarvamaśeṣataḥ \|*  
*ātmano jīvitārthāya tato me praṇati vraja \|\| 7 \|\|*

Shed your pride and come to pay me homage, fool, if you value life.’ 7

## \|\| 5.34.8 \|\|

*ityuktaḥsaṃprahasyainaṃ dūtaṃ prāhajanārdanaḥ \|*  
*nijacihnamahaṃ cakraṃ samutsṛkṣye tvayīti vai \|\| 8 \|\|*

Hearing this, Janārdana gave a laugh and said, ‘Take this message back to Pauṇḍraka: “I’ll surely send my discus-emblem to you. 8

## \|\| 5.34.9 \|\|

*vācyaś ca paiṇḍrako gatvā tvayā dūta vaco mama \|*  
*jñātas tvadvākyasadbhāvo yatkāryaṃ tadvidhīyatām \|\| 9 \|\|*

I understand the import of your words: what must be done will indeed occur. 9

## \|\| 5.34.10 \|\|

*gṛhītacihnaveṣohamāgamiṣyāmi te puram \|*  
*utsrakṣyāmi ca taccakraṃ nijacihnamasaṃśayam \|\| 10 \|\|*

With my emblem in my hand, I’ll come to your city and present it to you without a doubt. 10

## \|\| 5.34.11 \|\|

*ājñāpūrvaṃ ca yadidamāgaccheti tvayoditam \|*  
*saṃpādayiṣye śvastubhyaṃ samāgamyāvilaṃbitam \|\| 11 \|\|*

Just say the word, and I’ll appear to you on time tomorrow. 11

## \|\| 5.34.12 \|\|

*śaraṇaṃ te samabhyetya kartāsmi nṛpate tathā \|*  
*yathā tvatto bhayaṃ bhūyo na me kiñcid bhaviṣyati \|\| 12 \|\|*

Once I receive your protection, sire, I’ll do what’s needed so I need not fear you.”’ 12

## \|\| 5.34.13 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktepagate dūte saṃsmṛtyābhyāgataṃ hariḥ \|*  
*garutmantamathāruhya tvaritastatpuraṃ yayau \|\| 13 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the emissary departed. Hari then mounted Garuḍa, whom he summoned with a thought, and proceeded swiftly to the city where Pauṇḍraka resided. 13

## \|\| 5.34.14 \|\|

*tatastu keśavodyogaṃ śrutvā kāśīpatistadā \|*  
*sarvasainyaparivāraḥ pārṣṇigrāha upāyayau \|\| 14 \|\|*

When the king of Kāśī learned of Keśava’s intention, he set out to act as Pauṇḍraka’s rearguard with all his troops. 14

## \|\| 5.34.15 \|\|

*tato balena mahatā kāśīrājabalena ca \|*  
*paiṇḍrako vāsudevo 'sau keśavābhimukho yayau \|\| 15 \|\|*

Pauṇḍraka Vāsudeva advanced to counter Keśava with his own extensive force accompanied by the royal army. 15

## \|\| 5.34.16 \|\|

*taṃ dadarśa harirdūrādudārasyandane sthitam \|*  
*cakrahastaṃ gadāśārṅgabāhuṃ pāṇigatāṃbujam \|\| 16 \|\|*

Hari saw Pauṇḍraka in the distance, riding in a splendid chariot, with discus, mace, sword and lotus in his hands. 16

## \|\| 5.34.17 \|\|

*sragdharaṃ pītavasanaṃ suparmaracitadhvajam \|*  
*vakṣasthale kṛtaṃ cāsya śrīvatsaṃ dadṛśe hariḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

He wore a garland, held a bow like Śārṅga and his banner showed Garuḍa. Hari noticed that he even had a mark like Śrīvatsa on his chest. 17

## \|\| 5.34.18 \|\|

*kirīṭakuṇḍaladharaṃ nānāratnopaśobhitam \|*  
*taṃ dṛṣṭvā bhāvagaṃbhīraṃ jahāsa garuḍadhvajaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Seeing Pauṇḍraka clad in yellow garments with a diadem and earrings, the Garuḍa-banner’s rightful owner laughed aloud. 18

## \|\| 5.34.19 \|\|

*yuyudhe ca balenāsya hastyaśvabalinā dvija \|*  
*nistriṃśāsigadāśūlaśaktikārmukaśālinā \|\| 19 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa battled Pauṇḍraka’s army with its elephants, cavalry and foot soldiers, brahmin, armed with swords and lances, clubs and pikes, spears and bows. 19

## \|\| 5.34.20 \|\|

*kṣaṇena śārṅganirmuktaiḥ śarair arividāraṇaiḥ \|*  
*gadācakranipātaiś ca sūdayāmāsa tadbalam \|\| 20 \|\|*

In a trice, he routed it with enemy-cleaving arrows loosed from Śārṅga and blows of mace and discus. 20

## \|\| 5.34.21 \|\|

*kāśīrājabalaṃ caivaṃ kṣayaṃ nītvā janārdanaḥ \|*  
*uvāca paiṇḍrakaṃ mūḍhamātmacihnopalakṣitam \|\| 21 \|\|*

Janārdana sent the king of Kāśī’s army to its doom, then spoke to foolish Pauṇḍraka, who still displayed his Vaiṣṇava insignia. 21

## \|\| 5.34.22 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*paiṇḍrakoktaṃ tvayā yattudūtavaktreṇa māṃ prati \|*  
*samutsṛjeti cihnāni tatte saṃpādayāmyaham \|\| 22 \|\|*

The Lord:

Pauṇḍraka, your emissary said I must renounce my emblems, so now I’ll let you have them. 22

## \|\| 5.34.23 \|\|

*cakrametatsamutsṛṣṭaṃ gadeyaṃ te visarjitā \|*  
*garutmāneṣa cotsṛṣṭaḥsamārohatu te dhvajam \|\| 23 \|\|*

I’ll send this discus and this mace, and here’s Garuḍa. Put him on your standard! 23

## \|\| 5.34.24 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuccārya vimuktena cakreṇāsau vidāritaḥ \|*  
*pātito gadayā bhagno dhvajaścāsya garutmatā \|\| 24 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, Hari cast his discus, cutting Pauṇḍraka to pieces. He struck him with his mace, while Garuḍa downed the bogus emblem on his standard. 24

## \|\| 5.34.25 \|\|

*tato hāhākṛte loke kāśīpuryadhipo balī \|*  
*yuyudhe vāsudevena mitrasyāpacitau sthitaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

People cried in anguish as the mighty king of Kāśī fought with Vāsudeva to avenge his ally. 25

## \|\| 5.34.26 \|\|

*tataḥ śārṅgadhanurmuktaiś chittvā tasya śiraḥ śaraiḥ \|*  
*kāśīpuryāṃ sa cikṣepa kurvaṃllokasya vismayam \|\| 26 \|\|*

With shafts he loosed from Śārṅga, Hari severed the royal head and flung it into Kāśī, spreading panic among the populace. 26

## \|\| 5.34.27 \|\|

*hatvā ta pauḍrakaṃ śauri kāśīrājaṃ ca sānugam \|*  
*punardvāravatīṃ prāpto reme svargagato yatā \|\| 27 \|\|*

After slaying Pauṇḍraka, the king of Kāśī and his followers, the scion of Śūra returned to Dvārakā, where he indulged in heavenly pleasures. 27

## \|\| 5.34.28 \|\|

*tacchiraḥ patitaṃ tatradṛṣṭvā kāśīpateḥ pure \|*  
*janaḥ kimetadityāha cchinnaṃ keneti vismitaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Seeing the king of Kāśī’s head that landed in the city, the bewildered residents wondered what had happened and who had done the deed. 28

## \|\| 5.34.29 \|\|

*jñātvā taṃ vāsudevena hataṃ tasya sutastataḥ \|*  
*purohitena sahitastoṣayāmāsa śaṅkaram \|\| 29 \|\|*

When the prince learned that his father had been killed by Vāsudeva, guided by his family priest, he propitiated Śiva. 29

## \|\| 5.34.30 \|\|

*avimukte mahākṣetre toṣitastena śaṅkaraḥ \|*  
*varaṃ vṛṇīṣveti tadā taṃ provāca nṛpātmajam \|\| 30 \|\|*

At the sacred site of Avimukta, the royal scion gratified that deity, who then offered him a boon. 30

## \|\| 5.34.31 \|\|

*sa vavre bhagavankṛtyā pitṛhanturvadhāya me \|*  
*samutiṣṭhatu kṛṣṇasya tvatprasādānmaheśvara \|\| 31 \|\|*

The prince replied: ‘By your grace, Lord Śiva, let a phantom rise to bring about the death of Kṛṣṇa, my father’s murderer.’ 31

## \|\| 5.34.32 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ bhaviṣyatītyukte dakṣiṇāgneranantaram \|*  
*mahākṛtyā samuttasthau tasyaivāgnervināśanī \|\| 32 \|\|*

Parāśara:

‘It will be so,’ replied the god, and from the fire in the southernmost of the prince’s sacrificial hearths arose at once a monstrous phantom. 32

## \|\| 5.34.33 \|\|

*tato jvālākarālāsyā jvalatkeśakapālikā \|*  
*kṛṣṇakṛṣṇeti kupitā kṛtyā dvāravarīṃ yayau \|\| 33 \|\|*

Her gaping maw blazed with fire; her hair was a mass of flames. Crying Kṛṣṇa’s name repeatedly, the raging ghoul set out for Dvārakā. 33

## \|\| 5.34.34 \|\|

*tāmavekṣya janastrāsādvicalallocano mune \|*  
*yayau śaraṇyaṃ jagatāṃ śaraṇaṃ madhusūdanam \|\| 34 \|\|*

When the people saw her, sage, their eyes began to dart with terror and they fled for protection to Madhu’s subduer, the refuge of the world. 34

## \|\| 5.34.35 \|\|

*kāśīrājasuteneyamārādhya vṛṣabhadhvajam \|*  
*utpāditā mahākṛtyetyavagamyātha cakriṇā \|\| 35 \|\|*

The discus-wielder understood that this great spectre had been conjured forth by bull-bannered Śiva when the king of Kāśī’s son had worshipped him. 35

## \|\| 5.34.36 \|\|

*jahikṛtyāmimāmugrāṃ vahnijvālājaṭālakām \|*  
*cakramutsṛṣṭamakṣeṣu krīḍāsaktena līlayā \|\| 36 \|\|*

In the middle of a game of dice, Kṛṣṇa casually released his discus with the order to destroy the frightful apparition whose locks were a mass of blazing fire. 36

## \|\| 5.34.37 \|\|

*tadagnimālājaṭilajvālodgārātibhīṣaṇām \|*  
*kṛtyāmanujagāmāśu viṣṇucakraṃ sudarśanam \|\| 37 \|\|*

Viṣṇu’s weapon Sudarśana set off at once towards the dreadful fire-belching spectre, her hair a fiery garland. 37

## \|\| 5.34.38 \|\|

*cakrapratāpanirdagdhā kṛtyā māheśvarī tadā \|*  
*nanāśa veginī vegāttadapyanujagāma tām \|\| 38 \|\|*

Confounded by the discus’s blow, that phantom born of mighty Śiva fled as quickly as she could, but the weapon in pursuit was just as fast. 38

## \|\| 5.34.39 \|\|

*kṛtyā vārāṇasīmeva praviveśa tvarānvitā \|*  
*viṣṇucakrapratihataprabhāvā munisattama \|\| 39 \|\|*

As she hastened into Vārāṇasī, best of sages, Viṣṇu’s discus sapped her strength. 39

## \|\| 5.34.40 \|\|

*tataḥ kāśībalaṃ bhūri pramathānāṃ tathā balam \|*  
*samastaśastrāstrayutaṃ cakrasyābhimukhaṃ yayau \|\| 40 \|\|*

The king of Kāśī’s army, with a mighty force of Pramathas, armed with every kind of weapon and with missiles, met Sudarśana. 40

## \|\| 5.34.41 \|\|

*śastrāstramokṣacaturaṃ dagdhvā tadbalamojasā \|*  
*kṛtyā garbhamaśeṣāṃ tāṃ tadā vārāṇasīṃpurīm \|\| 41 \|\|*

Hari’s discus burned the armies with its energy, even though they knew the use of weapons. Then it burned down all of Vārāṇasī, source of the apparition, 41

## \|\| 5.34.42 \|\|

*sabhūbhṛdbhṛtyapaurāṃ tu sāśvamātaṅgamānavām \|*  
*aśeṣagoṣṭhakośāṃ tāṃ durnirikṣyāṃ surair api \|\| 42 \|\|*

Along with its king, his minions, subjects, horses, elephants, men and all its stores and granaries such as even deities rarely see. 42

## \|\| 5.34.43 \|\|

*jvālāpariṣkṛtāśeṣagṛhaprākāracatvarām \|*  
*dadāha taddhareścakraṃ sakalāmeva tāṃ purīm \|\| 43 \|\|*

Until every residence, square and rampart was engulfed by flames, Hari’s discus burned it all. 43

## \|\| 5.34.44 \|\|

*akṣīṇāmarṣamatyugraṃ sādhyasādhanasaspṛham \|*  
*taccakraṃ prasphuraddīpti viṣṇorabhyāyayau karam \|\| 44 \|\|*

Sudarśana, ablaze with splendour, then returned to Viṣṇu’s hand with undiminished energy and a thirst for further missions so easily accomplished. 44

## Chapter 35 - Balarāma threatens Hastināpura

## \|\| 5.35.1 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*bhūya evāhamicchāmi balabhadrasya dhīmataḥ \|*  
*śrotuṃ parākramaṃ brahman tanmamākhyātumarhasi \|\| 1 \|\|*

I’d like to hear some more about wise Balarāma’s prowess. Kindly tell me, brahmin. 1

## \|\| 5.35.2 \|\|

*yamunākarṣaṇādīni śrutāni bhagavanmayā \|*  
*tatkathyatāṃ mahābhāga yadanyatkṛtavānbalaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

I’ve heard how he dragged the Yamunā and other feats, master, but tell me, fortunate sage, what other things he did. 2

## \|\| 5.35.3 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*maitreya śruyatāṃ karma yadrāmeṇābhavatkṛtam \|*  
*anante nāprameyena śeṣeṇa dharaṇīdhṛtā \|\| 3 \|\|*

Listen, Maitreya, to this feat performed by Balarāma, who in truth is Śeṣa, the eternal, immeasurable upholder of the earth. 3

## \|\| 5.35.4 \|\|

*suyodhanasya tanayāṃ svayaṃvarakṛtakṣaṇām \|*  
*balādādattavānvīraḥ sāṃbo jāṃbavatīsutaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Jāmbavatī’s son, the hero Sāmba, seized the daughter of Duryodhana as she was preparing for her svayaṃvara. 4

## \|\| 5.35.5 \|\|

*tataḥ kruddhāmahāvīryāḥ karṇaduryodhanādayaḥ \|*  
*bhīṣmadroṇādayaścainaṃ babandhuryudhi nirjitam \|\| 5 \|\|*

The mighty warriors Karṇa, Duryodhana, Bhīṣma, Droṇa and the rest were furious. They worsted Sāmba in battle and imprisoned him. 5

## \|\| 5.35.6 \|\|

*tacchutvā yādavāḥsarve krodhaṃ duryodhanādiṣu \|*  
*maitreya cakruḥ kṛṣṇaś ca tānnihantuṃ mahodyamam \|\| 6 \|\|*

Hearing this, the Yādavas were all enraged by Duryodhana and his friends, Maitreya, and devised a scheme to slay them. 6

## \|\| 5.35.7 \|\|

*tānnivārya balaḥ prāha madalolakalākṣaram \|*  
*mokṣyanti te madvacanādyāsyāmyeko hi kauravān \|\| 7 \|\|*

But Balarāma butted in and, slurring his words in drunkenness, exclaimed, ‘I’ll go alone to the Kauravas and make them set him free.’ 7

## \|\| 5.35.8 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*baladevastato dṛṣṭvā nagaraṃ nāgasāhvayam \|*  
*bāhyopavanamadhye 'bhūnna viveśa ca tatpuram \|\| 8 \|\|*

When he reached Hastināpura, the city named for elephants, he didn’t enter, but waited on its outskirts in a grove. 8

## \|\| 5.35.9 \|\|

*balamāgatamājñāya bhūpā duryodhanādayaḥ \|*  
*gāmarghyamudakaṃ caiva rāmāya pratyavedayan \|\| 9 \|\|*

When Duryodhana and the other princes heard that he’d arrived, they presented him with cattle and some water as a gift. 9

## \|\| 5.35.10 \|\|

*gṛhītvā vidhivatsarvaṃ tatastānāha kauravān \|*  
*ājñāpayatyugrasenaḥsāṃbamāśu vimuñcata \|\| 10 \|\|*

Balarāma duly accepted these, but then addressed the Kauravas: ‘Ugrasena orders you: set Sāmba free at once.’ 10

## \|\| 5.35.11 \|\|

*tatastadvacanaṃ śrutvā bhīṣmadroṇādayo nṛpāḥ \|*  
*karmaduryodhanādyāś ca cukṣubhurdvijasattama \|\| 11 \|\|*

Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Karṇa, Duryodhana and the others heard his words, best of brahmins, and flew into a rage. 11

## \|\| 5.35.12 \|\|

*ūcuś ca kupitāḥsarve bāhlikādyāś ca kauravāḥ \|*  
*arājyārhaṃ yadorvaśamavekṣya musalāyudha \|\| 12 \|\|*

All the furious Kauravas, Bālhīkas and the rest, who considered Yadu’s lineage unworthy of a kingdom, said to the wielder of the plough: 12

## \|\| 5.35.13 \|\|

*bhobhoḥ kimetadbhavatā balabhadreritaṃ vacaḥ \|*  
*ājñāṃ kurukulotthānāṃ yādavaḥ kaḥ pradāsyati \|\| 13 \|\|*

‘Hey! Hey! What’s this you say, Balarāma? Who among the Yādavas gives orders to Kuru clansmen? 13

## \|\| 5.35.14 \|\|

*ugraseno 'pi yadyājñāṃ kauravāṇāṃ pradāsyati \|*  
*tadalaṃ pāṇḍuraiś chatrair nṛpayogyair viḍaṃbanaiḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

If Ugrasena commands the Kauravas, we might as well give up our white umbrellas, the symbols of our sovereignty. 14

## \|\| 5.35.15 \|\|

*tadgaccha bala mā vā tvaṃ sāṃbamanyāyaceṣṭitam \|*  
*vimokṣyāmo na bhavataścograsenasya śāsanāt \|\| 15 \|\|*

Go or stay, Balarāma, but we won’t release ill-mannered Sāmba just as you or Ugrasena wish it. 15

## \|\| 5.35.16 \|\|

*praṇatiryā kṛtāsmākaṃ mānyānāṃ kukurāndhakaiḥ \|*  
*na nāma sā kṛtā keyamājñā svāmini bhṛtyataḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

You Kukuras and Andhakas have never shown the respect that we deserve, but what’s this order from a servant to a master? 16

## \|\| 5.35.17 \|\|

*garvamāropitā yūyaṃ samānāsanabhojanaiḥ \|*  
*ko doṣo bhavatāṃ nītiryatprītyā nāvalokitā \|\| 17 \|\|*

Just because you sit and eat with us, you’ve grown arrogant. We didn’t think it wrong to show you kindness, because of our affection. 17

## \|\| 5.35.18 \|\|

*asmābhir argho bhavato yoyaṃ bala niveditaḥ \|*  
*premṇaitannaitadasmākaṃ kulādyuṣmatkulocitam \|\| 18 \|\|*

We sent you gifts of welcome because we liked you, Balarāma, but it doesn’t really suit your clan to receive a gift from ours.’ 18

## \|\| 5.35.19 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvā kuravaḥ sāṃbaṃ muñcāmo na hareḥsutam \|*  
*kṛtaitaniścayastūrṇaṃ viviśurgajasāhvayam \|\| 19 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, the Kauravas refused to release Sāmba, son of Hari, and, being in agreement, they returned at once to the city named for elephants. 19

## \|\| 5.35.20 \|\|

*mattaḥ kopena cāghūrmastatodhikṣepajanmanā \|*  
*utthāya pārṣṇyā vasudhāṃ jaghāna sa halāyudhaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

Mad with fury at this insult, plough-wielding Balarāma spun around, leapt up and kicked the earth. 20

## \|\| 5.35.21 \|\|

*tato vidāritā pṛthvī pārṣṇighātānmahātmanaḥ \|*  
*āsphoṭayāmāsa tadā diśaḥ śabdena pūrayan \|\| 21 \|\|*

The earth, struck by that mighty being, split asunder, and the sound of the fracture spread in all directions. 21

## \|\| 5.35.22 \|\|

*uvāca cātitāmrākṣo bhṛkuṭīkuṭilānanaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

With reddened eyes and knitted brows, he shouted, ‘Ha! Such is the haughty arrogance of these worthless, wicked people. 22

## \|\| 5.35.23 \|\|

*aha madāvalepoyamasārāṇāṃ durātmanām \|*  
*kauravāṇāṃ mahīpatvamasmākaṃ kila kālajam \|*  
*ugrasenasya yenājñāṃ manyante 'dyapi laṅghanam \|\| 23 \|\|*

The sovereignty of the Kauravas and ours is the gift of fate, as is the fact they think today they can ignore the command of Ugrasena. 23

## \|\| 5.35.24 \|\|

*ugrasenaḥ samadhyāste sudhārmā na śacīpatiḥ \|*  
*dhiṅmānuṣaśatocchiṣṭe tuṣṭireṣāṃ nṛpāsane \|\| 24 \|\|*

Śacī’s husband and other gods duly follow Ugrasena’s orders, and he holds court in Sudharmā, Indra’s assembly hall. 24

## \|\| 5.35.25 \|\|

*pārijātataroḥ puṣpamañjarīrvanitājanaḥ \|*  
*bibharti yasya bhatyānāṃ so 'pyeṣāṃ na mahīpatiḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

I curse their pride in a royal throne that has been occupied by a hundred men! Is not Ugrasena, whose servants’ wives wear garlands from the Pārijāta tree, lord of the earth? 25

## \|\| 5.35.26 \|\|

*samastabhūbhṛtāṃ nātha ugrasenaḥ sa tiṣṭhatu \|*  
*adya niṣkauravāmurvī kṛtvā yāsyāmi tatpurīm \|\| 26 \|\|*

May Ugrasena remain the master of every monarch. Before I enter his city today, I’ll rid this world of Kauravas. 26

## \|\| 5.35.27 \|\|

*karṇaṃ duryodhanaṃ droṇamadya bhīṣmaṃ sabāhlikam \|*  
*duḥśasanādīnbhūriṃ ca bhūriśravasameva ca \|\| 27 \|\|*

This very day, I’ll kill Karṇa, Duryodhana, Droṇa, Bhīṣma, Bāhlika, Duḥśāsana and his brothers, Bhūri, Bhūriśravas, 27

## \|\| 5.35.28 \|\|

*somadattaṃ śalaṃ caiva bhīmārjunayudhiṣṭhirān \|*  
*yamau ca kauravāṃścānyānhatvā sāśvarathadvipān \|\| 28 \|\|*

Somadatta, Śala, Bhīma, Arjuna, Yudhiṣṭhira and the twins Sahadeva and Nakula, as well as the other Kauravas, along with their horses, chariots and elephants. 28

## \|\| 5.35.29 \|\|

*vīramādāya taṃ sāṃbaṃ sapatnīkaṃ tataḥ purīm \|*  
*dvārakāmugrasenādīngatvā drakṣyāmi bāndhavān \|\| 29 \|\|*

Then I’ll rescue brave Sāmba and his wife, and bring them back to Dvārakā, where I’ll see Ugrasena and my other kin once more. 29

## \|\| 5.35.30 \|\|

*atha vā kauravāvāsaṃ samastaiḥ kurubhiḥ saha \|*  
*bhāgīrathyāṃ kṣipāmyāśu nagaraṃ nāgasāhvayam \|\| 30 \|\|*

Better still, I’ll take the Kauravas’ capital, the city named for elephants, along with the Kurus themselves, and throw them in the Gaṅgā River.’ 30

## \|\| 5.35.31 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvā madaraktākṣaḥ karṣaṇādhomukhaṃ halam \|*  
*prākāravapradurgasya cakarṣa musalāyudhaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, his eyes still red with drunkenness, plough-wielding Balarāma laid his weapon, blade down, upon the city wall and pulled. 31

## \|\| 5.35.32 \|\|

*āghūrṇitaṃ tatsahasā tato vai hāstinaṃ puram \|*  
*dṛṣṭvā saṃkṣubdhahṛdayāścukṣubhuḥsarvakauravāḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

Seeing Hastināpura abruptly start to topple, the Kauravas’ hearts all trembled and they cried aloud, 32

## \|\| 5.35.33 \|\|

*rāmarāma mahābāho kṣamyatāṃ kṣamyatāṃ tvayā \|*  
*upasaṃhriyatāṃ kopaḥ prasīda musalāyudha \|\| 33 \|\|*

‘Balarāma, Balarāma! Forgive us, mighty warrior, forgive us. Don’t be angry. Be gracious, plough-wielding hero. 33

## \|\| 5.35.34 \|\|

*eṣa sāṃbaḥsapatnīkastavaniryātito balāt \|*  
*avijñātaprabhāvāṇāṃ kṣmayatāmaparādhinām \|\| 34 \|\|*

Here are Sāmba and his wife. We’ll set them free for you, Balarāma. We didn’t know how strong you were. Forgive us our offence.’ 34

## \|\| 5.35.35 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tato niryātayāmāsuḥsāṃbaṃ patnīsamanvitam \|*  
*niṣkramya svapurāttūrṇaṃ kauravā munipuṅgava \|\| 35 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The Kauravas immediately came out of the city, best of sages, and handed over Sāmba and his wife. 35

## \|\| 5.35.36 \|\|

*bhīṣmadroṇakṛpādīnāṃ praṇamya vadatāṃ priyam \|*  
*kṣāntameva mayetyāha balo balavatāṃ varaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

Balarāma, bowing, spoke to Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Kṛpa and the other Kauravas as they tried to calm him. ‘I forgive you,’ said that best of heroes. 36

## \|\| 5.35.37 \|\|

*adyāpyāghūrṇitākāraṃ lakṣyate tatpuraṃ dvija \|*  
*eṣa prabhāvo rāmasya balaśauryopalakṣaṇaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

Even to this very day, brahmin, Hastināpura seems to list, such was the power of Balarāma, as shown by his strength and courage. 37

## \|\| 5.35.38 \|\|

*tatastu kauravāḥsāṃbaṃ saṃpūjya bali nā saha \|*  
*preṣayāmāsurudvāhadhanabhāryāsamanvitam \|\| 38 \|\|*

The Kauravas then paid respects to Balarāma and sent off Sāmba and his wife with wedding gifts. 38

## Chapter 36 - Balarāma defeats the giant monkey Dvivida

## \|\| 5.36.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*maitreyaitadbalaṃ tasya balasya balaśālinaḥ \|*  
*kṛtaṃ yadanyattenābhūttadapi śrūyatāṃ tvayā \|\| 1 \|\|*

Listen, Maitreya. Now you’ll hear about another feat performed by mighty Balarāma. 1

## \|\| 5.36.2 \|\|

*narakasyāsuredrasya devapakṣavirodhinaḥ \|*  
*sakhābhavanmahāvīryo dvivido vānararṣabhaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

There was a monkey, Dvivida by name, a powerful warrior and ally of Naraka the demon king and rival of the deities. 2

## \|\| 5.36.3 \|\|

*vairānubandhaṃ balavānsa cakāra surānprati \|*  
*narakaṃ hatavānkṛṣṇo devarājena coditaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

After Kṛṣṇa, at Indra’s urging, had slain Naraka, that mighty beast then became the gods’ sworn enemy, saying: 3

## \|\| 5.36.4 \|\|

*kariṣye sarvadevānāṃ tasmādetatpratīkriyām \|*  
*yajñavidhvaṃsanaṃ kurvan martyalokakṣayaṃ tathā \|\| 4 \|\|*

‘Because of this, I’ll take revenge on all the gods by disrupting every sacrifice and destroying the world of men.’ 4

## \|\| 5.36.5 \|\|

*tato vidhvaṃsayāmāsa yajñānajñānamohitaḥ \|*  
*bibheda sādhumaryādāṃ kṣayaṃ cakre ca dehinām \|\| 5 \|\|*

Accordingly, misled by ignorance, he prevented acts of worship, overstepped the limits of the wise and wrought destruction on living beings. 5

## \|\| 5.36.6 \|\|

*dadāha savanāndeśānpuragramāntarāṇi ca \|*  
*kvacicca parvatākṣepergramādīnsamacūrṇayat \|\| 6 \|\|*

That wanton creature burned the land, the cities, towns and everything between them. Here and there he crushed the villages and other settlements by hurling mountains down on them. 6

## \|\| 5.36.7 \|\|

*śailānutpāṭya toyeṣu mumocāṃbunidhau tathā \|*  
*punaścārmavamadhyasthaḥ kṣobhayāmāsa sāgaram \|\| 7 \|\|*

He picked up massifs and dropped them in the seas and rivers, and churned the ocean while standing in its waters. 7

## \|\| 5.36.8 \|\|

*tena vikṣobhitaścābdhirudvelo dvija jāyate \|*  
*plāvayaṃstīrajāngramānpurādīnativegavān \|\| 8 \|\|*

Stirred by Dvivida, brahmin, the ocean broke its banks and swiftly flooded towns and cities and other places on its shores. 8

## \|\| 5.36.9 \|\|

*kāmarūpī mahārūpī kṛtvā sasyānyaśeṣataḥ \|*  
*luṭhanbhramaṇasaṃmardaiḥ saṃcūrmayati vānaraḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

That monkey, who could take on any shape at will, appeared in monstrous form and ruined all the crops by rolling, roaming and trampling on them. 9

## \|\| 5.36.10 \|\|

*tena viprakṛtaṃ sarvaṃ jagadetaddurātmanā \|*  
*niḥsvādhyāyavaṣaṭkāraṃ maitreyāsītsuduḥkhitam \|\| 10 \|\|*

This whole world, ravaged by that evil beast and deprived of Vedic recitation with ritual cries of vaṣaṭ, Maitreya, was filled with misery. 10

## \|\| 5.36.11 \|\|

*ekadāraivatodyāne papau pānaṃ halā yudhaḥ \|*  
*revatī ca mahābhāgā tathaivānyā varastriyaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

One day, plough-wielding Balarāma was drinking in the garden of Raivata with fortunate Revatī and some other lovely women. 11

## \|\| 5.36.12 \|\|

*udgīyamāno vilasallalanāmaulimadhyagaḥ \|*  
*reme yadukulaśreṣṭhaḥ kubera iva nandane \|\| 12 \|\|*

While they sang his praises, that foremost of the Yadu clan enjoyed himself, surrounded by a splendid throng of girls, like Kubera on Mount Mandara. 12

## \|\| 5.36.13 \|\|

*tataḥsa vānaro 'bhyetya gṛhītvā sīriṇo halam \|*  
*musalaṃ ca cakārāsya saṃmukhaṃ ca viḍaṃbanam \|\| 13 \|\|*

But then Dvivida appeared and, snatching Balarāma’s plough and club, he mocked the Yādava to his face. 13

## \|\| 5.36.14 \|\|

*tathaiva yoṣitāṃ tāsāṃ jahāsābhimukhaṃ kapiḥ \|*  
*pānapūrmāś ca karakāñcikṣepāhatya vai tadā \|\| 14 \|\|*

That monkey laughed at Balarāma in front of all the women and kicked the wine-filled vessels over, smashing all of them. 14

## \|\| 5.36.15 \|\|

*tataḥ kopaparītātmā bhartsayāmāsa taṃ halī \|*  
*tathāpi tamavajñāya cakrekilakiladhvanim \|\| 15 \|\|*

Furious Balarāma cursed the monkey, but Dvivida, ignoring him, just chattered back. 15

## \|\| 5.36.16 \|\|

*tataḥ smayitvā sa balo jagrāha musalaṃ ruṣā \|*  
*so 'pi śailaśilāṃ bhīmāṃ jagrāha plavagottamaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Balarāma leapt up and seized his club in fury, but the magic monkey grabbed a boulder of enormous size and hurled it. 16

## \|\| 5.36.17 \|\|

*cikṣepa sa ca tāṃ kṣiptāṃ musalena sahasradhā \|*  
*vibheda yādavaśreṣṭhaḥsā papāta mahītale \|\| 17 \|\|*

The hero of the Yādavas smashed that rock into a thousand pieces with his club and brought it to the ground. 17

## \|\| 5.36.18 \|\|

*atha tanmusalaṃ cāsau samullaṅghya plavaṅgamaḥ \|*  
*vegenāgatya roṣeṇa kareṇorasyatāḍayat \|\| 18 \|\|*

The monkey flew up in the air, dodged the club and, rushing forward, beat his paws against his chest in anger. 18

## \|\| 5.36.19 \|\|

*tato balena kopena muṣṭinā mūrdhni tāḍitaḥ \|*  
*papāta rudhirodgārī dvividaḥ kṣīṇajīvitaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

Raging Balarāma struck Dvivida on the head; the monkey dropped down to the ground, spewing blood, his life force spent. 19

## \|\| 5.36.20 \|\|

*patatā taccharīreṇa gireḥ śṛgamaśīryata \|*  
*maitreya śatadhā vajrivajremeva vidāritam \|\| 20 \|\|*

Dvivida’s body fell on a mountain and smashed its peak in a hundred pieces, Maitreya, as if struck by Indra’s thunderbolt. 20

## \|\| 5.36.21 \|\|

*puṣpavṛṣṭiṃ tato devā rāmasyopari cikṣipuḥ \|*  
*praśaśaṃsustato 'bhyetya sādhvetatte mahatkṛtam \|\| 21 \|\|*

The deities caused flowers to fall like rain on Balarāma and, descending from the heavens, praised him, saying, ‘Excellent indeed is this feat that you’ve accomplished. 21

## \|\| 5.36.22 \|\|

*anena duṣṭakapinā daityapakṣopakāriṇā \|*  
*jagannirākṛtaṃ vīra diṣṭyā sa kṣayamāgataḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

All the world was troubled by this wicked monkey doing favours for the Daityas, hero. Luckily he’s dead.’ 22

## \|\| 5.36.23 \|\|

*ityuktvā divamājagmurdevā hṛṣṭaḥsaguhyakāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Many peerless deeds like this were done by prudent Balarāma, who was really Śeṣa, supporter of the earth. 23

## Chapter 37 - The destruction of the Yādavas

## \|\| 5.37.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ daityavadhaṃ kṛṣṇe baladeva sahāyavān \|*  
*cakre duṣṭakṣitīśānāṃ tathaiva jagataḥ kṛte \|\| 1 \|\|*

Thus Kṛṣṇa, helped by Balarāma, destroyed the Daityas and the evil kings to benefit the world. 1

## \|\| 5.37.2 \|\|

*kṣiteś ca bhāraṃ bhagavānphālgunena samanvitaḥ \|*  
*avatārayāmāsa vibhuḥsamastākṣohiṇīvadhāt \|\| 2 \|\|*

With Arjuna’s assistance, Lord Hari eased the burden of the earth by destroying many armies. 2

## \|\| 5.37.3 \|\|

*kṛtvā bhārāvataramaṃ bhuvo hatvākhilānnṛpān \|*  
*śāpavyājena viprāṇāmupasaṃhṛtavānkulam \|\| 3 \|\|*

After he’d accomplished this and brought down all the monarchs, he then annihilated his own clan, the Yādavas, owing to a brahmin’s curse. 3

## \|\| 5.37.4 \|\|

*utsṛjya dvārakāṃ kṛṣṇastyaktvā mānuṣyamātmanaḥ \|*  
*sāṃśo viṣṇumayaṃ sthānaṃ praviveśa mune nijam \|\| 4 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa then abandoned Dvārakā and left his mortal body. This self-born being, with all his manifested aspects, then resumed his natural state as Viṣṇu. 4

## \|\| 5.37.5 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*sa vipraśāpavyājena saṃjahresvakulaṃ katham \|*  
*kathaṃ ca mānuṣaṃ dehamutsasarjajanārdanaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Why did he destroy his clan owing to the brahmin’s curse, and how did Janārdana leave his mortal form? 5

## \|\| 5.37.6 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*viśvamitrastathākaṇvo nāradaścamahāmuniḥ \|*  
*piṇḍārake mahārīrthe dṛṣṭvā yadukumārakaiḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Parāśara:

One day, at the sacred site of Piṇḍāraka, some young boys of the Yadu clan spied Viśvāmitra, Kaṇva and the great sage Nārada. 6

## \|\| 5.37.7 \|\|

*tataste yauvanonmattā bhāvikāryapracoditāḥ \|*  
*sāṃbaṃ jāṃbavatīputraṃ bhūṣayitvā striyaṃ yathā \|\| 7 \|\|*

These fine lads, impelled by fate, dressed Sāmba, son of Jāmbavatī, as a woman. 7

## \|\| 5.37.8 \|\|

*praśritāstānmunīnūcuḥ praṇipātapuraḥsaram \|*  
*iyaṃ strī putrakāmā vai brūta kiṃ janayiṣyati \|\| 8 \|\|*

They then approached the sages and, bowing with respect, said, ‘This is Babhru’s wife, and she desires a son. Will her baby be a boy or girl?’ 8

## \|\| 5.37.9 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*divyajñānopapannāste vipralabdhāḥ kumārakaiḥ \|*  
*munayaḥ kupitāḥ procurmusalaṃ janayiṣyati \|\| 9 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The sages, endowed with divine perception, felt insulted by the boys and angrily declared, ‘She will bear a pestle, which will end the clan of Yādavas!’ 9

## \|\| 5.37.10 \|\|

*sarvayādavasaṃhārakāraṇaṃ bhuvanottaram \|*  
*yenākhilakulotsādau yādavānāṃ bhaviṣyati \|\| 10 \|\|*

Thus addressed by the sages, the youths told Ugrasena what they’d done, and Sāmba later brought forth a pestle from his belly. 10

## \|\| 5.37.11 \|\|

*ityuktāste kumārāstu ācacakṣuryathātatham \|*  
*ugrasenāya musalaṃ jajñe sāṃbasya codarāt \|\| 11 \|\|*

Ugrasena had that iron pestle ground to dust. This was thrown into the ocean, where it grew as reeds. 11

## \|\| 5.37.12 \|\|

*tadugraseno musalamayaścūrṇamakārayat \|*  
*jajñe taderakāyūrṇaṃ prakṣiptaṃ tair mahodadhau \|\| 12 \|\|*

When the Andhaka clansmen destroyed the pestle, brahmin, there remained one blade-like splinter they couldn’t crush. 12

## \|\| 5.37.13 \|\|

*musalasyātha lohasya pūrṇitasya tu yādavaiḥ \|*  
*khaṇḍaṃ cūrṇitaśeṣaṃ tu tato yattomarākṛti \|\| 13 \|\|*

This they also cast into the sea, where it was swallowed by a fish. The fish was caught by fishermen, and a hunter known as Jaras found the fragment in its belly. 13

## \|\| 5.37.14 \|\|

*tadapyaṃbunidhau kṣiptaṃ matsyo jagrāha jālibhiḥ \|*  
*ghātitasyodarāttasya lubdho jagraha tajjārāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Although the slayer of Madhu foresaw the final outcome, the lord had no desire to change the course of fate. 14

## \|\| 5.37.15 \|\|

*vijñātaparamārthopi bhagavānmadhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*naicchattadanyathā kartuṃ vidhinā yatsamīhitam \|\| 15 \|\|*

The gods dispatched a messenger to Keśava, who bowed before him, saying, ‘Lord, I’m an envoy sent in secret by the deities. 15

## \|\| 5.37.16 \|\|

*devaiś ca prahito vāyuḥ praṇipattyāha keśavam \|*  
*rajasyevamahaṃ dūtaḥ prahito bhagavansuraiḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Mighty Indra with the Vasus, Aśvins, Maruts, Ādityas, Rudras, Sādhyas and the others wish to tell you this, so listen carefully, Lord. 16

## \|\| 5.37.17 \|\|

*vasvaśvimarudādityarudrasādhyādibhiḥ saha \|*  
*vijñāpayati śakrastvāṃ tadidaṃ śrūyatāṃ vibho \|\| 17 \|\|*

It’s more than a hundred years since you, Lord, propitiated by the thirteen gods, descended to the earth to ease her burden. 17

## \|\| 5.37.18 \|\|

*bhārāvataraṇārthāya varṣāṇāmadhikaṃ śatam \|*  
*bhagavānavatīrṇotra tridaśeḥsaha coditaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

The wicked Daityas have been destroyed and the burden of the earth relieved. May we thirteen deities in threefold heavens always have you as our leader. 18

## \|\| 5.37.19 \|\|

*durvṛttā nihatā daityā bhuvo bhāro 'vatāritaḥ \|*  
*tvayā manāthāstridaśā bhavantu tridive sadā \|\| 19 \|\|*

More than a hundred years have passed, universal Lord. Now, if it pleases you, return to heaven. 19

## \|\| 5.37.20 \|\|

*tadatītaṃ jagannātha varṣāṇāmadhikaṃ śatam \|*  
*idānīṃ gamyatāṃ svargo bhavatā yadi rocate \|\| 20 \|\|*

The deities also said that, if you’re happy here, then you should remain as long as your dependants need you.’ 20

## \|\| 5.37.21 \|\|

*devair vijñāpyate deva tathātraiva ratistava \|*  
*tatsthīyatāṃ yathākālamāstheyamanujīvibhiḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

The Lord:

All you’ve said is known to me, messenger, and I’ve already begun the Yādavas’ destruction. 21

## \|\| 5.37.22 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*yattvamātthākhilaṃ dūta vedmy etad aham apy uta \|*  
*prārabdha eva hi mayā yādavānāṃparikṣayaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Even now, the burden of the earth will not be eased until the Yādavas are destroyed. Before I leave, I’ll annihilate them all in seven short nights. 22

## \|\| 5.37.23 \|\|

*bhuvo nādyāpi bhāro 'yaṃ yādavairanibarhitaiḥ \|*  
*avatārya karomyetat saptarātreṇa satvaraḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

When the ocean has reclaimed the land I took for Dvārakā, and I’ve destroyed the clan, I’ll then return to the abode of the thirteen deities. 23

## \|\| 5.37.24 \|\|

*yathā gṛhītamaṃbhodherdattvāhaṃ dvārakābhuvam \|*  
*yādavānupasaṃhṛtya yāsyāmi tridaśālayam \|\| 24 \|\|*

Tell the king of gods and others that, when I’ve left this mortal form, I’ll come back with Balarāma. 24

## \|\| 5.37.25 \|\|

*manuṣyadehamutsṛjya saṃkarṣamasahāyavān \|*  
*prāpta evāsmi mantavyo devendreṇa tathāmaraiḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Jarāsandha and the other kings who were a burden on the earth have all been killed, yet even a single Yādava boy is no less a load than they. 25

## \|\| 5.37.26 \|\|

*jarāsaṃdhādayo ye 'nye nihatā bhārahetavaḥ \|*  
*kṣitestebhyaḥ kumāro 'pi yadūnāṃ nāpacīyate \|\| 26 \|\|*

Tell the gods that I’ll return to guard their realm when I’ve removed this weighty burden from the earth. 26

## \|\| 5.37.27 \|\|

*tadetaṃ sumahābhāram avatārya kṣiter aham \|*  
*yāsyāmyamaralokasya pālanāya bravīhi tān \|\| 27 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus addressed by Vāsudeva, the heavenly envoy bowed to him, Maitreya, and, with the speed of the deities, returned to Indra’s presence. 27

## \|\| 5.37.28 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityukto vāsudevena devadūtaḥ praṇamya tam \|*  
*maitreya divyayā gatyā devarājāntikaṃ yayau \|\| 28 \|\|*

By day and night, the lord observed bad omens in the sky, on the ground and in the air, portending the ruin of Dvārakā. 28

## \|\| 5.37.29 \|\|

*bhagavānapyathotpātāndivyabhaumāntarikṣajān \|*  
*dadarśa dvārakāpuryāṃ vināśāya divāniśam \|\| 29 \|\|*

Noting these, he then addressed the Yādavas, ‘Behold these dreadful portents! We’ll go at once to Prabhāsa to ward them off.’ 29

## \|\| 5.37.30 \|\|

*tāndṛṣṭvā yādavānāha paśyadhvamatidāruṇān \|*  
*mahotpātāñchamāyaiṣāṃ prabhāsaṃ yāma mā ciram \|\| 30 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Uddhava, his foremost devotee, said to Hari with a bow, ‘Tell me, Lord, what I must do, as I suspect you’ll reabsorb the clan. 30

## \|\| 5.37.31 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evamukte tu kṛṣṇena yādavapravārastataḥ \|*  
*mahābhāgavataḥ prāha praṇipatyoddhavo harim \|\| 31 \|\|*

Signs portending its destruction have been seen, eternal Lord.’ 31

## \|\| 5.37.32 \|\|

*bhagavanyanmayā kāryaṃ tadājñāpaya sāṃpratam \|*  
*manye kulamidaṃ sarvaṃ bhagavānsaṃhariṣyati \|\| 32 \|\|*

The Lord:

Go with the speed of deities, which you’ll acquire through my mercy, to the auspicious ashram of Badarīka on Mount Gandhamādana, residence of Nara and Nārāyaṇa, whose existence purifies the world. 32

## \|\| 5.37.33 \|\|

*nāśāyāsya nimittāni kulasyācyuta lakṣaye \|\| 33 \|\|*

Contemplate me there and, with my blessing, you’ll achieve perfection. I’ll return to heaven when I’ve reabsorbed the clan. 33

## \|\| 5.37.34 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*gaccha tvaṃ divyayā gatyā matprasādasamutthayā \|*  
*yadbadaryāśramaṃ puṇyaṃ gandhamādanaparvate \|*  
*naranārāyaṇasthāne tatpavitraṃ mahītale \|\| 34 \|\|*

The moment I depart from Dvārakā, the ocean will reclaim it. 34

## \|\| 5.37.35 \|\|

*manmanā matprasādena tatra siddhimavāpsyasi \|*  
*ahaṃ svargaṃ gamiṣyāmi hyupasaṃhṛtya vaikulam \|\| 35 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, Uddhava bowed to Hari and left for Nara and Nārāyaṇa’s abode as suggested. 35

## \|\| 5.37.36 \|\|

*dvārakāṃ ca mayā tyaktāṃ samudraḥ plāvayiṣyati \|*  
*madveśma caikaṃ muktvātu bhayānmatto jalāśaye \|*  
*tatra sannihitaścāhaṃ bhaktānāṃ hitakāmyayā \|\| 36 \|\|*

Mounted on swift chariots, the Yādavas then all set out for Prabhāsa, brahmin, with Kṛṣṇa, Balarāma and the rest. 36

## \|\| 5.37.37 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktaḥ praṇipatyainaṃ jagāmāśu tapevanam \|*  
*naranārāyaṇasthānaṃ keśavenānumoditaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

On reaching that location, the Kukura and Andhaka clansmen bathed and then, encouraged by Vāsudeva, began to drink in earnest. 37

## \|\| 5.37.38 \|\|

*tataste yādavāḥsarve rathānāruhya śīghragān \|*  
*prabhāsaṃ prayayuḥsārdhaṃ kṛṣṇarāmādibhir dvija \|\| 38 \|\|*

Friction rose among them as they drank. This friction sparked an argument, and a fight flared up, which led to their destruction. 38

## \|\| 5.37.39 \|\|

*prabhāsaṃ samanuprāptāḥ kukurāndhakavṛṣṇayaḥ \|*  
*cakrustatra mahāpānaṃ vāsudevena coditāḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

Victims of their destiny, they struck each other with weapons and, when the weapons were exhausted, they seized the reeds that grew nearby. 39

## \|\| 5.37.40 \|\|

*pibatāṃ tatra caiteṣāṃ saṃgharṣeṇa parasparam \|*  
*ativādendhano jajñe kalahāgniḥ kṣayāvahaḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

The reeds were thunderbolts in their hands, with which they slew each other in that terrible affray. 40

## \|\| 5.37.41 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*svaṃsvaṃ vai bhuñjatāṃ teṣāṃ kalahaḥ kiṃnimittakaḥ \|*  
*saṃgharṣo vā dvijaśreṣṭha tanmamākhyātumarhasi \|\| 41 \|\|*

Led by Pradyumna and Sāmba, Kṛtavarman, Sātyaki, Aniruddha, Pṛthu and Vipṛthu, 41

## \|\| 5.37.42 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*mṛṣṭaṃ madīyamannaṃ te na mṛṣṭamiti jalpatām \|*  
*mṛṣṭāmṛṣṭakathā jajñe saṃgharṣakalahau tataḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

Cāruvarman, Cāruka, Akrūra and many others, brahmin, they fought with bolts of lightning in the form of reeds. 42

## \|\| 5.37.43 \|\|

*tataścānyonyamabhyetya kridhasaṃraktalocanāḥ \|*  
*jaghnuḥ parasparaṃ te tu śastrair daivabalātkṛtāḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

Hari tried to stop them, but the Yādavas all thought he’d come to their assistance, so battled on regardless. 43

## \|\| 5.37.44 \|\|

*kṣīṇaśastrāś ca jagṛhuḥ pratyāsannāmathairakām \|\| 44 \|\|*

Furious Kṛṣṇa grabbed some reeds, which became a club of iron for the Yādavas’ destruction. 44

## \|\| 5.37.45 \|\|

*erakā tu gṛhītā vai vajrabhūteva lakṣyate \|*  
*tayā parasparaṃ jaghnuḥsaṃprahāre sudāruṇe \|\| 45 \|\|*

Wielding this weapon, he slew the murderous clansmen, leaving none alive, while others clashed together and slaughtered one another. 45

## \|\| 5.37.46 \|\|

*pradyumnasāṃbapramukhāḥ kṛtavarmātha sātyakiḥ \|*  
*aniruddhādayaścānye pṛthurvipṛthureva ca \|\| 46 \|\|*

Jaitra, the discus-wielder’s chariot, best of brahmins, was swept into the ocean with its steeds as its driver, Dāruka, looked on. 46

## \|\| 5.37.47 \|\|

*cāruvarmā cārukaś ca tathākrūrādayo dvija \|*  
*erakārūpibhir vajrais te nijaghnuḥ parasparam \|\| 47 \|\|*

Hari’s discus, club and bow, his quivers, conch and sword all circled him respectfully, then departed by the solar path. 47

## \|\| 5.37.48 \|\|

*nivārayāmāsa hariryādavāṃste ca keśavam \|*  
*sahāyaṃ menirerīṇāṃ prāptaṃ jaghnuḥ parasparam \|\| 48 \|\|*

Soon, great sage, not one Yādava remained alive, except for mighty Kṛṣṇa himself and Dāruka. 48

## \|\| 5.37.49 \|\|

*kṛṣṇo 'pi kupitasteṣāmerakāmuṣṭimādade \|*  
*vadhāya so 'pi musalaṃ muṣṭarlauhamabhūttadā \|\| 49 \|\|*

As they wandered, Dāruka and Kṛṣṇa saw Balarāma seated by a tree with a giant serpent coming from his mouth. 49

## \|\| 5.37.50 \|\|

*jaghāna tena niḥśeṣānyādavānātatāyinaḥ \|*  
*jaghnuste sahasābhyetya tathānyepi parasparam \|\| 50 \|\|*

That massive coiling creature emerged from Balarāma and set off for the ocean, worshipped by the siddhas and other nāgas. 50

## \|\| 5.37.51 \|\|

*tataścārṇavamadhyena jaitro 'sau cakriṇo rathaḥ \|*  
*paśyato dārukasyātha prāyādaśvair dhṛto dvija \|\| 51 \|\|*

The Ocean came to welcome him with gifts of hospitality, and he slid into its waters while foremost serpents sang his praises. 51

## \|\| 5.37.52 \|\|

*cakraṃ gadā tathā śārṅga tūṇīśaṅkhosireva ca \|*  
*pradakṣiṇaṃ hariṃ kṛtvā jagmurādityavartmanā \|\| 52 \|\|*

Seeing Balarāma had withdrawn, Keśava said to Dāruka, ‘Report all this to Vasudeva and to Ugrasena: 52

## \|\| 5.37.53 \|\|

*kṣaṇena nābhavatkaścidyādavānāmaghātitaḥ \|*  
*ṛte kṛṣṇaṃ mahātmānaṃ dārukaṃ ca mahāmune \|\| 53 \|\|*

Balarāma has departed and the Yādavas are no more. I’ll repose in yoga, then I’ll leave my mortal form. 53

## \|\| 5.37.54 \|\|

*caṅkramyamāṇau tau rāmaṃ vṛkṣamūle kṛtāsanam \|*  
*dadṛśāte mukhāccāsya niṣkrāmantaṃ mahoragam \|\| 54 \|\|*

Tell Āhuka and Dvārakā’s inhabitants that the ocean will soon reclaim their city. 54

## \|\| 5.37.55 \|\|

*niṣkramya sa makhāttasya mahābhogo bhujaṅgamaḥ \|*  
*prayayāvarṇavaṃ siddhaiḥ pujyamānastathoragaiḥ \|\| 55 \|\|*

They should prepare for Arjuna’s arrival, as they cannot stay in Dvārakā. When the son of Pāṇḍu leaves, 55

## \|\| 5.37.56 \|\|

*tatorghyamādāya tadā jaladhiḥsaṃmukhaṃ yayau \|*  
*praviveśa tatastoyaṃ pūjitaḥ pannagottamaiḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

They must follow that Kuru scion wherever he may lead. 56

## \|\| 5.37.57 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā balasya niryāṇaṃ dārukaṃ prāha keśavaḥ \|*  
*idaṃ sarvaṃ samācakṣva vasudevograsenayoḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

Go and repeat my words to Arjuna, son of Kuntī: he must protect these folk, my kin, with all his might. 57

## \|\| 5.37.58 \|\|

*niryāṇaṃ balabhadrasya yādavānāṃ tathā kṣayam \|*  
*yogo sthitvāhamapyetatparityakṣye kalevaram \|\| 58 \|\|*

Accompanied by Arjuna, you must lead them all from Dvārakā, and Vajra will become the Yadus’ king.’ 58

## \|\| 5.37.59 \|\|

*vācyaś ca dvārakāvāsī janaḥ sarvas tathāhukaḥ \|*  
*yathomāṃ nagarīṃ sarvāṃ samudraḥ plāvayiṣyati \|\| 59 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, Dāruka bowed to Kṛṣṇa many times, circled him respectfully, then set out as instructed. 59

## \|\| 5.37.60 \|\|

*tasmādbhavadbhiḥ sarvais tu pratīkṣyo hyarjunāgamaḥ \|*  
*na stheyaṃ dvārakāmadhye niṣkrānte tatra pāṇḍave \|\| 60 \|\|*

When the prudent driver had departed, he did as Kṛṣṇa ordered him in Dvārakā, leading Arjuna to the city and setting Vajra on the throne. 60

## \|\| 5.37.61 \|\|

*tenaiva saha gantavyāṃ yatra yāti sa kauravaḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

Lord Govinda, who maintained the highest form of Vāsudeva among the living, now merged into the Absolute. 61

## \|\| 5.37.62 \|\|

*gatvā ca brūhi kainteyamarjunaṃ vacanānmama \|*  
*pālanīyastvayā śaktyā jano 'yaṃ matparigrahaḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

Respecting the words of the brahmin Durvāsas, Kṛṣṇa engaged in yoga, best of sages, seated with one foot upon his knee. 62

## \|\| 5.37.63 \|\|

*tvamarjunena sahito dvāravatyāṃ tathā janam \|*  
*gṛhītvā yādi vajraś ca yadurājo bhaviṣyati \|\| 63 \|\|*

The hunter known as Jaras appeared at that location. His arrow bore the remnant shard of iron from the pestle at its tip. 63

## \|\| 5.37.64 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityukto dārukaḥ kṛṣṇaṃ praṇipatya punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*pradakṣiṇaṃ ca bahuśaḥ kṛtvā prāyādyathoditam \|\| 64 \|\|*

Standing at a distance, he mistook the foot of Kṛṣṇa for a deer, best of brahmins, and, with that very missile, he shot him in the sole. 64

## \|\| 5.37.65 \|\|

*sa ca gatvā tadācaṣṭa dvārakāyāṃ tathār'junam \|*  
*ānināya mahābuddhirvajraṃ cakre tathā nṛpam \|\| 65 \|\|*

Drawing closer, Jaras saw the four-armed being. He threw himself upon the ground and begged repeatedly for mercy: 65

## \|\| 5.37.66 \|\|

*bhagavānapi govindo vāsudevātmakaṃ param \|*  
*brahmātmani samāropya sarvabhūteṣvadhārayat \|*  
*niṣprapañce mahābhāga saṃyojyātmānamātmani \|*  
*turyāvasthasalīlaṃ ca śetesma puruṣottamaḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

‘I didn’t know what I was doing and mistook you for a deer. Forgive me. I’m consumed by shame, so please don’t punish me anymore.’ 66

## \|\| 5.37.67 \|\|

*saṃmānayandvijavaco durvāsā yaduvāca ha \|*  
*yogayukto 'bhavatpādaṃ kṛtvā jānuni sattama \|\| 67 \|\|*

Parāśara:

‘Have no fear,’ the Lord replied. ‘Go with my blessing, hunter, to the heavens where gods reside.’ 67

## \|\| 5.37.68 \|\|

*āyayau sa jarānāma tadā tatra sa lubdhakaḥ \|*  
*musalāvaśeṣalohaikasāyakanyastatomaraḥ \|\| 68 \|\|*

Parāśara:

As soon as he had spoken, a celestial chariot appeared. The hunter climbed aboard and departed for the heavenly realms, with Kṛṣṇa’s blessing. 68

## \|\| 5.37.69 \|\|

*sa tatpādaṃ mṛgākaramavekṣyārādavisthitaḥ \|*  
*tale vivyādha tenaiva tomareṇa dvijottama \|\| 69 \|\|*

After Jaras had set off, the lord merged with the highest, eternal, inconceivable, purest form of Vāsudeva, 69

## \|\| 5.37.70 \|\|

*tataś ca dadṛśe tatra cadurbāhudharaṃ naram \|*  
*praṇipatyāha caivainaṃ prasīdeti punaḥ punaḥ \|\| 70 \|\|*

Which is birthless, ageless, indestructible, immeasurable and universal. He then transcended the threefold state and abandoned his mortal form. 70

## Chapter 38 - Arjuna leads the women from Dvārakā

## \|\| 5.38.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*arjunopi tadānvīkṣya rāmakṛṣṇakalevare \|*  
*saṃskāraṃ laṃbhayāmāsa tathānyeṣāmanukramāt \|\| 1 \|\|*

On finding the remains of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, Arjuna performed the rites for them and the other Yādavas in turn. 1

## \|\| 5.38.2 \|\|

*aṣṭau mahiṣyaḥ kathitā rukmiṇīpramukhāstu yāḥ \|*  
*upaguhya harerdeha viviśustā hutāśanam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa’s eight consorts, whom I mentioned, led by Rukmiṇī, embraced the corpse of Hari and ascended his funerary pyre. 2

## \|\| 5.38.3 \|\|

*revatī cāpi rāmasya dehamāśliṣya sattamā \|*  
*viveśa jvalitaṃ vāhniṃ tatsaṃgāhlādaśītalam \|\| 3 \|\|*

Revatī embraced Balarāma’s body, best of sages, and entered the blazing fire, which seemed cool to her on account of her joyous union with her husband. 3

## \|\| 5.38.4 \|\|

*ugrasenastu tacchutvā tathaivānakadundubhiḥ \|*  
*devakī rohiṇī caiva viviśurjātavedasam \|\| 4 \|\|*

Hearing of this, Ugrasena, Vasudeva, Devakī and Rohiṇī also entered the flames. 4

## \|\| 5.38.5 \|\|

*tatorjunaḥ pretakāryaṃ kṛtvā teṣāṃ yathāvidhi \|*  
*niścakrāma janaṃ sarve gṛhītvā vajrameva ca \|\| 5 \|\|*

After Arjuna had duly carried out rituals for them all, he led the people, including Vajra, from the city. 5

## \|\| 5.38.6 \|\|

*dvāravatyā viniṣkrāntāḥ kṛṣṇapatnyaḥ sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*vajraṃ janaṃ ca kauteyaḥ pālayañchanakair yayau \|\| 6 \|\|*

Watching over Kṛṣṇa’s myriad consorts, with Vajra and the others as they abandoned Dvārakā, the son of Kuntī set forth slowly. 6

## \|\| 5.38.7 \|\|

*sabhā sudharmā kṛṣṇena kartyaloke samujjhite \|*  
*svargaṃ jagāma maitreya pārijātaś ca pādapaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

When Kṛṣṇa left this mortal realm, Maitreya, the assembly hall Sudharmā and the Pārijāta tree both rose to heaven. 7

## \|\| 5.38.8 \|\|

*yasmindine hariryāto divaṃ saṃtyajya medinīm \|*  
*tasminnevāvatīrṇo 'yaṃ kālakāyo balī kaliḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

On the very day that Hari left the earth for heaven, this dark and potent Kali age descended on the world. 8

## \|\| 5.38.9 \|\|

*plāvayāmāsa tāṃ śūnyāṃ dvārakāṃ ca mahodadhiḥ \|*  
*vāsudevagṛhaṃ tvekaṃ na plāvayati sāgaraḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

The mighty ocean flooded empty Dvārakā, sparing just the Yadu god’s abode. 9

## \|\| 5.38.10 \|\|

*nātikrāntumalaṃ brahmaṃstadadyāpi mahodadhiḥ \|*  
*nityaṃ sannihitastatra bhagavānkeśavo yataḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Even now, the ocean cannot take it, brahmin, as Lord Keśava always dwells there. 10

## \|\| 5.38.11 \|\|

*tadatīva mahāpuṇyaṃ sarvapātakanāśanam \|*  
*viṣṇuśriyānvitaṃ sthānaṃ dṛṣṭvā pāmādvimucyate \|\| 11 \|\|*

Having seen that place of great good fortune, where Viṣṇu, who removes all wickedness, disports himself, one is freed from sin. 11

## \|\| 5.38.12 \|\|

*pārthaḥ pañcanade deśe bahudhānyadhanānvite \|*  
*cakāra vāsaṃ sarvasya janasya munisattamaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

In the land that’s watered by five rivers and abounds in grain, Arjuna, son of Pṛthā, made a home for all the people, best of sages. 12

## \|\| 5.38.13 \|\|

*tato lobhaḥsamabhavatpārthenaikena dhanvinā \|*  
*dṛṣṭvāstriyo nīyamānā dasyūnāṃ nihateśvarāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Seeing women bereft of husbands, led by the archer Arjuna alone, the cupidity of certain bandits was aroused. 13

## \|\| 5.38.14 \|\|

*tataste pāpakamāṇo lobhopahṛtacetasaḥ \|*  
*ābhīrā mantrayāmāsuḥsametyānyantadurmadāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Those wicked men whose hearts were filled with avarice met with fierce Ābhīra tribesmen and conferred with them: 14

## \|\| 5.38.15 \|\|

*ayamekor'juno dhanvī strījanaṃ nihateśvaram \|*  
*nayatyasmānatikramya dhigetadbhavatāṃ balam \|\| 15 \|\|*

‘Here, the single archer Arjuna, having overrun us, is leading widowed women through our lands. Shame upon your army! 15

## \|\| 5.38.16 \|\|

*hatvā garvasamārūḍho bhīṣmadromajayadrathān \|*  
*karṇādīṃś ca na jānāti balaṃ grāmanivāsinām \|\| 16 \|\|*

Slaying Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Jayadratha, Karṇa and the others, Arjuna grew proud, but he doesn’t know how strong we villagers can be. 16

## \|\| 5.38.17 \|\|

*yaṣṭihastānavekṣyāsmāndhanuṣpāṇiḥsa durmatiḥ \|*  
*sarvānevāvajānāti kiṃ vo bāhubhir unnataiḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Come! Come! Take up your staves! This fool looks down on us. What use are your strong arms?’ 17

## \|\| 5.38.18 \|\|

*tato yaṣṭipraharaṇādasyavo loṣṭadhāriṇaḥ \|*  
*sahasraśobhyadhāvanta taṃ janaṃ nihateśvaram \|\| 18 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thousands of plundering bandits armed with staves then fell on the defenceless throng. 18

## \|\| 5.38.19 \|\|

*tato nirbhartsya kauteyaḥ prāhābhīrānhasanniva \|*  
*nivartadhvamadharmajñā yadi na stha mumūrṣavaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

The son of Kuntī checked the tribes and, with a laugh, he said, ‘Retreat now—ignorant of virtue—if you do not want to die.’ 19

## \|\| 5.38.20 \|\|

*avajñāya vacastasya jagṛhuste tadā dhanam \|*  
*strīdhanaṃ caiva maitreya viṣvaksena piragraham \|\| 20 \|\|*

But they ignored his warning and seized the women—once the wives of mighty Kṛṣṇa—Maitreya, along with their possessions. 20

## \|\| 5.38.21 \|\|

*tatorjuno dhanurdivyaṃ gāṇḍīvamajaraṃ yudhi \|*  
*āropayitumārebhe na śaśāka ca vīryavān \|\| 21 \|\|*

When that hero Arjuna tried to string Gāṇḍīva, his heavenly bow that never failed in battle, he found it was impossible. 21

## \|\| 5.38.22 \|\|

*cakāra sajyaṃ kṛcchrāccataccābhūcchithilaṃ punaḥ \|*  
*na sasmāra tatostrāṇi cintayannapi pāṇḍavaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

He strove to tighten up the string, but it came undone again. The son of Pāṇḍu tried to summon magic weapons, but his memory also failed him. 22

## \|\| 5.38.23 \|\|

*śarānmumoca caiteṣu pārthā vairiṣvamarṣitaḥ \|*  
*tvagbhedaṃ de paraṃ cakrurastā gāṇḍīvadhanvinā \|\| 23 \|\|*

Impatient Arjuna, wielder of Gāṇḍīva, loosed arrows at his foes, but they barely scratched their skin. 23

## \|\| 5.38.24 \|\|

*vahninā ye 'kṣayā dattāḥ śarāstepi kṣayaṃ yayuḥ \|*  
*yuddhyataḥsaha gopālair arjunasya bhavakṣaye \|\| 24 \|\|*

Arjuna’s unfailing shafts, gifts from Agni, were destroyed in battle with the herdsmen, and now his very life was threatened. 24

## \|\| 5.38.25 \|\|

*acintayacca kainteyaḥ kṛṣṇasyaiva hi tadbalam \|*  
*yanmayā śarasaṃghātaiḥ sakalā bhūbhṛto hatāḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

The son of Kuntī recalled how Kṛṣṇa’s power had let him vanquish hosts of earthly kings with showers of arrows. 25

## \|\| 5.38.26 \|\|

*miṣataḥ pāṇḍuputrasya tatastāḥ pramadottamāḥ \|*  
*ābhīrair apakṛṣyanta kāmaṃ cānyāḥ pradudruvuḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

While Pāṇḍu’s son looked on, some splendid women were seized by Ābhīras, while other women ran towards them lustily. 26

## \|\| 5.38.27 \|\|

*tataḥ śareṣu kṣīṇeṣu dhanuṣkoṭyā dhanañjayaḥ \|*  
*jaghāna dasyūṃste cāsya prahārāñjahasurmune \|\| 27 \|\|*

When all his arrows were exhausted, Dhanaṃjaya struck the brigands with his bow, sage, but they merely mocked his blows. 27

## \|\| 5.38.28 \|\|

*prekṣatastasya pārthasya puṣṇyandhakavarastriyaḥ \|*  
*jagmurādāya te mlecchāḥ samastā munisattama \|\| 28 \|\|*

While Arjuna was watching, the tribesmen all set off, best of sages, taking those lovely Vṛṣṇi and Andhaka women with them. 28

## \|\| 5.38.29 \|\|

*tataḥsuduḥkhito jiṣṇuḥ kaṣṭaṃ kaṣṭamiti bruvan \|*  
*aho bhagavatānena muṣṭosmīti ruroda vai \|\| 29 \|\|*

Broken-hearted and lamenting, Arjuna cried out, ‘Alas, alas! I’ve been forsaken by the lord. 29

## \|\| 5.38.30 \|\|

*taddhanustāni śastrāṇi sa rathaste ca vājinaḥ \|*  
*sarvamekapade naṣṭaṃ dānamaśrotriye yathā \|\| 30 \|\|*

This bow, these weapons, this chariot with its steeds—all is wasted in an instant, like donations to a brahmin who’s unworthy. 30

## \|\| 5.38.31 \|\|

*ahotibalabaddaivaṃ vinā tena mahātmanā \|*  
*yadasāmarthyayuktepi nicavarge jayapradam \|\| 31 \|\|*

Alas! Powerful indeed is destiny. Without great Kṛṣṇa by my side, Fate awarded victory to the lesser party, even though they’re weak. 31

## \|\| 5.38.32 \|\|

*tau bāhū sa ca me muṣṭiḥ sthānaṃ tatsosmi cārjunaḥ \|*  
*puṇyenaiva vinā tena gataṃ sarvamasāratām \|\| 32 \|\|*

These are my arms, this is my fist, here is my place and I am Arjuna! But without auspicious Kṛṣṇa, it’s all in vain. 32

## \|\| 5.38.33 \|\|

*mamārjunatvaṃ bhīmasya bhīmatvaṃ tatkṛte dhruvam \|*  
*vinā tena yadā bhīrair jitohaṃ rathināṃ varaḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

The Arjuna-ness of Arjuna and Bhīma’s Bhīma-ness were doubtless due to Kṛṣṇa. Without him, I’ve been defeated by mere tribesmen. How otherwise could this happen?’ 33

## \|\| 5.38.34 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*itthaṃ vadanyayau jiṣmuridraprasthaṃ purottamam \|*  
*cakāra tatra rājānaṃ vajraṃ yādavanandanam \|\| 34 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, Arjuna departed for the wondrous city known as Mathurā. There he enthroned Vajra, delighter of the Yādavas. 34

## \|\| 5.38.35 \|\|

*sa dadarśa tato vyāsaṃ phālgunaḥ kānanāśrayam \|*  
*tamupetya mahābhāgaṃ vinayenābyavādayat \|\| 35 \|\|*

In Mathurā, Arjuna found Vyāsa living in a forest ashram and, approaching that blessed seer, he greeted him respectfully. 35

## \|\| 5.38.36 \|\|

*taṃ vandamānaṃ caraṇāvavalokya muniściram \|*  
*uvāca vākyaṃ vicchāyaḥ kathamadya tvamīdṛśaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

The sage looked long at Arjuna lying at his feet and asked, ‘What’s the reason for your lacklustre appearance? 36

## \|\| 5.38.37 \|\|

*avīrajonugamanaṃ brahmahatyā kṛtātha vā \|*  
*dṛḍhāśābhaṅgaduḥkhīva bhraṣṭacchāyo 'si sāṃpratam \|\| 37 \|\|*

Have you had sex with a menstruating woman? Have you killed a brahmin? You’re so pale you’ve surely endured some tragedy. 37

## \|\| 5.38.38 \|\|

*sāṃtānikādayo vāte yācamānā nirākṛtāḥ \|*  
*agamyastrīratirvā tvaṃ yenāsi vigataprabhaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

Have your prayers for a son or something else all failed? Have you had sex with a woman who shouldn’t be approached? Is that why you look so jaded? 38

## \|\| 5.38.39 \|\|

*bhuṅte 'pradāyaviprebhyo mṛṣṭamekotha vā bhavān \|*  
*kiṃ vā kṛpaṇavittāni hṛtāni bhavatār'juna \|\| 39 \|\|*

Have you been eating delicacies alone, not offering them to brahmins first? Have you stolen a poor man’s goods, Arjuna? 39

## \|\| 5.38.40 \|\|

*kacinnu śurpavātasya gocaratvaṃ gator'juna \|*  
*duṣṭacakṣurhato vāsi niḥśrīkaḥ kathamanyathā \|\| 40 \|\|*

Were you downwind from a winnowing fan, Arjuna, or has the evil eye befallen you? Why else would you look so pale? 40

## \|\| 5.38.41 \|\|

*spṛṣṭo nakhāṃbhasā vātha ghaṭavāryukṣitopi vā \|*  
*kena tvaṃ vāsi vicchāyo nyūnair vā yudhi nījātaḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

Were you touched by water from someone’s fingernails, or splashed with water from a pitcher? Does that explain your pale complexion? Or have you lost a battle to inferior opponents?’ 41

## \|\| 5.38.42 \|\|

*śrīparāśarauvāca*  
*tataḥ pārtho viniśvāsya śrūyatāṃ bhagavanniti \|*  
*proktvā yathāvadācaṣṭe vyāsāyātmaparābhavam \|\| 42 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Arjuna, son of Pṛthā, sighed and said, ‘Listen’, then explained to Vyāsa how he’d been defeated. 42

## \|\| 5.38.43 \|\|

*arjuna uvāca*  
*yadbalaṃ yacca mattejo yadvīryaṃ yaḥ parākramaḥ \|*  
*yā śrīśchāyā ca naḥ sosmānparityajya harirgataḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

Arjuna:

Hari, who was our strength, energy, courage, prowess, glory and lustre, has abandoned us and gone away. 43

## \|\| 5.38.44 \|\|

*īśvareṇāpi mahatā smitapūrvābhibhāṣiṇā \|*  
*hīnā vayaṃ mune tena jātāstṛṇamayā iva \|\| 44 \|\|*

Without that great being, sage, who was one of us and always addressed us with a smile, we’re merely men of straw. 44

## \|\| 5.38.45 \|\|

*astrāṇāṃ sāyakānāṃ ca gāṇḍīvasya tathā mama \|*  
*sāratā yābhavanmūrtiḥsa gataḥ puruṣottamaḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

The highest spirit, who was the power invested in my missiles, arrows and my bow, Gāṇḍīva, has left. 45

## \|\| 5.38.46 \|\|

*yasyāvalokanādasmāñchrīrjayaḥ saṃpadunnatiḥ \|*  
*na tatyāja sa govindastyaktvāsmānbhagavāngataḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

As long as we could see him, our glory, victory, fortune and prosperity never failed, but Lord Govinda has deserted us and gone away. 46

## \|\| 5.38.47 \|\|

*bhīṣmadroṇāṅgarājādyā s tathā duryodhanādayaḥ \|*  
*yatprabhāvena nirdagdhāḥ sa kṛṣṇastyaktavānbhuvam \|\| 47 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa—by whose prowess Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Karṇa, king of Aṅga, Duryodhana and the other Kauravas were consumed—has left this world. 47

## \|\| 5.38.48 \|\|

*niryauvanā gataśrīkā naṣṭacchāyeva medinī \|*  
*vibhāti tāta naikohaṃ virahe tasya cakriṇaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

The earth, like me, has lost its youth, deprived of glory, robbed of beauty. Neither I nor the rest of the world, dear Vyāsa, will shine again without that discus-wielding Kṛṣṇa. 48

## \|\| 5.38.49 \|\|

*yasya prabhāvādbhīṣmādyair mayy agnau śalabhāyatam \|*  
*vinā tenādya kṛṣṇena gopālaurasmi nirjitaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

Because of my faith in him, Bhīṣma and the others were drawn like moths to my flame, but, in Kṛṣṇa’s absence, I’ve been beaten by some cattle-herders. 49

## \|\| 5.38.50 \|\|

*gāṇḍīvastriṣu lokeṣu khyātiṃ yadanubhāvataḥ \|*  
*gatastena vinā'bhīralaguḍaiḥ sa tiraskṛtaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

With my faith in Kṛṣṇa, Gāṇḍīva, my bow, was famed in all three worlds, but, without him, it’s no match for tribal staves. 50

## \|\| 5.38.51 \|\|

*strīsahasrāṇyanekāni mannāthāni mahāmune \|*  
*yatato mama nītāni dasyubhir laguḍāyudhaiḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

Despite my efforts, mighty sage, the women in my charge were carried off in thousands by brigands armed with sticks. 51

## \|\| 5.38.52 \|\|

*ānīyamānamābhīraiḥ kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇāvarodhanam \|*  
*hṛtaṃ yaṣṭipraharaṇaiḥ paribhūya balaṃ mama \|\| 52 \|\|*

All of Kṛṣṇa’s dependants have been seized by Ābhīras, who defeated me with blows of staves and robbed me of my strength, Vyāsa. 52

## \|\| 5.38.53 \|\|

*niḥśrīkatā na me citraṃ yajjīvāmi tadadbhutam \|*  
*nīcāvamānapaṅkāṅkī nirlajjosmi pitāmaha \|\| 53 \|\|*

It’s no surprise that I am pale—it’s amazing that I’m still alive! Heaped with abuse by inferior men, grandsire, I’ve no shame left. 53

## \|\| 5.38.54 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca*  
*alaṃ te vrīḍayā pārtha na tvaṃ śocitum arhasi \|*  
*avehi sarvabhūteṣu kālasya gatirīdṛśī \|\| 54 \|\|*

Vyāsa:

That’s enough of modesty, Arjuna. Grieving doesn’t suit you. You need to understand no being is immune from the vagaries of Time. 54

## \|\| 5.38.55 \|\|

*kālo bhavāya bhūtānāmabhavāya ca pāṇḍava \|*  
*kālamūlam idaṃ jñātvā bhava sthairyaparo 'rjuna \|\| 55 \|\|*

Time brings all things into being, son of Pāṇḍu, then destroys them. Once you understand the world is ruled by Time, Arjuna, hold on to strength while it endures. 55

## \|\| 5.38.56 \|\|

*nadyaḥ samudrā girayaḥsakalā ca vasuṃdharā \|*  
*devā manuṣyāḥ paśavastaravaś ca sarīsṛpāḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

The rivers, oceans, mountains and all the world with gods and mortals, animals, trees, insects and reptiles were all brought forth by Time. 56

## \|\| 5.38.57 \|\|

*sṛṣṭāḥ kālena kālena punar yāsyanti saṃkṣayam \|*  
*kālātmakamidaṃ sarvaṃ jñātvā śamamavāpnuhi \|\| 57 \|\|*

And Time will bring them to an end again. When you see that Time is at the heart of this, you’ll find peace. 57

## \|\| 5.38.58 \|\|

*kālasvarūpī bhagavānkṛṣṇaḥ kamalalocanaḥ \|*  
*yaccāttha kṛṣṇamāhātmyaṃ tattathaiva dhanañjaya \|\| 58 \|\|*

Kṛṣṇa’s greatness is just as you’ve described it, Dhanaṃjaya. He came to earth to ease the burden of the world. 58

## \|\| 5.38.59 \|\|

*bhārāvatārakāryārthamavatīrṇasya medinīm \|*  
*bhārākrāntā dharā yātā devānāṃ samitiṃ purā \|\| 59 \|\|*

In former times, the Earth, oppressed by weight, approached the assembly of the gods, and Janārdana, who also takes the form of Time, descended to the world on her behalf. 59

## \|\| 5.38.60 \|\|

*tadarthamavatīrṇo 'sau kālarūpī janārdanaḥ \|*  
*tacca niṣpādinaṃ kāryamaśeṣā bhūbhujo hatāḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

That task has been accomplished, Arjuna, as all the kings are slain and the race of Andhakas and Vṛṣṇis is no more. 60

## \|\| 5.38.61 \|\|

*vaṣṇyandhakakulaṃ sarvaṃ tathā pārthopasaṃhṛtam \|*  
*na kiñcidanyatkartavyaṃ tasya bhūmitale prabhoḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

In this world, the lord has nothing more to do and, hence, his task completed, he has departed in accordance with his desire. 61

## \|\| 5.38.62 \|\|

*ato gataḥsa bhagavānkatakṛtyo yathecchayā \|*  
*sṛṣṭiṃ sarge karotyeṣa devadevaḥ sthitau sthitim \|*  
*antentāya samarthoyaṃ sāṃprataṃ vai yathā gataḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

At the moment of creation, the god of gods brings forth the world; while it exists, he keeps it and, in the end, he is capable of its destruction, as he has shown just now. 62

## \|\| 5.38.63 \|\|

*tasmātyārtha na saṃtāpastvayā kāryaḥ parābhave \|*  
*bhavanti bhāvāḥ kāleṣu puruṣāṇāṃ yataḥ stutiḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

Don’t dwell on being beaten, Arjuna, as men have prowess only for a fraction of their lives. 63

## \|\| 5.38.64 \|\|

*tvayaikena hatā bhīṣmadroṇakarmādayo raṇe \|*  
*teṣāmarjuna kālotthaḥ kiṃ nyūnābhibhavo na saḥ \|\| 64 \|\|*

Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Karṇa and the other kings were slain by you alone, but their death was really brought about by Time. Wasn’t your defeat by lesser men the same? 64

## \|\| 5.38.65 \|\|

*viṣṇostasya prabhāveṇa yathā teṣāṃ parābhavaḥ \|*  
*kṛtastathaiva bhavato dasyubhyaḥ sa parābhavaḥ \|\| 65 \|\|*

Just as you defeated them with your faith in Viṣṇu, your defeat by bandits is, in the end, attributable to Time. 65

## \|\| 5.38.66 \|\|

*sa deveśaḥ śarīrāṇi samāviśya jagatsthitim \|*  
*karoti sarvabhūtānāṃ nāśamante jagatpatiḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

That deity, master of the universe, assuming different forms, preserves the world and finally destroys creation. 66

## \|\| 5.38.67 \|\|

*bhagodaye te kainteya sahāyo 'bhūjjanārdanaḥ \|*  
*tathānte tadvipakṣāste keśavena vināśitāḥ \|\| 67 \|\|*

During your own lifetime Janārdana was your ally, son of Kuntī, but now your life is drawing to a close, your enemies are overseen by Keśava. 67

## \|\| 5.38.68 \|\|

*kaḥ śraddaddhyātsagāṅgoyānhanyāstvaṃ kauravāniti \|*  
*ābhīrebhyaś ca bhavataḥ kaḥ śraddadhyātparābhavam \|\| 68 \|\|*

Who’d believe that you would slay the Kauravas, including Bhīṣma, son of Gaṅgā? And who’d believe the Ābhīras defeated you? 68

## \|\| 5.38.69 \|\|

*pārthaitatsarvabhūtasya harerlīlāvicoṣṭitam \|*  
*tvayā yatkaurāvā dhvastā yadābhīrair bhavāñjitaḥ \|\| 69 \|\|*

That you, the son of Pṛthā, beat the Kauravas but were yourself defeated by Ābhīras is just the playful sport of Hari, the universal being. 69

## \|\| 5.38.70 \|\|

*gṛhītā dasyubhir yāś ca bhavāñchocati tāḥstriyaḥ \|*  
*etasyāhaṃ yathāvṛttaṃ kathayāmi tavārjuna \|\| 70 \|\|*

You’re grieving for the women seized by bandits, Arjuna, but I’ll tell you why this happened. 70

## \|\| 5.38.71 \|\|

*aṣṭāvakaḥ purā vipro jalavāsarato 'bhavat \|*  
*bahūnvarṣagaṇānpārtha gṛṇanbrahma sanātanam \|\| 71 \|\|*

Long ago, a brahmin known as Aṣṭāvakra vowed to live in water, son of Pṛthā, and he spent many years in worship of the everlasting Absolute. 71

## \|\| 5.38.72 \|\|

*jiteṣvasurasaṃghaṣu merupṛṣṭhe mahotsavaḥ \|*  
*babhūva tatra gacchantyo dadṛśustaṃ surastriyaḥ \|\| 72 \|\|*

When the armies of the demigods had been defeated, a great celebration was held on the foothills of Mount Meru. Some heavenly womenfolk saw that brahmin as they journeyed to the festival. 72

## \|\| 5.38.73 \|\|

*raṃbhātilottamādyāstu śataśo 'tha sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*tuṣṭuvustaṃ mahātmānaṃ praśaśaṃsuś ca pāṇḍava \|\| 73 \|\|*

Hundreds and thousands of apsarases, Rambhā and Tilottamā among them, son of Pāṇḍu, extolled that great ascetic and sang his praises. 73

## \|\| 5.38.74 \|\|

*ākaṇṭhamagnaṃ salile jaṭābhāravahaṃ munim \|*  
*vinayāvanatāścainaṃ praṇemustotratatparāḥ \|\| 74 \|\|*

Intent upon their eulogies, they reverently bowed before the sage as he stood neck-deep in water, with his matted locks piled upon his head. 74

## \|\| 5.38.75 \|\|

*yathāyathā prasanno 'sau tuṣṭuvustaṃ tathātathā \|*  
*sarvāstāḥ kauravaśreṣṭha taṃ variṣṭhaṃ dvijanmanām \|\| 75 \|\|*

All those apsarases, best of Kauravas, sang the praises that they thought would please that foremost brahmin. 75

## \|\| 5.38.76 \|\|

*aṣṭāvakra uvāca*  
*prasanno 'haṃ mahābhāgā bhavatīnāṃ yadiṣyate \|*  
*mattastadvrayatāṃ sarvaṃ pradāsyāmyatidurlabham \|\| 76 \|\|*

Aṣṭāvakra:

‘You’ve pleased me, blessed women. Ask for anything you desire, and I shall grant it, even if it’s hard to come by.’ 76

## \|\| 5.38.77 \|\|

*raṃbhātilottamādyāstaṃ vaidikyopsarasobruvan \|*  
*prasanne tvayyaparyāptaṃ kimasmākamiti dvija \|\| 77 \|\|*

Rambhā, Tilottamā and other apsarases, celebrated in the Vedas, replied to him, ‘Isn’t it sufficient, brahmin, that you’re pleased with us?’ 77

## \|\| 5.38.78 \|\|

*itarāstvabruvanvipra prasanno bhagavānyadi \|*  
*tadicchāmaḥ patiṃ prāptuṃ viprendra puruṣottama \|\| 78 \|\|*

But some among them said, ‘Brahmin, if indeed you’re gratified, we’d like to have the best of mankind for our husband.’ 78

## \|\| 5.38.79 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca*  
*evaṃ bhaviṣyatītyuktvā hyuttatāra jalānmuniḥ \|*  
*tamuttīrṇaṃ ca dṛdṛśurvirūpaṃ vakramaṣṭadhā \|\| 79 \|\|*

Vyāsa:

‘As you wish,’ the sage replied, emerging from the waters, but then the apsarases saw that he was very ugly, as his body was deformed (vakra) in eight respects (aṣṭa). 79

## \|\| 5.38.80 \|\|

*taṃ dṛṣṭvā gūhamānānāṃ yāsāṃ hāsaḥ sphuṭo 'bhavat \|*  
*tāḥ śaśāpa muniḥ kopamavāpya kurunandana \|\| 80 \|\|*

Seeing this, some tried to hide their mirth, but others burst out laughing, joy of Kurus, and the sage in fury cursed them: 80

## \|\| 5.38.81 \|\|

*yasmādvikṛtarūpaṃ māṃ matvā hīsā vamānanā \|*  
*bhavatībhiḥ kṛtā tasmādetaṃ śāpaṃ dadāmi vaḥ \|\| 81 \|\|*

‘Because you think I’m ugly and you mock me, I pronounce this curse on you: 81

## \|\| 5.38.82 \|\|

*matprasādena bhartāraṃ labdhvā tu puruṣottamam \|*  
*macchāpopahatāḥsarvā dasyuhastaṃ gamiṣyatha \|\| 82 \|\|*

Through my blessing, Kṛṣṇa, the best of men, will be your husband, but cursed by me, you’ll all be ruined and fall into the hands of bandits.’ 82

## \|\| 5.38.83 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca*  
*ityudīritamākarṇya munistābhiḥ prasāditaḥ \|*  
*punaḥsuredralokaṃ vai prāha bhūyo gamiṣyatha \|\| 83 \|\|*

Vyāsa:

Hearing this, the apsarases soothed the sage to some extent, so he promised in the end that they’d attain the realm of Indra, king of gods. 83

## \|\| 5.38.84 \|\|

*evaṃ tasya muneḥ śāpādaṣṭāvakrasya cakriṇam \|*  
*bhartāraṃ prāpya tā yātā dasyuhastaṃ surāṅganāḥ \|\| 84 \|\|*

That’s why, on account of Aṣṭāvakra’s curse, those splendid women first had Keśava as their husband, but were later seized by brigands. 84

## \|\| 5.38.85 \|\|

*tattvāya nātra kartavyaḥ śoko 'lpopi hi pāṇḍava \|*  
*tenaivākhilanāthena sarvaṃ tadupasaṃhṛtam \|\| 85 \|\|*

For this reason, son of Pāṇḍu, you shouldn’t grieve for them at all. All this ruin was brought about by the universal lord. 85

## \|\| 5.38.86 \|\|

*bhavatāṃ copasaṃhāra āsannastena pāṇḍava \|*  
*balaṃ lejas tathā vīryaṃ māhātmyaṃ copasaṃhṛtam \|\| 86 \|\|*

And now your own demise is near at hand, as he has withdrawn your strength and glory, your courage and your greatness. 86

## \|\| 5.38.87 \|\|

*jātasya niyato mṛtyuḥ patanaṃ ca tathonnateḥ \|*  
*viprayogāvamānastu saṃyogaḥ saṃcaye kṣayaḥ \|\| 87 \|\|*

Death is certain for every being that’s born, just as falling is assured for those who’ve risen. Union ends in separation, and all that’s gathered will be scattered. 87

## \|\| 5.38.88 \|\|

*vijñāya na budhāḥ śokaṃ na harṣamupayānti ye \|*  
*teṣāmevetare ceṣṭāṃ śikṣantaḥsaṃti tādṛśāḥ \|\| 88 \|\|*

Knowing this, wise people neither grieve nor cheer, and others who learn their habits become like them. 88

## \|\| 5.38.89 \|\|

*tasmāt tvayā naraśreṣṭha jñātvaitadbhrātṛbhiḥ saha \|*  
*parityajyākhilaṃ tantraṃ gantavyaṃ tapase vanam \|\| 89 \|\|*

For this reason, knowing this, you should leave behind this tangled life, outstanding man, and retire to the forest with your brothers to undertake austerities. 89

## \|\| 5.38.90 \|\|

*tadraccha dharmarājāya nivedyautadvaco mama \|*  
*paraśvo bhrātṛbhiḥ sārdha yathā yāsi tathā kuru \|\| 90 \|\|*

Go and give my orders to Yudhiṣṭhira, king of virtue, and prepare to set out with your brothers—not tomorrow, but the following day. 90

## \|\| 5.38.91 \|\|

*ityuktobhyetya pārthābhyāṃ yamābhyāṃ ca sahārjunaḥ \|*  
*dṛṣṭaṃ caivānubhūtaṃ ca sarvamākhyātavāṃs tathā \|\| 91 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, Arjuna met the other sons of Pṛthu, Yudhiṣṭhira and Bhīma, and the twins Nakula and Sahadeva, and told them all he’d seen and done. 91

## \|\| 5.38.92 \|\|

*vyāsavākyaṃ ca te sarve śrutvārjunamukheritam \|*  
*rājye parīkṣitaṃ kṛtvā yayuḥ pāṇḍusutā vanam \|\| 92 \|\|*

Hearing Vyāsa’s words conveyed by Arjuna, the five sons of Pāṇḍu set Parīkṣit on the throne and departed for the forest. 92

## \|\| 5.38.93 \|\|

*ityetattava maitreya vistareṇa mayoditam \|*  
*jātasya yadyadorvaśe vāsudevasya ceṣṭitam \|\| 93 \|\|*

Thus, Maitreya, I’ve described for you at length the deeds of Vāsudeva when he was born in Yadu’s lineage. 93
